# fused-render — Decision Log & Project Context

Purpose: this file + `SPEC.md` + `ARCHITECTURE.md` carry the *entire* design conversation.
A fresh session should be able to continue the project from these three files alone.

---

## What we are building (one paragraph)

A fully local file explorer: a Python server on `127.0.0.1` + a browser UI to browse the **entire computer's** filesystem and preview files. The core primitive is **renderable HTML**: any `.html` file previews live and gets an injected JS runtime with (a) `fused.runPython(pyPath, params)` — executes the `main()` function of a local Python file and returns its JSON result — and (b) `fused.params` — string key/values synced into the browser URL, so any view's state is bookmarkable/refreshable. **Preview templates** (parquet table, image, text …) are just renderable HTML files shipped inside the app that receive the target file as a reserved `_file` param — the same two primitives are the entire rendering power of the system. Local only, forever; never cloud — reframed, not overturned, by mounts (D102): the app itself still only ever reads local absolute paths; remote storage enters exclusively as an rclone-managed *mount* presenting as a local folder, so nothing downstream of the mount layer speaks a cloud protocol.

## Project timeline / conversation state

- 2026-07-03: initial spec drafted (SPEC.md), broad v1 with security layer, decorators, worker pools.
- 2026-07-03/04: extended design discussion trimmed it down hard (all decisions below).
- 2026-07-04: M1 scope locked; blueprint written (ARCHITECTURE.md); build delegated to a subagent with the main session architect-reviewing.
- 2026-07-04 (later): M1 shipped + verified; M2 sidebar/bookmarks shipped; dark theme, sortable listing, `_file` URL cleanup, no-cache, shell.js ES-module split (D23–D28). An authoring skill for agents lives in `skills/fused-render-authoring/` with an eval workspace beside it.
- Pre-existing partial code: `fused_render/_child.py`, `fused_render/executor.py`, `fused_render/__init__.py`, `pyproject.toml` (written before discussion finished; kept — they match the final design).
- 2026-07-07: shell migrated to React (D52) on the owner's direction; templates/runtime/server contracts unchanged.
- 2026-07-27: appearance follows the OS by default with a System/Light/Dark pin (§30, D134); the Linux tray glyph follows the desktop panel's colour scheme (D135).
- 2026-07-10: M16 pinned view specced + shipped (§25, D97): menu-bar popover with a live WKWebView of one pinned file's `/embed` view, detachable into a floating always-on-top window. Same day, D98: dropped the status-item menu entirely — the popover (with a native header row of all actions) is the app's single surface.

## Decisions (all explicitly made by the project owner)

| # | Decision | Choice | Rationale / rejected alternatives |
|---|---|---|---|
| D1 | Python entry point | Function named `main()` — plain convention, no decorator, no import | Rejected `@render` decorator: convention simpler, file works standalone |
| D2 | Filesystem scope | **Whole computer. No root-scoping concept at all.** `--start-dir` is only the initial UI location | Rejected serve-root model entirely |
| D3 | Security | **None in v1.** Only freebie kept: bind 127.0.0.1. Token auth, Origin/Host checks, sandboxed iframe = documented follow-ups (SPEC §9 keeps the threat note for later) | Owner: "forget about security, make it as simple as possible; all things are follow-ups" |
| D4 | HTML isolation | Originally chose sandboxed iframe + postMessage bridge; **superseded by D3** → plain same-origin iframe, runtime fetches server API directly | Bridge protocol deleted from scope |
| D5 | Python execution | Fresh subprocess per call, 30 s timeout | Rejected warm pool (later upgrade, API-compatible) and in-process (crash risk) |
| D6 | Return types | JSON-native only; DataFrame/bytes → clear error telling user to convert | Arrow/DataFrame encoding = follow-up for data-heavy templates |
| D7 | Param values | Strings only. `set()` rejects non-strings. User JSON-encodes himself | Rejected auto-typing (footguns) and JSON escape hatch (unneeded now) |
| D8 | Param↔URL sync | URL wins on load; first param write on a pristine history entry pushes one entry (flagged `fusedParamEntry` on `history.state`), every later write replaces — Back restores the as-loaded params, churn costs at most one entry per visit. **AMENDED BY D268:** "first write" means the first write attributable to a USER GESTURE; a write made before this document has seen any interaction (a page seeding a computed default at load) replaces instead and leaves the entry pristine, so the push is still there for the user's first real change. **EXTENDED BY D271:** a caller may ask for that same replace path explicitly (`set(k, v, {history: "replace"})`), because the gate is read when the write LANDS and a boot path continuing behind an `await` is already post-gesture | Was "`replaceState` always, no entries". First-change-push chosen over settle-debounce (timers/heuristics) and per-param opt-in (config burden). Flag on `history.state` travels with the entry, so after Back to the pristine entry the next *user-caused* write correctly pushes again. Read the last clause with D268: unqualified, it says a re-seed after Back re-pushes — which is exactly the trap D268 closes |
| D9 | Py path resolution | Relative → against the HTML file's dir; absolute → anywhere on disk (consistent with D2) | |
| D10 | Re-run on param change | Manual: author calls `runPython` inside `params.onChange` | Declarative binding = possible later layer |
| D11 | Template registry | **Server-side** (single source of truth). `stat` response carries `template` field; shell obeys | Rejected shell-side JS registry (migration pain once user overrides exist) |
| D12 | Shell dispatch | Exactly three-way: **template > html > fallback**. Shell has zero file-type special cases; image & text are ordinary templates | Owner explicitly moved image/text out of the shell into templates |
| D13 | M1 template set | parquet (paged table via pyarrow) + image + text. All other formats follow up | |
| D14 | Python env | The env the server was launched from. Good enough for v1 | Per-project venv detection = maybe later |
| D15 | Streaming results | Not required. Plain request/response | |
| D16 | Frontend stack | No framework, no build step; plain JS/CSS | Descoped Vite/React from original spec |
| D17 | Error DX | runPython failure the page doesn't catch → red traceback overlay auto-shown in iframe; python `print()` echoed to browser console | Architect default, unobjected |
| D18 | pyarrow | Normal dependency (not extras) | Simplest install UX; revisit if weight bothers |

### M2 decisions (2026-07-04, post-M1)

| # | Decision | Choice | Rationale / rejected alternatives |
|---|---|---|---|
| D19 | Next feature | Left sidebar: Home entry + bookmarks of the current right-side view | Owner-defined scope |
| D20 | Bookmark semantics | Save the **exact URL verbatim** at capture time (incl. all params); click = plain redirect; renamable + deletable | No structured bookmark model — URL is the whole state by design (PR-1/PR-7) |
| D21 | Bookmark storage | Browser localStorage (`fused.bookmarks`) | Owner chose simplest over server-side JSON file; export path trivial if migrated later |
| D22 | Bookmark naming | Default = basename of viewed path; rename covers the rest | Rejected params-in-name (noise) and prompt-at-create (friction) |

### Post-M2 refinements (2026-07-04, all owner-directed unless noted)

| # | Decision | Choice |
|---|---|---|
| D23 | Look | Dark theme everywhere; single palette via CSS vars; "Raw" header action removed (fallback card keeps a Download link) |
| D24 | Bookmark UX | "+ Bookmark" text button (not a star glyph); hover card shows target path + saved params; active bookmark + starred button highlighted (architect) |
| D25 | Listing sort | Sortable columns; sort state in URL params, dirs group first (URL-is-state philosophy) |
| D26 | `_file` plumbing | `_file` rides on the template iframe's own URL, not the shell URL (no path duplication); runtime falls back to shell URL for manual/legacy links |
| D27 | Caching | `Cache-Control: no-cache` on every response — stale JS caused half-old-UI bugs |
| D28 | Frontend structure | shell.js split into ES modules (see ARCHITECTURE §1/§6): one-way deps, pure store/format modules, no build step kept (architect, owner asked) |

### M3 decisions — DMG distribution (2026-07-04)

| # | Decision | Choice | Rationale / rejected alternatives |
|---|---|---|---|
| D29 | Packaging | Every desktop build ships its own CPython 3.12 and runs `python3 -m fused_render.app` — but from a **different source per platform**: macOS copies Homebrew's **framework** python into the .app, Linux and Windows unpack **python-build-standalone** (`cpython-3.12*`, uv-managed) | Rejected PyInstaller freeze: breaks subprocess executor (sys.executable) + seals env. **Corrected Jul 2026** (flagged stale in D177): this row used to say the .app bundles python-build-standalone, which was never true — py2app builds a standalone .app by copying a `Python.framework` tree, and a standalone build has no framework layout to copy, so `build_dmg.sh` hard-FATALs on a non-framework interpreter (`PYTHONFRAMEWORK` empty). The inaccuracy was not free: it is part of why D175's `PYTHONHOME` problem was a surprise, since the framework copy is exactly what lets the bundled interpreter self-locate its own `sys.prefix` — the property `app_interpreter()`'s probe now verifies instead of assuming (D172) |
| D30 | User py env in app | Bundled interpreter ONLY, no override; pyarrow+pandas preinstalled | Owner chose hermetic-simplest over override-setting; supersedes D14 for packaged app (dev installs keep D14) |
| D31 | Lifecycle | Menu bar icon via rumps: Open / Copy URL / Quit; LSUIElement, no Dock | Rejected fully-headless (invisible process) |
| D32 | Signing | Ad-hoc unsigned for now; signing/notarization hook left in build script | Developer ID ($99/yr) deferred until public distribution. **(Realized by D73: Developer ID signing + notarization now implemented in `build_dmg.sh`, credential-gated with ad-hoc fallback.)** |

### M3.5 decisions — packaging rework (2026-07-04, after owner-requested research)

| # | Decision | Choice | Rationale / rejected alternatives |
|---|---|---|---|
| D33 | Bundle builder | **py2app** replaces the hand-rolled shim+tarball assembly | Canonical rumps packager; modern py2app ships a real python interpreter in-bundle so `sys.executable` subprocess executor works; its stub executable gives proper LaunchServices/AppKit process identity — hand-rolled bash-shim launches were the likely cause of flaky NSStatusItem/event behavior under Finder |
| D34 | Dock presence | **Regular app (no LSUIElement)** — Dock icon + menu bar ✦ both | Owner expects Dock presence; Dock right-click → Quit permanently solves lifecycle confusion. Supersedes D31's LSUIElement detail (menu bar item stays) |
| D35 | DMG creation | `dmgbuild` (config-driven) replaces raw hdiutil; **Briefcase external-app mode** = designated future path for sign+notarize+DMG when Developer ID lands | Full Briefcase rejected: its app template breaks sys.executable. **(Superseded by D73 for signing/notarization: done in-place with `codesign`/`notarytool` in `build_dmg.sh`, not Briefcase. `dmgbuild` for the DMG itself stands.)** |

### Editing decisions (2026-07-05)

| # | Decision | Choice | Rationale / rejected alternatives |
|---|---|---|---|
| D36 | Cross-origin POST guard | The two mutating/executing endpoints (`POST /api/run`, `POST /api/fs/write`) require a custom `X-Fused: 1` request header; missing/wrong → 403 | Read endpoints are already safe cross-origin (the browser blocks a foreign page from reading our response), but a POST can be fired blind via a no-cors fetch by any website open in the same browser. A custom header forces a CORS preflight, which fails cross-origin since we send no CORS headers, so only our own same-origin pages get through. **NOT authentication** (D3 stands — no tokens, no user accounts): it only blocks blind cross-origin POSTs; anything that can already run our JS is unaffected. Cheap and removable if the descoped security layer lands. |
| D37 | File editing | `POST /api/fs/write` (atomic temp-file + `os.replace`, preserves mode) with optimistic locking on `st_mtime`; `code_template.html` is now an always-editable CodeMirror buffer (Save / Cmd+S, conflict banner offering Reload/Overwrite) | Supersedes SPEC §1 non-goal "no editing in v1". Optimistic lock (409 on stale/deleted mtime) chosen over no-lock (silent clobber) and over server-side locking (needs state, D-against SV-1). HTML "Source" view reuses the same editable template, so HTML editing came free |
| D38 | Bookmark updating | Opening a bookmark **via sidebar click** arms it (sessionStorage `fused.armedBookmark`); when view params diverge from the saved url on the same page, an "Update bookmark" button appears left of "+ Bookmark" and overwrites the bookmark's url with the current one. Changing pathname disarms **permanently** — only a new sidebar click re-arms (owner-chosen over re-arm-on-return). Shell observes param changes by wrapping `history.replaceState` (the iframe runtime writes params through it; no native event exists) | Keeps D20 verbatim-URL model — update = overwrite url, no structured diffing. sessionStorage so tracking survives refresh but not new tabs |

### Embed mode (2026-07-06)

| # | Decision | Choice | Rationale / rejected alternatives |
|---|---|---|---|
| D39 | Embed mode | `/embed/<path>?params` serves the same shell chrome-free: sidebar/breadcrumb/preview-header hidden via `body.embed` CSS, sidebar never initialized. Router prefix is dynamic (`/embed/` vs `/view/`, fixed at page load), so refresh, in-listing navigation, and param sync stay inside embed. View modules stay embed-unaware — CSS does all hiding | One server route + prefix switch reuses the whole shell; rejected separate embed page (duplication) and query-flag (`?embed=1` would collide with the user param namespace, D7/D8) |
| D40 | HTML view mode param | Rendered/Source toggle driven by reserved shell param `_mode=render\|source` (absent = render, and switching back to render deletes the key). Clicks write it via `history.replaceState` (D8); `?_mode=source` on view/embed/bookmark URLs opens straight into source | URL-is-state (D8/D20/D25): mode was the last piece of view state living only in JS. `_`-prefix already hidden from `fused.params` by the runtime, so no collision with user params |

### M4 — Live editing: autosave + auto-reload (2026-07-06, full spec = SPEC §13)

| # | Decision | Choice | Rationale / rejected alternatives |
|---|---|---|---|
| D41 | Editor autosave | code_template autosaves 250 ms after last edit (debounced), always-on, no toggle. Same optimistic lock as manual save; 409 → conflict banner shows and autosave suspends until resolved — never auto-overwrites. Manual Save/Cmd+S stay and cancel the pending timer | Always-on chosen over a setting: live-preview loop is the point; half-typed states reaching disk are normal (D17 overlay explains broken intermediates). Auto-overwrite rejected — would reduce the lock to decoration |
| D42 | File-change feed + auto-reload | New `GET /api/fs/events?path=A&path=B` SSE endpoint (async 200 ms stat loop, no watchdog dep, no X-Fused — read-only). All reload logic lives in the injected runtime: each rendered page watches its own file + `_file` + every `runPython` file (learned from new `resolved_py` field on `/api/run`, recorded on failures too) and reloads itself on change (300 ms debounce). `fused.autoReload(false)` opts out — the editor uses it; watching starts at DOMContentLoaded so the opt-out is race-free. Directory listings watch their dir and re-render | Full iframe reload over fine-grained re-run: runtime can't replay what a page did with a python result, and URL-is-state (D8/D20/D25) makes reload lossless. Runtime-side dependency tracking over a server-side dep map (server state/staleness for knowledge the page already has) and over static html analysis (misses dynamic paths). SSE over client polling (chatty) and over WebSocket (two-way machinery for one-way events) |

### Bookmark reordering (2026-07-06)

| # | Decision | Choice | Rationale / rejected alternatives |
|---|---|---|---|
| D43 | Bookmark drag-reorder | Native HTML5 drag-and-drop on sidebar rows; localStorage array order **is** display order (no `order` field, no schema change) — `moveBookmark(id, postRemovalIndex)` splices. Whole row draggable (link gets `draggable="false"` so the browser doesn't drag the URL); insert indicator via box-shadow (no layout shift); drag disabled during rename; tooltip suppressed while dragging. `draggedId` reset in `drop` as well as `dragend` — Chrome skips `dragend` when the re-render detaches the source row | Native DnD over vendoring Sortable.js (~40 KB for one short vertical list) and over pointer-event reimplementation (more code, same result). No drag handle — whole row is a bigger target and rows have no competing drag affordance |

### Bookmark folders (2026-07-06)

| # | Decision | Choice | Rationale / rejected alternatives |
|---|---|---|---|
| D44 | One-level bookmark folders | Drop a bookmark on the middle of a top-level bookmark → folder created around both (rename starts immediately). Same localStorage array, folders are `{type:"folder", name, collapsed, children:[bookmarks]}` — old flat data loads unchanged, no migration. Drop zones: 25/50/25 (above/into/below) where combine is possible, half-split otherwise; folders reorder at top level only, never nest; child rows ignore folder drags. Empty folders auto-pruned after every mutation (move, delete, create). Collapse persisted. Folder UI: inline-SVG folder icon in the star-glyph slot (icon + name toggle fold, no chevron), semibold name, child-count pill pinned far right that swaps for the hover actions, children indented behind a 1 px rail; collapsed folder holding the active bookmark gets the active highlight. **(One-level limit superseded by D121 — folders now nest to arbitrary depth; the UI language here otherwise stands.)** | One level deep over arbitrary nesting: schema already recursive so depth is pure UI work later, but nested drop-target semantics (cycle guards, indent-sensitive zones, collapsed-edge ambiguity) ~2.5× the cost for a rarely-needed depth. `children` array over flat `parentId` (rendering + reorder messier). Chevron removed after review — icon alignment with star glyphs reads better in a narrow sidebar; count pill carries the collapsed signal |

### M5 — Layout mode: split panes (2026-07-06, full spec = SPEC §14)

| # | Decision | Choice | Rationale / rejected alternatives |
|---|---|---|---|
| D45 | Layout mode | `/view/_panel?_layout=<tree>&<merged params>` (also `/embed/_panel` — layout mode works under both prefixes, so a layout can itself be embedded) — sentinel pathname intercepted by the shell `route()` before stat (zero server changes). Pane tree in reserved `_layout` param: `,` = row, `;` = column, `()` nesting; each segment = pane fs path + optional pane-local query, delimiter chars percent-encoded. Panes are `/embed/<path>` iframes (D39) with a pane bar: crumbs, split right/down (duplicates pane), maximize (transient, not in URL), close (a close that leaves one pane — or closing the last pane — exits to `<prefix>/<pane path>` in the active prefix; single-pane layouts exist only via hand-typed URLs). Entry = split right/down icon buttons in crumb-actions carrying current path+params (row `,` / col `;`). Layout view observes pane URLs (iframe load + pane `fused:urlchange`) and re-encodes `_layout` via replaceState — so bookmarks (D20) and the update-bookmark flow (D38) capture layout+params verbatim with zero bookmark-layer changes. New module `views/panel.js` (imports router only) | Reusing embed pages makes every pane a full navigable mini-explorer for free; rejected panes-as-bare-`/render`-iframes (loses listing/navigation/templates dispatch). Sentinel path over a new top-level route (`/layout`): stays inside the `/view`+`/embed` prefixes the server and router already handle. Sentinel pathname `_panel`, tree param `_layout` (sentinel renamed same-day from `_layout`, no backward compat kept). Reserved `_layout` param over `?layout=`: `_` prefix is already hidden from `fused.params` (PR-6), no user-param collision |
| D46 | Runtime param target | `fused.params` reads/writes the **topmost same-origin ancestor** window's URL (was: direct parent) — in normal view/embed, parent = top, so nothing changes; in layout mode all panes share the layout URL, so param merging and same-key cross-pane sync are structural. Notification: shell wraps pushState too (was: replaceState only) to dispatch `fused:urlchange`; runtime listens on the target window and notifies `onChange` only when the non-reserved param snapshot actually changed (diff guard kills loops/dupes; `set()`'s direct notify removed in favor of the event path) | Chosen over a manual sync layer in the layout module (watch each pane URL, merge up, propagate down, loop-guard — more code, more failure modes). Trade-off accepted: params are global session state by definition; pages wanting pane-private state namespace their own keys (documented, not enforced) |

### Post-M6 audit cleanup (2026-07-06)

| # | Decision | Choice | Rationale / rejected alternatives |
|---|---|---|---|
| D49 | Layout-family naming + codec ownership | Vocabulary line drawn: **"layout" = the family** (the `_layout` param, `views/layout-codec.js`), **modes name themselves** (`renderPanel`/`stopPanel`/`panelUrl`/`.panel-*`/"Panel" label; tabs already consistent). Codec owns everything both modes share: the `_layout` codec, embed URL helpers, and the `fused:urlchange` hook (`attachEmbedUrlChange`/`detachEmbedUrlChange`, one expando `_fusedUrlHooked`); it imports `router.js` only (for the single `EMBED_PREFIX` — its duplicate + dead export deleted). Breadcrumb imports the segment encoder from the codec directly (panel.js re-export removed). Panel pane DOM now built via `createElement` like tabs (drops the escapeHtml/innerHTML template) | Audit findings (architect + reviewer, both flagged the re-export and the duplicated hook machinery). Rejected from the same audit: pane registry Map over DOM `data-id` lookups (vanilla idiom, indirection for nothing); centralizing the `_fusedParamBoundary` flag outside tabs.js (would leak tab knowledge into main.js; contract already documented in runtime/tabs/ARCHITECTURE) |

### M6 — Tab mode + folders-as-tabs (2026-07-06, full spec = SPEC §15)

| # | Decision | Choice | Rationale / rejected alternatives |
|---|---|---|---|
| D47 | Tab mode | `/view/_tab` + `/embed/_tab` sentinel (same interception as `_panel`, zero server changes); tab list = flat top-level `,` row of the same `_layout` codec (nested structure defensively flattened to leaves on parse). Tabs are `/embed/<path>` iframes, **lazy-mounted on first activation, kept alive hidden** — state survives switching, hidden tabs stay param-synced (runtime listens on top window, D46). Params are **tab-independent — no merged pool** (inverts D45's pooling for this mode): the tab shell sets `window._fusedParamBoundary = true` and the runtime climb (D46) stops below a boundary-marked ancestor, so each tab's pages target their own embed URL; the full pane query (user params included) is captured segment-local by the ordinary `_layout` sync. A segment path may itself be `_panel`/`_tab` — nested layouts flow through the ordinary embed pipeline; a nested panel keeps merged-pool semantics among its own panes (climb stops at the panel shell) while isolated from other tabs. Tab bar: label = basename of live path, per-tab ×, trailing `+` (new tab at start dir); **active tab NOT in URL** (refresh/bookmark restores first tab). Last tab close exits to plain view. Codec extracted to shared `views/layout-codec.js` (panel.js, new tabs.js, breadcrumb.js import it) | Keep-alive over recreate-on-switch: preserves scroll/editor state; hidden tabs stay live for free. Tab-independent params (owner call, reversing the initial merged design): a folder of bookmarks must reproduce each bookmark exactly — pooling would let the last child clobber same-key siblings; the boundary flag keeps the isolation structural (one runtime `if`, no sync layer). Active tab left out of URL: encoding it would flash "Update bookmark" on every switch (churn > value). Reusing the `_layout` param + codec over a new `_tabs` param: one codec, one escaping story, and panel↔tab URLs stay hand-convertible |
| D48 | Folder opens as tabs | Clicking a bookmark folder's **name/row** composes and opens `/view/_tab?_layout=<children>`: each child's **entire saved query stays segment-local** (D47 — every bookmark keeps exactly its own params, no hoisting, no collisions). Opening also expands a collapsed folder (sidebar mirrors the tabs); the **folder glyph** keeps the plain collapse/expand toggle (was: name and glyph both toggled). Folder click arms nothing (a folder isn't a bookmark); ★ Bookmark saves the composed URL as a normal bookmark, which then gets the full D38 update flow | Folder-as-dashboard was the original ask behind tab mode; a tab must reproduce its bookmark verbatim. Rejected: breadcrumb "Tabs" entry button (folder-only entry, owner call); encoding folder identity in the URL (a tab layout is a value, not a live link to the folder — later folder edits don't retro-sync, by design) |

### M7 — Custom template overrides (2026-07-06, full spec = SPEC §16)

| # | Decision | Choice | Rationale / rejected alternatives |
|---|---|---|---|
| D50 | User template registry | Self-contained template folders `~/.fused-render/<name>/template.html` (+ sibling `.py` readers/assets; name = arbitrary label) bound to extensions by `~/.fused-render/registry.json`: dotted keys → folder name or `null` (`{".parquet": "geo", ".tar.gz": "archive", ".png": null}`). `null` disables the built-in (shell raw/metadata fallback). Matching: longest-suffix, case-insensitive (dotted keys give compound-extension support the built-in `splitext` table can't express); precedence registry > built-in; `.html`/`.htm` exempt (renderable HTML stays the core semantic). Registry read per resolution (no restart); missing dir/file = no-op; invalid entry (bad JSON, unsafe name, missing template.html) → built-in fallback + `template_error` on stat. Unregistered folders inert (drafts). Server-only change — shell already obeys stat's `template` path, `/render` renders any path, M4 auto-reload gives the live template dev loop for free. Companion repo skill `fused-render-custom-templates` documents layout+registration and delegates authoring to `fused-render-authoring` | Hybrid chosen over pure convention dir (folder-name-as-extension dead-ends on many-ext-to-one and edit-in-place) and over manifest of absolute paths (loses the self-contained folder unit). Folder names as binding (`mp4.mov.webm/`) rejected: renames to change mappings, conflict rules. Convention fallback (unbound folder named like an ext auto-binds) rejected: two sources of truth, deleting a registry line wouldn't unregister. Name validation is correctness, not auth (D3 stands) |

### Layout URL grammar — parenthesized `_layout` (2026-07-06, spec = SPEC §14.1 LM-2)

| # | Decision | Choice | Rationale / rejected alternatives |
|---|---|---|---|
| D51 | `_layout` scope grammar | The entire `_layout` value is **parenthesized and emitted last**: `?global=1&_layout=(/a.html?x=1&y=2,/b.html?z=3)`. Parens delimit param scope visually (inside = iframe-local, outside = global) and structurally: **`&` is literal inside them** — segment queries read exactly as written, no `%26` soup. Wrap is codec-transparent (`parseLayout` already reads `(A,B)` as `A,B`); only the URL layer changed. All shell-query reads go through `layout-codec.js` `splitShellSearch` (balanced-paren scan; safe — literal parens inside segments are codec-escaped, so span parens are structural and balanced): panel.js, tabs.js, breadcrumb's bookmark-equality `sameSearch`. The runtime (standalone, no imports) duplicates the scan as `splitSearch`: get/getAll parse only the non-layout remainder; `set()` reinserts the raw span untouched and last — also fixes the old wart where `URLSearchParams.toString()` percent-mangled a readable `_layout` on the first `fused.params.set()`. **Strict read** (owner call): unwrapped `_layout` = not this grammar, reads as absent, key dropped on next sync — old-format bookmarks fall back to a single start-dir pane/tab. **Paste breakage accepted** (owner call): auto-linkers may eat the trailing `)`; unbalanced span = invalid layout (span still excluded from params), missing-layout fallback | Chosen over "enforce `_layout` last as position-only convention" (look without guarantee; `&` stays escaped) and over "raw tail after `_layout=`" (position-dependent; trailing hand-added `&debug=1` silently swallowed into last segment — parens make position a convention and the boundary explicit, params after `)` stay global). Lenient read of old grammar rejected (owner call): one grammar, no two-format parse paths. Auto-closing missing trailing parens rejected with it: same leniency, masks truncation |

### Shell → React migration (2026-07-07, owner-directed)

| # | Decision | Choice | Rationale / rejected alternatives |
|---|---|---|---|
| D52 | Shell framework | The host shell (routing, listing, preview chrome, sidebar/bookmarks, breadcrumb, panel/tab modes) is a **React 18 + Vite app** in `frontend/` (plain JS + JSX, no TypeScript), built into `fused_render/static/shell-dist/` — **committed output**, like the vendored template libs, so `pip install` needs no node. Supersedes D16 **for the shell only**: templates, `/render`, and the injected `runtime.js` stay plain HTML/JS — the rendering primitive is unchanged; only the logic behind the product scaffolding moved to React. Server change is one line (serve `shell-dist/index.html`). Same DOM structure/class names → `shell.css` reused verbatim (moved to `frontend/src/`). Route model preserved: a navigation epoch remounts the active view (the React equivalent of the vanilla `route()` DOM rebuild); `fused:urlchange` re-renders chrome only. The history replaceState/pushState wrapping stays in the bootstrap — pane runtimes depend on it (D46). Layout-mode iframe discipline: src frozen at mount (React never rewrites it), tabs render as a flat keyed list that only appends/removes (never re-parents), `_fusedParamBoundary` set at render / cleared on unmount | Owner asked for the migration ("templates and main rendering stay HTML, the logic shifts to React"). Vite+JSX chosen over no-build React (htm/vendored ESM): htm-templated code is a worse authoring surface and the repo already has a node build precedent (`scripts/vendor-codemirror`). Committed dist over build-on-install: keeps pipx/pip UX and the local-first, no-CDN stance. Plain JS over TS: matches the existing style constraints; TS is a separate decision if wanted |

| D53 | Shell language | The `frontend/` shell is **TypeScript** (strict; `tsc --noEmit` gated in `npm run build`, Vite/esbuild does the actual transpile). `.ts` for logic (`src/lib/`), `.tsx` for components. API/store/codec shapes are typed at the source (`Config`/`StatResult`/`FsEntry` in `lib/api.ts`, `Bookmark`/`BookmarkFolder` in `lib/bookmarks.ts`, `LayoutLeaf`/`LayoutSplit`/`LayoutNode` in `lib/layout-codec.ts`); window expandos shared with the runtime (`_fusedParamBoundary`, `_fusedUrlHooked`) declared in `src/global.d.ts`. Templates and `runtime.js` stay plain JS (unchanged trust: they are the no-build primitive). Supersedes D52's "plain JS" detail; everything else in D52 stands | Owner asked ("can we convert this to typescript"). Strict over loose: the URL/codec layer is exactly where typos hide, and the codebase is small enough to keep strict cheap. @types/react pinned to ^18 to match react 18.3 |

| D54 | Shell build output | `fused_render/static/shell-dist/` is **not committed** (gitignored). Dev machines are expected to have node: run `cd frontend && npm run build` (or `npm run watch`); the server fails at startup with that exact instruction when the dist is missing. Shipped artifacts get the shell at package-build time: a hatchling build hook (`scripts/hatch_build.py`, wired in pyproject) runs `npm install && npm run build` when building a wheel, and `artifacts` overrides the VCS-ignore so the output ships; the DMG pipeline pip-installs this repo into its build venv, so it inherits the same hook. Editable installs skip the hook (dev owns the build loop). Supersedes D52's committed-output detail; owner call | Owner: "don't commit build files; node on dev machines is fine; shipping puts everything in the dmg or the wheel". Committed dist had the usual costs: binary-ish diffs on every shell change, drift risk between source and checked-in bundle |

### openfused engine migration (2026-07-07) — REVERTED

> **Reverted 2026-07-07 (D67):** D55–D58 rolled back — the openfused backend, `@fused.udf` contract, PEP 723 venvs, and wheelhouse are removed; `executor.py`/`_child.py` with string-coerced params and the `{ok, result, error:{type,message,traceback}}` wire shape are restored. Rows kept for history.

| # | Decision | Choice | Rationale / rejected alternatives |
|---|---|---|---|
| D55 | Execution engine | **openfused** (dist `fused`) `LocalPythonComputeBackend` replaces `executor.py`/`_child.py` — new module `fused_render/engine.py`. Fresh subprocess per call retained (D5's isolation mechanism intact), 30 s timeout kept, result caching disabled (`cache_storage=None`). `requires-python` floor moves to **3.11** (tomllib for PEP 723 parsing; openfused's own floor). The dependency is a **direct-URL reference to the public nightly wheel** (`fused @ https://fused-magic.s3.amazonaws.com/fused-py-nightly/fused-0.0.0.post0-py3-none-any.whl` — owner call 2026-07-07; the wheel's filename version is deliberately unconstrained, the URL's content is what installs). Dev installs may override with the local sandbox checkout editable (`pip install -e ~/Documents/fused/sandbox/openfused`) | One engine shared with the fused platform instead of a bespoke runner; the backend interface gives a path to remote execution (Lambda/hosted) with no fused-render changes. Rejected keeping the hand-rolled executor alongside (two contracts to document and test) |
| D56 | Script contract | **`@fused.udf def main(...)`** replaces the bare `main()` convention (supersedes D1). Params arrive as **raw JSON types — no annotation coercion**; the calling JS owns types and passes numbers as numbers (inverts the old string-coercion model; see SPEC PY-4). Third-party deps are declared per-script via **PEP 723** `# /// script` inline headers, resolved by the backend into **cached per-requirements venvs** (uv, pip fallback, keyed interpreter+reqs under `~/.openfused/venvs`); no header = bare stdlib venv — the server's own env is no longer visible to scripts (supersedes D14/D30's "env that launched the server" model for user code). Plain `result = ...` scripts work for parameterless cases. New /api/run wire shape: see ARCHITECTURE §3 | udf registration is what lets the backend own param delivery and future remote transport; PEP 723 is the standard way to make a single .py carry its deps. Rejected keeping annotation coercion server-side: the engine no longer inspects signatures, and typed-JSON-from-JS is one rule instead of a coercion table |
| D57 | No backward compatibility | Pre-launch product: /api/run consumers, built-in templates, and examples migrated in place; the old `{ok, result, error:{type,message,traceback}}` shape, string-coerced params, and bare-`main()` support are **deleted**, not deprecated | Owner call 2026-07-07. One contract, no dual-shape parse paths (same posture as D51's strict read) |
| D58 | Packaged-app offline installs (wheelhouse) | The .app ships **`Contents/Resources/wheels/`** — wheels for the `[bundled]` extra list + pyarrow (cp312/arm64, transitive deps included), built by `build_dmg.sh` via `pip download`. `fused_render/app.py` sets **`PIP_FIND_LINKS`/`UV_FIND_LINKS`** at startup so openfused's PEP 723 venv builds resolve from the local wheelhouse first, falling back to PyPI when online (FIND_LINKS deliberately, not NO_INDEX — offline-first, not offline-only). Evolves DM-2/D30: the bundled interpreter still ships and runs the server, but user code no longer imports from its site-packages — the wheelhouse is what preserves offline first-use for the popular data stack | Without it, a fresh offline .app install could not run any script with a PEP 723 header (venv build needs an index). Known redundancy: the baked `[bundled]` site-packages are now dead weight for user code — flagged as a future slimming, not done in this change |

### M8 — Template modes (2026-07-07, full spec = SPEC §7/§16 PT-6..PT-12, CT-10..CT-11)

| # | Decision | Choice | Rationale / rejected alternatives |
|---|---|---|---|
| D59 | Folder-per-template + one namespace | Built-in templates refactor from flat files (`parquet_template.html` + `parquet_reader.py`) to **self-contained folders identical in shape to user templates** (D50): `fused_render/templates/<name>/` with `template.html` (required), sibling helpers (`reader.py`), optional `icon.svg`. Built-ins renamed to public names: `table` (was parquet), `csv`, `xlsx`, `tree` (was json), `markdown`, `image`, `media`, `pdf`, `code`, `text` — name = folder = registry ref = `_mode` value = tooltip label, **public stable API** once shipped. **One resolution rule everywhere:** name → `~/.fused-render/<name>/template.html` if present, else `fused_render/templates/<name>/template.html`, else unusable; user folder shadows a built-in by name (deliberate override channel). `vendor/` unchanged — no `template.html`, never resolves | One grammar for built-ins and user templates makes the mode-list registry (D60) expressible with plain names. Rejected: a mode name separate from the template name (name + label indirection) — one namespace, the template name is the mode; keeping flat built-in filenames as referenceable names (leaks `.html` suffixes into URLs/registry and blocks per-template icons/assets) |
| D60 | Ordered mode lists + registry splice | Extension mapping goes **1:n**: `TEMPLATES` becomes ext → ordered list of names, **first = default**; `code` appears as a secondary mode only where raw text is meaningful. Registry values (D50) grow to `list \| string \| null`: list = full ordered mode list, replace semantics; string = single-mode list (unchanged); `null` = disable (unchanged). **`"..."` splice token** expanded in place to the built-in list (**removed by D94** — `"..."` is now a dangling name, not a splice) — `.` is forbidden in names (CT-6) so it can't collide. Per-entry validation: unresolvable entries dropped + `template_error`; user value resolving to nothing → built-in list fallback. Stat replaces `template` with the **`templates` array** `[{mode, path, icon}]` — clean break, no compat alias (shell is same repo) | Code mode on binary formats (e.g. `.parquet`) rejected — garbage rendering. Merge/patch registry keys (`order`/`hide`/`default` objects) rejected for the flat list + splice: one value shape, replace semantics are predictable, and `"..."` covers "add without knowing built-in names" and future-built-in pickup without a merge algebra |
| D61 | `_mode` param + icon-only switcher | Non-default mode selected via reserved **`_mode=<name>` on the shell URL** (bookmarkable; same pattern as D40's render\|source toggle). Absent = default = `templates[0]`; selecting the default deletes the param; unknown/stale value → default, no error; switch swaps iframe src with a fresh document (`key={mode}`). Shell shows a switcher (right side of preview header) only when >1 mode: **icon-only buttons, name via `title` tooltip**, active = accent. Icons = **monochrome SVG masks** (`mask-image` + `currentColor`), loaded via existing `/api/fs/raw`; every built-in folder ships one; `icon: null` → shell-rendered first-letter placeholder in a rounded box. The `.html` Rendered\|Source pair migrates to the same switcher component (two fixed shell-baked entries; `.html` stays registry-exempt, CT-4). Accepted quirk: template params persist on the shell URL across switches — same-name params collide across modes, documented not prevented | URL-is-state (D8/D20/D40): mode is view state, so it rides the shell URL. Icon+label switcher buttons rejected by owner for icon-only + tooltip (header space; names are tooltips, not chrome). Mask-tinting over `<img>`/inline-SVG recoloring: active/inactive coloring free from `currentColor`, no per-state assets, no new endpoints |
| D62 | HTML modes via `_render` sentinel | `.html`/`.htm` get a real server-side mode list — `TEMPLATES[".html"] = ["_render", "code"]` — **hardcoded**, registry-exempt (CT-4 stands): users cannot rebind the extension or remove `_render`. A `_`-prefixed mode name is a **shell sentinel** (SPEC PT-12): no template folder behind it, resolver emits `{mode, path: null, icon: null}` without touching the filesystem; `_`-names in a registry list are invalid (dropped + `template_error` — sentinel namespace shell-owned). Shell: `HtmlPreview` deleted; `TemplatePreview` handles `_render` (iframe = `/render?path=<file itself>`, no `_file`; shell-baked eye icon) and filters unknown sentinels defensively; preview dispatch collapses to **two-way** (`templates` non-empty → TemplatePreview, else fallback — no extension checks in shell). `/api/config` drops `source_template` (shell `Config` type too) — code view arrives via stat like everything else. Old `_mode=source` bookmarks fall to default silently — accepted break, mode is now `code` | Framework flows through html like any extension (future html modes = add to server list); `_` prefix matches reserved-param convention (`_mode`, `_file`). Rejected: keeping the shell-hardcoded Rendered\|Source pair — no icon/framework flow, permanent special case in dispatch; making html registry-editable — would let users break the core render guarantee (§4). *(Revised by D73: the registry exemption is dropped — the `["_render", "code"]` default now ships in the built-in registry and users may rebind `.html`/`.htm`; the sentinel mechanics stand.)* |

### M9 — Python `api` mode (2026-07-07)

| # | Decision | Choice | Rationale / rejected alternatives |
|---|---|---|---|
| D63 | `api` template for `.py` | New built-in template folder `templates/api/` giving `.py` files a **swagger-style run form**: `TEMPLATES[".py"] = ["code", "api"]` — **code stays default** (owner call), `api` one switcher click away. `inspector.py` (stdlib-only, no PEP 723 header → bare venv) statically parses the target via `ast` — **never imports/executes it** — returning module docstring, PEP 723 deps, and the **`@fused.udf` function's** name/docstring/params (last decorated function wins, matching the engine's `_registered_udfs[-1]` pick) (annotation source via `ast.unparse`; defaults via `ast.literal_eval`, non-literal defaults shown as source, never evaluated). Template renders annotation-driven widgets (`int`/`float` → number, `bool` → checkbox, `str` → text, other/unannotated → JSON textarea with string fallback) and coerces JS-side per PY-4 (typed JSON, no server coercion); empty optional inputs are **omitted** so python defaults apply. Execute posts **directly to `/api/run`** (not `fused.runPython`) because the response panel needs the full wire shape — stdout/stderr/duration — that the runtime helper flattens; response card shows pretty-printed JSON `return_value`, collapsible stdout/stderr, red traceback on error, duration badge. Inputs persist to URL params on Execute (URL-is-state → shareable pre-filled runs). Re-inspect on file change is free: the runtime already watches `_file` (LR-1) and reloads the frame. No `@fused.udf` function → docstring + "nothing to execute" notice; syntax error → parse error shown, code mode still works | Owner calls 2026-07-07: name `api` (over swagger/run/udf), code-first default, **only the `@fused.udf`-decorated function shown** (no read-only listing of other top-level functions — one file = one entry point, SPEC §5), JSON-only result rendering v1 (rich renders for images/tables = follow-up), re-inspect on change. AST inspection over importing the module: imports execute top-level code and need deps installed — a doc view must be side-effect-free. Direct `/api/run` fetch accepted over extending runtime.js with a rich-result API: template-local need, no new runtime surface |

### Scientific-file templates — GeoTIFF / NetCDF (2026-07-07)

| # | Decision | Choice | Rationale / rejected alternatives |
|---|---|---|---|
| D64 | GeoTIFF / NetCDF preview templates + vendored sci decoders | Two new template folders (`geotiff/`, `netcdf/`, each `template.html` + `icon.svg`) adapted from Fused's battle-tested standalone sci viewers, restyled to the shell palette; input is the reserved `_file` param (drag-drop/URL/help chrome dropped). Decode is fully in-browser — no `reader.py` — via decoders vendored as single self-contained ESM bundles under `templates/vendor/` by `scripts/vendor-sci/build.sh` (Node 22 + esbuild, `--format=esm --minify`, **no `--splitting`** so one file per lib) — no CDN at runtime (D3). `.tif/.tiff → ["geotiff"]`, `.nc/.nc4/.cdf → ["netcdf"]` join `TEMPLATES` (binary formats: no `code` mode, same reasoning as `.parquet`, D60). GeoTIFF full-fetches small files and range-requests (`geotiff.fromUrl`) over the raw endpoint for >32 MiB (Starlette FileResponse honors Range). The sciViz core the two templates share (colormaps, stretch/stats/histogram, canvas draw, UI kit) is first-party ESM at `templates/shared/sciviz.mjs`, served from a dedicated `/template-shared/*` mount so `vendor/` stays strictly third-party; `shared/` has no `template.html`, so it can never resolve as a template name (PT-6). Zarr (a *directory* store — needs directory-template routing, a server/shell semantics change) is split into its own follow-up PR per review on the original combined PR. | Adapt the standalone viewers verbatim (7 rounds of browser QA) over rewriting decode/render. Only byte-identical helpers were extracted to sciviz.mjs; divergent helpers stay in-template. Dedicated `/template-shared` mount over nesting under `templates/vendor/`: vendor/ stays third-party-only |

### Zarr template + directory-template routing (2026-07-07)

| # | Decision | Choice | Rationale / rejected alternatives |
|---|---|---|---|
| D65 | Zarr preview template + directory-template routing | Zarr stores are *directories*, so preview needs the shell to render a template for a directory. A new **`DIR_TEMPLATES = {".zarr": ["zarr"]}`** table mirrors `TEMPLATES`'s ext→ordered-name-list shape (D60): when `_templates_for` is called with `is_dir`, it resolves the extension on the directory's basename (`os.path.splitext(os.path.basename(os.path.normpath(path)))`) through the **same `_resolve_mode_list`** as files, so `stat.templates` entries come out identically shaped `[{mode, path, icon}]`. Directory templates are **package-only**: the M7 user registry (D50) is a per-file *suffix* match with no coherent meaning for a directory, so it deliberately does not apply (documented in `_templates_for`). Shell dispatch (App.tsx) changes from `is_dir → Listing` to: a target with non-empty `templates` previews (even a directory) **unless** the shell-owned `?listing=1` forces the plain listing; a directory with no templates still lists. Directory previews render a **"Browse contents"** header action that navigates to `?listing=1`; embed mode hides the whole header, so the same action also rides as a `.preview-browse-chip` corner chip pinned over the iframe, CSS-revealed only in embed. `listing` never leaks into template params: it only takes effect on a directory, and when set it selects the listing view (no template iframe is mounted). The `zarr/` folder ships `template.html` (adapted verbatim from Fused's existing Zarr viewer, imports the shared sciViz core) + a chunked-grid `icon.svg`; zarrita 0.7.3 is vendored (`templates/vendor/zarrita.bundle.mjs`, ~1.4 MB — numcodecs inlines its WASM as base64) via `scripts/vendor-sci`. | Separate `DIR_TEMPLATES` over overloading `TEMPLATES` with a dir flag: the file case (the common one) stays a plain dict lookup and the directory case is explicit; reusing `_resolve_mode_list` keeps one resolution rule and one stat shape. Dispatch on `templates` not `is_dir` so no per-format logic leaks into the shell (D12 holds). `?listing=1` override kept minimal — one dispatch check + a `navigateUrl` link, zero new server routes. Registry-for-directories rejected as out of scope (no file-suffix rule maps cleanly onto a directory). Split from D63's PR per review on the original combined PR. *(Revised by D73: `DIR_TEMPLATES` is replaced by trailing-`/` registry keys — `".zarr/"` — and the user registry can bind directories after all.)* |

## Descoped / follow-up list (recorded, not built)

Security layer (token, Origin/Host validation, sandboxed bridge — see threat note SPEC §9; X-Fused preflight guard shipped, D36) · warm worker pool · DataFrame/Arrow returns · WebSocket/SSE push · exec console + structured logging · caching · search/sort/tree/keyboard-nav/hidden-file toggle · editing beyond code_template (text/markdown buffers, new-file/rename/delete APIs) · pushState opt-in · declarative param binding. *(Built since this list was written: M2 template set, file editing — D37; directory templates — D64.)*

### Engine rollback (2026-07-07)

| # | Decision | Choice | Rationale / rejected alternatives |
|---|---|---|---|
| D67 | Roll back the openfused engine | Reverts #8 (D55–D58), #18 (default data-stack venv deps), and #19 (D66 run-error logging, which existed to surface openfused backend 500s). `executor.py`/`_child.py` return: bare `main()` with annotation-driven string coercion (D1), server-env imports (D14/D30), old `{ok, result, error:{type,message,traceback}}` wire shape, `requires-python >=3.10`. Kept features that landed on top were retargeted, not reverted: template folders/mode lists (D59–D62) keep their new paths; built-in readers lose `@fused.udf` + PEP 723 headers; the `api` template (D63) inspects a bare `main()` and consumes the old wire shape | Owner call 2026-07-07: roll back all engine changes, keep everything else |

### Application log (2026-07-07)

| # | Decision | Choice | Rationale / rejected alternatives |
|---|---|---|---|
| D68 | Application log file for "Internal Server Error" / right-click-open diagnostics | Re-introduces the diagnostics half of D66 (rolled back with the engine in D67, though the *reason* — a Finder-launched .app has no visible stderr, so a bare "Internal Server Error" or a failed right-click "Open with FusedRender" leaves nothing to report — never went away), retargeted to the current `executor.py`/`_child.py` model. Three parts. **(1) `fused_render/logs.py`:** `setup_logging()` attaches a `RotatingFileHandler` (2 MB + 1 backup) to the **root** logger — uvicorn/library output propagates there too — writing a **per-process** `fused-render-<pid>.log` to the **system temp dir** by default (`FUSED_RENDER_LOG_DIR` overrides). Temp because the log is disposable diagnostic output with no retention policy: rotation bounds a single file's size, but nothing prunes the directory, so a permanent home (a dotdir, app support) would silently accumulate stale logs forever; temp storage is OS-reclaimed and clears on reboot, giving a fresh log per session. Per-pid (not a fixed name) so concurrent instances can't collide: a shared name is fine for *sequential* launches (the handler opens append, never truncates) but two live writers would interleave their lines, and size rotation is not multi-process safe (one process's rename + reopen leaves the other writing to the renamed fd, clobbered on the next rollover). The writer knows its own path via `getpid()`, so `Open logs` / the CLI print still resolve it. Idempotent (won't stack handlers); boot line records fused-render version / python / platform / `sys.prefix`. **(2) Server 500s self-report + browser request log:** a catch-all FastAPI `exception_handler(Exception)` puts the full traceback in both the JSON 500 body (fine under D3 — local single-user tool, the only reader owns the machine, so the existing D17 overlay renders it screenshot-ably) and the log, tagged with the request method + path. The existing `no_cache` middleware also grew a **per-request access line** (`<method> <path> -> <status> (<ms>)`, SPEC SV-3) so the log reconstructs the call sequence a page made — the context for *which* request 500'd; a request that raises is logged before the re-raise so the trail stays complete. Static-asset mounts (`/static`, `/template-assets`, `/template-shared`) are skipped — high-volume and never the failure, they'd just churn the rotating file. `uvicorn.access` couldn't carry this: it's `propagate=False` (never reaches our root handler) and the menu-bar app runs uvicorn at `log_level="warning"`, which suppresses it — a middleware works identically for both entry points. **Startup** is explicit too: the boot line timestamps every launch, the CLI logs its bind + start dir, and the app logs `app starting` / `starting server` / `server ready`. **(3) Right-click-open trail:** `app.py` logs the Finder open-files event (raw filenames), dock-reopen, server bootstrap start/ready, and — instead of the old `print` that went nowhere — the readiness failure; the "server did not become ready" path and the new **"Open logs"** menu item (reveal in Finder) round it out. `executor.run_python` logs every failed run (with the worker traceback) and now returns the normal wire shape on an `OSError` spawn failure rather than 500-ing unlabeled. Both entry points (`cli.py`, `app.py`) call `setup_logging()` first; the CLI also prints the log path | Owner-directed 2026-07-07: an application log to diagnose Internal Server Error / right-click-open failures. Root logger over a package logger: capture what stderr would have said, not just our own lines. Traceback in the response body over a bare 500 (D66 precedent): every silent 500 becomes a screenshot. System temp dir over a persistent location for the default: rotation bounds a single file but nothing prunes the folder, so ephemeral storage avoids piling up stale logs; a persistent dir is one env var (`FUSED_RENDER_LOG_DIR`) away. Structured logging / exec console stays on the backlog; this is tracebacks + boot/open context only |

### Fused engine, optional (2026-07-07 — supersedes D67's posture, full spec = SPEC §5.6 PY-12..PY-14)

| # | Decision | Choice | Rationale / rejected alternatives |
|---|---|---|---|
| D69 | Re-introduce the fused engine as **optional with fallback** | New `engine.py` (the D55 engine resurrected, adapted): when the `fused` package is **importable**, `/api/run` executes through `LocalPythonComputeBackend` — fresh subprocess per call, PEP 723 requirements + a default data-stack set resolved into cached venvs, params via `_params.json`. Absent, `executor.py`/`_child.py` run **unchanged** — no hard dependency, `requires-python` floor stays `>=3.10` (a `python_version >= "3.11"` marker on the optional `[fused]` extra; the D55 nightly-wheel URL). `FUSED_RENDER_ENGINE` = `auto`\|`fused`\|`builtin`; the choice is logged and surfaces in `/api/config.engine`. **Contract when fused runs it**: `@fused.udf`-decorated function (any name, last wins, raw-JSON params) or `result =` script — plus a **bare-`main()` compat bridge** (annotation coercion + script-dir cwd, matching the built-in executor) so pages and built-in templates behave identically under either engine. **One wire shape** on both paths: the D1 `{ok, result, error:{type,message,traceback}, stdout}` (fused adds `stderr`/`duration_ms`) — the fused engine adapts, consumers never fork. User source is compiled as its own unit under its real filename (leading `from __future__` imports stay valid; tracebacks carry exact file/line, plumbing frames stripped). `api` template inspector recognizes both entrypoint forms | Owner call 2026-07-07: use fused to run code **if it's available**. Differs from D55/D57 exactly where D67 hurt: no hard wheel dependency, no floor bump, no contract break for existing pages (the bridge), one wire shape (no consumer migration). Rejected re-imposing D57's fused-only contract: built-in templates and existing pages must run under both engines since availability varies per install |
| D70 | `FUSED_RENDER_ENGINE` default flips from `auto` to `builtin` | `_select_engine()`'s unset-env default changes from `auto` (opportunistic: use fused iff importable) to `builtin` (never touch `fused`, even if it's importable). `auto` and `fused` are unchanged and still opt in to the D69 engine explicitly. Only the silent, install-dependent default behavior changes — a `pip install "fused-render[fused]"` next to fused-render no longer silently switches which engine runs a page's code | Owner call 2026-07-08: an install-order-dependent default (whether some *other* package happens to be importable) was surprising — same fused-render install could run pages under two different contracts depending on what else shares the environment. Explicit opt-in (`auto`/`fused`) over `auto`-by-default: engine choice should be a decision, not incidental to what's on `sys.path` |
| D71 | Export moves from a CLI subcommand to `POST /api/export` | `fused-render export <page.html> --out <dir>` (the standalone, no-server-required CLI form added for M10) is removed; `cli.py` goes back to `serve`-only. The identical `export_page()` logic (`export.py`, unchanged) is now called from a new endpoint on the already-running server — `POST /api/export {"page", "out"}` (both absolute paths, same convention as every other endpoint), guarded by the same `_require_fused` X-Fused header check as `/api/run`/`/api/fs/write`. Success returns `{"out", "entrypoints", "assets"}`; an `ExportError` returns a `400 {"error"}`, matching `/api/run`'s error shape rather than the CLI's stderr+exit-code convention | Owner call 2026-07-08: export is a one-off local build step used from tooling that already talks to the running server (or will, e.g. a future in-shell "Deploy" UI) — a second process-invocation surface (parse `sys.argv`, print to stdout/stderr, `SystemExit`) duplicated what an HTTP endpoint already gives for free, and diverged from every other export-like action (`/api/run`, `/api/fs/write`) in error shape and auth. No behavior change to export.py itself — only how it's invoked |
### Built-in helpers run in-process (2026-07-07)

| # | Decision | Choice | Rationale / rejected alternatives |
|---|---|---|---|
| D72 | First-party helpers execute in-process | `run_python` splits by target: a `.py` on an **explicit allowlist** (`executor.INPROCESS_HELPERS` — the `table`/`csv`/`xlsx` `reader.py` and the `api` `inspector.py`) runs **in the server process** (`executor._run_inprocess`); everything else — the `api` template's Run button (the user's own `.py`), user-authored template readers under `~/.fused-render/`, **and every other shipped `templates/` helper** — keeps the **fresh-subprocess-per-call** isolation (D5/PY-6). Boundary is exact realpath membership in the allowlist, **not** a "path under `templates/`" check: that was the original design but it silently swept in later-added shipped helpers (the `claude/` chat agent, the geo/h3/las/vector/zarr browsers + tile servers) that are slow or long-running — running those in-process skips the 30 s timeout and can pin a FastAPI threadpool worker indefinitely (Bugbot, PR #26). An allowlist makes the safe subprocess path the default; a new reader is added only after confirming it is fast, bounded, and free of user-code execution (symlink-safe; correctness, not auth — D3 stands). Param binding was extracted to `fused_render/_binding.py` so both paths coerce identically; `_child.py` imports it top-level (`import _binding`) because it runs as a standalone script and its own dir is `sys.path[0]` (works dev-from-source). In-process runs catch `BaseException` → error dict (a helper fault can't kill the server thread), mutate no process-global state — the helper module is built with `module_from_spec` + `exec_module`, never inserted into `sys.modules`, and `sys.path` is left untouched — so they stay safe under the `asyncio.to_thread` threadpool, do **not** capture stdout (helpers don't print; a `sys.stdout` redirect is process-global and would race), and have **no timeout** (allowlisted helpers are bounded local-file reads / `ast` parses). Safe because the allowlisted helpers are trusted and **never import or execute user code** — the readers open a data file, the inspector `ast`-parses without importing (D63). | **The macOS TCC bug this fixes:** the server runs *in* the menu-bar app process (`app.py`), so its own file reads reuse the one Downloads/Desktop/Documents grant the user gave the app. But data-file previews read through `fused.runPython` → a **fresh `Contents/MacOS/python` subprocess** — a *different*, only **ad-hoc-signed** Mach-O (`build_dmg.sh`; no Developer ID yet, D35) that macOS TCC does not attribute to the app, so every preview / pagination / slider tick on a file under a protected folder re-prompted. Moving the trusted readers in-process makes that access the app's again → prompt once, not per click. Secondary win: no per-call pandas/pyarrow re-import (≈1 s) — they stay warm in the server. Rejected: warm worker pool (the sanctioned PY-6 v2 upgrade) — still a separate process identity, so it wouldn't eliminate the prompt, and it would break "no stale state" for *user* code; left as the general path for user scripts. Full elimination for user code needs Developer ID signing + shared TCC identity (D35 → now D73). Accepted trade: a native crash in a first-party reader (e.g. a corrupt file) now takes the server thread instead of an isolated child — low risk on read paths, and the same libraries read the bytes either way |

### Code signing & notarization (2026-07-07)

| # | Decision | Choice | Rationale / rejected alternatives |
|---|---|---|---|
| D73 | Developer ID signing + notarization in `build_dmg.sh` | Replaces D35's deferred "Briefcase external-app" plan with **in-place `codesign`/`notarytool` in the existing script** (Briefcase's app template breaks `sys.executable`, so it was never a fit for the actual signing step — only its `codesign` invocation was ever going to be used). **Credential-driven, keychain-based:** `FUSED_RENDER_CODESIGN_IDENTITY` (a `Developer ID Application: …` string or cert SHA-1) is used verbatim; unset → auto-detect a single `Developer ID Application` cert via `security find-identity -v -p codesigning` (several → hard stop asking to pick; none → **ad-hoc fallback**, so a plain `bash scripts/build_dmg.sh` still works for local testing). `FUSED_RENDER_CODESIGN_KEYCHAIN` optionally targets a dedicated keychain (CI). Developer-ID mode signs **inside-out** (every nested Mach-O by magic-byte enumeration, then the `.app`) with **`--options runtime --timestamp`** and a generated **entitlements** plist — `disable-library-validation` (bundled CPython loads third-party native libs not signed by our Team), `allow-jit` + `allow-unsigned-executable-memory`, `allow-dyld-environment-variables` (`app.py` sets `PYTHONHOME`). `--deep` deliberately dropped (Apple advises against it for distribution; skips nested executables → notarization rejects). Notarization is opt-in via `FUSED_RENDER_NOTARY_PROFILE` (a stored `notarytool store-credentials` profile): submit the DMG `--wait`, then `stapler staple` + `validate`; ad-hoc + notary profile = hard error. Info.plist gains the `NS*FolderUsageDescription` strings (Desktop/Documents/Downloads/Removable/Network) so the TCC prompt explains itself. Docs: `docs/signing.md` (get an identity, store notary creds, CI keychain recipe, troubleshooting) + README build section. | **This is the general form of the D72 fix.** One stable Team ID signing the app stub *and* the nested `python` the executor spawns makes macOS attribute the worker's protected-folder access to the app → the Downloads/Desktop/Documents prompt appears **once**, covering user scripts (the `api` Run button, user template readers) that D72's in-process split can't touch without breaking isolation. Notarization is the other half of shippability (Gatekeeper, no right-click-Open dance). Keychain-based identity + notary profile keeps secrets out of the repo/CLI. Ad-hoc fallback preserved so the dev/local build is unchanged. Usage strings don't change *when* the prompt fires (D72 + signing do) but a reasonless prompt reads as malware. Rejected: full Briefcase (breaks `sys.executable`, D35); committing a fixed identity/team (secrets, per-developer identities differ) — everything is resolved from the keychain at build time |

### Layout params go pane-local (2026-07-08 — supersedes D45/D46's merged pool, LM-3/LM-7/LM-9 rewritten)

| # | Decision | Choice | Rationale / rejected alternatives |
|---|---|---|---|
| D72 | No automatic param promotion in layout modes — params are pane-local, globals only by hand | Panel mode adopts tab mode's TM-3 contract: the panel shell sets `window._fusedParamBoundary = true` (render-time set, cleared on unmount), so every pane's runtime targets its own `/embed` URL and the full pane query — user params included — is captured segment-local inside `_layout` by the ordinary sync. The breadcrumb Split entry no longer partitions params (`_`/`sort`/`order`/`listing` → pane, rest → pool): the current view's whole query goes into each segment; `panelUrl` is called with no top-level pool. Global params survive as a hand-typed convention: the runtime's get/getAll now merge the same-origin ancestor chain *above* the boundary (nearest wins, pane-local wins over all) and listen for `fused:urlchange` on those ancestors too, so `?city=sf&_layout=(…)` typed on the shell URL is readable from every pane — but `set()` never writes above the boundary (a pane setting a globally-present key shadows it locally), and the shells only pass top-level params through untouched | Owner call 2026-07-08: the promote-some/keep-some split (D45's LM-3 partition + D46's climb-to-shell) was complex and surprising — which params ended up where depended on a hardcoded key list. One rule now: everything rides the segment; global is opt-in by typing it. Rejected keeping automatic promotion for non-underscore keys (the removed behavior), and rejected dropping global support entirely (read-only ancestor fallback is cheap and keeps deliberate cross-pane linking possible). D46's structural cross-pane sync consequence (LM-9 auto-linking) is intentionally gone |
| D73 | Template bindings unified into one registry format: built-in table → `templates/registry.json`, trailing-`/` directory keys, `*` wildcard segments, `.html` exemption dropped | The `TEMPLATES` and `DIR_TEMPLATES` dicts leave `server.py`: built-in bindings ship as **`fused_render/templates/registry.json`**, exactly the user-registry format (SPEC §16), read per resolution by the same loader as the user file (CT-5 symmetry; editable installs get live edits free). One matcher serves both: keys are **dot-anchored suffix patterns** — compound (`.xyz.json`), `*` wildcard = exactly one whole non-empty segment (partial wildcards invalid), and a **trailing `/` marks a directory key** (`.zarr/`), replacing `DIR_TEMPLATES`. Specificity: more segments > fewer; tie broken rightmost-first, literal beats `*`; a match needs a non-empty stem (a file literally named `.json` doesn't match `.json`); precedence stays any-user-match > built-in. **Revises D65:** the trailing-`/` convention gives a directory binding the coherent suffix-match meaning D65 said was missing, so directory templates are user-bindable too. **Revises D62/CT-4:** `.html`/`.htm` become ordinary keys — their `["_render", "code"]` default simply ships in the built-in registry; users may rebind (owner call: "user can shoot themselves"). Sentinels: server keeps `KNOWN_SENTINELS = {"_render"}`; those names are referenceable from **any** registry list (so a rebind can keep/restore `_render` by listing it explicitly (the `"..."` splice that once did this was removed, D94)), all other `_` names stay invalid per CT-6 | Owner call 2026-07-08. Motivation: bindings were split across two code dicts and one JSON file with two different match rules (`splitext` vs longest-suffix) — now one format, one matcher, one precedence story covering files, compound extensions, wildcards, and directories. Per-call read of the built-in registry chosen over load-once ("whichever is simpler" — one loader, no second code path); a parse failure of package data surfaces as `template_error`, and a test pins the shipped file (parses, every name resolves). Rejected partial wildcards (`.geo*.json`): precedence between overlapping partials has no obvious answer, whole-segment `*` keeps specificity a two-rule sort. Rejected making the built-in registry user-editable in place — it ships inside the package; the override channel remains `~/.fused-render` |

| D74 | File-change feed moves from SSE to WebSocket | `/api/fs/events` becomes a **WebSocket** endpoint (same path, same repeated-`path` query params, same 200 ms stat loop). Messages are JSON text: `{"path", "mtime"}` on change, `{"keepalive": true}` every 15 s. Clients (runtime.js LR-4, Listing.tsx LS-1) swap `EventSource` for `WebSocket` and add their own 1 s reconnect-on-close (EventSource auto-reconnected; WS doesn't). `websockets` becomes a hard dependency — plain uvicorn ships no WS protocol implementation | **Revises D42's "SSE over WebSocket" call.** Bug 2026-07-08: SSE rides ordinary HTTP/1.1, and Chrome caps HTTP/1.1 at **6 connections per origin** — a 6-pane panel of runPython views held all 6 sockets open with change feeds, so every subsequent fetch (`/api/run` on a param change!) queued browser-side forever; closing one pane released a socket and everything drained. WebSockets live in a separate, much larger browser connection pool, so N open panes no longer starve request/response traffic. Rejected multiplexing one SSE stream through the shell via postMessage (complex, and still breaks at 6 open tabs since the cap is per-origin browser-wide); rejected HTTP/2 (uvicorn doesn't speak it) |

### Bookmarks move server-side (2026-07-08 — supersedes D21/SB-5's localStorage store)

| # | Decision | Choice | Rationale / rejected alternatives |
|---|---|---|---|
| D75 | Bookmarks persist to `~/.fused-render/bookmarks.json` behind the server; shell-state backends live in a new `fused_render/shell/` package | The store moves off localStorage to a file, via **`GET /api/bookmarks` → `{exists, bookmarks}`** and **`PUT /api/bookmarks`** (whole-tree, atomic temp-file+`os.replace`, **last-write-wins**, no locking per D3). Server code lands in **`fused_render/shell/`** — `storage.py` (the `~/.fused-render` home dir, resolved via `home_dir()` with a `FUSED_RENDER_HOME` test override, + atomic `read_json`/`write_json`) and `bookmarks.py` (an `APIRouter` `create_app` includes) — kept out of `server.py`'s fs/render internals; the next shell-state resource is a new module here reusing `storage`. The PUT carries the same `X-Fused` D3 guard as the other writes (duplicated locally, **not** imported from `server`, to keep shell↛server acyclic). Frontend (`lib/bookmarks.ts`): reads stay **synchronous off an in-memory cache** (React renders can't await), hydrated once at boot by `hydrateBookmarks()`; mutations become **async** — clone → `await` PUT → advance cache (**no optimistic update, no rollback**: a localhost PUT of this tiny tree is sub-ms, and advancing the cache only after a successful write means the UI never shows unpersisted state). The event bus (`notifyBookmarksChanged`/`useBookmarksVersion`) is unchanged — still the house re-render signal. **One-time import:** `exists` is false only until the file is first written; on boot, `!exists` + non-empty legacy `localStorage["fused.bookmarks"]` → import it once (PUT flips `exists` true forever, so deleting every bookmark later never re-imports). Legacy localStorage is left dormant as a read fallback, never written again. `fused.armedBookmark` stays in sessionStorage (D38, ephemeral). | Owner call 2026-07-08. D21/SB-5 chose localStorage but flagged the server-file move as a "trivial export" for later — this is that move (bookmarks are now shareable/portable per-machine, not per-browser-profile). Rejected **TanStack Query** (owner asked, then agreed): 3 tiny single-writer last-write-wins resources don't need request caching/dedup/pagination, and its refetch-on-focus/reconnect defaults actively fight last-write-wins — the hand-rolled cache mirrors the existing `App.tsx:useStat` house pattern with no dependency; revisit only if a resource needs polling / cross-window live sync / cross-resource invalidation. Rejected **optimistic updates** (owner asked): nothing to hide at localhost latency, and await-then-reflect is simpler *and* more correct (surfaces a failed write instead of a fake success). Rejected **auto-import + clear localStorage** (kept dormant fallback) and **fresh start** (would drop existing bookmarks). The `exists` flag over a bare emptiness check is load-bearing — an emptiness check would re-import after the user deletes everything. Rejected importing `server._require_fused` into `shell/` (import cycle — `server` imports the router). |

| D76 | User templates + registry move under `~/.fused-render/templates/` (revises D50/D59/D73's path) | The user override channel nests one level down: `USER_TEMPLATES_DIR = home_dir()/templates` and `USER_REGISTRY = .../templates/registry.json` (server.py imports `home_dir` from `shell/storage.py` — server→shell, still acyclic). The home dir is now a clean container of siblings: **`~/.fused-render/{bookmarks.json, templates/{<name>/, registry.json}}`**. Resolution rule, key grammar, precedence, per-entry validation — all unchanged (PT-6/CT-3/D73); only the two path constants + the paths quoted in docs/error strings move. **No auto-migration** — an existing user moves their own folders (owner call). | Owner call 2026-07-08. Once `bookmarks.json` (D75) landed in `~/.fused-render` and 1–2 more shell-state files are coming, user template folders + `registry.json` sitting loose at the home root mixed two unrelated concerns in one flat dir; a `templates/` subdir separates the template override channel from shell state so each is a clean namespace. Rejected auto-migrating on-disk template folders at startup (owner declined — moving a user's real files silently is surprising and risky, unlike the bookmarks localStorage import which only reads a browser key); the home dir is created lazily on first write regardless, so a fresh install needs nothing. |

| D77 | Bookmarks poll the server every 30 s for cross-tab convergence (revises D75's "no live sync") | `main.tsx` runs a 30 s `setInterval` calling `refreshBookmarks()` — a plain `GET /api/bookmarks` routed through the same serial queue as mutations/hydration, so it never overwrites an in-flight local write. It swaps the in-memory cache and fires `notifyBookmarksChanged()` **only when the tree actually differs** (JSON compare), so an idle tab doesn't re-render every tick. No import logic (hydrate owns the one-time import); a failed poll is logged and skipped. Convergence is **eventual (≤30 s)**; simultaneous writes stay **last-write-wins** (unchanged). | Owner call 2026-07-08. D75 rejected cross-tab sync, but the per-tab in-memory cache (vs the old shared localStorage, which every read re-parsed) made another tab's edits invisible until a full reload — a regression Bugbot flagged — and let a stale tab clobber a fresher file. A 30 s poll is the cheap fix: readers converge and the clobber window shrinks to ≤30 s, with none of the WebSocket live-sync machinery the file-change feed needs (D74). Rejected instant WS/broadcast sync (overkill for a tiny, rarely-edited resource — the whole reason TanStack was rejected in D75) and per-mutation read-modify-write (refreshes only the write path, not the displayed list). |
| D78 | Annotation logic is fully contained in the annotate template; views expose only stable ids | The annotate view template owns ALL annotation code, including off-page anchor handling: an `ID_RESOLVERS` table inside `templates/annotate/template.html` recognizes the stable element ids paged views render — `__fr_r<row>_c<col>` (table), `__fr_s<sheet>_r<row>_c<col>` (xlsx), `__fr_page_<n>` (pdf) — labels an unmounted id as off-page ("row 5" / "Alpha · row 3" / "page 3" chip, not "detached"), and navigates to it on reveal by writing the ordinary `offset`/`sheet` shell-URL params the paged view already watches (xlsx sheet names are read from the framed view's `#sheet` select options). View templates carry NO annotation code, hooks, or references — the ids are inert, deep-linkable markup useful on their own. Supersedes the interim `window.__fusedAnnotateAnchorResolver` per-view hook, removed the same day it landed. | Owner call 2026-07-08: "all the code for different view types lives in the annotation template itself — we are not injecting any code into other views." The per-view resolver hook was an extensibility seam (a custom view could teach annotate its paging), but it scattered annotation-aware code across templates — exactly what the template rebuild (SPEC §17) exists to prevent. Accepted trade-offs: annotate hardcodes the stock templates' id shapes and page size (100), and can no longer ask a view whether a row is truly gone from the data — a comment past the data's end keeps its "row N" chip instead of turning "detached". |

### Deploy — hosted publish through the fused CLI (2026-07-08, M11)

| # | Decision | Choice | Rationale / rejected alternatives |
|---|---|---|---|
