| D78 | Deploy button/modal orchestrates the `fused share` CLI; fused-render still mints and hosts nothing | A **Deploy** action in the preview header (pages carrying the `_render` mode, SPEC §19) opens a modal that: resolves the **fused CLI** (`FUSED_RENDER_FUSED_BIN` verbatim/split → the server venv's own bin/ → PATH) and, when missing, offers a one-click `POST /api/deploy/install` that pip-installs **the wheel pinned by the `deploy.PINNED_FUSED_REQUIREMENT` code constant** (sync-tested against pyproject's `[fused]` extra; Python ≥ 3.11 per the wheel's marker and a pip-capable interpreter, else the manual hint — an earlier metadata-read approach was dropped: dist-info is absent on source runs/stripped app bundles and **stale on editable installs that predate the extra**, which made the button report "metadata unavailable" exactly when it mattered); reads **hosted envs** (`fused`/`aws`, never `local`) directly from the CLI's `~/.openfused/envs.json` (`OPENFUSED_ENVS_FILE` honored), defaulting to the managed **`fused`-backend** env; **re-exports the page to a fresh temp bundle** per deploy (§18 machinery, deleted afterwards) and runs `fused share create <bundle> --public` with `OPENFUSED_ENV=<chosen env>` on the child; keeps a **thin per-page pointer** at `~/.fused-render/deployments.json` (env/backend/token/url/status) so the header shows a deployed dot with zero CLI cost, the URL is re-showable (`create` returns it exactly once; AWS returns none — url stays null there), and a redeploy hits the **same token** (`repoint`; revoked tombstone → `recreate --same-token` + repoint with best-effort re-revoke on failure; absent token → fresh create). `share list --all` is reconciled on modal open and backs the modal's per-env **share list**, joined to local pages via the pointer store. Deploys are **public capability links only** in v1. Server code is `fused_render/deploy.py` (an APIRouter beside shell/bookmarks; X-Fused on POSTs); tests drive a stub CLI through the `FUSED_RENDER_FUSED_BIN` seam. | The flow app already proved this exact shape (its spec/app/deploy-project.md): the CLI is the one mutation boundary, output is already JSON, `share list` is truth, and the app keeps only a pointer — reusing the pattern means zero fused-repo changes and no credential ever transits fused-render. Rejected **importing fused as a library** in-process: heavy import, version-couples the server to the wheel, and bypasses the supported share surface; the CLI-child pattern also lets the package live in a *different* interpreter than the server. Rejected **hardcoding the wheel URL** for install (pyproject metadata is already the pin). Rejected **auth-gated deploys** for v1: per the fused repo's spec/serve/fused-render.md, `html-run`/`html-asset` are `inherit`, so a browser `<img>`/asset GET can't attach a bearer on an authed mount — public is the only posture that works fully today; the modal states the link is public. Rejected a per-row deployed badge in directory listings (a pointer-store join per listing row for a niche signal; the preview-header dot + the modal's share list cover discovery). Deferred: rotate-link (one `share recreate` call away), gate pickers when authed asset delivery lands upstream. |

### Deploy in the packaged .app — one autodetected CLI, bundled (2026-07-08)

| # | Decision | Choice | Rationale / rejected alternatives |
|---|---|---|---|
| D79 | fused CLI resolution is explicit-or-in-interpreter only; the .app bundles the CLI | `deploy.fused_cli` has **exactly two sources**: (1) the explicit `FUSED_RENDER_FUSED_BIN` override (verbatim/split; spawned with `PYTHONHOME`/`PYTHONPATH` **scrubbed** — inside the .app those are bundle-scoped and would break any external Python, the las template's external-spawn precedent), and (2) the **one autodetection**: the `fused` package importable in the server's own interpreter, run as `[sys.executable, fused_render/_fused_cli.py]` — a shim that sets `argv[0]="fused"` and calls `fused._cli.main()` (the executor's proven nested-interpreter spawn pattern; env kept). D78's venv-bin scan and PATH lookup are **removed** (owner call): a venv with the console script always has the package importable, so bin-scanning was redundant, and PATH/well-known-dir probing could silently pick up an unrelated `fused` the user never chose for this server. The **DMG build ships the CLI**: `build_dmg.sh` installs `[bundled,app,fused]`, `setup_py2app.py` force-copies `fused` + data-bearing deps (botocore models, anthropic tokenizer data, keyring/pluggy entry-point packages, cryptography + `_cffi_backend`), a post-build smoke test runs real verbs through the shim before signing, and a 3-line `Contents/Resources/bin/fused` wrapper (bundled python + shim, `PYTHONHOME` set like the build's own smoke tests) exposes the same CLI for terminal setup (`cloud setup`/`cloud login`/`env create`) — `/api/deploy/config.setup_cli` names it when frozen so the modal's guidance points somewhere real. The wrapper sits under `Resources`, not `MacOS`: a first build attempt put it in `Contents/MacOS/` and Developer-ID signing refused the bundle seal ("code object is not signed at all / In subcomponent: …fused") — everything in a bundle's `MacOS/` is nested code that must carry a code signature, which a shell script can't; under `Resources` it is sealed by the resource rules, the bundle format's intended home for helper scripts. `ty` deliberately not shipped (Rust binary console script; fused's verify scanner skips with a warning when absent). | Owner call 2026-07-08 ("only a single one autodetects, otherwise the user should have explicitly overridden it"), reconsidering deploy for the .app: the bundle has **no pip and no console scripts, and its notarized seal cannot be pip-installed into at runtime** — so on-demand install is structurally impossible there, and the previous "install with a Python on your PATH" message was a dead end (Finder launches don't even get the user's shell PATH). Shipping the pinned wheel in the bundle follows the `[bundled]` extra's exact philosophy (packaged users can't pip install → the interpreter ships the stack) and makes deploy zero-setup. Rejected: PATH/well-known-dir autodetection (silent, version-skewed CLI pickup the user never configured); vendoring `uv` to provision a managed env outside the bundle (heavier, second package manager to sign and update, unnecessary once the wheel ships in-bundle — revisit only if CLI updates must decouple from app releases); in-app OAuth login UI (deferred — the wrapper covers interactive setup). |

### Preferences page (2026-07-08, M12)

| # | Decision | Choice | Rationale / rejected alternatives |
|---|---|---|---|
| D80 | A `/view/_prefs` sentinel page, entered bottom-left; engine becomes a per-request preference | A muted gear row pinned to the **sidebar's bottom edge** (margin-top:auto — unobtrusive per the owner's ask) navigates to **`/view/_prefs`**, a shell-owned sentinel like `_panel`/`_tab` (`/view` only). Four sections, each a thin client over an existing backend: **logs** (path from `logs.log_path` via `GET /api/prefs`, revealed with the existing `/api/fs/reveal`); **execution engine** — a persisted pref in `~/.fused-render/prefs.json` (`shell/prefs.py`, the D75 shell-state pattern), `builtin` default, **read per /api/run request** so switching applies to the next run with no restart (CT-5's no-restart discipline), degrading to builtin with a visible note while the fused backend isn't importable; `FUSED_RENDER_ENGINE` stays the process override that locks the switch (`server._select_engine` became `_forced_engine`: unset now returns None and defers to the pref instead of hardcoding builtin); **deployments** — the per-env `share list` view with **revoke by env+token** (`deploy.revoke_mount`), which also covers mounts with no local pointer and flips any matching pointer so per-page state stays consistent; **template registry** — `GET /api/templates/registry`, the merged read-only bindings table (splice-expanded lists, `disabled` nulls, builtin/user/user-override source per row). | Owner ask 2026-07-08 (logs location, Fused/Local execution switch, see-and-revoke shares, later + registry view). Engine-as-preference chosen over restart-required env var editing: the engine seam (D69) already dispatches per call, so per-request pref reads make the switch free; the env var is kept as the stronger process pin rather than removed (scripts/CI rely on forcing). Revoke-by-token added instead of forcing every revoke through a page pointer — the prefs page lists mounts that have no pointer (CLI/another machine), and the fused CLI's owner-binding already guards misuse. Registry view is deliberately read-only: bindings live in the registry files (D73's design); an editing UI would duplicate that grammar's validation for little gain. Rejected a modal for prefs (the page is bookmarkable, holds four sections, and the sentinel-route pattern already exists) and a top-level nav item (the ask was unobtrusive). |

### Claude sidecar key rename, bookmark history, session restore (2026-07-09)

| # | Decision | Choice | Rationale / rejected alternatives |
|---|---|---|---|
| D82 | Claude sidecar on-disk key renamed `sessions` → `claudeSessions`; no migration | The `<file>.json` sidecar written by `fused_render/templates/claude/agent.py` stores its transcript list under `"claudeSessions"` (was `"sessions"`). Only the **on-disk key** changes — the `runPython` call contract stays exactly as-is: `main(action="sessions", ...)` and the `{"sessions": [...]}` response shape are unchanged (`_sessions()` reads the renamed key from disk but still returns it under `"sessions"`). Sidecars written by older code keep their old `"sessions"` key and are read as empty (their entries never appear again) — no migration | Owner call 2026-07-09 (memory `url-shapes-are-literal`: only the sidecar key was in scope, not the action name or response shape, which are the external contract with `template.html`). Old sessions being silently dropped is accepted — the sidecar is a convenience cache, not a durable transcript store |
| D83 | Bookmark create/update mirrored into the target file's `.html.json` sidecar as `bookmarkHistory`; delete is a no-op | New `POST /api/bookmarks/history` (`fused_render/shell/bookmarks.py`, same `X-Fused` guard as the PUT) upserts-by-`id` into a `bookmarkHistory` array in `<file>.json` (or `<dir>.json`) — the same sidecar the claude template owns via `claudeSessions`. Create-vs-update can't be told apart from the whole-tree bookmark PUT (D75), so the frontend (`addBookmark`/`updateBookmarkUrl` in `lib/bookmarks.ts`) fires this call itself, fire-and-forget, after its own mutation commits. A bookmark whose url resolves to a sentinel (`_panel`/`_tab`) or a path that doesn't exist on disk gets `{"recorded": false}` — no file touched. **Delete never removes a history entry** — the sidecar is a permanent record of every bookmark a file ever had, by design. Both `agent.py`'s `_load_sidecar` and the server's writer were hardened to preserve unknown keys on read-modify-write, so `claudeSessions` and `bookmarkHistory` never clobber each other (only a true simultaneous write loses a delta — accepted, D3) | Owner call 2026-07-09. Rejected detecting create/update server-side (the whole-tree PUT carries no diff); rejected wiring `renameBookmark`/`setBookmarkIcon` (out of scope — a stored entry's `name`/`icon` can go stale after a later rename, noted as a follow-up) |
| D84 | Per-file `lastSession` sidecar: opening a file with an empty query replays its last URL params | Each viewed **file** (not directories, not embed-mode panes) gets a `lastSession: {search, updated_at}` key in its `.html.json` sidecar — `search` is the shell query string verbatim, no leading `?` (same "store the URL literally" posture as bookmarks). `GET`/`PUT /api/session` (`fused_render/server.py`) read/write it; `PUT` carries the `X-Fused` guard (D36), `GET` doesn't (read-only). On opening a file with an **empty** shell query (listing click, DMG/Finder open, root redirect) the frontend (`lib/session.ts`, wired into `App.tsx`'s `StatView`) restores the stored `search` via `history.replaceState` before the preview mounts. Opening with a **non-empty** query (bookmark, hand-typed, refresh) lets those params win — no restore — and, if they include any key other than `_mode`, upserts them as the new `lastSession`. While viewing, any qualifying param change is upserted (400 ms debounced, fire-and-forget). Dropping params back down to empty/`_mode`-only leaves the stored `lastSession` untouched (a later bare open re-applies the old one) — an accepted quirk, not a bug | Owner call 2026-07-09. Only a shell-query-empty vs non-empty check is needed to discriminate "finder/redirect open" from "bookmark/hand-typed open" — every code path that opens a file with pre-set params already treats them as authoritative. Rejected clearing `lastSession` when params drop to empty (simpler to leave it, and matches "only track once a qualifying param exists"); rejected doing this server-side (param changes are client-side `replaceState`/`pushState` and never reach the server). Companion fix: `agent.py`'s `_load_sidecar` hardened (shared with D83) so a sidecar holding only `lastSession` (claude never used on that file) survives a later claude turn intact |
| D96 | History view: a `history` template renders any `<ext>.json` sidecar; `.html`/`.parquet` targets only | New `fused_render/templates/history/` — `template.html` + `icon.svg`, no `.py` (JSON is browser-parseable, same posture as `tree`). Registry: wildcard `".*.json": ["history", "tree", "code"]` (matches any compound `<ext>.json`, beats bare `.json` by specificity; no `annotate` — annotating the sidecar log itself doesn't make sense) and `history` appended to both `.html` and `.parquet`. The template resolves sidecar vs target from `_file` (HV-3: any `.json` whose stem still carries its own extension is the sidecar itself), reads via `fused.readFile`, validates **per top-level key** against an inline schema (hand-rolled subset validator) so one corrupt key warns only its own section (HV-4); unknown keys render as a collapsed raw section, not corruption (HV-5). Claude sessions navigate to `_mode=claude&session_id=` and bookmark/lastSession entries replay their stored `search` via `window.top.location` (HV-7); comments are display-only (HV-8). Never writes the sidecar | Owner call 2026-07-09. Template-not-shell posture follows annotate-v2 (D78): all logic stays in the template. Read-only comments chosen over URL-seeding a logged comment into annotate (owner rejected synthesizing URLs; annotate's live store is the URL `comments` param, the sidecar log is write-only). Validation inline in the template (owner) rather than a vendored JSON-Schema lib — four known keys need only a small subset. Registry key generalized from `.html.json` to the `.*.json` wildcard, and target scope widened to `.html` + `.parquet`, after the initial `.html`-only ship (owner edit 2026-07-09, Bugbot flagged the narrower SPEC/tests as stale, not the code); broaden further later by adding registry keys |
| D97 | Pinned view (M16): menu-bar NSPopover + WKWebView showing one pinned file's `/embed` view; detachable into a floating always-on-top window | With a pin set, left-click on the status item toggles an `NSPopover` (transient, 420×560) anchored to it; right/ctrl-click opens the existing rumps menu; no pin → menu on every click, current behavior untouched (PV-2). The popover's `WKWebView` loads `http://127.0.0.1:<port>/embed/<path>` — the same chrome-free page shell panes iframe, so ANY registry-renderable path is pinnable (html, parquet, images, directories) with the full runtime for free (PV-1). Menu gains "Pin File…" (NSOpenPanel) / "Show Pinned View" / "Unpin" (PV-3). Webview created lazily, kept alive across open/close so view state survives (PV-4). Drag-off detaches (`popoverShouldDetach:` YES) and the detached window is raised to `NSFloatingWindowLevel` — the literal "always on top" ask (PV-5). Pin persisted at `APP_SUPPORT_DIR/pin.json`. New dep `pyobjc-framework-WebKit` in the `[app]` extra + py2app packages, lazily imported like rumps (PV-6). AppKit code in `fused_render/menubar_pin.py`, pure-python store in `fused_render/pin_store.py` (PV-7) | Owner call 2026-07-10, chose popover over (b) standalone floating NSPanel (popover gives both quick-glance and, via detach, always-on-top; panel-only loses the menu-bar-anchored glance) and (c) WKWebView inside an NSMenu item (NSMenu tracking mode fights webview events — known flaky). `/embed` over `/render?path=` because the latter is html-only and chrome policy already solved for panes. Single pin in v1 — pin list is a follow-up |
| D98 | Status-item menu removed: the popover is the app's single surface; all actions move into a native header row inside it | Any click (left/right/ctrl) toggles the popover — no NSMenu on the status item. Header: Open in Browser / Copy URL / Pin… (→ Change… when pinned) / Unpin (hidden when unpinned) / Logs / Quit, native NSButtons above the webview; popover opens even before server-ready (placeholder body) so Quit is always reachable (PV-2/PV-3/PV-8). The rumps menu is still built but only ATTACHED if the popover controller fails to construct — never-unquittable guarantee (PV-8) | Owner call 2026-07-10, same day as D97: "not intuitive for the user to right click on an item in the menu bar" — the left-click-popover / right-click-menu split shipped hours earlier was undiscoverable. Header is native, not web chrome, so Quit works when the server is dead. Rejected keeping a trimmed menu on right-click (two surfaces for six actions) |
| D99 | *(AMENDED BY D270: what is queued during the 400 ms window is a key→value DELTA plus the pathname it was aimed at, not the formed URL this row's first implementation replayed — the flush merges, and a traversal or a pathname change discards. Everything below stands.)* Coalesced history writes in the runtime: params apply instantly via a pending-search overlay; the `replaceState` lands at most every 400 ms (trailing flush; flush on pagehide) | WebKit (Safari / the popover's WKWebView) hard-limits history writes to 100 per 30 s and THROWS SecurityError past it — scrubbing a slider in the pinned popover killed the chart's handler mid-scrub (Chrome has no limit, so the browser shell never hit it). `fused.params` readers (`get`/`getAll`/`set` rebuilds) go through `targetSearch()` = pending overlay else `location.search`, so values are live during the debounce window; the once-per-visit first-change-push (D51 semantics) stays immediate so Back gets its entry; all writes are try/caught so a throttle hit can never propagate into a caller again | Found 2026-07-10 testing M16. Overlay chosen over plain debounce (stale URL reads would freeze the chart during scrub) and over throttling in templates (every template would have to know; the runtime owns URL sync). 400 ms ⇒ ~75 writes/30 s, safe margin under WebKit's 100 |
| D100 | History comment rows deep-link into annotate via an id-only `comment` param; annotate imports the logged comment on a boot-time sidecar lookup | The history view's comment rows (D96) become **navigable**: a row with a string `id` opens the target with `_mode=annotate&comment=<id>` (same `navigateShell`/`wireClick` affordance as claude-session rows), an **id-only** deep link mirroring the claude `session_id` resume contract — the id is the whole contract and is never cleared after use. Annotate's `template.html` grows a boot-time focus resolver (runs once per page-load, after the initial render/frame wiring): it reads `comment`, focuses the id if it's in the live URL `comments` store, else does a **one-shot sidecar lookup** — a single id-keyed read of `<file>.json`'s `comments` log, strips the server stamps (`recorded_at`/`updated_at`), upserts the entry into the live set (re-recording it, a harmless upsert no-op), then focuses. Focus mirrors the existing cross-view card click: `switchView(c.view)` first when the comment's view differs (the reload re-runs the resolver on the right view), else `setActive`+`reveal`. Unreadable/unparseable sidecar or missing id fails silently. All logic stays inside the two templates (containment, HV-1/§17). Supersedes the read-only-comments sub-call in D96 | Owner reversed the 2026-07-09 read-only call on 2026-07-10. The earlier rejection was of **synthesizing** comment URLs from the write-only sidecar; the id-only param avoids that — it seeds nothing but an id (the `session_id` precedent already blessed for claude), and annotate stays the single source that resolves it. The live URL param remains the sole live store; the sidecar read is one boot-time id lookup for a comment absent from the live set, not a live-store sync. Rejected clearing `comment` after use (kept the resume-contract symmetry with `session_id`) and copying full comment fields into the history→annotate URL (id-only keeps the link short and lets annotate own the shape) |
| D101 | Explicit-delete tombstones (`deleted_at`) + full-state hydration on the comment deep link; absence never deletes | Annotate's delete button now sends the id as `deleted_ids` on the SAME `record` call that drops it from the URL array: `_record(file, comments, deleted_ids)` stamps `deleted_at` (server `time.time()` SECONDS, matching recorded_at/updated_at) and bumps `updated_at` on each named sidecar `comments` log entry in the same atomic read-merge-write as the upserts (unknown ids ignored; nothing recorded AND nothing tombstoned never touches disk). The tombstone is PERMANENT: `_record` strips any incoming `deleted_at` and never clears a stored one, so a stale bookmarked URL that still carries the deleted comment — or the hydration merge's live-wins rule — cannot silently resurrect it in the log. The history→annotate deep-link resolver (D100) is upgraded from a single-entry import to **full-state hydration**: for an id absent from the live URL store it imports EVERY non-tombstoned log entry (stripping `recorded_at`/`updated_at`/`deleted_at`), merges into the live set (live wins by id), saves once, then focuses; a tombstoned wanted id gets no import and no focus — deleted stays deleted. History rows render tombstoned comments dimmed + struck (`.ev.deleted .title`, line-through) with a " · deleted" tooltip and are inert (no deep link). All logic stays in the two templates + `annotate.py`. Supersedes D100's single-entry import | Owner call 2026-07-10. Absence-based deletion is **impossible** by construction: each shared URL carries only its own review subset (D100), so a comment missing from a `record` array means "not in this review", not "deleted" — the only way to express intent-to-delete is an explicit tombstone. Tombstone-on-the-same-record-call chosen over a separate `delete` action (the first iteration): two fire-and-forget read-modify-writes can interleave so the record's whole-log write lands after the delete's and silently drops the fresh `deleted_at`; one call, one write, no ordering to get wrong. Permanent over clear-on-re-record (the first iteration): any old URL still holding the comment would re-record it on the next unrelated save and silently undo the delete; with delete undo rejected by the owner there is no legitimate clearer, so the stamp is one-way (hand-edit the sidecar to revive). Full-state hydration (over D100's single-entry import) lands a deep link in the comment's *whole* live context, not one orphaned pin. An earlier iteration let clicking a tombstoned history row resurrect the comment; owner rejected it same day (resurrection re-recorded the id, silently clearing the tombstone and bringing the comment back everywhere) — deleted rows are now inert. Kept `deleted_at` in SECONDS (recorded_at/updated_at precedent), not the comment's own ms `createdAt` — server stamps are seconds by design (D83/D84, HV-6). The history SCHEMA needs no change: its subset validator ignores unknown entry fields (HV-6), so `deleted_at` passes untouched |
| D102 | Mounts — remote storage via rclone-managed mounts as local paths (`shell/mounts.py`, `/view/_mounts`) | A mount = a named remote-storage mount: an rclone remote spec (`s3remote:bucket/prefix`) + a mountpoint under `home_dir()/mounts/<name>`. Mount lifecycle goes through a detached `rclone rcd` daemon over its local HTTP API (`mount/mount`, `mount/unmount`, `mount/listmounts`), spawn-or-reuse via `home_dir()/rcd.json` — the tile-server daemon pattern. Mounts deliberately **survive server restarts** (rcd is `start_new_session`); a fresh server adopts them via listmounts, and per-mount opt-in `automount` remounts flagged ones at startup in a background thread. Unmount-while-busy (measured EBUSY when a tile daemon holds a file open) asks both daemon state files (`~/.cache/fused-render-geotiff-v2/daemon.json`, `~/.cache/fused-render-gridv2/daemon.json` — parallel implementations, both always handled) to `/quit`, retries once, then fails loudly; never force-unmounts. `/api/fs/walk` clamps to `WALK_MAX_ENTRIES_REMOTE` (2 000) under the mounts dir — every dir listed in a mount is a remote LIST call. Credentials live exclusively in rclone's config: S3-compatible remotes are created non-interactively from the page; OAuth backends (Drive…) are set up with `rclone config` in a terminal (in-app OAuth deferred). vfs flags validated by measurement on a 204MB COG + 362MB parquet: `CacheMode full` (warm reads ~0.01s vs 15–130s cold), `CacheMaxAge 24h` (the 1h default's eviction is what made revisits slow), 8M/64M chunked reads; `--vfs-read-ahead` measured a net LOSS (slows the parquet footer read) and left out. Cold first-reads are bounded by the remote file's *layout* (row-group size, overview levels), not the viewer — remote-optimized formats are the fix, documented not coded. macOS mounts via `nfsmount` (built-in NFS client, no macFUSE kext); Linux via FUSE `mount`; Windows via rclone's default WinFsp `mount` (D132 — WinFsp user-installed, not bundled) | Owner-directed 2026-07-10 after a validated prototype (S3 sentinel-cogs COG tiles + GDrive rendered through unmodified readers/tile servers). **D2/D3 reframed, not overturned**: the app still only ever sees local absolute paths; remoteness lives in the mount layer. rcd over per-mount child processes: a pkill'd server orphaned its mount child (measured), and rcd fixes orphaning/supervision/OS-specific unmount in one move. Mountpoints under the dotdir over OS-standard locations: `/Volumes`+`/mnt` need root, and user-visible dirs (`~/Documents`) invite iCloud/Spotlight to crawl a network mount. A desktop-client tier (Google Drive Desktop's `~/Library/CloudStorage` as mount-free mounts) was built, verified, then dropped — detection and install guidance are macOS-only (no official Drive/OneDrive Linux clients), and one OS-agnostic mechanism keeps the concept simple; users can still browse/bookmark those paths directly |
| D103 | rclone bundled into the packaged macOS app (`build_dmg.sh`, `shell/mounts.py`) — D102's mounts (rclone) drops from a user prerequisite to zero-setup | `build_dmg.sh` downloads a pinned rclone release (`v1.74.4-osx-arm64`, matching this script's Apple Silicon-only py2app target — see the FRAMEWORK_PYTHON note) against its published sha256, stages it under `build/rclone-bin/<version>/` (cached — reruns skip the download unless the pin changes), then after the py2app build copies it to `Contents/Resources/bin/rclone` — same spot as the existing bundled `fused` CLI wrapper (D-fused/§19 DP-3) — with a `rclone version` smoke test before signing. It's a real Mach-O binary (a statically-linked Go build, only system frameworks in its `otool -L`), so the existing signing sweep (§5, walks the whole bundle by magic-byte detection) signs it with no dedicated rule. `shell/mounts.py`'s `rclone_bin()` — previously the single `shutil.which("rclone")` choke point every mount call went through — now checks `sys.frozen == "macosx_app"` first (the same check as `deploy.py`'s `_setup_cli_hint`) and resolves the bundled path off `sys.executable`, falling back to `shutil.which` when unpackaged (dev checkout, Linux) so the source workflow is untouched | Owner-directed 2026-07-14: rclone was the app's one remaining external prerequisite (README) for a feature (D102) otherwise fully self-contained; bundling removes it. arm64-only and hardcoded-version-plus-checksum were explicit owner calls over a universal2 lipo'd binary and an always-fetch-latest build (matches the build script's existing single-arch Apple Silicon target and its already-immutable-per-run venv/icon caching pattern) |
### Directory views — the built-in listing becomes a mode (2026-07-09, M13)

| # | Decision | Choice | Rationale / rejected alternatives |
|---|---|---|---|
| D81 | Directory views: the built-in listing becomes the `_listing` sentinel, a universal `/` directory key, `?listing=1` removed (revises D65/D73) | Adds a **third way to render a directory beyond zarr**: user-defined directory-view templates, ordered beside the built-in listing in one mode list. **Server:** `KNOWN_SENTINELS` grows to **`{"_render", "_listing"}`**; `_listing` is the shell's built-in directory listing, emitted `{mode:"_listing", path:null, icon:null}` without touching the FS, referenceable from any registry list exactly like `_render`. The matcher (`_key_segments`/`_match_registry`) gains a **universal directory key** — the bare `/`, a **zero-segment** key that matches *any* directory at the **lowest** specificity (below every dot-anchored dir key, so `.zarr/` > `/`), stem = the whole basename. Built-in `templates/registry.json` ships **`"/": ["_listing"]`** and changes **`".zarr/"` from `["zarr"]` to `["zarr", "_listing"]`** — so every directory now carries a non-empty `templates` list (≥ `["_listing"]`) and dispatch is uniform (a directory previews its default mode like a file; `is_dir` no longer forks the shell except as a safety net). **Shell:** `App.tsx` dispatch drops the `?listing=1`/`forceListing` branch — a directory routes to `Preview` (it always has modes) with `dir + empty templates → Listing` kept only as the safety net for a `null`-disabled dir. `Preview.tsx`'s `TemplatePreview` renders `<Listing fsPath>` in the body when the active mode is `_listing` (the same special-case shape as `_render`'s no-`_file` iframe), including a plain folder's single `_listing` mode — so the preview header is uniform across files and dirs; `ModeSwitcher`/`modeTitle` gain a shell-baked `_listing` list icon + "Listing" label. Annotate is a template mode (D78) bound only to file keys, so it never lands in a directory's list and never applies to `_listing`. **`?listing=1` is removed** (revises D65/PT-13): the listing is reachable only via `_mode=_listing`. The normal-view **"Browse contents" button is gone** — the switcher's `_listing` icon is now that affordance (and a peer mode you can switch back from, unlike the old one-way trip); the embed-only `.preview-browse-chip` is repointed to **toggle `_mode=_listing`** so embedded directories keep their members within reach. Per-directory-**name** binding (literal, non-dot-anchored keys) is **deferred**. | Owner call 2026-07-09. Custom directory views were impossible because the listing lived *outside* the mode list — a shell fallback, not a mode — so nothing could sit beside it or default over it, and no registry grammar could bind a view to a plain extension-less folder. Promoting the listing to the `_listing` sentinel makes it an orderable peer (`["_listing", "gallery"]`, `["gallery", "_listing"]`), and the universal `/` key is the missing grammar for "every folder"; users compose by listing modes explicitly (`"/": ["_listing", "gallery"]`; the `"..."` splice was later removed, D94). **Registry-driven** default (`"/": ["_listing"]`) chosen over leaving listing a pure shell fallback (owner) — the model would otherwise be half-applied (couldn't order or switch the listing); shipping it as data keeps dispatch uniform and modes composable. **`?listing=1` removed** (owner) collapses two ways-to-the-listing into one mechanism (`_mode`) and upgrades zarr's one-way "Browse contents" into a real switchable mode. The plain-folder bare-render special case was dropped (owner) so the header is uniform. Rejected: keeping listing a fallback-only view (half-model); forcing `_listing` to always be present (owner keeps the D73 "user can shoot themselves" posture — dropping it forgoes the listing for those dirs, an explicit `_listing` entry restores it); per-name binding now (needs a non-dot-anchored literal-key grammar with its own precedence story — deferred until a real need). Accepted break: old `?listing=1` bookmarks ignore the dropped param — a plain folder still lists (its default), a `.zarr` bookmark opens the map, not members (same class as D62's `_mode=source` break). Milestone **M13**; PT-12/PT-13, CT-2/CT-3 updated. |
### M15 — Template management view (2026-07-09, full spec = SPEC §23)

| # | Decision | Choice | Rationale / rejected alternatives |
|---|---|---|---|
| D86 | Sources abstraction for template provenance | Generalizes the core/user pair (§7/§16) into an ordered `Source {id, label, editable, precedence}` list (SPEC TV-1); today exactly two ship (`core` precedence 0, `user` precedence 100, unchanged directories/precedence from D50/D73/D76). Effective binding resolution (highest-precedence match wins) is unchanged — sources are a provenance/presentation layer over PT-6/CT-3, not a new resolution rule (TV-2). Modeled, not built: a third (org/project) source can be appended later with zero UI rework | Chosen so the inventory/bindings UI (§23) never hardcodes "two sources" — every list, filter, and picker groups by the sources array instead of an `is_core` boolean. Rejected a boolean/enum core-vs-user flag: cheaper today, but the frozen contract explicitly anticipates a third source, and retrofitting a list later would touch every UI surface that currently branches on the boolean. Rejected building a third source now — no concrete need yet, the model is the deliverable |
| D87 | Registry PUT/reset operate per-key, not whole-file | `PUT /api/templates/registry` (TV-5) takes `{key, value}` and does a read-modify-write of **that key only** against the user registry file (atomic via the existing `read_json`/`write_json` helpers); `POST /api/templates/registry/reset` (TV-6) deletes one key the same way. Neither endpoint accepts or writes the whole registry object | Bookmarks (D75) and prefs use whole-tree last-write-wins PUTs — fine there because each is edited from one UI surface with one shape. The registry is edited one row at a time from a table with potentially many independent rows open across a session (a row editor saves and closes, another row opens); a whole-file PUT built from a client's possibly-stale full copy would silently drop a concurrent edit to a different key. Per-key RMW removes that lost-update window without adding server state or locking (SV-1 stands — one open+modify+atomic-replace, not a held lock) |
| D88 | Editing template internals stays in the file explorer, not a second editor | The Templates view (§23) never edits `template.html`/`reader.py`/css/icon file contents; its header states so explicitly (TV-18). Users use the existing file explorer + `code` template (`templates/code/`) + `/api/fs/write`, which already has autosave, optimistic locking, and conflict handling (D37/D41) | The alternative — embedding a CodeMirror buffer in the Row editor or an inventory row for "quick edits" — would duplicate a feature that already exists and already handles the hard parts (save conflicts, autosave). "Open in explorer" (TV-10/TV-16) gets a user to the real file in one click; a second, lesser editor inside Templates would be scope creep with no capability the file explorer lacks |
| D89 | Export zips contain template folders only, never registry.json | `GET /api/templates/export` (TV-7) zips the selected templates' folder contents at top level; no registry bindings are included. **Revised 2026-07-09 (owner):** export is not user-only — **core templates are exportable too** (user-shadow-wins resolution), since a template folder is portable regardless of source; names travel as repeated `names=` params so a comma in a folder name round-trips. Import (TV-8/TV-9) never writes or merges registry entries — an imported template lands inert (CT-7) until a user explicitly binds it via the Row editor | A template folder is portable; a registry binding is a local, potentially conflicting choice — the destination machine may already use that extension differently, or not want it re-pointed at all. Shipping bindings in the zip would mean import silently rewrites someone else's registry as a side effect of moving files. Rejected auto-merging matching registry keys on import: reintroduces full conflict semantics (what if the destination already binds `.parquet` to something else?) at the registry-key level on top of the folder-level conflicts import already resolves — two conflict systems instead of one |
| D90 | Two-step staged import: stage + manifest, then commit with per-item resolution | `POST /api/templates/import` (TV-8) unpacks the uploaded zip to `home_dir()/.import-staging/<importId>/` and returns a manifest (valid/invalid/`conflictsExisting` per top-level folder) without touching `USER_TEMPLATES_DIR`; `POST /api/templates/import/{importId}/commit` (TV-9) applies a per-item `overwrite|skip|keep-both` resolution and moves from staging. Hardening at stage time: uncompressed size > 50 MB, entry count > 2000, or a single entry > 25 MB rejects the whole upload (zip-bomb guard); any entry that's absolute, contains `..`, normalizes outside the staging root, or is a symlink rejects the whole upload (zip-slip guard); `importId` is a `secrets.token_hex` token, staging dirs expire and are swept opportunistically per call | Conflicts can only be known after inspecting the zip's contents against what's already installed, and the ask was **per-conflict** resolution, not one global overwrite/skip flag — that needs a durable server-side step between "uploaded" and "committed" so the UI can show a table and let the user decide per item. Rejected one-shot import with a global flag: no per-item control, and an all-or-nothing reject on any conflict punishes an import bringing 9 new templates and 1 name collision. Zip-slip/zip-bomb guards are non-negotiable server-side regardless of any client-side zip preview, since the upload is untrusted input reaching the filesystem |
| D91 | UI exposes the full key grammar, including null/disable, not a simplified subset | The Row editor's key pattern builder (create mode) covers all four CT-3 key shapes — simple, compound, wildcard, directory — with live preview and client-side validation (server re-validates, authoritative); "Disable for this type" (writes `null`) is a first-class action next to Save/Reset (TV-15) | The registry grammar already supports compound (`.tar.gz`), wildcard (`.*.json`), directory (`.zarr/`), and null-disable keys server-side (§16) — some already used by the shipped built-in registry. A UI that only handled simple `.ext` keys would make those existing, valid registry states unreachable or un-undoable except by hand-editing `registry.json`, defeating the point of shipping a management UI at all. Rejected a simple-keys-only UI with "edit the JSON yourself" for the rest: makes the UI a subset tool rather than a full replacement for manual registry editing |
| D92 | Templates management ships as its own route, not folded into Preferences | New sentinel route `/view/_templates` + `Templates.tsx` (TV-11), reachable from a Sidebar button beside the Preferences gear (TV-12) — not a new section on `/view/_prefs`. **Revised 2026-07-09 (owner):** the read-only registry glance was *removed* from Preferences (PF-7, §20.5) once this view shipped — one home for bindings, not a glance in Preferences plus an editor here. The `GET /api/templates/registry` endpoint stays (unchanged contract, TV-4); it is now consumed by the Templates view instead of Preferences | The feature's surface — a bindings table, a multi-field Row editor modal, an inventory panel, and a multi-step import wizard — is materially larger than any existing Preferences section (§20's four are each a thin client over one existing backend) and has its own mutation-triggered refresh lifecycle. Folding it in would turn Preferences into a mixed bag of one-off toggles and a full CRUD surface. Rejected replacing PF-7 in place: breaks the "quick read-only glance" Preferences currently serves, and the frozen contract explicitly keeps the old endpoint working for it. Rejected a modal launched from Preferences instead of a route: loses URL-addressability/bookmarkability of a specific filter or row-editor state, inconsistent with every other shell surface (D8/D20/D40) being reachable via URL |
| D93 | User templates are deletable with an export-first prompt; core never | `POST /api/templates/delete` (TV-19) removes one folder under `USER_TEMPLATES_DIR` only — **core is read-only**, a core-only name 404s and its folder is untouched; unsafe names → 400, symlinks rejected. The Library Delete action (user rows only) opens a confirm modal offering "Export & delete" (downloads a recovery zip first, deletes only if that resolves), "Delete without export", or Cancel. Registry bindings are **not** rewritten — a binding to the deleted name resolves broken until rebound (folder-only, like export/import). **(Bindings stance and modal buttons superseded by D109: two default-on checkboxes — export-first + a `cleanRegistry` user-registry sweep — with Delete/Cancel only.)** | Owner ask 2026-07-09 (delete was previously deferred to the file explorer). Export-first because a delete is destructive and a template folder is the only copy; letting the user grab a zip in the same gesture makes it recoverable without a trash/undo system (D3 single-user, no server-side trash). Rejected cascading the delete into registry edits: two mutations in one destructive click, and a broken binding is visible + fixable in the same view; rejected a soft-delete/trash (no infra for it, file explorer already reveals the folder) |
| D94 | The `/view/_templates` tab lives in the URL (`?tab=`), switched via navigateUrl | The active tab (bindings default / `?tab=library`) is derived from `location.search`; switching calls `navigateUrl` (pushState + `fused:navigate`) so the nav epoch bumps, App remounts the view, and it re-derives the tab — and browser back/forward moves between tabs via popstate | Owner ask 2026-07-09 (back/forward should work across tabs). Chosen navigateUrl over raw `history.pushState`: the nav epoch (`useNavEpoch`) listens to `popstate`/`fused:navigate`, not the `fused:urlchange` that a bare pushState fires, so a raw pushState changed the URL but never re-rendered the view. Deriving from the URL instead of local state keeps "URL is state" (D40) consistent and needs no popstate listener — the epoch remount already re-reads it |
| D95 | Splice token `"..."` removed; empty list `[]` disables like `null` | The list-splice grammar (D60/CT-11) is **gone**: a `"..."` registry entry is no longer expanded to the built-in list. It resolves to no folder, so it is treated as an ordinary **dangling name** — dropped from the rendered mode list with a `template_error`, surfaced as a broken (`exists:false`) ref in the Templates view. `PUT /api/templates/registry` **accepts** it — a dangling name is saved, not rejected, so a binding with one bad entry is never un-savable — and the row editor shows a warning naming it (nothing auto-removed, the user decides). PUT rejects only structurally invalid entries (non-string/empty). Separately, an **empty list `[]` now disables** previews for a type, exactly like `null` (no built-in fallback) — `_names_from_value` maps both to disabled. Built-in `registry.json` never used a splice, so nothing shipped breaks; the directory-view compose example (D81) becomes an explicit list | Owner call 2026-07-09. The splice was a hidden indirection — a binding's real mode list wasn't visible in the JSON, and a dangling/typo'd entry masqueraded as intentional; making every name literal keeps the registry WYSIWYG and lets the management UI flag dead pointers. Empty-list-disables removes a footgun where `[]` silently fell through to the built-in list instead of meaning "nothing", and gives the UI a natural disable path that isn't `null`-only |

### Explorer search — streamed breadth-first walk (2026-07-09, M14)

| # | Decision | Choice | Rationale / rejected alternatives |
|---|---|---|---|
| D85 | The explorer search walks breadth-first, streams NDJSON, and scores client-side; the entry cap is a safety valve, not a budget | `/api/fs/walk` rewritten from `os.walk` (depth-first) to a FIFO `_walk_bfs` generator: **every depth-N entry emits before any depth-N+1**, so an early stop always leaves complete shallow coverage. `?stream=1` streams `{"entries":[…]}` NDJSON batches (500/line) + one terminal `{done, truncated, total}` line; client disconnect closes the generator and cancels the walk. Non-stream shape unchanged for old clients. Pruning: `.git`/`.venv` join `WALK_IGNORE_DIRS` (never emitted nor descended, hidden mode or not); macOS packages (`.app`/`.framework`/`.bundle`/`.photoslibrary`) emit as one leaf entry, never descended (Finder semantics). Cap raised 20k → **200k** and reframed as a memory/latency valve — BFS means it only ever cuts the deepest levels of pathological trees, and the UI reports it (live `N matches · M scanned…` counter, `+`-suffixed final count, zero-match message naming the covered count). The shell fetches **one hidden-inclusive dataset** (`hidden=1` always) and gates dot-entries at display time by query intent (dot-leading segment shows them) — `.py` is an extension search, intent flips never refetch. Scoring stays client-side, incremental: flushes throttled to 200 ms, each scoring only newly arrived entries merged into the prior ranked list; full re-scan only on query/intent change. Results page in 250-row chunks via a sentinel row + IntersectionObserver; the query mirrors to the URL debounced 200 ms (Safari throttles `replaceState` and then throws). | Owner report 2026-07-09: search unusable — depth-first + 20k cap let `~/Desktop` eat 15,926 slots so Movies/Music/Pictures had **zero** searchable children; `/Applications` burned 7,557 entries inside one `.app`; first paint blocked ~1 s on the full walk; a later report found per-chunk full re-scoring saturated the main thread (stuck stale-dim, dead clicks) — hence the throttle + incremental merge. Client-side scoring kept over a server-side paged search (owner probed this): ranked fuzzy search must scan the full corpus per query wherever it lives, the "server" is the same machine (moving ~30 MB between processes saves nothing), per-keystroke ranking then costs an HTTP round trip + Python-speed matching, and the local-tool precedent (fzf, VS Code Quick Open, Everything) is corpus-in-matcher-memory. Revisit only as a persistent Spotlight-style index if the cap ever genuinely bites. Rejected: walk-more-on-scroll (can't rank what you haven't walked — scroll pages the *rendered* rows instead); a second hidden-only walk per dot-query (double fetch, wrong-dataset races — the old `forHidden` state machine dissolved once one dataset carried both). |

### Bookmark files groundwork (2026-07-10)

| # | Decision | Choice | Rationale / rejected alternatives |
|---|---|---|---|
| D97 | Bookmark names are globally unique, case-insensitive; duplicates auto-suffix, existing files migrate on GET | The namespace is **all bookmarks** — top-level and folder children together (folder names are a separate namespace, untouched). The uniqueness key is the **sanitized filename stem, lowercased** (`sanitizeBookmarkStem`: `/ \ :` and control chars → `-`): case-insensitive (`notes` collides with `Notes`) and sanitize-collision-proof (`a/b` collides with `a:b` — both would export as `a-b.bookmark`). **Going forward** the frontend enforces it inside `addBookmark`/`renameBookmark` (`lib/bookmarks.ts` `uniqueNameIn`, exported preview `uniqueBookmarkName`): a colliding create/rename silently becomes `<name>-1`, `<name>-2`, ... — the first suffix free against every current name — never a rejection. **Existing files** migrate once in `GET /api/bookmarks` (`shell/bookmarks.py` `_dedupe_names`): oldest by `created_at` keeps its name, each newer duplicate gets the first free suffix (a pre-existing literal `x-1` is dodged, the duplicate jumps to `x-2`); the migrated tree is written back only when something changed, so the routine GET stays read-only and the migration is idempotent | Groundwork for bookmark files: a bookmark's name becomes its `<name>.bookmark` filename, and filesystems are one-name-one-file (case-insensitive on the default macOS volume — hence case-insensitive here). Auto-suffix chosen over rejecting the mutation: SB-3's one-click no-prompt capture has no UI moment to report a name conflict, and Finder's own "copy 2" precedent matches. Migration on GET (not PUT, not startup) because GET is the single load path every shell boot already hits; PUT-side validation was rejected — the server would have to either reject (breaking last-write-wins whole-tree PUTs from older tabs) or silently rewrite what the client just committed (cache/server divergence, D77 poll notwithstanding) |
| D98 | "Save to disk" writes a portable `<name>.bookmark` (format v1) into the deepest common ancestor dir of the bookmark's targets; the frontend computes the file, the server only validates and writes | File format v1: `{version: 1, name, icon?, kind: single\|panel\|tab, path?, search}` — `path` (single only) is the target relative to the `.bookmark` file's own dir; `search` is the bookmark url's search string verbatim, no leading `?`, except panel/tab `_layout` leaf paths are rewritten relative to the save dir (grammar, nesting, per-leaf queries and global params untouched). Save dir = the single target's containing dir, or the deepest common ancestor directory of all layout leaves, compared path-segment-wise (`/a/bc` vs `/a/b` share `/a`, never a string prefix) over each leaf's containing dir — every leaf sits strictly under it, so relative paths never contain `..`. Filename = sanitized bookmark name + `.bookmark` (`/ \ :` and control chars → `-`; names are already globally unique, D97). Split: `lib/bookmark-file.ts` (`bookmarkSaveTarget`) computes `{dir, filename, content}` reusing the router codec and the `_layout` codec; `POST /api/bookmarks/export` (X-Fused-guarded) validates — absolute existing dir, bare `*.bookmark` filename, content parses as JSON with an int `version` — and writes verbatim, overwrite allowed. Not savable (button greyed, title says why; the enabled title shows the exact destination): any leaf without an absolute fs path, zero leaves, or no common root (different Windows drive letters) | Relative paths keep the file portable — a folder moved or synced with its `.bookmark` inside still resolves; the common-ancestor rule is the deepest dir that can express every leaf without `..`. Frontend-computes/server-writes because the `_layout` grammar's only parser lives in the shell codec (`splitShellSearch` — plain query parsing cannot read it); duplicating it in Python would be a second parser to drift, so the server stays a dumb validated writer. Overwrite-not-reject: the name is globally unique (D97), so an existing file is a stale snapshot of the same bookmark and a re-save is a refresh |
| D99 | Double-clicking a `.bookmark` in Finder opens the described view: the app registers the doc type (Owner) and routes to a `/view/_bookmark` sentinel; the frontend resolves relative paths, the server only reads. macOS only | Doc-type registration (`scripts/setup_py2app.py`): a new `CFBundleDocumentTypes` entry at `LSHandlerRank: Owner` — unlike the existing extensions, `.bookmark` is our own format — backed by a `UTExportedTypeDeclarations` for `io.fused.render.bookmark` (conforms to `public.json`; identifier deliberately NOT branch-suffixed — every build describes the same format). Open path: `app.py view_url_path` (module-level, AppKit-free, testable) maps any Finder-opened `*.bookmark` (case-insensitive) to `/view/_bookmark?file=<url-encoded abs path>` instead of `/view/<path>`; the existing pending-open queue and single-instance handoff are untouched. The `_bookmark` sentinel (App.tsx + `views/BookmarkOpen.tsx`) calls `GET /api/bookmark-file?path=` (`shell/bookmarks.py`, unguarded read-only GET like `GET /api/bookmarks`) which validates — absolute path, exists, `*.bookmark`, JSON dict with `version == 1` (anything else 400s with a clear message, forward-compat) — and returns `{dir, bookmark}`; `lib/bookmark-file.ts` `bookmarkOpenUrl` (the exact inverse of D98's relativize, same file) absolutizes `path` / every `_layout` leaf against `dir` (already-absolute paths pass through, defensive) and the view `location.replace()`s to the result — single, `_panel` or `_tab` by `kind`. Browsing to a `.bookmark` in the explorer renders the same redirector instead of StatView. Errors render in place, never redirect (no loops) | Frontend-resolves/server-reads mirrors D98's split for the same reason: the `_layout` parser lives only in the shell codec, and Python must not grow a second one. Sentinel page over a server-side 302 because the resolution needs that codec, which only runs in the browser. macOS only: the doc-type plumbing is py2app/LaunchServices; other platforms still open the file via the explorer path. `replace` over `assign` so Back never re-enters the redirect page |

### Explorer search — gitignore-driven pruning (2026-07-10)

| # | Decision | Choice | Rationale / rejected alternatives |
|---|---|---|---|
| D100 | The walk prunes what each repo's own gitignore ignores, via a streaming `check-ignore` co-process; the hardcoded dir set shrinks to a universal floor; search excludes rather than dims (revises SR-2, D85's pruning + D-#75's walk dimming) | Inside a git repository, `_walk_bfs` drops every entry the repo's gitignore rules ignore — not emitted, not descended. Verdicts come from `_IgnoreOracle`: one long-lived `git check-ignore --stdin -z -v -n` subprocess per repo answering path queries incrementally (measured ~14 µs/query; `-v -n` echoes all four NUL fields for every query, making the stream pairable, and exposes the matched pattern's sign so negations like `!keep.log` are honored — a match on a `!` pattern is NOT ignored). Queries are chunked (200/write) so git's stdout can't fill the pipe mid-write (co-process deadlock). Repo tracking rides the BFS queue: each dir inherits its parent's repo root, a `.git` child (dir or worktree/submodule gitfile) starts a nested repo with its own rules, and a walk rooted below a repo top resolves it via one `git rev-parse --show-toplevel`. A `.gitignore` with NO repo in scope (un-inited project, Obsidian vault — the owner's content-engine/.env report) starts a standalone ignore root: the oracle grafts the directory onto a shared lazily-created empty `GIT_DIR` as its `GIT_WORK_TREE`, so the same check-ignore co-process honors standalone .gitignore files, cascading and negations included. Oracles are LRU-capped (`WALK_MAX_ORACLES` = 8) and all closed when the walk generator ends; any git failure marks that oracle broken → no pruning for that repo (optimization, never a dependency). `WALK_IGNORE_DIRS` shrinks in role to the universal floor (`node_modules`, `__pycache__`, `venv`, `.venv`, `.git`, `site-packages` — new) covering non-repo junk and `.git` itself. Consequence: walk entries no longer carry the `ignored` dimming flag (#75's per-batch `_annotate_ignored` removed — nothing ignored survives to dim); `/api/fs/list` keeps dimming. Measured: fused-render repo walk 292 entries in 43 ms (gitignored `shell-dist`, lockfile churn etc. gone). | Owner call 2026-07-10 ("check if the folder has .gitignore and ignore those files — makes much more sense than hardcoding"): the hardcoded set could never enumerate every ecosystem's junk (`dist`, `build`, `.next`, `target`, `.turbo`, …), while each repo's gitignore is the project's own authoritative noise list. Search-excludes-vs-listing-dims mirrors VS Code (explorer shows gitignored, Quick Open hides). Rejected: parsing .gitignore in Python (reimplementing git's semantics — nested files, excludesfile, negation precedence — when `check-ignore` is the authority, same posture as #75's `_git_ignored`); one subprocess per directory (thousands of ~10 ms spawns per walk); one giant upfront batch per repo (the walk discovers paths incrementally — a co-process answers as it goes); pruning by the `ignored` flag client-side (entries would still be walked, streamed, and scored — the win is not descending at all). No user toggle yet — add "search ignored files too" only if someone actually needs to find a build artifact. |

### Bookmark localStorage migration removed (2026-07-14)

| # | Decision | Choice | Rationale / rejected alternatives |
|---|---|---|---|
| D104 | The one-time legacy `localStorage["fused.bookmarks"]` import (D75) is deleted outright, not just left dormant | `lib/bookmarks.ts` drops `LS_KEY`, `MIGRATED_KEY`, `legacyMigrated`/`markLegacyMigrated`, `readLegacy`, and `mergeLegacy`; `hydrateBookmarks()` is a plain GET-into-cache with no import branch. The server's `exists` flag on `GET /api/bookmarks` (`shell/bookmarks.py`) is unchanged — it still distinguishes absent/corrupt from a valid (possibly empty) file — but its docstring/comments no longer describe it as gating a localStorage import. D21/D75's historical entries are left as-is (they record what was true when written) | Owner call 2026-07-14: every pre-D75 install has long since had its bookmarks migrated to `~/.fused-render/bookmarks.json` on first boot after that release; no interim version is being supported going forward, so the dormant fallback code is dead weight. Rejected keeping it "just in case" — the whole point of the `MIGRATED_KEY` marker (which already decoupled the import from `exists`) was to run the merge at most once per install; those installs have all run it by now |
| D105 | "New template" scaffolds from a starter kit shipped outside `templates/`, with a self-contained authoring skill; extensions chosen at create time; "Open in Claude" spawns Terminal (macOS-only) | New backend endpoints (TV-20/TV-21). **Starter kit lives at `fused_render/template_starter/`** — inside the package (a `git add` makes hatchling ship it, `.claude/` dot-dir and all; a wheel-build test confirms it) but **deliberately outside `fused_render/templates/`** so the inventory scanner (which walks `TEMPLATES_DIR`/`USER_TEMPLATES_DIR`) never sees it and it never resolves as a template. It holds `template.html` (required), `reader.py` (optional read-only data-prep stub), `CLAUDE.md` (authoring contract), and **`.claude/skills/template-authoring/SKILL.md`** — a **self-contained** authoring skill copied into every new template, so each scaffolded folder carries its own guidance (no dependency on a repo-level skill). **`condition.py` is documented but NOT scaffolded by default** (the common case is unconditional; the author adds one only when gating). `POST /api/templates/new {name, extensions}` copies the kit into `USER_TEMPLATES_DIR/<name>` and, for each extension, **appends** `name` to whatever list that key currently resolves to (its user override, or the core default) via the **same per-key RMW as D87** (`_apply_binding`, shared with PUT) — never a whole-list replace, so an existing multi-mode binding keeps its other templates. Extensions are asked at create time, then editable via the ordinary Row editor (TV-15). `POST /api/templates/open-in-claude {name}` runs `claude` in the folder via `osascript`/Terminal.app; `_claude_bin()` is replicated from `templates/claude/agent.py` (not imported — a template folder is not an import root) | Owner ask (M15 follow-up): make authoring a real flow, not "hand-create a folder and hand-edit registry.json". Starter-outside-`templates/` keeps the scaffold from polluting the inventory or shadowing a name while staying wheel-shipped (one source dir, copied per template). Per-template self-contained skill chosen over pointing at a repo skill so an exported/moved template stays authorable anywhere. `condition.py` left unscaffolded to keep the default template minimal — a gate is opt-in, and an empty/always-true `condition.py` would be a footgun (fail-closed on a broken one). Extensions-at-create reuse D87's per-key RMW rather than a whole-file write so a create never clobbers concurrent binding edits. macOS-only Terminal spawn because `osascript`/Terminal.app is the only wired path today; other platforms return a clear error rather than a broken half-launch (Linux/Windows terminal spawn deferred until needed) |
| D106 | Starter-kit authoring skills are **build-time copies of the canonical repo skills** — single source, no committed duplicate (refines D105) | Replaces D105's bespoke `.claude/skills/template-authoring/SKILL.md` (deleted). The scaffolded template now carries the **two canonical skills** — `fused-render-authoring` (writing `template.html` + `reader.py`, the `fused` runtime bridge) and `fused-render-custom-templates` (registration, registry keys, `condition.py`, `icon.svg`) — copied verbatim from the repo-level `skills/<name>/`, which stay the sole source of truth. Two delivery paths, both keying off `skills/`: (1) **wheel build** — the hatchling hook (`scripts/hatch_build.py`) copies the two skills into `fused_render/template_starter/.claude/skills/` at build time, gitignored there and shipped via pyproject `artifacts` (same not-committed-but-packaged pattern as the Vite shell, D54); `POST /api/templates/new`'s copytree then carries them into the new folder. (2) **editable/dev install** — the gitignored starter skills dir is absent, so `_ensure_starter_skills(dest)` resolves the repo `skills/` dir (`../skills` relative to the package) and copies the two folders directly after scaffolding. If neither source exists, creation proceeds **without** skills (never fails). `CLAUDE.md` in the starter is a lean redirect (what the folder is + a pointer to the two skills), no contract content duplicated | Owner ask: skill content should have a single source. The bespoke per-template skill (D105) duplicated the canonical skills' contract, so edits drifted between two copies. Build-time copy keeps the wheel self-contained (an exported/moved template still carries its guidance) while committing zero duplicates — the same trade-off D54 already accepts for the shell bundle. The dev fallback exists because editable installs skip the build hook (D54), so tests and dev scaffolds resolve `skills/` at create time instead |

### Template binding recommendations in export/import (2026-07-15)

| # | Decision | Choice | Rationale / rejected alternatives |
|---|---|---|---|
| D107 | Export zips carry a root `recommendation.json` — a **template → keys** map of each exported template's bindings, built by reverse lookup over the MERGED registry; recommendations only, never registry rows (refines D89) | `GET /api/templates/export` (TV-7) always writes a root-level `recommendation.json`: `{"version": 1, "recommendations": {"<template>": ["<registry key>", …]}}` (TV-22). Built per export by reverse lookup over the **effective merged** registry (user shadows core per key, TV-2): a template maps to every key whose effective list contains its name. Templates with no bindings are omitted; the file is written even when the map is empty; names and key lists sorted (deterministic zips). Import staging (TV-8/TV-23) parses it and excludes it from stray-file warnings; malformed JSON/shape → non-fatal warning, recommendations dropped (the folders still stage); `version != 1` silently ignored (forward-compat); CT-3-invalid keys filtered at staging with a per-key warning. Each valid staged item gains `recommendedKeys: [{key, status}]`, status ∈ `new` \| `already-bound` \| `disabled` (key has a user `null`/`[]` override) | Owner ask 2026-07-15: an exported template should carry *what it was for* — the receiving machine otherwise gets an inert folder and a guessing game. **Template → keys chosen over registry-key → list slices**: a slice would encode the author's mode *ordering*, and applying it on import would clobber the importer's own list order/composition for that key; a template→keys map only says "these keys suggest me", leaving ordering entirely to the importer's registry (append-only, D108). Merged-registry reverse lookup over builtin-or-user-file reads: what the author actually *sees* bound is the effective merged value — exporting a core template whose key the user re-ordered should recommend the user's reality, not a stale core row. Refines (not overturns) D89: still no `registry.json` in the zip, still nothing auto-merged — the sidecar is an offer the wizard surfaces, applied only per explicit user tick |
| D108 | Import commit applies accepted recommendations **append-only** after the folder moves, against **final** names; rename-follows-bindings; disabled keys re-enable only on explicit opt-in | `POST …/import/{id}/commit` (TV-9) gains optional `bindings: {originalStagedName: [keys]}` (TV-24). The whole map is CT-3-validated **before any move** — a 400 leaves the stage intact; a corrupt user registry is refused up front. After the moves, bindings resolve through the landed-name map: skipped/invalid templates' bindings are ignored; a keep-both rename binds the **new** folder name (the binding follows what actually landed). Application mirrors TV-20/D105's additive posture per key: already-bound → no-op; key only in core → the user entry is created as the **full core list + appended name** (never a shorter shadow); user-disabled key → re-enabled as core list + name, but **only when a binding for that key was explicitly requested**; appends always land at the END, never reordering existing user bindings. Response gains `bindingsApplied: [{key, template}]`. Wizard (TV-25): master toggle + per-template chips — `new` default ON, `disabled` default OFF (amber "disabled by you" badge, warn line on re-enable), `already-bound` inert (green badge, never sent), "+ add" custom-key chip; skip greys the strip, keep-both shows "will bind as `<renamed>`", commit button counts bindings, step 3 lists what applied. A zip without the sidecar leaves the wizard unchanged | Append-only over replace/insert-at-author's-position: the importer's existing mode order is their deliberate state (D87's per-key posture); an import must extend, never rearrange. Bind-after-moves-by-final-name because binding a name that never landed (skip) or landed renamed (keep-both) would create dangling or wrong refs — the landed map is ground truth. Full-core-list-plus-name for core-only keys reuses D105's rule: a user entry that shadows core must start as a faithful copy, or the import silently *removes* modes. Disabled-keys-opt-in (default-OFF chip + warning) because `null`/`[]` is an explicit user decision (D95) — an import silently undoing it would be the registry-rewrite side effect D89 rejected; surfacing it as an amber opt-in keeps the user the author of the re-enable. Validate-before-move so a bad bindings map can never half-apply an import (the stage survives a 400, retryable) |
| D109 | Delete modal becomes two default-checked checkboxes (export zip first, clean registry bindings) + exactly **Delete**/**Cancel**; the opt-out bindings sweep **supersedes D93's "registry bindings are not rewritten"** | The confirm modal's three buttons ("Export & delete" / "Delete without export" / Cancel) are replaced: two checkboxes, both CHECKED by default — "Export zip before deleting" (keeps D92/TV-16's export-first guarantee: the delete proceeds only after the recovery-zip download resolves) and "Remove registry bindings for this template" — plus a danger **Delete** and **Cancel**. `POST /api/templates/delete` (TV-19) gains optional `cleanRegistry: boolean` (default false; the modal always sends it explicitly): when true, after the folder delete the **user** registry is swept — every user key whose value references the deleted name drops it, and a key whose value the sweep EMPTIES is **removed entirely** (revert to core), never left as `[]`, which D95 defines as *disabled*. Name matching is exact (names are folder identities, not lowercased like keys); the core registry is never touched; a corrupt user registry is refused (400) BEFORE the rmtree, so a refusal leaves the folder intact. Response gains `registryKeysCleaned: [keys]` when the flag was set | Owner ask 2026-07-15, superseding D93's never-rewrite stance: D93 rejected the cascade as "two mutations in one destructive click", but with the sweep surfaced as a visible default-on checkbox the user can untick, the user authors both mutations in the same gesture — the objection was to *hidden* coupling, not to cleanup itself. Checkboxes over three buttons: the old buttons fused the export question into the primary action's identity and never asked the bindings question at all; two orthogonal toggles + one danger button state each choice explicitly. Emptied-override-removes-key over leaving `[]`: a sweep that left `[]` would convert "remove this template's bindings" into "disable previews for the type" (D95) — a stronger action the user never picked; removing the key reverts to core, the least-surprise state |
| D110 | Read-only truthfulness for archive previews and mounts (SPEC RO-7/RO-8): a **preview** copy from the zip/tar readers lands **0444** (written tmp + `os.replace`, so re-previews and concurrent previews are race-free), and a mount record carries a persisted `read_only` flag — re-detected non-mutatingly on every attach (rc `operations/fsinfo` write features + `config/get` anonymous-S3 check, shared predicate with `_public_object_url`; inconclusive probes persist nothing), overridable via a strict-boolean `read_only` in the create body that detection never overwrites — that `server._writable` folds in (mtime-cached, no store parse per stat), flipping `stat.writable` and the `/api/fs/write` 403 together | Two holes let rw templates edit files whose real backing store never takes the write: (a) an archive member previews as an ordinary writable temp copy, so "saved" edits land in the throwaway extract and silently never reach the archive; (b) under a mount `os.access(W_OK)` always says writable — with `CacheMode=full` a kernel write "succeeds" into the local VFS cache and only fails at the async upload (anonymous `aws-open` being the built-in case). The chmod route for previews reuses the ENTIRE existing RO contract (RO-1..RO-6) with zero template/server changes — the permission bit *is* the marking; deliberate extract/extract_all keeps writable output AND its fail-loud EACCES on write-protected targets (the preview mechanism must not smuggle silent replacement into user exports). Mount detection is deliberately non-mutating: a real write probe would drop junk objects into user buckets, and the VFS cache makes a kernel-mount probe a liar, so structurally write-less backends (http) and credential-less S3 are flagged and anything else — including IAM-restricted creds, which only a real write could expose — stays rw with the existing 403/conflict path as backstop. Re-detect-per-attach (rather than detect-once) so credential changes converge without recreating the mount; tri-state probes so a transient rcd hiccup can't freeze a wrong verdict; mounts.json writers serialized (`_store_lock`) now that the automount thread persists flags. Rejected mounting RO remotes with the VFS `ReadOnly` option for now — it is the deeper fix (kernel-truthful `W_OK`, covers RO-3 writer gates too) but per-mount vfs options split the mount/serve VFS instance unless the serve's option set forks identically, which needs its own validation; named follow-up in RO-8. `mount_view` exposes the flag; the Mounts card shows a "— read-only" hint |

### Windows Explorer "Open with" ships in the wheel (2026-07-15)

| # | Decision | Choice | Rationale / rejected alternatives |
|---|---|---|---|
| D105 | `winopen.py` and its assets are committed and packaged, reversing D-19e63cd's "keep off upstream" | `fused_render/winopen.py`, `fused_render/assets/fused-render.ico`, `fused_render/assets/file_icons/file.ico` (the generic fallback — the per-category icons aren't rendering right yet, so only this one ships for now; `_icon_for_ext` degrades any missing variant to `fused-render.ico`), and the `scripts/windows/` helpers are tracked; `[project.gui-scripts] fused-render-open = "fused_render.winopen:main"` is back in `pyproject.toml`. Hatchling's wheel builder only excludes files matched by `.gitignore` — a file kept out of the wheel via the *untracked/`.git/info/exclude`* route (as this one was) is invisible to that exclusion logic and gets packaged anyway whenever the person building the wheel happens to have it on disk, but is silently absent from any wheel built off a clean checkout (e.g. CI). That mismatch meant `--register` worked for some customers and failed with `No module named fused_render.winopen` for others, purely depending on who built their wheel | A customer's install failed at the file-opener registration step because her wheel came from a clean-checkout build (the new CI release pipeline) that never had this untracked file. Another customer's identical-looking instructions had worked, because she'd been handed a wheel built by hand on a machine where the file physically existed. Keeping a "supported" feature real only on one person's machine is a footgun that scales with every customer install; shipping it for real is the fix, not telling people which wheel to ask for |

### GitHub deep links — fused-render:// clone into the workspace (2026-07-15)

| # | Decision | Choice | Rationale / rejected alternatives |
|---|---|---|---|
| D110 | `fused-render://open?git=<github tree URL>` deep links: OS protocol handler → server confirm page → sparse clone into `~/Documents/Fused` → open `index.html`/folder; trust-on-confirm (SPEC §26) | The link carries the original GitHub URL verbatim as the `git` query param (author copies + prefixes; owner's shape call — action in host position, payloads as params, so future kinds add params, not grammar; the `git` value reads to end-of-string so unencoded URLs survive). macOS `CFBundleURLTypes` + `application:openURLs:` in app.py; Windows an HKCU `fused-render` URL-protocol class in the same `--register` as the Open-With keys; both entry points only ferry the raw URL to `GET /clone?src=…` — parsing, validation and cloning live server-side in `deeplink.py`. Nothing touches disk before the confirm page's button: the page (self-contained `static/clone.html`) previews via read-only `GET /api/clone/info` and its button fires the X-Fused-guarded `POST /api/clone`, which runs the user's own git (`clone --filter=blob:none --sparse` + `sparse-checkout set`) so private repos ride existing credentials. Destination = `<Fused dir>/<subpath basename>` holding the repo root (`.git` kept); re-click = `pull --ff-only`, dirty/diverged trees surface git's error, a foreign dir at the destination is refused, never clobbered. First `/tree/` segment = ref (single-segment refs only — the URL grammar can't delimit slashed branch names). The confirm page states the trust boundary in plain words: once opened, repo content renders same-origin and can run Python via `/api/run` — confirming the clone IS the trust grant (owner call: trust-on-confirm over sandboxing cloned content) | Owner asks 2026-07-15. Protocol-only (no hosted `https://…/open` redirect page) accepted knowing GitHub READMEs strip custom schemes — links work in Slack/docs/terminal, the redirect page can come later without changing the scheme. Sparse clone over GitHub tarballs: update-on-reclick needs `.git`, and tarballs would special-case private repos. Nesting accepted (`<dest>/<subpath>` skeleton dirs visible) over a hidden `.repos` + symlink scheme — less magic, git semantics intact, symlinks are second-class on Windows. Localhost `http://127.0.0.1:<port>/clone` links rejected as the carrier: branch-dependent port, server must already run, and a bare cross-origin GET link must never mutate disk anyway |

### Fused account — in-app login & setup (2026-07-15)

| # | Decision | Choice | Rationale / rejected alternatives |
|---|---|---|---|
| D111 | In-app Fused account surface (SPEC §27, M18): `/view/_account` + `/api/account/*` do `fused cloud login`, `cloud setup`, `env default/delete`, and `cloud logout` from the app; scope = the **managed `fused` backend only**, AWS provisioning stays terminal; the §1 "no authentication/user accounts" non-goal is **clarified, not reversed** | Login: spawn `fused cloud login --no-browser`, capture the authorize URL from child output (PYTHONUNBUFFERED=1), client opens it, `OPENFUSED_LOGIN_RETURN_URL` (loopback-validated) 302s the browser back into the app; completion polled via the DP-2b presence signal, deeper truth via `fused cloud orgs` (`?probe=1`). Setup: `fused cloud setup` as the one tracked background job (202 + job_id, polled `{state, detail}` with the CLI's own lines, 409 when signed out or busy, env-name defaults `fused`/`fused-<env>`). Logout kills-and-waits-out any in-flight login before the CLI deletes credentials. Deploy modal's DP-2b warning and no-envs guidance become working buttons (sign in in place / route to the setup panel); DP-16's bundled terminal wrapper demoted to a power-user escape hatch. Full contract: SPEC §27 (AC-1…AC-11) | Owner ask 2026-07-15 ("login and setup directly in fused-render, no copying CLI commands out of the app"). Mechanics ported from the flow app's proven connect-fused surface rather than reinvented — same CLI, same failure modes already shaken out (stdout buffering, return-URL hook, job polling, logout/login race). Managed-backend-only scope mirrors flow's line: AWS `env create`/`infra serve` provisioning is a long, credential-heavy, terminal-native flow with poor modal ergonomics — not worth blocking the 90% path for. Non-goal clarified because the surface manages the *fused CLI's* credentials for deploy targets; fused-render itself still has no accounts/tokens/users (D3 stands, X-Fused guard unchanged) |
| D112 | The account surface **shells the fused CLI** (subprocess through the DP-3 seam, extracted to `fusedcli.py`) — never the fused Python API in-process, and fused-render never reads or writes a credential itself | All auth/env state lives where the CLI puts it (JWT file, OS-keyring secrets store, envs.json); the app reads presence/status only. The seam module is shared by deploy.py and account.py, both routers mutually acyclic and neither importing server.py | In-process `fused.agent_core` calls were rejected: they'd bind fused-render to fused's internal APIs and version (the app must also drive an EXTERNAL `FUSED_RENDER_FUSED_BIN` CLI it can't import), couple the server's process to the CLI's credential/keyring behavior, and put token bytes in this process's memory for no gain. Subprocess keeps the CLI the single authority (DP-2b's posture), matches deploy.py and flow, and costs only stdout parsing + status polling — both proven. Cost accepted: `login`/`setup` have no `--json`, so the login URL is regex-captured from output and completion is polled |
