| D113 | **Stale-request cancellation for `runPython` (SPEC RH-9).** A third `opts` arg: `opts.key` (a string) names a **latest-wins channel** — a newer call on a key aborts the prior in-flight call on that same key; `opts.signal` (a standard `AbortSignal`) composes with it. A superseded/aborted call rejects with a plain `AbortError`, which the runtime's `unhandledrejection` handler treats as benign (no traceback overlay, no console noise). Implemented identically in the local runtime (`static/runtime.js`, fetch to `/api/run`) and the hosted/exported runtime (`fused` repo `widgets/static/fused_render_runtime.js`); the sine example passes `{ key: "sine" }` to demonstrate | The slider loop (`onChange` → `runPython`) fires one request per intermediate value while scrubbing; without cancellation every stale value is still computed server-side and its response can land out of order over the value the slider stopped on. Aborting the fetch frees the browser connection and lets the server drop the now-irrelevant subprocess when it notices the closed socket. AbortError is swallowed (not surfaced) so the existing fire-and-forget pattern (`onChange(draw)`, no `.catch`) keeps working — the losing re-render simply stops at its `await` without an overlay | Owner ask 2026-07-16 ("moving a slider through values should cancel the requests that are no longer relevant"). **Opt-in by key, not automatic by pyPath:** RH-4 allows concurrent calls, and shipped templates fire concurrent same-file calls that must all complete — `claude/agent.py` poll loops, `tar`/`sqlite` readers keyed by action, the netcdf/zarr tile browsers — so auto-cancelling by path would break them. Keying is a deliberate author choice; the default (no key) leaves RH-4 untouched. `AbortSignal` is the web-standard escape hatch for authors who want raw control; the `key` convenience is the one-liner that covers the slider case. Rejected "never-resolve superseded promise" (would strand suspended async frames and hide the reason) in favour of a real AbortError that authors *can* inspect but need not catch |
| D114 | **Make `runPython` stale-request cancellation the DEFAULT (SPEC RH-9, supersedes D113's opt-in).** The channel key now **defaults to the `.py` path** instead of being absent: a new call for a file aborts the prior in-flight call for that same file with no author effort, so an ordinary `runPython("./x.py", params)` slider loop cancels its own stale scrubs. `opts.key` regroups the channel; **`opts.key: null` opts out** (fully concurrent, D113's old default); `opts.signal` composes unchanged. A **superseded** call now **never settles** (instead of rejecting with `AbortError`); an abort from the caller's own `signal` still rejects with `AbortError`. Shipped templates that fire concurrent same-file calls were audited and given `opts.key: null` at the call sites that must all complete (writes, poll loops, detached-worker kickoffs). Local + hosted runtimes stay in lockstep; the sine example now demonstrates the zero-config default | Owner ask 2026-07-16 ("create new PRs for it being default"). The dominant `runPython` use is "recompute this view from current params", where the last value is the only one that matters — making that the default means every slider/scrub page gets smooth behaviour for free instead of each author remembering a `key`. The `.py` path is the natural channel: a page almost always calls one file per logical view. **Never-settle (reversing D113's "rejected never-resolve"):** with cancellation opt-in, only authors who keyed in saw the rejection and could filter `AbortError`; as a *default* it must be invisible to pages that already wrap `runPython` in `try/catch` (the canonical authoring pattern) — a rejected `AbortError` would flash a stale error through their own catch while the latest value is still computing. Never-settling makes the stale continuation simply stop; the "stranded async frame" worry from D113 is bounded (an unreferenced suspended awaiter + its pending promise form a GC-collectible cycle). The safety cost — auto-cancel would abort in-flight writes/polls — is paid once via the `opts.key: null` template audit, and is documented so page authors hitting the same pattern know the escape hatch. `opts.signal` keeps the standard `AbortError` reject because there the caller explicitly asked to abort and expects to observe it |

### Canvas layout viewer — the first conditional template (2026-07-13, SPEC §28)

| # | Decision | Choice | Rationale / rejected alternatives |
|---|---|---|---|
| D114 | `canvas.toml` gets a gated `canvas` mode offered only for genuine canvas files: the gate content-sniffs (basename `canvas.toml` + `type == "canvas"`), and `.toml` lists `canvas` first | Two coupled calls. (1) **Condition gate (CT-12):** `templates/canvas/condition.py` `method(target_path)` returns True only when the basename is literally `canvas.toml` (cheap pre-check, no I/O) AND a `tomllib` parse yields top-level `type == "canvas"` — with a 2 MB size guard before the open and a catch-all that fails closed to False (an ordinary `.toml` is not a `template_error`). The basename check runs first so a plain `.toml` never pays the open/parse cost, and under deferred CT-12 (post-merge semantics) the gate is not run at stat time at all: stat marks the entry `conditional` and `/api/fs/conditions` evaluates it in the background. (2) **Mode ordering:** `registry.json` binds `".toml": ["canvas", "code", "annotate"]` — `canvas` FIRST among the gated offers; the immediate default stays the first unconditional entry (`code`, PT-8), with `canvas` joining the switcher when its verdict allows and vanishing when it doesn't (plain/malformed/mis-named toml), exactly as before this template existed. The reader (`reader.py`) is a bare `main()` + `fused.udf(main)` shim (the las/usd/pdf house pattern), not a directly-decorated `main` | First consumer of the CT-12 conditional mechanism, so the gate's shape sets precedent. Content-sniff over extension-only because `.toml` is a shared config extension (pyproject, ruff, poetry, …) — a `.canvas.toml` compound key was rejected (Fused writes the file as literally `canvas.toml`, no compound extension exists to bind) and a bare `.toml → canvas` default was rejected (it would hijack every config file's preview). Basename-before-parse keeps the per-stat cost off the hot path for the common non-canvas `.toml`. Canvas-first ordering chosen over appending `canvas` last because when the file IS a canvas the layout viewer is the point of opening it; the gate guarantees this only bites real canvases, so `code`-first for everything else is preserved. Bare-main+shim over a directly-decorated `@fused.udf main`: a decorated `main`'s `__call__` sandboxes filesystem access (a canvas read fails), and the runner already invokes the registered udf via its raw `_fn`, so the shim runs identically under the engine while keeping `main` a plain callable for the built-in executor and tests — the same reason las/usd/pdf use it |
| D115 | Sidebar **Recents** — last 3 opened files, urls updating live (SPEC §29) | New shell state backend `shell/recents.py` + `~/.fused-render/recents.json` (`{collapsed, entries: [{url, openedAt}]}`): urls stored verbatim incl. query (D20 posture), newest first, deduped by target fs path, capped at 20 (buffer so 3 valid rows survive missing-file filtering); `GET /api/recents` hides entries whose file no longer exists WITHOUT deleting them; `POST /api/recents/open {url}` accepts only an existing file's `/view/` url (directories, `_`-sentinel routes, embed urls no-op with `recorded: false`); `PUT /api/recents/collapsed` persists the fold with the data (D44 posture). Recording rides the StatView seam: `useRecentsTracking` (lib/recents.ts) is mounted beside the session hooks — records once when the stat confirms a file, then re-records the current url on every param write (`fused:urlchange`, 500 ms debounce), so an entry always carries the file's LATEST params; the currently-open file stays listed. Sidebar: top 3 as basename rows (D22), click = query-preserving `navigateUrl`, arms nothing; heading toggles the fold, count pill as the collapsed signal (no chevron — D44 visual language); section hidden while empty. **Display order is stable slots, not raw MRU (RC-11, owner revision after testing):** the data stays strict MRU server-side, but the visible 3 use session-scoped slots — a displayed file keeps its row (re-open/param update changes it in place), only a not-displayed file entering at the top moves anything, a vanished file's slot fills from the bottom without reshuffling survivors, and boot seeds slots from MRU. Rows keyed by fs path (url mutates every param write — a url key would remount/flash the row); the store notifies only when the visible slice (slot order, slot urls, collapse) changed — url updates DO notify so hrefs stay fresh (RC-3), re-rendering in place with zero movement, and no-op refreshes render nothing | Owner decisions 2026-07-16: live url updates over open-time snapshots (a recent should reopen where you left the file — session restore's posture, surfaced cross-file); files only; missing files hidden silently, never pruned (a mount reconnect or undelete brings them back); the open file included. Server-side dedupe/cap/filter keeps the client dumb — the hook just POSTs the current url and refreshes. The StatView tracking seam was chosen over hooking router.navigate: it already owns the confirmed-file gate (isDir from stat) and the urlchange listener wiring, so live updates and file-vs-dir classification cost zero new plumbing; the server rejects non-file urls as the backstop |
| D116 | **Recent errors for a deployed page** — surface a deployed mount's captured runtime failures (traceback, output tails, params) inside fused-render, read through the fused CLI's `fused share errors`. New read-only endpoints `GET /api/deploy/errors?env&token[&limit&since&until&kind&entrypoint]` (newest-first summaries) and `GET /api/deploy/error?env&token&err_id` (one full record); a shared `DeploymentErrors` component renders an expandable list, mounted below the current-deployment card in the Deploy modal (auto-loads for the one deployed page) and lazily under a per-row "Recent errors" toggle on the account Deployments list (no CLI call per row until opened) | `deploy.py` shells `fused share errors …` through the same `_run_share` seam as `share list`/`create` (D112 — subprocess, never the fused Python API; fused-render mints no URLs and reads no records itself). Deployed pages answer a failing request with an opaque `500 {"error","id"}` (the fused repo's `spec/serve/error-reporting.md`); the id is the join key, and only the mount's owner — through their management credentials / the CLI — can read the record back, so the debugging loop that exists locally (the runtime overlay) finally closes for a hosted page. Read-only, so no `X-Fused` guard (matches `/status`, `/shares`); filters pass through verbatim (the CLI parses `2h`/`3d` sugar). A CLI predating `share errors` fails with click's "No such command", translated to an upgrade hint. Lazy per-row mount keeps listing a busy env from fanning out one subprocess per deployed mount. This is the fused-render consumer of the `share errors` API added in fusedio/fused#337, served by the managed Fused service | Owner ask 2026-07-17 ("prospectively implement, for a deployed file, seeing the most recent errors, using the fused API just implemented"). Prospective: the pinned fused wheel predates `share errors`, so the verb lands with a wheel bump — the endpoints already degrade to the upgrade hint until then. Two surfaces because "a deployed file" maps to the per-page Deploy modal (the direct answer) while the account Deployments list already enumerates every mount, so the same component serves both for near-zero extra cost |

### DMG size optimizations (2026-07-17)

| # | Decision | Choice | Rationale / rejected alternatives |
|---|---|---|---|
| D116 | **Bundle prune pass in build_dmg.sh (step 4a)** — after py2app, before the smoke tests and codesign: delete package `tests`/`test` directories under Resources/lib, all `__pycache__` trees, pip/setuptools/wheel/pkg_resources/PyObjCTest, and the copied Python.framework's developer-only corners (stdlib `test`, idlelib, ensurepip, lib2to3, tkinter, include/Headers/share). Plus a `FUSED_RENDER_SKIP_CODESIGN` escape hatch that skips the whole signing step for size-measurement/dev builds (default behavior unchanged) | py2app's `packages` option whole-copies every forced package (a deliberate D33 choice — the runtime-import blindness documented in setup_py2app.py), which drags ~100 MB of package test suites and ~160 MB of stale `__pycache__` into the bundle. None of it is reachable: nothing in fused_render or any supported user path imports from a `tests` package, the GUI is rumps/pyobjc (tkinter unused), and the bundle has no pip by design (SPEC §19 DP-3). Prune placed BEFORE the existing smoke tests so they validate the pruned bundle, and before codesign so the seal stays valid. Rejected: py2app `excludes` (doesn't reach into whole-copied packages) and zip-freezing (breaks the D33 executor model) |
| D117 | **DMG compression UDZO → ULFO (LZFSE)** | One-line dmgbuild settings change | LZFSE compresses this bundle notably tighter than zlib and decompresses faster on mount. ULFO needs macOS 10.11+ to open — well under the app's LSMinimumSystemVersion 11.0, so no compatibility cost. ULMO (lzma) rejected: marginally smaller but much slower to build and mount |
| D118 | **Strip debug + local symbols (`strip -S -x`) from all bundled .so/.dylib** in the 4a prune pass, before codesign | `find`-driven sweep, per-file `\|\| true` so a stray non-Mach-O file can't fail the build; smoke tests (duckdb via _child.py, fused CLI verbs) run after and gate regressions | Wheels ship unstripped native libs (pxr's libusd_ms.dylib alone: 67 MB, ~96k symbols). Debug/local symbols are dead weight in a distributed app — global symbols kept, so dlopen and inter-lib linking are untouched. Must precede signing: stripping rewrites the file and would break an existing signature |
| D120 | **Declarative bundle manifest (embedded `<script type="application/fused-bundle">`) with `include` globs + computed-`rawUrl` runtime resolution (SPEC EX-7, EX-4a)** | A page declares its own bundle set in-repo via one embedded `<script type="application/fused-bundle">` JSON block; `export.plan_export` extracts it, expands `include` globs (`data/*.json`, `tiles/**/*.png`) against the page dir through the existing safety gauntlet (`_reject_unsafe_rel`/`_within_page_dir`), and folds them **beneath** the caller's EX-6 `include`. The block is **stripped before the `fused.*` dependency scan** (so a JSON value like `"fused.writeFile( decoy"` can't false-positive) and is **unversioned + forward-lenient** (the `type` attr is the discriminator; unknown keys ignored so future directives are additive). Zero-match glob → warning; missing literal → error. Two supporting changes: (1) `_LITERAL_CALL` gains a `(?=\s*[,)])` lookahead so `fused.rawUrl("data/" + name)` is counted as a *computed* path, not mis-collected as a bogus literal `data/` asset (a latent scanner bug); (2) hosted runtime `assetKeyFor` (fused repo `fused_render_runtime.js`) resolves a computed path against the bundled key set (exact `CFG.assets[path]` fast path, else `normalizeAssetKey` → membership in `Object.values(CFG.assets)`), so `_asset` serves it by key. Local `fused.rawUrl`/`readFile` now resolve page-relative for real: the runtime sends the page's own path as `base` to `/api/fs/raw`, which joins a relative `path` against `dirname(base)` (mirrors `/api/run`'s `html` resolve) — so one `fused.rawUrl("data/" + name)` works local AND hosted (RH-1). | Owner ask 2026-07-17: kill the two ugliest export artifacts — a hand-maintained `RAW_URLS` literal table and a fake `_bundle*()` function whose only job was to be seen by the literal scan — with a reviewable, reproducible, in-repo declaration; no fake code. **Embedded `<script>` over a sidecar `fused.bundle.json`** (owner call): fused-render pages are single self-contained HTML files, so the manifest should travel inside the file; the exporter already reads the page HTML, and a single well-delimited block parsed by `json.loads` is more robust than the call-site regex. **No `version` field, forward-lenient** (owner call): the block is additive as the design evolves — an older exporter must tolerate a newer page. **`include` only, `exclude` warned-not-applied** (from the leak analysis): an `include`-only block leaks nothing a hosted page doesn't already expose (the served asset map enumerates every file the globs resolve to), but `exclude` would publish the *withheld* file names in the public page source — so exclusion stays on the EX-6 `/api/export`/Deploy-modal path, kept on the deployment record (D78), off the artifact. **Scanner lookahead** fixes a real misclassification (`rawUrl("p"+x)` previously tried to bundle a file literally named `p`), correct independent of the manifest. **`base` server-side resolution** over client-side page-dir derivation: reuses the exact contract runPython already uses (`html`), avoids duplicating the mode-specific page-path logic in the runtime, and adds no new filesystem exposure (a relative path resolves *more* narrowly than the prior cwd-relative). Verified end-to-end: buildings_to_hexagons exports all 13 `data/*.json` via `["data/*.json"]` with `loadData` collapsed to one `fused.rawUrl("data/" + name)`; the hosted runtime (run under node with a simulated seed) resolves that computed path to the right `_asset?name=` URL. |
| D119 | **usd-core is fetched on demand, not bundled** — removed from the `[bundled]` extra (and from reader.py's PEP 723 header); `convert_worker.py:_ensure_pxr` downloads the pinned usd-core wheel from PyPI on the first USD-file conversion, unpacks it (a .whl is a zip — no pip, the packaged app ships none by design, SPEC §19 DP-3) into `~/.fused-render/usd-site/<version>-<cpXY>/` and prepends it to sys.path | Same large-binary-on-first-use posture as pandoc/typst (docs/install_worker.py). Progress rides the worker's existing progress.json (stage `install-usd`), so the template's convert poll loop shows the download with ZERO page changes — no new reader action, no new HTML. Wheel selected from the PyPI JSON API by pinned version + `cp{maj}{min}` + platform (macosx universal2 / manylinux x86_64 / win_amd64); a failed install surfaces as the mesh layer's best-effort `meshError` | Investigation finding: the packaged app NEVER actually contained pxr — it isn't in setup_py2app's `packages` and modulegraph can't trace the worker's runtime import, so packaged USD mesh extraction was silently broken (`meshError: No module named 'pxr'`). This change therefore FIXES packaged .usd/.usdz mesh preview (verified end-to-end against the built DMG's own python: cold install → mesh.glb, warm re-run) and drops 224 MB from pip-installed `[bundled]` dev environments; DMG size is unchanged. Subtlety: the site dir goes onto sys.path before it exists, and importlib caches the "nothing there" finder — `importlib.invalidate_caches()` after the unpack is load-bearing. Tradeoff accepted: the FIRST preview of a .usd/.usdz file needs network (one ~25 MB wheel download); splat-only NuRec captures and .ply/.obj/.stl/.glb paths never need pxr at all. A dedicated install-worker + reader action (the typst pattern verbatim) was rejected as strictly more moving parts for the same UX — the convert worker already owns a polled progress file |
| D120 | **Hosted bundles mirror the page folder; imported modules auto-bundle** (SPEC EX-1/EX-2/EX-7) | Two coordinated changes so a deployed page's Python "just works" with the same relative layout it authored in. (A) The hosting layer (`fused` repo `build_html_artifact`) keys every bundled file by its **real page-relative path** instead of relocating assets under an `assets/` prefix — at serve time files land under the project root, which is already the runtime's cwd **and** `sys.path[0]`, so a bare `open("data.csv")` and `import helpers` resolve with no rewriting (`openfused.asset_path` is no longer required — and, since it anchors under `<root>/assets/`, no longer applies to fused-render pages). (B) `export.py` now discovers first-party modules a bundled entrypoint imports (static `ast` scan, transitive) and ships them as a new manifest **`resources`** category — bundled so `import` resolves, but NOT on the `_asset` allow-list, so a module's source is never web-served (a page fetches assets, never resources). | Local/hosted parity: fused-render already runs a page's code from its real folder (PT-3), so the hosted path should too — the previous flatten-to-`code/` + relocate-to-`assets/` scheme broke `open()` (needed `asset_path`) and `import` (module not bundled at all). Root-keying is the minimal fix because the Lambda handler already roots cwd + `sys.path[0]` at the extraction dir. Resources kept separate from assets (rather than shipped as web-served assets) so auto-discovery can't silently expose helper source over HTTP. A single-segment key colliding with a handler-internal root file (`fused.py`, `_params.json`, `_runner.py`, …) is rejected at build time — the one real cost of a flat root vs. the old `assets/` sandbox. | Only absolute imports resolving to a `<name>.py` beside the page are bundled; stdlib/third-party and subpackages are left alone, and a relative `from . import x` is skipped (a hosted entrypoint runs flattened, no package context) — documented as a known limit. Fuller fidelity (running each entrypoint as its real file via `runpy` so `__file__`/relative-package imports also work) was deferred as Layer C — it needs the `@fused.udf`/`result=` dispatch reworked and adds risk for a rarer case. Cross-repo: manifest `resources[]` threaded through `load_html_bundle`/`build_html_artifact`, the Fused-backend inline upload (`cli._read_html_bundle_source`), specs (`fused` `spec/serve/fused-render.md`, this repo's `docs/EXPORT.md`). |

### Nested bookmark folders (2026-07-20)

| # | Decision | Choice | Rationale / rejected alternatives |
|---|---|---|---|
| D121 | **Nested bookmark folders to arbitrary depth (revises D44)** | `BookmarkFolder.children` becomes `BookmarkItem[]` — folders nest recursively, unlimited depth. Frontend (`lib/bookmarks.ts`): recursive `findById`/`removeById`/`containerOf`/`isDescendant` helpers replace the repeated "top level, else one level of children" scans; `allBookmarks()` flattens all depths (armed-bookmark lookup keeps working); `prune()` cascades bottom-up so an emptied nested folder can empty its parent; `deleteFolder`/`toggleFolder`/rename/icon/url mutators find nested folders. `moveItem` drops the folders-top-level-only guard; new **cycle guard**: a folder never moves into itself or any of its own descendants (`isDescendant`, checked before removal; a destination inside the moved subtree aborts with no write). Sidebar: recursive `renderItems` — nested `.folder-children` divs compound the existing rail/indent CSS per level (no CSS change); collapse hides the whole subtree; collapsed-folder active highlight recurses over all descendants. DnD: "into" zone now on any folder row at any depth for dragged bookmarks AND folders; drag-over a dragged folder's own descendants is a dead target; bookmark-onto-bookmark combine (`createFolderWith`) works at any depth — the new folder replaces the target in its own parent's children (owner revision after first hands-on test; initially shipped top-level-only for scope control); search recurses all depths (ancestor folder-name match pulls in its subtree); folder-as-tabs uses bookmark children only (nested folders skipped, not flattened). Server (`shell/bookmarks.py`): `_dedupe_names` collects bookmarks at every depth (name namespace stays global per D97, folder names untouched); `_strip_invalid_children` (the interim crash guard) replaced by recursive `_sanitize_tree` — keep an entry iff it's a dict with a string `url` or a `type:"folder"` with list `children`, same rule at top level, migrate-on-GET write-only-when-changed posture kept | Owner call 2026-07-20: a hand-edited `bookmarks.json` with a nested folder blanked the whole shell (every folder child rendered as a bookmark row; `bookmarkSaveTarget` threw on the missing `.url`). D44 chose one level because nested drop-target semantics looked ~2.5× the cost for a rarely-needed depth — but the schema was already recursive by design, and the depth is now actually wanted. Rejected keeping the strip-on-GET sanitizer as the fix (treats wanted data as corruption); rejected a fixed depth cap (the data model doesn't need one, and the compounding indent rail keeps deep trees legible enough — revisit if real trees get absurd); combine into nested folders was initially rejected to contain drop-zone complexity (the exact cost D44 flagged) but reinstated the same day after the owner's first hands-on test — creating a subfolder by dropping bookmark-on-bookmark inside a folder is the natural gesture, and generalizing `createFolderWith` to "replace target in its own parent array" cost little. No migration needed — old one-level files are already valid nested trees |

### Tile-daemon CORS + access token (2026-07-20)

| # | Decision | Choice | Rationale / rejected alternatives |
|---|---|---|---|
| D122 | **Open CORS (`Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *`) on the standalone tile daemons, gated by a per-daemon access token — distinct posture from D36's main-server guard** | The four persistent localhost tile daemons — `geotiff/tile_server.py`, `netcdf/grid_tile_server.py`, `map/vector_tile_server.py`, `zarr_aoi/tile_server.py` — each bind their own random port on `127.0.0.1` (so `runPython`'s ~700ms per-call subprocess cost doesn't cap MapLibre pan/zoom responsiveness) and answer every endpoint with `Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *`. Endpoints are read-only GETs (`/meta`, `/tile/{z}/{x}/{y}.png` or `.mvt`, `/hist`, `/value`/`/probe`, `/stats`; `map/`'s `/open` is the one POST, an async warm-up); templates run in the shell's iframe (a different port = different origin), so their `<img>`/`fetch`/MapLibre requests need the response readable cross-origin, which is why the D36 no-CORS trick can't be reused here. **Access control is a per-daemon token, not CORS.** Each daemon mints `secrets.token_urlsafe(32)` at startup, writes it into its state file (`~/.cache/fused-render-*/daemon.json`) next to the port, and returns it from `main(action="ensure")` alongside the port. Every endpoint except `/ping` (a version-only liveness probe) requires `?t=<token>`; a missing/wrong token is a 403 before any dispatch. The templates thread the token into every request: the three sci-viz templates append it in their one `dURL()` chokepoint, `pyramid/` appends it to its `/ltile` URL, and `map/`'s `map_render.py` folds it into the `tile_url`/`status_url`/`meta_url` query strings it hands the client. Token must ride the query string (not a header) because `<img>`/MapLibre tile sources can't set custom headers. `/quit` is token-gated too (the respawn path in `main()` passes the stale daemon's own token); `/ping` stays open so liveness needs no secret. | Contrast with D36 (main `server.py`): D36 withholds CORS headers to make a custom-header preflight fail, blocking blind cross-origin POSTs — that works only because the main server's same-origin shell JS never needs to read a foreign response. The tile daemons have the opposite requirement (their only client *is* a cross-port iframe reading tile bytes), so open CORS is deliberate, not an oversight. But `127.0.0.1` binding is **not** an access boundary against the real threat here — a malicious page in the *same browser* can fetch `http://127.0.0.1:<port>/…` cross-origin, and open CORS lets it read the reply; loopback only stops a different *machine*. Originally (first cut of this decision) the only obstacle was the random ephemeral port — obscurity, not access control. The token closes that: a foreign page can't produce a valid request even if it guesses the port, so the read-only tile/metadata bytes stop being cross-origin-readable. The token lives in the state file, so it's only as private as the local filesystem — consistent with D3 (local code execution is already out of scope; this guards the *browser* boundary, which the port-only scheme left open). Rejected a header-based token (breaks `<img>`/MapLibre tile loads); rejected reflecting the main origin instead of `*` (needs the daemon to know the main port, and the token already gates access so CORS breadth no longer matters). |

### Bundled learn content as a read-only archive mount (2026-07-21)

| # | Decision | Choice | Rationale / rejected alternatives |
|---|---|---|---|
| D123 | **The repo's `learn/` content ships inside the app as `Contents/Resources/learn.zip` and appears at startup as a builtin read-only mount** | `build_dmg.sh` step 4e (after rclone bundling 4d, before signing — Resources content must exist before the bundle seal) zips `learn/` fresh every run (`zip -qr -X`, `.DS_Store` excluded) into `Contents/Resources/learn.zip` and smoke-tests it with the just-bundled rclone (`lsf ":archive:<zip>"`) — the exact binary shipped must read the exact zip shipped, so an rclone bump that drops the archive backend fails the BUILD. At startup, `run_automount` first calls `ensure_learn_mount()` (before the list snapshot and its empty early-return, so a fresh install with zero user mounts still gets it): under `_store_lock` it upserts a mount record `{name: "learn", remote: ":archive:<bundle>/learn.zip", read_only: true, read_only_user: true, builtin: "learn"}` — rclone v1.74's archive backend (verified nfsmount + read-only on macOS) mounts the zip like any remote. `read_only` is pinned user-level (`read_only_user`) so attach-time detection never reconsiders it and all three read-only layers engage via the existing plumbing (VFS ReadOnly, serve read_only, kernel rdonly). The `builtin: "learn"` marker keeps the record distinct from a user mount that happens to be named "learn" (that mount is warned about and never clobbered); the remote is refreshed each startup so a moved/upgraded bundle never leaves a stale path; the record is removed when the zip is absent (dev checkouts — `FUSED_RENDER_LEARN_ZIP` env override is the dev path). `mount_view` exposes `builtin` so the UI can later hide delete. `ensure_learn_mount` never raises — a storage failure must not break automount | Copying `learn/` loose into Resources was rejected: no mount semantics — the point is to exercise and present the bundled content through the same mounts surface as remote data. A persistent unmarked mount record was rejected: the bundle path goes stale after every upgrade/relocation, and a user deleting the record would be permanent, the wrong semantics for default content (remove-when-absent + refresh-remote + builtin marker give upgrade-proof, default-content behavior instead) |

### HTML files presented as launchable apps (2026-07-21)

| # | Decision | Choice | Rationale / rejected alternatives |
|---|---|---|---|
| D124 | **Every `.html`/`.htm` file row is styled as a launchable app: app-tile icon, accent-leaning tint, "App" chip, "Open App" verb** | `FileIcons.tsx`: the html glyph changes from a globe to a rounded app tile with a play triangle (same house SVG style); new exported `isAppEntry(name, isDir)` (true for `.html`/`.htm` files, driven by the same `EXT_VARIANT` map) is the single shared definition. `shell.css`: `.file-icon--html` tint moves from `#cd9d75` to `#c9d95e` — html is the **only** file category allowed to lean toward the accent yellow (`--accent #E5FF44`); the "never the accent yellow" icon-tint rule is amended with this one carve-out. New `.app-chip`: a small (10px/14px) muted-accent bordered pill rendered after the filename on html rows in both the main listing and search results; sized well under the 16px icon height so row height never changes (no-jumpy-lists rule). `Listing.tsx`: the row context menu's first entry reads "Open App" for html rows, "Open" otherwise — labels only, behavior/routing untouched. No manifest, no entrypoint marker, no per-folder configuration. | Owner goal 2026-07-21: educate brand-new users that html files are apps you launch, not documents you view. Session constraints — folders move between users (kills explorer-side metadata) and multiple apps can live in one dir (kills an `index.html` convention as sole mechanism) — reframed the problem: rather than distinguishing *the* entrypoint from other html, treat **every** html file as launchable and make it look that way. Strongest education-per-complexity: zero authoring concepts (no manifest/marker/pin to learn), the visual + verb carry the whole lesson. Rejected for now: sidecar/in-file entrypoint manifests (only needed if helper-html clutter appears; if so, invert — mark *helpers* for demotion, since apps are the common case); heuristic content sniffing (false positives, surprising UI); folder-as-app-bundle (changes the explorer's identity, decide separately). Accepted blur: an html *document* (exported report, saved page) is styled as an app — harmless, it opens and shows content. Variant key stays `"html"` internally (rename to `"app"` = CSS + comment churn, zero user-visible gain). `Preview.tsx` untouched — its menu has no "Open" action (the file is already open). |

### Fused account folded into Preferences as a tab (2026-07-21)

| # | Decision | Choice | Rationale / rejected alternatives |
|---|---|---|---|
| D125 | **The sidebar footer drops its dedicated "Fused account" tile; the account surface becomes a "Fused account" tab on the Preferences page, offered only when Deploy is enabled; the signed-in dot moves onto the Preferences tile itself** | `Sidebar.tsx`: the footer's four-button 2×2 grid (Templates/Mounts/Fused account/Preferences, D111-era addition) goes back to a single row of three (`.sidebar-footer` `display: flex` again, not `grid`) — Templates, Mounts, Preferences. The green signed-in dot (`account-signedin-dot`, same affordance as the deploy dot) rides the Preferences icon instead, gated on **both** `useDeployEnabled()` (new shared hook, `lib/prefs.ts`, factored out of `Preview.tsx`'s private one) and `useAccountLoggedIn()` — a dot for a feature the user hasn't opted into (Deploy) would be a mystery indicator, and clicking the dot does nothing special: it's not its own click target (far too small to hit reliably), it rides the Preferences button's existing one. `views/Account.tsx`'s default-exported page component becomes a named `AccountPanel()` with no page wrapper (the outer `.prefs-page` div moves up to `Preferences.tsx`, which now owns it). `views/Preferences.tsx` gains a two-tab layout (**Render preferences** / **Fused account**, `?tab=account` in the URL — same bookmarkable-tab pattern as Templates' bindings/library split); the account tab button only renders while `prefs.deploy.enabled` is true, and requesting it via URL while disabled silently falls back to Render preferences. The old `/view/_account` sentinel isn't deleted — `App.tsx` redirects it (render-time `history.replaceState`, the same technique the `/` → start-dir redirect already uses) to `/view/_prefs?tab=account`, so existing bookmarks and the Deploy modal's "Set up hosted environment" button (updated to link straight to the new URL) keep working. Preferences' own Deploy-toggle section (renamed from "Deployments" to **"Deploy to Fused account"** — distinct now from the account tab's own "Deployments" share list) drops its `navigateUrl("/view/_account")` link in favor of an in-page `setTab("account")` call, and only shows that link once Deploy is enabled (matching the tab's own gating). | Owner ask 2026-07-21: the account tile read as a second, separate "account" surface competing with Templates/Mounts/Preferences, when in fact fused-render has no account concept of its own — the only reason the corner needs an account affordance at all is Deploy, which is itself opt-in. Folding the dot into Preferences and the panel into a conditional tab makes the sidebar footer reflect that dependency instead of presenting Account as a peer feature. A popover/dropdown on the Preferences tile was considered and rejected: every other sentinel route (`_prefs`, `_templates`, `_mounts`, the old `_account`) is a bookmarkable navigation, not a modal (D80's rationale for Preferences applies here too), so a tab keeps the same pattern rather than introducing a new one. Deleting `/view/_account` outright was rejected: it's linked from the Deploy modal and may be bookmarked, and a redirect costs nothing. |

### Persistent server-down banner (2026-07-22)

| # | Decision | Choice | Rationale / rejected alternatives |
|---|---|---|---|
| D126 | **A persistent bottom-right banner health-checks the backend and, when it's gone, tells the user to reopen the fused-render app** | New self-contained `components/ServerStatusBanner.tsx`, mounted once in `App.tsx`'s `#app` root (sibling to Sidebar/`#main`) so it survives the epoch-keyed view remounts. It polls `GET /api/config` (already the cheapest no-param endpoint — no new backend surface) every 5s with a plain `fetch(..., { cache: "no-store" })` and a 4s `AbortController` timeout so a hung request counts as a failure and probes never pile up (an in-flight guard also prevents overlap). The banner appears only after **2 consecutive failures** (one blip ≠ server gone); any success resets the counter and, if the banner was up, flips it to a green "Reconnected — fused-render is back." state that auto-dismisses after 5s. Polling pauses while `document.visibilityState === "hidden"`; `visibilitychange`→visible, window `online`, and window `focus` each trigger an immediate probe, and the down state offers a "Check again" button doing the same. Styling: `.server-status*` in `shell.css` next to `.listing-toast*` — `position: fixed; bottom/right: 16px; z-index: 1100` (above the toast's 1000), `--error`-tinted border/title for down, existing `--success` for reconnected, `--bg-alt`/`--border`/`--fg`/`--fg-muted` otherwise, max-width 360px, `role="status"`/`aria-live="polite"`. Copy (owner-approved, exact): title "fused-render isn't running", body "The app that powers this page has stopped or was closed. Reopen the fused-render app, and this page will reconnect on its own." | The backend is a native app the user launches, not a CLI service — when it dies, the only remedy is reopening the app, so the copy says exactly that instead of suggesting commands. Bottom-right (owner-chosen; initially bottom-left) keeps it clear of the listing toast (bottom-center) and content. Polling continues at the same 5s cadence while down so recovery is noticed promptly and the page "reconnects on its own" as the copy promises. Piggybacking on existing fetches (show banner when any API call fails) was rejected: an idle page makes no calls, which is precisely when the user needs to be told the server died. Reusing `Toast.tsx`/`ErrorBanner.tsx` rejected: both are view-owned/transient; this is app-shell-owned and persistent. |

### Bookmarks flag missing-file targets; Recents stays hidden-when-missing (2026-07-22)

| # | Decision | Choice | Rationale / rejected alternatives |
|---|---|---|---|
| D127 | **`GET /api/bookmarks` gains a `missing` id side-channel (target confirmed gone from disk); the sidebar flags those rows instead of silently rendering them as valid. Recents (D115) is explicitly left unchanged** | Bookmarks previously had NO existence check at all — a stale bookmark rendered identically to a live one. `fused_render/shell/pathops.py` gains a generic (file-OR-directory) mount-safe existence trio — `mount_exists`/`local_exists`/`exists` — mirroring the existing files-only `is_file` trio (`mount_exists` uses `mounts.rc_stat_for`, fails open on "indeterminate"; unlike Recents' files-only D22 contract, a bookmark may target a directory listing). `bookmarks.py` splits the existing `_fs_path_from_url` into a decode-only `_decode_fs_path` (shared) and the exists-gated wrapper (unchanged behavior, still only used by the history sidecar), flattens the tree to its bookmark leaves (`_flatten_bookmarks`, arbitrary folder depth per D121), and fans the existence checks out concurrently on a dedicated `ThreadPoolExecutor` under one 1.5s wall-clock budget (`_MISSING_CHECK_BUDGET_S`/`_MISSING_CHECK_POOL`) — mirroring `recents.py`'s `CHECK_BUDGET_S`/`_CHECK_POOL`/`_keep_entry` shape exactly, including walking `candidates`/`tasks` by index rather than iterating the `done` set (a `set` of `Task`s has no defined order, which would make the returned id list nondeterministic). `GET /api/bookmarks` becomes `async def` and returns `{exists, bookmarks, missing}` — `missing` is computed fresh every GET and NEVER written into `bookmarks.json` or merged into a bookmark's own fields, so it can never round-trip through the whole-tree `PUT` (D75's contract is untouched). Frontend `bookmarks.ts` tracks the ids in a separate `missingIds` set (not part of `cache`/`BookmarkItem`), exposed via `isBookmarkMissing(id)`; both `hydrateBookmarks` and the existing 30s `refreshBookmarks` poll (D77) refresh it, and the poll's change-detection now also fires on a missing-set change alone (a target can vanish/reappear with the tree itself untouched). `main.tsx` additionally refreshes on window `focus` (immediate, not waiting out the rest of the 30s tick — the common "fixed the file, switched back" case), in-flight guarded the same way `ServerStatusBanner`'s health probe is (D126) so a burst of focus events can't stack redundant GETs on the shared bookmark mutation queue and delay real edits behind them. `Sidebar.tsx`: a flagged `BookmarkRow` gets a `.missing` class (a styling hook only — the name keeps its normal `--fg` color, unchanged from a live bookmark, on owner request) plus a small always-visible `⚠` badge (`--error`-colored, own `position: relative; z-index: 1` stacking context so the row's stretched-link overlay doesn't swallow its hover/title) between the name and the hover-only action buttons; the hover card (`TooltipContent`) adds a "File not found" line. Nothing is auto-removed — same posture as Recents' own missing handling, just visible instead of hidden. **Recents is deliberately left as-is**: D115 already made an explicit owner call to hide (not flag) a missing recent, and that call stands — this feature is Bookmarks-only. | User ask: flag bookmarks/recents whose target no longer exists. Investigation showed Recents already solves this (by hiding, D115) while Bookmarks had zero handling — confirmed with the user to leave Recents' existing behavior untouched and scope this to Bookmarks. Flag-not-hide (vs. mirroring Recents' hide) was the deliberate choice for Bookmarks: unlike Recents (an MRU jump list where a stale entry is disposable), a bookmark is a named, user-curated, permanent entry — silently hiding it would look like data loss, and Templates' existing `exists:false`/broken-ref pattern (D95, `RowEditorModal`'s "surface, don't auto-remove" convention) was the established precedent to follow instead. A per-bookmark persisted `exists` field (baked into the tree) was rejected: it would round-trip through the whole-tree `PUT` and either go stale between polls or require every mutation path to recompute it — a side-channel keeps the persisted format and the D75 whole-tree-PUT contract completely untouched. Reusing `_fs_path_from_url`'s raw `os.path.exists` as-is (instead of the new mount-safe `pathops.exists`) was rejected for the new bulk GET-time check: that call already predates this change and is only exercised on rare, user-initiated writes, but a check running on every 30s poll must not risk the documented NFS-mount GETATTR wedge (`pathops.py`'s load-bearing invariant) — hence the new `pathops.exists`/`mount_exists`/`local_exists` trio rather than calling the existing helper directly. |

### Server-down banner gains a "Start fused-render" launch deep link (2026-07-23)

| # | Decision | Choice | Rationale / rejected alternatives |
|---|---|---|---|
| D128 | **New action-only deep link `fused-render://launch`: both native handlers make sure the app/server is running and open NO browser tab; the D126 down-banner links to it as "Start fused-render"** | Recognition lives in `deeplink.py` as `is_launch_url(src)`, shared by both platforms: accepted forms are `fused-render://launch`, a trailing-slash variant, and the slashless `fused-render:launch` (some carriers strip the empty authority), all case-insensitive. The action carries **no payload by definition** — any query string or extra path makes the link NOT a launch link (same D110 grammar: action in host position, payloads as query params; a future launch payload would be a new query param, and silently ignoring one today would freeze "ignored" into the contract). `github_url_from`'s unsupported-link error now names both actions. macOS (`app.py` `application_openURLs_`): a launch URL sets `state["docs"]` like any deep link (suppressing the bootstrap's auto-opened home tab) and is then dropped — never turned into a target, never queued on `state["pending"]`, no tab in either the ready or still-booting state; the OS having launched/foregrounded the app IS the action, the server boot is already in flight. Windows (`winopen.py` `_open()`): `is_launch_url` → `_ensure_server(requested_port)` (which already reuses a live server or spawns one detached) then return without `webbrowser.open` — the import of `deeplink` stays lazy inside the existing `fused-render:` branch so the common file double-click path never pays for fastapi. Banner (`ServerStatusBanner.tsx` down state): a plain `<a className="server-status-launch" href="fused-render://launch">Start fused-render</a>` between the body and the Check-again button; `.server-status-launch` shares the `.server-status-retry` rule block (same padding/border/radius/hover) plus `display: inline-block; color: inherit; text-decoration: none; margin-right: 8px`. Owner-approved D126 copy is untouched. | The D126 banner tells the user to "reopen the fused-render app" but makes them go find it — a click target that does the reopening removes that scavenger hunt. No tab on purpose: the banner page already polls `/api/config` every 5s and flips to "Reconnected" by itself (D126), so a tab opened by the handler would always be a duplicate of the page the user is already looking at. Opening the home tab "just in case" was rejected for exactly that reason; so was making `launch` accept-and-ignore payloads (grammar strictness is cheap now, impossible to add back later). A `navigator.registerProtocolHandler`/JS-side launch was never on the table — only the OS protocol registration can start a dead native app. |

### Linux open-with self-integration, standard-MIME tiering, deep links (2026-07-22)

| # | Decision | Choice | Rationale / rejected alternatives |
|---|---|---|---|
| D129 | **Linux "Open with" uses standard freedesktop MIME types where one exists, custom glob types only for orphan extensions (macOS "Alternate rank" parity)** | The shared association table (`scripts/file_associations.json`, derived from `fused_render.winopen`) gains a nullable `standard_mime` per extension, populated from a curated `winopen._STANDARD_MIME_FOR_TOKEN` map (single source of truth, consumed by both desktop packagers AND the Linux runtime). An extension with a reliable shared-mime-info type (`.py`→`text/x-python`, `.png`→`image/png`, `.csv`→`text/csv`, `.pdf`, `.json`, office OOXML, `application/vnd.sqlite3`, `application/geo+json`, …) registers under that type; only orphans with no standard type (parquet, pmtiles, fgb, laz, gpkg, the 3D-model family, ambiguous `.ts`, …) get a custom `application/x-fused-render-<token>` glob type. The `.desktop` `MimeType=` list emits the effective type per extension (deduped) + `x-scheme-handler/fused-render`; the MIME-package XML defines glob types ONLY for orphans. No `xdg-mime default` is ever set for a file type. | Was: one custom glob type per extension for ALL extensions — which redefines the global type identity of files the shared database already names (a `.py` file becoming `application/x-fused-render-py` system-wide is a hijack), and, combined with an install-time default, would steal the user's chosen default app. The macOS build already solved this with LaunchServices "Alternate rank" (`setup_py2app.py`): custom UTI only for owned/orphan extensions, Alternate (never-steal) for the rest. Porting that posture to Linux means: reuse standard types, mint new identity only for orphans, and never claim a file-type default. Conservatism is deliberate — a WRONG standard type is worse than the custom fallback, because the DE resolves a file to whatever type its globs assign, so naming a type the DB won't assign drops the association entirely; hence uncertain types are left null. A test asserts the runtime-generated XML/MimeType (`fused_render._mime_package`) is byte-identical to the packaging-generated ones, so the two can't drift. |
| D130 | **The Linux AppImage self-integrates its `.desktop`/MIME/icon at first supervisor start; no third-party AppImage integrator required (revises gate (d))** | New `supervisor/_linux/integration.py`, kicked off on a daemon thread from `core.run` via an optional `_backend.integrate` hook (Linux-only; Windows registers via its installer, macOS via the bundle, so both omit it). From a real AppImage (`$APPIMAGE` points at an existing file) it writes, idempotently, `$XDG_DATA_HOME/applications/fused-render.desktop` (Exec=`<quoted $APPIMAGE> %u`, absolute icon path, generated `MimeType=`), `$XDG_DATA_HOME/mime/packages/fused-render.xml` (orphan glob types, generated at runtime from `fused_render._mime_package` — no `scripts/` import), and the icon under `hicolor/256x256`, then best-effort runs `update-mime-database`, `update-desktop-database`, and `xdg-mime default fused-render.desktop x-scheme-handler/fused-render`. A stamp (app version + AppImage path + content hash) under the state dir skips unchanged runs and re-integrates on move/upgrade. `%u` (not `%f`) is required so the DE also delivers `fused-render://` deep links to `Exec` (D110 on Linux). | Was: gate (d) "open-with works **after user-level AppImage integration**" — i.e. depended on the user having appimaged / Gear Lever / a manual desktop-file drop. An AppImage has no install step, so nothing registers it with the DE out of the box; leaning on a third-party integrator is not parity with the macOS/Windows builds, where associations work immediately post-install. Self-integration at first run closes that gap. Discipline mirrors `startup.py`: acts only when packaged (dev `python -m …` is a silent no-op), every step is log-and-continue (a missing `update-*` tool, an unwritable dir — none may stop the app starting), and it runs off the startup path so `update-*` shell-outs never delay the server/tray. Only the deep-link **scheme** gets an `xdg-mime default` (a scheme needs one to route at all); file types deliberately get none — the never-steal-defaults rule from D129. |

### Desktop state standardizes on the flat ~/.fused-render dotdir across Linux and Windows (2026-07-23)

| # | Decision | Choice | Rationale / rejected alternatives |
|---|---|---|---|
| D131 | **The desktop supervisor's durable state IS the flat `~/.fused-render` dotdir on Linux AND Windows — shared with the dev/CLI by design, no `desktop/` namespace anywhere (state or cache); cache stays OS-native, runtime stays on the tmpfs; no migration (pre-release)** | `supervisor/paths.py`: Linux `discover_linux()` and Windows `discover()` both set `root = Path.home()/".fused-render"` and **`state = root` itself**, with `logs/` and `temp/` as subdirs of it. The supervisor sets `FUSED_RENDER_HOME=paths.state` and `FUSED_RENDER_BRANCH=""`, so `shell/storage.home_dir()` resolves to `~/.fused-render` and mounts land at `~/.fused-render/mounts/<name>` — byte-for-byte what the released macOS app and the dev/CLI already do (macOS never sets `FUSED_RENDER_HOME`, so `home_dir()` falls through to the same dir). Cache stays OS-native and disposable, also without a desktop segment — Linux `$XDG_CACHE_HOME/fused-render` (fallback `~/.cache/fused-render`), Windows `%LOCALAPPDATA%\FusedRender\cache` (fallback `<root>/cache`; a missing `%LOCALAPPDATA%` only steers cache, never fatal). Linux runtime stays on `linux_runtime_dir()` (`$XDG_RUNTIME_DIR/fused-render`, tmpfs, `0700`; fallback `$XDG_CACHE_HOME/fused-render/runtime`). The Linux integration stamp (`_linux/integration.py`) is a single namespaced file (`desktop-integration.json`) under `paths.state`, so it coexists safely with CLI files; the `.desktop`/MIME/icon files it writes still go to `$XDG_DATA_HOME` (D130, unchanged). Nothing in the supervisor purges or wholesale-resets `paths.state` — a hard invariant now that state is the shared root. | One-known-place intent, taken all the way: the desktop app and the dev/CLI intentionally SHARE state — same `mounts.json`, prefs, templates — because "all user config in one known place" is the product, and macOS already ships exactly this. Supersedes the earlier D131 `desktop/`-subtree layout: the user rejected the namespace; the isolation it bought protected against dev-vs-desktop last-write-wins on shared files, but sharing is the intent, and the desktop supervisor only ever runs one server against the dir at a time. Cache is deliberately kept OS-native: it holds disposable GBs (uv/rclone/duckdb) that must stay out of backup scope. Runtime is deliberately kept on `$XDG_RUNTIME_DIR`: sockets/locks belong on the per-user tmpfs at `0700`. No migration because Linux/Windows are pre-release; macOS needs no change (already flat). Rejected: a full XDG layout (scatters state across three roots and buries the user-facing mounts under `~/.local/share`); the `desktop/` namespaced subtree (splits the user's config into two places and diverges from macOS). |
| D132 | **Windows remote mounts enabled through the existing rcd path; WinFsp is user-installed (rclone's own stance), not bundled — pending the licensing call (extends D102/D103)** *(2026-07-24: the not-bundled stance is superseded by D133 — the installer now bundles and chain-installs the WinFsp MSI; everything else here stands, including the runtime detector + friendly message as the declined-UAC fallback)* | The mount stack already routes everything through `rclone rcd`'s HTTP API and `mounts.py`'s `mountType` selection already sends non-darwin to rclone's default `mount` (which is WinFsp on Windows), so only two POSIX assumptions needed fixing in `shell/mounts.py`: (1) **the mountpoint leaf must NOT pre-exist** — WinFsp creates it and refuses to mount over an existing dir, so `attach_mount` skips `os.makedirs(leaf)` on win32, `os.rmdir`s a stale EMPTY leaf, and errors on a non-empty one (POSIX still pre-creates the leaf); (2) **`_force_unmount` never shells out to `umount`/`diskutil` on win32** — a WinFsp mount vanishes with its serving process, so force-unmount = poll `os.path.ismount` within the 15 s budget and report (it does NOT kill rcd: that would tear down every mount, and the shared `_kill_current_rcd` is POSIX-only — it escalates through `signal.SIGKILL`, absent on Windows). A `_winfsp_available()` detector (system DLL under `%ProgramFiles(x86)%\WinFsp\bin\winfsp-x64.dll`, `ctypes.util.find_library` fallback; vacuously True off-Windows) gates only the mount-CREATION path — checked AFTER `attach_mount`'s already-mounted adopt branch returns, so a live mount that survived a restart (re-attached by `run_automount`) is never rejected on a detector false-negative — and returns a friendly "install WinFsp from https://winfsp.dev/rel/" message when the driver is missing for a genuinely new mount. Production rcd teardown already works via the supervisor's Job Object (grandchild auto-enrollment). **Deferred:** bundling the WinFsp MSI in `installer.iss`, dev-mode rcd persistence on Windows, and Windows CI. **Unverified live** (needs a real Windows box + WinFsp kernel driver): the leaf-must-not-exist rule and `os.path.ismount` on WinFsp reparse points — both documented rclone behavior, tracked as the post-merge validation step. | WinFsp is GPLv3-with-exception against this repo's intentionally-unset license (`pyproject.toml`), so — mirroring rclone's own distribution stance — the user installs it; MSI chaining is revisited after the license call. Directory mounts (not drive letters) preserve the D2/D3 invariant that the app only ever sees local absolute paths under `mounts_dir()`. Force-unmount as process-teardown-only: no `umount` equivalent exists on Windows and the macOS escalation ladder is an NFS-flap workaround with no Windows analog. |
| D133 | **WinFsp is bundled in the Windows installer and auto-installed when missing (reverses D132's user-installs stance)** | `build_windows_installer.ps1` downloads the pinned WinFsp MSI (`v2.1` / `2.1.25156`) against its published SHA256 (same discipline as the rclone/zig pins) and stages it as `winfsp.msi` in the bundle root. `installer.iss` carries it as a `dontcopy` [Files] entry and, at `ssPostInstall` (after the payload swap), `InstallWinFsp()` skips entirely when the driver is already present (`{commonpf32}\WinFsp\bin\winfsp-{x64,a64}.dll` — mirroring `_winfsp_available()`), else `ExtractTemporaryFile`s it to `{tmp}` (auto-deleted, never persisted in the payload) and runs `msiexec /i … /qn /norestart` through `ShellExec('runas', …)` — the app itself stays `PrivilegesRequired=lowest`, so that verb raises the ONE UAC prompt of the whole setup. A declined prompt or a failing MSI is only `Log`ged, never a setup failure: mounts then degrade to `shell/mounts.py`'s runtime "install WinFsp" message (that detector + message stay in place as the fallback), so the install succeeds either way | Owner-directed 2026-07-24: end users shouldn't have to install WinFsp by hand from winfsp.dev for mounts to work — the licensing hesitation behind D132's deferral is resolved in favor of shipping it. Chained-MSI over a merged payload: WinFsp is a kernel driver needing an elevated per-machine MSI, which must stay separate from the per-user app install; `runas`+`/qn` over ShellExecuting the MSI with full UI keeps setup one-click (a single UAC consent, no second wizard) |

### Download page adds Windows/Linux; latest.json publish centralized into one job (2026-07-24)

| # | Decision | Choice | Rationale / rejected alternatives |
|---|---|---|---|
| D133 | **render.fused.io download page gains Windows/Linux sections; `latest.json` is now written once, after all three OS builds, merged against the live manifest instead of each build job racing to write it** | `scripts/download_page/index.html` gets a Windows section (`#windows-exe`) and a Linux section (`#linux-appimage`), same before/after-manifest pattern as the existing macOS section — falls back to the GitHub releases page, upgraded to the direct CDN link once `/latest.json` loads. `write_manifest.py` now takes only `VERSION` as required; `DMG_URL`/`DMG_SHA256`/`WHL_URL`/`WINDOWS_URL`/`LINUX_URL` are each optional and, when unset, fall back to whatever is already in the manifest at `EXISTING_MANIFEST_PATH` (if given). `.github/workflows/release.yml`'s three OS jobs (`build-sign-notarize-release`, `build-windows-release`, `build-linux-release`) now expose `version` + artifact-URL job outputs — written only right after that platform's own artifact upload succeeds, not after later unrelated steps (per-platform update manifest, CloudFront invalidation, `gh release upload`) whose failure shouldn't withhold an otherwise-good link — instead of publishing the root manifest themselves; a new `publish-download-page` job (`needs` all three, `if: '!cancelled() && ...'` — deliberately not `always()`, which would also fire on a manually cancelled run) downloads the live `latest.json` (a non-404 fetch failure hard-exits the step rather than silently treating it as empty and blanking every platform this run didn't touch), merges in whatever this run produced, and re-uploads `latest.json`/`index.html`/`og-image.png` + invalidates the CloudFront root paths — the three OS jobs keep invalidating only their own artifact prefixes. The CI role's IAM policy (`terraform/main/iam_github.tf` in the application repo) gains a scoped `s3:GetObject` on `arn:aws:s3:::fused-render/latest.json` (it previously had `PutObject` only), plus a `s3:ListBucket` on the bucket scoped via an `s3:prefix` condition to that same key — `GetObject` alone makes S3 answer a missing object with 403 rather than 404, which would make the first-publish-is-safe check above refuse to ever create the object. | The page's `<meta name="description">` already promised "macOS, Windows, and Linux" (both native installers have existed since D130-D132) but the body only ever had a macOS section — this closes that gap. A single writer for the root manifest was chosen over each OS job doing its own read-modify-write: those three jobs run in parallel on a normal tag push, and a race between three writers each reading a stale copy would silently drop whichever platform lost the race — worse than the small added latency of waiting for all three to finish (the page is already explicitly cosmetic/best-effort, `continue-on-error` throughout). Merging against the live manifest (rather than assuming every run has every platform's facts) specifically protects a `workflow_dispatch` scoped to one platform: rebuilding only Windows must not blank out the still-good macOS/Linux links. |


### Appearance follows the OS by default, with a Light/Dark pin; Linux tray icon follows the desktop panel (2026-07-27)

| # | Decision | Choice | Rationale / rejected alternatives |
|---|---|---|---|
| D134 | **fused-render resolves its appearance from the OS light/dark preference instead of being dark by constant; a shell menu offers System (default) / Light / Dark, persisted per browser profile. Shell chrome and twelve tier-1 view templates gain real light palettes; every other template is explicitly classified and left alone** | Full contract in SPEC §30 (AP-1..AP-15). Shape: `frontend/src/lib/theme.ts` owns the single `localStorage` key `fused-render:theme` (best-effort read/write, the `viewstate.ts`/`sidebarstate.ts` posture), resolves it against `prefers-color-scheme`, and writes the resolved value to `data-theme` on `<html>` — an attribute write, never a re-render, which is what keeps it clear of the standing "a React re-render must never touch a live iframe" rule (D45). The FIRST application is an **inline `<head>` script in `frontend/index.html`**, ahead of every stylesheet, duplicating the key + resolution rule (nothing importable exists that early; a test pins the spellings). `App` mounts `useThemeSync()` for later changes: another window's override via the `storage` event, and — while the setting is System — the OS flipping mid-session, including macOS's automatic sunset switch. `shell.css` is fully tokenized: all 61 hex + 58 `rgba()` literals move into `:root` **unchanged** (verified by expanding both palettes back out and diffing — dark is value-identical), and `:root[data-theme="light"]` redefines the whole set; translucent washes become `rgba(var(--tint), a)` with `--tint` flipping white→black, and light mode maps `--accent` from the brand lime to a same-hue deep olive-lime that clears 4.5:1 both ways so one token still serves text, border, fill and wash. The switch is an **Appearance section on the Preferences page** (`views/Preferences.tsx`, §20) — the ordinary `prefs-section` + `prefs-radio` shape, and the one section on that page not backed by `/api/prefs`, since the preference is per-browser-profile `localStorage` with no server store. View documents are reached by `static/runtime.js`, which resolves the same key in the view's own document and writes `data-theme` there — **only when `<html>` carries `data-fused-theme`**. Tier-1 templates (AP-9) each get a `:root` dark palette + `:root[data-theme="light"]` twin with every stylesheet colour tokenized; `code` additionally drops `CM.oneDark` in light mode and reconfigures the RUNNING CodeMirror view (`StateEffect.reconfigure` off a `MutationObserver` on `data-theme`) rather than rebuilding it. | Users on a light desktop got a dark app with no way out, and users wanting dark pinned had no guarantee either. **Reading in the view rather than pushing from the shell** was the key call: it means the shell never reaches into an iframe, so no theme change can remount/reload a pane or lose scroll, selection or unsaved edits — the `storage` event already fans a change out to every same-origin document for free. **Opt-in via an `<html>` attribute** (rejected: a `<meta>`, and rejected: theming every rendered document): `runtime.js` is injected at the top of `<head>` and is parser-blocking, so a `<meta>` further down isn't parsed yet — and user-authored `.html` views must keep owning their CSS entirely and receive no signal at all. **Inline bootstrap** was rejected-alternative-free: applying the theme from React would flash dark for a light-mode user on every load, which is the one thing the feature can't do. **`--tint` as an rgb triple** over duplicating ~40 wash rules per theme. **One darkened `--accent`** over adding a second accent-for-text token: the lime is unreadable on white as text and near-invisible as a hairline, and 56 call sites use it for all four roles. **Tokenizing every literal** (rather than only the obvious ones) is what the light palette needs to be complete, and a test enforces it so the next colour added can't silently be dark-only. The tier lists are exhaustive over `templates/` with a test asserting it, so a new template can't skip classification. Explicitly rejected: any server-side settings store/endpoint/config file (this is per-browser-profile state — a browser and the desktop window may legitimately differ); migrating or overriding `excel`/`log_studio`/`tableau`'s own toggles and private keys; light palettes for the media/geospatial/studio groups (deferred — accepted consequence: they render dark inside a light shell); restyling dark. `vector` keeps its carto dark-matter basemap and its maplibre paint expressions dark: those are JS strings where `var()` can't resolve, swapping the style at runtime would tear down the added data layers, and light chrome over a dark map is the normal look for a mapping view (`map` is itself light-by-design/exempt). |
| D135 | **The Linux tray glyph is tinted from the desktop's XDG-portal colour-scheme preference instead of unconditionally white, and re-tints live; white stays the fallback for every failure mode** | `_tint_white()` mapped every glyph pixel to white and `_icon_pixmap` was `lru_cache(maxsize=1)`d on the path alone, so the pixmap was computed once per process and could never change — on a light panel that is a white glyph on a light background, effectively invisible (confirmed on Linux). `_linux/tray.py` gains a generic `_tint(image, rgb)` (with `_tint_white` kept as the dark-panel wrapper) and reads `org.freedesktop.portal.Settings.Read("org.freedesktop.appearance", "color-scheme")` on the bus the tray already holds — `0` no preference, `1` prefer dark, `2` prefer light. Only an explicit `2` gets the dark glyph (`_TINT_DARK`, a near-black rather than pure black so it reads as a glyph on off-white panels); `0`, `1`, an unknown value, an absent portal, a D-Bus error, a timeout and a malformed reply all keep white. The read happens BEFORE the interfaces are exported, so the first `IconPixmap` served is already right. `SettingChanged` re-tints and emits SNI `NewIcon` — `IconPixmap` therefore reads through a one-element ref the handler swaps, and `NewIcon` fires only when the tint actually changed. `_icon_pixmap` stays cached (bring-up retries forever on watcher-less sessions) but keys on the tint too, `maxsize=2` so the two variants coexist. `_pack_argb32` and `_ICON_SIZE` untouched; dark-panel output byte-identical (asserted). | Icon tinting is cosmetic, so the entire path is best-effort: nothing about it may prevent the tray registering, hence white-on-any-failure rather than a hard dependency on the portal (many desktops expose none, and the tray already retries bring-up forever on watcher-less sessions — those users must see no regression). The portal setting was chosen over reading a DE-specific gsettings key or shelling out: it is the cross-desktop standard, and `dbus-fast` is already a Linux dependency on a connection that's already open, so this adds none. Live re-tint via `NewIcon` rather than re-registering the status item, so the icon never blinks out of the panel — mirroring the shell's live-repaint choice (D134). Deliberately NOT wired to the in-app System/Light/Dark override: the tray follows the *desktop*, the app follows its own setting, and conflating them would make the tray unreadable whenever the two disagreed. macOS and Windows are untouched and already correct — `menubar-template.png` is a real template image recoloured by the OS, and the Windows tray uses the full-colour `.ico`. |

### App call log: one record per API call a page makes, plus fifteen rounds of adversarial review (2026-07-25)

| # | Decision | Choice | Rationale / rejected alternatives |
|---|---|---|---|
| D136 | **The app call log: one bounded structured record per API call a page makes, written at the middleware, read by a `calls` view template + a `fused-render calls` CLI** | New `fused_render/calls.py` + `templates/calls/` (SPEC §31, CL-1..CL-17; design and rejected alternatives in `docs/CALL_LOG_DESIGN.md`). The record is the serve plane's error record (the `fused` repo's `spec/serve/error-reporting.md`) **widened to successes** — same field names, units and caps — so a page's local numbers and its deployed ones are comparable and render in one viewer. **Write path:** the ASGI middleware creates the record on the way in (`request.state.fused_call`), route handlers enrich it in place (`/api/run` adds the resolved `.py`, params, engine, output tails, traceback; `/api/fs/write` adds bytes + conflict/readonly), and the middleware writes it on the way out — one write point, so a new endpoint is logged by default and fail-open has exactly one place to be correct. A 500 records from the `except` branch because `@app.exception_handler(Exception)` runs in `ServerErrorMiddleware`, outside user middleware. **Attribution:** `runtime.js` sends `X-Fused-Page`/`-Target`/`-Call`; that header is the whole test for "is this an app call", so the shell's own requests are excluded by construction rather than by an endpoint blocklist that would drift. **Store:** append-only `~/.fused-render/calls/<date>-<pid>.calls.jsonl` behind a bounded queue + background writer thread; three independent bounds (per-record caps, a per-page token bucket, retention by age AND directory size). **Two consequences of how this codebase already works:** superseded calls (D114 latest-wins cancellation) are counted but excluded from every latency percentile, and a new `page-error` record (via a `window.onerror` hook the runtime lacked) covers the case where NO call happened. The executor gained `duration_ms` + a `stderr` tail so both engines' records match; `calls/reader.py` joined `INPROCESS_HELPERS` (D72); reading the log never appends to it (shape-matched, since the reader runs from the package, the staged core copy, or a user fork). Preferences carries capture/redaction/retention | Owner-directed 2026-07-24: "a log of the API calls my app makes — what the call was, its stdout/stderr/error/result size, and graphs of response time, call count, response size". **Reusing the serve record shape** over inventing a local one: it is already shipped, already capped, and it makes phase 3 (deployed apps) a widening rather than a second format. **JSONL over the `<file>.json` sidecar** (D82–D84, which `history`/§24 already renders and which would have been the tempting per-page home): every sidecar writer does a whole-file read-merge-write, which at call volume is O(n²) plus a lost-update race — the sidecar is right for low-frequency history, wrong for a firehose. **Over the app log** (`logs.py`): that file is disposable by design (D68) and unparseable prose, and a call log must survive a reboot to graph last week. **Over SQLite:** it would query beautifully, but it adds a concurrency story (two servers, WAL files) to a repo whose whole persistence posture is atomic whole-file writes + last-write-wins; JSONL keeps that posture and stays greppable by an agent with no tooling — and `.jsonl`'s existing `duckdb` binding queries it with no new code. **Capture ON by default** (owner posture): a diagnostic you must enable before the thing you wanted to diagnose is worthless — the interesting call already happened. **Params recorded by default**, same named trade-off the serve spec makes (they are the inputs the author's code already received, usually the whole repro, and already visible in the URL), with a `keys`/`off` pref for a page that passes a secret. **Excluding superseded calls from percentiles** is not a nicety: with D114 on by default, a 40-tick slider scrub would otherwise report "40 calls, p95 3.2s" for one user-perceived request — the chart would lie in the most common interaction the product has. **A template, not shell code** (HV-1/D78 containment) and a **CT-12 conditional peer**, so a page nobody ran grows no dead mode and a user can fork the whole viewer. Rejected for phase 1: client-side reconciliation of the exact superseded/`client_ms` values (the server's `disconnected` inference covers most of it; revisit once the charts show which distinction is actually missed), sampling/coalescing `/api/fs/raw` (log it plainly first, coalesce if the volume proves it), a header chip and a history-view section (shell UI, phase 2), and deployed-app records (needs serve-plane work in the `fused` repo, and per-call records for anonymous traffic must aggregate rather than enumerate — phase 3) |
| D137 | **Four corrections to the call log found by adversarial review of D136: the page must REPORT supersession (the server cannot infer it), "this file" must match a call's entrypoint, reads must be bounded by the window, and the size trim must never delete a live file** | (1) **CL-5a — supersession is client-reported.** D136 relied on `asyncio.CancelledError` out of `call_next` to infer an abandoned call; **that exception never fires** — aborting a fetch does not raise into the handler, so the run completed and was recorded as an ordinary success. Verified with a real browser scrub: 10 ticks produced 10 `ok` records, `stale=0`, all counted in p50/p95. `runtime.js` now stamps a stable `X-Fused-Call` id per call and POSTs abandoned ids to `/api/calls/event` (`kind: "superseded"`, batched per macrotask, flushed on `pagehide`, `keepalive` **not** `sendBeacon` — a beacon cannot set the `X-Fused` header the endpoint requires); `finish()` stamps the outcome from a TTL'd, hard-bounded mark set, consumed once. (2) **The Calls view on a `.py` showed nothing** — the mode the registry offers for a data file, whose `condition.py` gate correctly confirmed it had history, rendered an empty state, because the `page` filter matched only `page`/`target_file` and a `.py` is never a record's `page` (the `.html` is). It now also matches `entrypoint`. (3) **Reads were O(store), not O(window)** — `since` was applied after parsing every record, so a one-hour question parsed the whole retention window (measured 395 ms/op on a 20 MB store, ×4 ops per render, polled every 2 s while following). Files are now read backwards from the tail in blocks, a file whose mtime predates the window is skipped whole, and within a file the first out-of-window record ends it (measured 0.5 ms — ~790×). Files are skipped but never stopped at: same-day files from different pids interleave in time. (4) **The size trim deleted live files** — trimming is whole-file, and when today's file was the only one (exactly the single-day-burst case the cap exists for) the sweep unlinked the file the writer had open, silently discarding the day. Today-dated files are no longer candidates, a new `MAX_FILE_BYTES` per-file roll (`<date>-<pid>-<part>`) bounds within-day growth instead, and a store still over cap logs a warning rather than pretending the cap held. Retention also now sweeps on a day roll, not only at startup and on a 24 h timer | The D136 tests passed while (1) was completely broken, which is the lesson worth recording: `test_superseded_calls_are_counted_but_never_in_the_percentiles` wrote records with `outcome="superseded"` directly and asserted the rollup excluded them — it tested the **consumer** and nothing tested that anything ever **produces** the value. Both halves were individually correct and the seam between them was empty; `TestClient` cannot model a socket abort, so nothing pushed against it. The new tests drive the real path, and one asserts the `runtime.js` wiring textually since no test in the suite executes it. The §6.2 deferral of client reporting to phase 2 was simply wrong — it traded away the feature's central guarantee for ~30 lines, on the assumption that a server-side substitute existed. It turned out cheap anyway because of a timing accident: the client knows at abort time, ~1 s before the still-running handler finishes, so the report lands before the record is written and no append-only mutation is needed. **Still not fixed, deliberately:** a closed tab or reload is reported by nobody and records as `ok` (needs `request.is_disconnected()`), and the abandoned subprocess still runs to completion — `runtime.js`'s long-standing comment claiming the server "drop[s] the now-irrelevant subprocess when it sees the closed socket" is false, and always was. Both are separate changes; the second is an efficiency bug this log merely made visible |
| D138 | **Call-log follow-ups: null keys omitted on write (a healthy record no longer reads as ERROR), the hot path stops re-reading prefs.json, Preferences navigates in-app, `err_id` becomes a real join key, and client-disconnect detection is abandoned as infeasible** | (1) **Null-valued keys are dropped at write time.** A narrow record (a `stat`, a raw read) was mostly `null`s, and the field NAME `"error": null` made every *successful* call render as **ERROR** in `log_studio` — which the registry offers for `.calls.jsonl` and which infers a level by sniffing the raw line for level words (owner report, from the raw store view). Now only a record that really failed contains the word, so the inference is right rather than wrong; records also shrink ~20%. (2) **The hot path no longer reads `prefs.json` per call.** `enabled()` plus the redaction mode opened and parsed it on every call, and `is_first_party` resolved three realpaths — measured 2.76 ms/run (7.0%) of pure logging overhead, now 0.99 ms (2.4%). A 1 s snapshot cache, invalidated by the prefs endpoint on write so CT-5's no-restart rule still holds. (3) **Preferences' call-log action navigates the explorer to the store** (`navigate(dir, {isDir:true})`) instead of revealing it in Finder/Explorer, with reveal kept as a second button: the explorer is *how you reach the view* — a `.calls.jsonl` opens in the `calls` template — whereas revealing in the OS file manager only gets you raw text (owner call). (4) **`err_id` is wired.** It was documented as the join key to a failure and never set.
