Worth stating plainly because it is a live question every time: the grant is `addRules` for one `toolName`, **not** the CLI's separate `setMode` update, so it never turns on `auto` — verified end to end (after allow-all on an `Edit`, a second `Edit` passed and a `Write` in the same turn still parked its own card). **The run tree is created `0700`, its files `0600`** (owner). The whole conversation lives there — `out.jsonl` is the transcript, `meta.json` the user's message, `perm/*.req.json` every tool payload — under a temp root that is world-readable on a typical Linux box, and everything was landing at the umask default: verified `drwxr-xr-x` on the run dir and `-rw-r--r--` on a 42 KB `out.jsonl` before the fix. Modes now come from the `mkdir`/`open` itself rather than a `chmod` afterwards, so nothing is briefly readable, and `_private_dir` walks the chain because `os.makedirs`' `mode` has reached only the leaf since 3.7. The deliberate non-action is that **existing** directories are not tightened: the chain begins at a directory we do not own, and a stray `chmod` on the temp root would be a far worse bug than the disclosure being fixed — the run dir, always freshly created here, is the level that holds the data. Only `.res.json` had been 0600, because that one was written through an `os.open` with an explicit mode for unrelated reasons (the O_EXCL latch) — which is the tell worth remembering: the one file that got it right got it right *by accident*, as a side effect of a different requirement, so the other seven were never noticed. macOS' per-user temp root hid all of it there. **Two follow-ups the privacy fix itself caused, and they pull in opposite directions** (owner P2 + Bugbot, same round): a `0700` root is unusable *shared*, because the first account to open a chat owns the namespace and every other local user can no longer create a run at all — while anything writable by them is either a world-writable directory we made or the very disclosure the `0700` prevents. There is no mode that satisfies both, so the sharing goes instead: the root is now `fused_render_claude-<uid>` (POSIX only; Windows' temp dir is already per-user). Separately the create was a TOCTOU — two runs starting at once both find the root missing, both `mkdir`, and the loser propagated `FileExistsError` out of `_start`, so one chat silently never sent its message (reproduced at 2 of 8 concurrent first runs). Parents now accept an existing directory; the **leaf** stays exclusive, because that one is the private boundary and finding it already there is a collision rather than a race to shrug off. And then the per-uid path, being *predictable*, became the next problem (Bugbot, High): another account can pre-create `fused_render_claude-<victim-uid>/runs` under a world-writable temp root, and owning the **parent** is enough — the sticky bit that stops one account renaming another's entry protects our entries in `/tmp` but is **not inherited** by a directory they made, so they rename the 0700 run dir aside the instant after `mkdir` returns and leave a world-readable one for the transcript to land in. `_require_private` now vouches for any existing directory in our tree (real directory by `lstat` so a symlink cannot pass, owned by our euid, no group/other write) and raises otherwise: a planted directory can deny the chat, but a refusal is not a disclosure. Same round, the leftover-key mechanism leaked through its own bookkeeping (Bugbot): `covered` was hand-listed, so naming both sides of an `a || b` marked the *loser* covered as well and the leftover dump skipped it — invisible on the card, authorised by `updatedInput`. It is now derived from what was actually rendered. And "rendered" had to be sharpened twice more, both times because the derivation was still describing an intention rather than an outcome. **Non-empty** is the second (Bugbot, High): `buildPermCard` emits a `<pre>` only for a truthy body, so a key marked covered while rendering as nothing was hidden from *both* surfaces at once — reproduced as `Write {"file_path": "/notes.md", "content": ""}`, whose card is a bare path with no body, identical to an ordinary path-only approve, while the empty content **truncates the file**. `show()` now records a key only when there is text to see, so empty values fall through to the leftover dump and print as the `""` they are. The general invariant is now one test — every input key is either rendered in the summary or named in the dump — and it carries a `bool(text)` guard, because `"" in shown` is always true in Python and *that* is precisely why the earlier alternation test could not catch this: the assertion designed to prove nothing was hidden read an empty value as present in every card it looked at. And the dump lost a key a *third* way (owner P2, next round): it was built by assigning each leftover into a `{}`, and `__proto__` is the one name that cannot be assigned — the input arrives via `res.json()`, which like `JSON.parse` *defines* an own `__proto__` key, so `Object.keys` lists it and the dump owes the user a rendering of it, but assignment reaches `Object.prototype`'s legacy setter, creates no own property, and `JSON.stringify` prints `{}` over a field `updatedInput` authorises in full (for a primitive value the setter does not even change a prototype — it simply discards it). `Object.fromEntries` defines rather than assigns, so the key survives; the guard is a node probe that parses the input *inside* node, because a JS object literal in the test would go through assignment and hide the case under test. The old test asserted on the source strings of this block, which is precisely why it did not notice — so it now runs `leftoverInput` instead, the same treatment `summarizePermission` already had. The pattern across this whole round is worth naming: **each hardening step created the next finding** — 0600 on the files exposed the shared-parent question, the shared-parent fix exposed the race, and the race fix had to be reconciled with multi-user usability. **Releasing a claim is only safe while nobody has been told an answer** (Bugbot, third round). The unlink-on-failed-write added above was copied into *both* writers, and it is right in only one of them: `agent.py` frees the claim because the write is all that happened and `_decide` returns an error, so a retry should be able to win it; `permission_server`'s timeout write has already handed claude a deny by the time it fails, so freeing the latch lets a later Allow land and the card reads "✓ Allowed" for a tool that was refused. The server now keeps its claim, which costs a card reading "could not record that decision" — the truth. Worth recording because the symmetry is exactly the trap: the same three lines, the same failure, opposite correct answers, and the thing that distinguishes them is not visible at the call site. Alongside it, a **low-severity crash in the same commit**: `int(float("inf"))` raises `OverflowError`, which is *not* a `ValueError`, so `FUSED_RENDER_PERMISSION_TIMEOUT=inf` (or `1e400`) took down `agent.py` at **import** — every action in the template, not just the setting — while the docstring promised a fallback. Now caught, and clamped to the int32-millisecond ceiling the CLI applies to a per-server MCP timeout, since past that a bigger number is not a longer wait. **The card shows the input whole — no truncation, no dropped keys** (owner P1, plus a Bugbot finding of the same shape). `summarizePermission` clipped Bash commands, Edit strings, Write bodies and unknown-tool JSON at 1200 chars while `permission_server` returned `updatedInput` **unchanged**, so an Allow authorised bytes the card had elided. That is not a display nit: the input is model-authored, so a prompt-injected model that knows where the cut falls puts something benign in front of it and the real payload behind it, and the user clicks Allow on the part they can read. Bugbot's separate report was the same hole by omission — `Grep`/`Glob` resolved `file_path || path || pattern`, so naming a path hid the query being authorised. Fixed together: nothing truncates (the `<pre>` was already `max-height` + `overflow: auto`, so length is a scrolling problem rather than a disclosure one), the pattern is the body for the search tools, and each case now declares which `input` keys it renders so `buildPermCard` can print whatever is left over — a key the curated view has no case for is shown verbatim instead of trusted to be unimportant. The lesson worth keeping is that **a summary in a security prompt is a different object from a summary anywhere else**: everywhere else eliding detail is a kindness, here it is the attack. Pinned by a node probe that runs the card's real summariser over a table of tool inputs (a 5 KB command whose last line is the destructive one; a `Grep` with both a pattern and a path); eight of those tests fail against the pre-fix template. **A card can also switch the mode, not just answer the request** (owner ask): "Allow, and let Claude decide from here" sends `setMode` on the same `updatedPermissions` channel the allow-all uses for `addRules`, which re-points the **running** session — measured, a turn that carded Edit/Write/Write in the strictest mode carded only the Edit once the first card switched. Three guards, because this is the broadest button on a permission card: it appears only while a stricter mode is in force, only alongside an allow, and only for `SWITCHABLE_MODES` (`acceptEdits`/`auto`) — `bypassPermissions` is unreachable from a card by any route and is re-validated in `permission_server`, since that is the side that actually hands the CLI its payload. The click also writes the `permission` URL param: a `setMode` dies with the process exactly like a session rule, so without that the next turn silently goes back to asking. **And that param is emphatically not the same fact as the mode the run is in** (Bugbot, next round) — reading it as one deleted the button at the only moment it was needed. The picker applies to the *next* spawn, so switching it to "Claude decides" mid-turn leaves the live session strict and still carding, while every card built after the switch concluded it was already in `auto` and dropped the escalation — the user asks for fewer prompts, keeps getting them, and the one control that could grant the request has disappeared. `poll` now reports the run's real `mode`, **derived rather than stored** (`_live_mode`): the mode recorded in `meta.json` at spawn, re-pointed by each allow whose `setMode` reached disk, ordered by `created_at` rather than by id because ids lead with `HH%M%S` and misorder a run spanning midnight. Deriving it means it cannot drift from what claude was actually told — a deny carrying a mode, or a mode outside `SWITCHABLE_MODES`, moves nothing. An absent `mode` shows the button, because the two directions are not symmetric: a needless button is a no-op click, a missing one is the bug. The gate moved into `permChoices` for the same reason `leftoverInput` did — it was previously pinned by a substring assertion (`WHOLE_TOOL_GRANTABLE.has(p.tool)` appears in the file), which is a test of the source rather than of the behaviour, and the extraction broke it while the code stayed correct. Which is the one thing this button has over the allow-all — the mode was *already* a persisted param, so the escalation the user asked for can outlive the turn without inventing the grant store that cross-turn allow-all would need. **How many cards you get became a picker** (owner ask, same round): `permission` rides the URL beside model/effort and maps onto the CLI's own `--permission-mode` — *ask every time* (default, no flag), *auto-accept edits* (`acceptEdits`), *Claude decides* (`auto`). Measured, not assumed: under `acceptEdits` an Edit applied with no card while `WebFetch` still parked one; under `auto` the classifier vouched for an edit+write. The bridge stays wired in all three, because the mode only decides how much is auto-approved *before* the prompt tool is consulted and whatever is left must stay answerable — a mode that skipped the bridge would reintroduce the exact silent refusal this entry exists to remove. `bypassPermissions` is deliberately absent (the owner's goal was "have Claude evaluate the request", not "no cards"), and an unknown value falls back to the strictest rather than erroring: extra auto-approval must be opted into, never handed over by a mangled param. Two more from the same round: **the expiry is latched, not labelled** — `poll` marking a leftover request `expired` writes it, and `decide` records an expiry once the run is not alive, because both orderings previously let a click land on disk after the run died and left the card reading "✓ Allowed" for a tool claude never ran (Bugbot); and **`FUSED_RENDER_PERMISSION_TIMEOUT` is now read on this side as well**, since `agent.py` stamps its own value into the generated `mcp.json` as both the server's env and the CLI's per-call ceiling — a constant here silently overwrote the user's setting, so the variable documented as configurable was not one (owner verified 5 → 3600). **Two independent ceilings for an abandoned card**, because there is no third party to notice: the server denies itself after 1 h *and writes that verdict down* (a returned-but-unrecorded deny left the card reading "waiting for you" forever on a page that re-attached), and the generated `mcp.json` sets the per-server call timeout above it so our sentence wins over the CLI's MCP-timeout error. What no test here can catch is the CLI changing its half of the contract — the result schema, the flag names, or which tools a prompt tool un-gates (that last one was found by reading the binary, not the docs, and is exactly the kind of thing that silently comes back). |
| D162 | **The markdown graph drops its per-note size cap, and a note is named by its file — never by a frontmatter `title:` or a leading `# H1`** | Two changes to `templates/markdown/graph.py`, both narrowing what the layer claims to know. (1) `MAX_NOTE_BYTES` (256 KB) and the whole `skipped_large` channel are gone — from `_walk`, `_assemble`, both scan signatures, both payloads, and the notices in `templates/markdown/template.html` and `templates/graph/template.html`. `_walk` still stats every note, but only for the index's `(mtime_ns, size)` cache key. (2) `parse_note` no longer emits `title`, and `_display_title(rel)` takes only a path: every `title` in every payload is the filename with directory and extension stripped. `PARSER_VERSION` 1 → 2, which invalidates every cached row wholesale (MD-8), so no migration. `_parse_frontmatter` keeps its tiny YAML subset for `tags` alone. SPEC gains **MD-3a** (naming) and rewrites **MD-10** (bounded by count, not size). The `_note_payload` fallback for a note absent from the scan stays — it is still reachable via a skipped directory or a cap, just no longer by being large. | **The size cap failed in a way the notice hid.** A skipped `.md` landed in NEITHER `notes` nor `assets` — `_walk` routes only non-notes to `assets` — so it was absent from both candidate indexes, and every `[[…]]` aimed at it resolved to `None` and drew a **ghost**: the graph asserted the note did not exist while it sat on disk, which is exactly the "unknown masquerading as missing" error D159 fixed elsewhere and this reintroduced by another route. Found on this repo: `DECISIONS.md` (323 KB) was the one skipped file. Both halves of the cap's original rationale were wrong — a long-lived decision log is precisely a note people want backlinks into, and the read cost it avoided is already paid once and cached by MD-8, so a big note is read on the open after it changes and stat-only forever after. Raising the number was rejected as the fix: any ceiling reproduces the ghost for whatever sits above it, and the walk's real budget is `MAX_ENTRIES` (a file count) which is untouched. Also corrected a comment in `template.html` that justified the Live Preview's whole-document decoration walk by citing this cap — the editor's bound has always been its own `MAX_BYTES` (2 MB), so the justification was already false and a 500 KB note already took that walk. **Naming by file** is the owner's call, and Obsidian's rule: a frontmatter `title:` or an `# H1` renaming a note meant the label on a graph node was not the string you would search for, rename, or type inside `[[…]]`, while an H1 that merely repeated the filename made the two agree often enough to hide the cases where they did not. Observed directly in a folder graph whose nodes read `— module overview`, `— specs`, `— exposed surface` — H1 fragments, useless as identifiers. Headings are still parsed in full; they are just headings. A side benefit that fell out: the name needs only a path, so a note the scan never parsed still has one. |
| D163 | **The link graph is laid out in bands by folder — one block per folder, wrapped to the surface — and only the horizontal axis is simulated** | `templates/shared/graph-canvas.js`, shared by both graph surfaces, stops being a free 2D force layout. `graph.py` now sends each note node a `dir` (its folder relative to the scan root, `null` for a ghost or a tag) so the canvas never has to recover one by splitting an id. The canvas groups nodes into one band per distinct folder ordered by depth then name, folderless nodes into trailing `unresolved`/`tags` bands, and owns `y` outright — eased toward the band, never simulated, except for a node the user dragged (`userPinned`, distinct from the `pinned` that setData sets on the focus). A band WRAPS: its nodes are dealt round-robin across `ceil(count * spread / usable)` lanes and the band grows to hold them, so the same folder is one airy line in a window and a compact block in a 320px panel. In-row pitch is `max(REST, widest measured label + 20)` via `measureText`. Three force rules follow: repulsion is horizontal and per-LANE; an edge holds `spread` only within a lane; an edge across BANDS pulls toward `ex = 0`; an edge across lanes of one band applies no horizontal force. Edge alpha washes out with edges-per-node. Band names draw in screen space at the left edge, inside a gutter `frameAll` fits around; separators sit in the `BAND_GAP` between adjacent bands only. Covered by 8 new behavioural tests in `test_graph_canvas.py`, whose harness gained a pumpable rAF queue and a ctx that records `arc`/`fillText` — positions are read out of the draw calls, so a layout that computes right and draws wrong still fails. `test_markdown_template.py`'s disc-seeding source assertion is replaced by one of them. SPEC gains **MD-19a**. | Owner feedback, verbatim: the graph "is not helpful", and what was wanted was which layer/folder the linking files are in. A free layout spends two axes on nothing nameable, so spending one on the tree is the cheapest way to make position mean something. **Every subsequent constant here was measured, and four of them are fixes for a failure the previous attempt caused** — the sim was run headlessly against real payloads and screenshotted in the app after each. (1) One row per folder ran labels together — observed as `READMEauthoring`, `catalogcomments`. (2) Nine nodes in a row is ~1080px, which in a 320px panel fits only at a zoom below the `> 0.7` threshold that hides labels: measured 0/9 labels drawn, i.e. strictly worse than the layout it replaced, which at least packed a square blob. Wrapping fixed both at once. (3) Band-wide horizontal repulsion then silently UNDID the wrap over the following frames — the block re-spread into one row and labels vanished again; confirmed by screenshot showing named bands and anonymous dots. Repulsion had to become per-lane. (4) Cross-lane edge alignment then collapsed a near-complete folder onto a single x, squeezing same-lane neighbours to a fraction of their spacing — labels overlapping from the opposite direction. Lanes are a wrapping artifact, so that force was removed entirely. (5) Reserving the full half-label-width as fit padding demanded 130px of margin against a 200px block, zooming out far enough to hide EVERY label to avoid clipping ONE; capped at 28px, accepting that an outermost long name can overhang. (6) `LANE_GAP` 30 → 38 because a label reaches up into the lane above it. Rejected: a fixed band pitch (a 14-folder graph, this repo's own, made the stack several times the canvas height); colouring by folder instead of positioning by it (the owner asked for layer/depth, and colour needs a legend to decode anyway); routing or bundling the edges (the honest content of a near-complete graph is "these all link to each other", which no arrangement of 30 curves states better than a faint texture). The hairball and the label overlap were both reported by the owner directly; neither was visible in any source diff, which is why the harness now reads positions out of `arc()` rather than asserting on the file. |
| D164 | **Graph positions are assigned, not simulated: barycenter-ordered bands with measured label slots, curved edges, and a per-frame label declutter; backlinks group by linking note** | The spring sim in `templates/shared/graph-canvas.js` (REST/REPULSION/MAX_SPEED and the three per-lane force rules of D163) is replaced by a deterministic `layout()`: within each band, nodes are ORDERED by the mean x of their neighbours (three alternating Sugiyama-style sweeps; an exact tie between a linked and an unlinked node resolves toward the linked one, because a stable sort otherwise strands it every sweep), then SPACED by a slot grid — each label owns `max(SLOT_MIN 60, measured width + SLOT_PAD 18)`, columns are as wide as the widest slot they hold, lanes wrap when a band's slots outgrow the surface. The whole layout shifts so the focus sits on the canvas mid-line; the stack centres vertically (minus the trailing BAND_GAP, which off-centred everything by half a gap). `step()` is now a glide — 0.2 ease with a 0.5px snap — so re-sends stay calm: carried-over nodes glide to new assignments, NEW nodes are born at theirs. Edges draw as vertical S-curves (`bezierCurveTo` through the mid-y; a same-band edge bows downward instead), the density wash floor drops to 0.18 with the focus note's own edges held at `wash * 2.2`, and which labels PRINT is decided per frame: candidates are ranked focus > hovered neighbourhood > degree and any label whose box would intersect one already kept is dropped for the frame (hover summons it back). Alternate bands carry a 4% foreground fill instead of separator hairlines. The backlinks list renders one row per LINKING NOTE with a muted `×N` for multiplicity, instead of one row per link. `test_graph_canvas.py` re-pins the geometry (settled-on-first-draw, column alignment, slot spacing, drag-collision label drop); `test_markdown_template.py` swaps the sim-constant pins for `REPULSION not in source` plus the slot constants. | Owner feedback, a screenshot after D163 shipped: labels printed over each other (`configuratiobservability`) and the edges fused into one central rope. The cause is structural, not a constant: the sim ran two forces that want opposite things — in-lane springs holding labels apart, cross-band pulls aligning linked notes into columns — and in the hub-shaped graph this panel actually shows (everything links to README) the column pulls all point at one x, outnumber the springs, and win. No tuning fixes a fight between forces; ordering + slots answer both wants with nothing left to solve, and overlap becomes impossible by construction rather than discouraged by tuning. The declutter pass is the owner's own suggestion ("hide the label if text/lines overlap it? only show it on hover or appropriate zoom") — the slots cover what the layout placed, the declutter covers what it did not: a node dragged onto a neighbour, two labels passing mid-glide. **Rejected: one node per row, label beside the dot** — built, shipped to a screenshot, and rejected on sight ("it looks bad"): solving overlap by construction is right but a vertical list reads as a file tree, not a graph, so the fix had to keep nodes spread in both axes. Rejected: keeping the sim and only hiding colliding labels — the rope of edges stays, and a graph whose labels MUST hide is worse than one whose labels cannot collide. Rejected: routing/bundling the curved edges (same grounds as D163: a near-complete folder's honest content is a faint texture). Verified against the live panel by screenshot at depth 1, 2 and `all` (205 notes) before the tests were rewritten, since D163's failure was exactly a layout that passed its tests and failed on sight. |
| D165 | **The tag concept is removed from the markdown template — for now** | Tags come out of every layer at once, so no half of the feature survives to rot: `templates/markdown/graph.py` loses `_TAG`, the tag block in `parse_note` and the `"tags"` key from its row (PARSER_VERSION 2 → 3, so a cached row still carrying tags is invalidated rather than replayed), `"tags"` from the `note` and `candidates` payloads, and the tag-node loop from `_graph_nodes_and_edges`; `templates/markdown/template.html` loses the `.tag` rule, the `#tags` sidebar element and the code that filled it, `tagWidget`, `TAG_RE` and its decoration pass, `URL_NODES` (it existed only to keep a URL fragment from becoming a tag), and the `#tag` autocomplete branch; `templates/shared/graph-canvas.js` loses `TAG_BAND`, its band label and rank, and the accent fill for `kind === "tag"` (accent is now the focus node alone). `_parse_frontmatter` goes with it: `tags` was the only frontmatter key anything read, so the tiny YAML subset had no remaining caller — `_frontmatter_span` stays, because the block must still be masked out of the body (MD-3). SPEC MD-3/MD-14/MD-18a/MD-19a/MD-20 rewritten; `test_markdown_graph.py`, `test_markdown_live_preview.py`, `test_markdown_template.py` and `test_graph_canvas.py` keep the ghost/unresolved halves of the tests that covered both and gain behaviour tests that a `#word` produces no node and no widget. | Owner: "remove the tag concept for now." The graph this template is actually read in is a code-adjacent vault, and there the hashtag rule fired on prose and code fragments rather than on deliberate tags — `#backer`, `#dsamed-environments` and their kind arrived as accent-coloured nodes in their own band, indistinguishable in weight from real notes. The grouping a tag buys is also the one thing the layout already gives for free (D163: a band per folder), so the noise was paying for a second, worse grouping axis. Removing rather than filtering: every filter considered (require frontmatter, require a minimum count, blacklist code-ish shapes) is a heuristic guessing at intent, and a link layer whose rules need guessing is exactly what MD-3 exists to avoid. **"For now" is literal — this is one coherent, revertable commit**, and the concept may come back behind better parsing (an explicit frontmatter-only `tags:`, or a tag pass that knows it is inside a code-adjacent document); the tiny YAML parser comes back with whatever frontmatter key is read next. Verified by screenshot against the live dev server on a real vault: no tag nodes, no `tags` band, focus still accent, unresolved band and ghost styling untouched, no tag chips in the sidebar, no console errors. **Two unrelated removals ride along, both "this was never used":** (1) `VAULT_MARKERS` drops `.fused-graph.json`, leaving `(".obsidian", ".git")` — the file was specified in MD-12 as a marker whose *contents* were unbuilt, which made it a marker only a fused user who had read the SPEC could ever have placed, and `.obsidian/` plus `.git` cover every vault anyone actually keeps; MD-12's "not built" clause now names per-vault tuning generally rather than a filename nothing reads, and the marker-climb test loses that parametrize case. (2) A confirmed layout bug in `place()` in `templates/shared/graph-canvas.js`: lanes were `ceil(total slot width / usable)` with **no cap at the band's node count**, and a band's height is `lanes * LANE_GAP` — so a band of two very long labels asked for five lanes, filled two, and still grew to five, pushing every band below it down and making `frameAll` zoom the whole graph out to fit space nothing was drawn in. Now `min(row.length, …)` with a floor of 1. The canvas harness gains an opt-in `LABEL_W` hook so one test can have *measured* labels (every other geometry test is pinned against the unmeasured `SLOT_MIN` slot, which is why the hook is opt-in rather than a default `measureText`), and the new test fails at 288 against 168 on the uncapped code. *(Historical rows D157/D159 and `docs/markdown-graph.html` still describe `.fused-graph.json`; both are records of the conversation at the time and are deliberately not rewritten.)* |

### Templates decoupled from the package: env plumbing, not imports (2026-07-29)

| # | Decision | Choice | Rationale / rejected alternatives |
|---|---|---|---|
| D166 | **A template learns about its environment from EXPORTED ENV VARS, never by importing `fused_render` — and the worker's `PYTHONPATH` injection that made those imports appear to work is removed** | Contract in SPEC PY-15 (PY-6a marked retired). The server exports `FUSED_RENDER_HOME_DIR` (already branch-resolved), `FUSED_RENDER_MOUNTS_DIR` and `FUSED_RENDER_ORIGIN` once before it starts serving (`server.export_app_env` + `set_server_origin_env`, called from both startup seams — `app.py` and `cli.py`), and `shell/mounts.export_ro_mounts_env` publishes `FUSED_RENDER_RO_MOUNTS` from `_write`, so it tracks every store mutation. `templates/shared/appenv.py` is the one reader: stdlib-only, values resolved **per call**. Migrated: `markdown/graph.py` (mount refusal + index dir), `annotate/annotate.py` and `claude/agent.py` (`mount_read_only`), `zarr_aoi/tile_server.py` (its inlined `_home_dir`, plus the `_branch.branch_port` origin fallback DELETED outright), `graph/condition.py` and `reader/condition.py`'s home-dir fallback. `executor._child_env()` and `_child.py`'s matching `sys.path.append` both go. One allowlisted exception: `reader/condition.py`'s `shell.prefs` read, an in-server-only pref, not a mount fact. | The imports were all `try: from fused_render... except: <fallback>` and worked only because `_child_env()` put the package on the child's `PYTHONPATH`. The **fused** local backend deliberately strips `PYTHONPATH`/`PYTHONHOME`/`VIRTUAL_ENV` from children for venv hermeticity (`python_compute.py`) while inheriting every other var untouched — so under that engine every guarded import took its fallback branch on every run, SILENTLY: a read-only mount looked writable (`os.access` lies under `CacheMode=full`), a vault got refused for the wrong reason, a tile daemon read from the baseline port. Env vars cross that boundary intact, which is why the fix needs no change in the `openfused` repo. **Env over per-request param injection**: the consumers sit deep inside the templates (`annotate._sidecar_writable` → `_save_sidecar`, `claude._mount_read_only`), so threading a value down would mean changing `main()` signatures in both — and there is no race to worry about, because this is global state any two concurrent runs would want identically. **Derived answers over the store**: `appenv` never reads `mounts.json`; it gets the mountpoint LIST, so the schema (`read_only_user`, legacy records, future flags) and all mount POLICY stay in `shell/mounts.py` and the mount-agnostic rule holds — a template may ASK a fact, never branch on how mounts work. **Values exported already-resolved**: the branch nesting and `_branch.sanitize` had TWO copies (one of them inlined in `zarr_aoi/tile_server.py` with a comment begging for lockstep maintenance); exporting the output of `storage.home_dir()` deletes the copy instead of pinning it with a test. **Deleting the `branch_port()` origin fallback is a fix, not a regression**: it yields the baseline `1777`, wrong under any `--port` override (the desktop launcher auto-picks a free port), so it pointed every read at a dead port and surfaced as an opaque zarr *"No group found in store"*; absent `FUSED_RENDER_ORIGIN` now raises a message that says what is actually wrong. **The fail-closed/degrade branches were kept and are now tested directly** — they stop being safety nets and become the primary path when no server exported the vars, so "no detector => refuse the walk" and "no RO list => the genuine `os.access` rule" each have their own test. Rejected: leaving the `PYTHONPATH` injection in as a belt-and-braces (it would preserve exactly the engine-dependent divergence this ends, and a half-removal — one half gone, one kept — is the worst state of all, so a test pins the absence of both); a `fused_render`-lite shim package importable from templates (a second import surface to keep in sync, and still unreachable in a uv-venv daemon); passing the facts as run params (see above). *(Bookkeeping: `DECISIONS.md` carries TWO rows numbered **D72** — in-process helpers, and layout params. Flagged, not renumbered: both are referenced by ID from code comments and SPEC, so a rename would silently invalidate those citations. This row takes the next number after the true maximum, D165.)* |

### fused.ai latency: warm CLI process and streaming (2026-07-29)

| # | Decision | Choice | Rationale / rejected alternatives |
|---|---|---|---|
| D168 | **`fused.ai` prewarms the claude CLI (prewarm-use-once-respawn) and streams NDJSON to the page** (amends D159, the fused.ai decision) | Measured on the D159 shape: the CLI is a Node program whose startup alone costs **~1.5–2.5 s per call** — at haiku/low-effort sizes it dominated the whole call. The process now runs with `--input-format stream-json --output-format stream-json --include-partial-messages --verbose` (mandatory with stream-json output; the CLI exits 1 without it), so it can be spawned BEFORE the prompt exists and fed the user message over stdin later. A one-slot warm pool holds one pre-spawned process keyed by its spawn config `(binary, model, system prompt, max_tokens)` — a warm process is only usable on an exact config match, since model and system prompt are argv/env at spawn. The slot converges on the **last-used config** (seeded with the defaults at server startup, fire-and-forget so readiness never waits and a missing binary skips it), a take is lock-guarded so concurrent requests never share a process (the loser spawns fresh, same code path), a replacement prewarm fires on every request, and a warm process that fails before producing anything gets one fresh-spawn retry. `{"stream": true}` on `/api/ai` switches the response to `text/x-ndjson` — `{"type":"chunk","text"}` per text_delta, closed by a `{"type":"done"}` frame carrying the same result schema (or the error, when it struck after HTTP 200 already left; validation errors before the first byte are still plain 400/502 JSON) — and `fused.ai` grows `opts.onChunk`. Non-streaming responses are byte-identical to D159's. `_run_claude_cli` is replaced by `_spawn_claude_stream` as the mockable seam. | **Why D159's subprocess-per-call needed amending but not abandoning**: the isolation property it bought (each call sees a prompt-fresh process, D70 discipline) is kept in full — a probe on 2.1.220 showed **context accumulates across user messages inside one process** (message 2 recalled message 1 verbatim, no per-message reset), which is exactly why multi-turn reuse was rejected: a reused process would leak one page's prompt into another's completion. Prewarm-use-once-respawn changes only the SPAWN TIMING: the ~2 s startup moves off the critical path into the idle gap between calls, and every process still serves exactly one prompt before being reaped. Streaming attacks the other half of perceived latency — time-to-first-token instead of time-to-last — and always spawning with `--include-partial-messages` means one warm process serves both modes (the extra `stream_event` lines are skipped when not streaming). Rejected: a multi-process pool (one slot already covers the one-page-hammering-one-config case that actually occurs); per-message context reset (no CLI flag for it); keeping the old `--output-format json` path alongside (two subprocess shapes to test for zero behavioural difference). |
| D169 | **The warm pool becomes ONE persistent claude process, reset per request over its stdin protocol** (amends D168) | The prewarm-use-once-respawn pool is replaced by a single long-lived instance (`_AiSession`) that every `/api/ai` call flows through. Per request, over the CLI's stream-json stdin: **`/clear`** as a plain user message (~0.7 s — emits a `conversation_reset` event, a fresh session_id, and a zero-cost local result; fully wipes the conversation, which is what carries D159's isolation guarantee now), then a **`set_model` control_request carrying BOTH `model` and the undocumented-but-real `system_prompt` field** (~0 ms, applies from the next turn; sent unconditionally on every request — the instance carries no config state between requests, only the process handle), then the **thinking budget**: absent or `low` effort sends **`set_max_thinking_tokens` 0** — the universal no-thinking switch, effective on EVERY model — while `medium`/`high`/`xhigh` sends **`set_max_thinking_tokens` null** (resets to the session default; mandatory each such request, because the clamp PERSISTS across `/clear`, unlike `effortLevel` which `/clear` resets) followed by an **`apply_flag_settings` control_request with `effortLevel`** (~10 ms, the same code path as the interactive `/effort`; `xhigh` joins the accepted values). The clamp is what `low` means because `effortLevel` only works on effort-capable models (sonnet/opus class) — haiku, the default, silently ignores it AND thinks by default in stream-json mode (a one-word answer measured 159 output tokens / ~5 s until the clamp restored ~tens of tokens), and the budget clamp overrides `effortLevel` in either send order, so the two are never combined on the low path. Omitted effort therefore defaults to `low` — which IS behavioral parity with the pre-rewrite code, whose env var clamped thinking to zero on every call unconditionally (the old `medium` default only mapped to a now-gone output cap). Each control_response is awaited (10 s control timeout; a wedged reconfiguration kills and respawns). All mechanisms live-probed on claude 2.1.220. Fallout: `--max-turns 1` is dropped from argv (the instance is multi-turn by design); the `CLAUDE_CODE_MAX_OUTPUT_TOKENS` and `MAX_THINKING_TOKENS` env overrides are deleted (Claude Code owns effort/thinking semantics — with `/effort low` a one-line answer measured 2.8 s wall, 7 output tokens); `max_tokens`/`opts.maxTokens` is **removed entirely** (no per-call mechanism in the CLI; effort is the only knob), and `_AI_EFFORT_TOKENS` with it. Concurrent requests **serialize** through the one instance behind a lock — the fresh-spawn-for-the-loser path is deleted. Health: a dead instance respawns on the next request; a mid-call failure before any delta was delivered gets one fresh-spawn retry (as before); reaps are `asyncio.shield`ed so cancellation (client disconnect, shutdown) cannot orphan the process or its temp system-prompt file. | Why single-instance beats the use-once pool it replaces: the pool only ever had ONE slot keyed by exact config, so the first call per config — and every call in an alternating-config page — still paid the full ~2 s spawn; the reset protocol makes **every** call warm at ~0.7 s reconfig, holds memory flat (~350 MB, one Node process) instead of churning a spawn per call, and needs no config-tuple matching at all. Accepted tradeoffs, named: **serialized concurrency** (local single-user app, calls are seconds — a queue beats a per-call spawn for the loser); **undocumented-field risk** — `set_model.system_prompt` is probed behavior, pinned to ≥2.1.220, and the failure mode is bounded by construction: a control error discards the instance and retries ONCE on a fresh spawn whose argv carries the requested config; a transient rejection heals there, while a CLI that rejects the field on every instance surfaces as a clean `ai_error` naming the rejection — one retry, no crashloop, no hang (both pinned by test). Rejected: keeping the pool alongside as a fallback (two lifecycles to test; the respawn path IS the fallback); skip-set_model-when-unchanged config tracking (a ~0 ms request is not worth carrying mutable state whose staleness would be invisible); resetting via `--resume`/session juggling (persists to disk, `--no-session-persistence` is the point); trusting fire-and-forget control writes without awaiting responses (observed safe, but the error branch is where the graceful degradation lives). |

*(Bookkeeping: D168/D169 amend D159 and were authored in parallel with D166's own env-plumbing work on `origin/main` — both branches independently claimed D166 the same day. D166 stays with the already-merged env-plumbing decision; the fused.ai warm-process work renumbers to D168/D169, with every code/SPEC citation updated to match.)*

### Engine parity, ahead of the built-in executor's removal (2026-07-29)

| # | Decision | Choice | Rationale / rejected alternatives |
|---|---|---|---|
| D167 | **Param binding has one implementation, embedded rather than re-stated: `engine.build_code` ships `_binding.py`'s own source into the generated wrapper** | The fused engine's child cannot `import fused_render` (the local backend strips PYTHONPATH), so the binding logic has to travel inside the generated code. It used to travel as a hand-written copy (`_fused_coerce`/`_fused_bind`) and had silently drifted in two ways: the copy resolved *string* annotations (`inspect.signature(..., eval_str=True)`) while `_binding` did not, so a template with `from __future__ import annotations` returned `"7"` under the built-in engine and `7` under the fused one; and the copy raised bare `TypeError` where `_binding` raises `ParamError`, so one bad param surfaced two different `error.type` values on the wire (PY-14). Fixed on both ends: `_binding.bind_params` gained the `eval_str` resolution (with the NameError/TypeError fallback to "no coercion"), and `build_code` now reads `_binding.py` via `importlib.resources` (zip/frozen-safe) and `exec`s it into a private namespace whose `__name__` is `"__main__"` — private because the user's module shares the wrapper's globals and `coerce`/`bind_params`/`ParamError` are plausible template names; `"__main__"` because traceback omits that module prefix, so the final line stays `ParamError: …` and `_split_error` recovers the same `error.type` the built-in worker reports from `type(e).__name__`. Rejected: keeping the copy and hand-syncing it (the drift above is the evidence that fails), and installing the package into the script venv (it is a user-code venv, not ours). `tests/test_engine_parity.py` runs one table of 14 coercion cases through the built-in engine, the generated wrapper, and — when `fused` is importable — the real backend, comparing bound values *and* `error.type`; `test_engine.py` adds the structural half (patch the source the wrapper reads, the patch must reach the child). |
| D170 | **SUPERSEDED by D172 (the interpreter switch), which removed `DEFAULT_REQUIREMENTS` entirely.** **`DEFAULT_REQUIREMENTS` is not a mirror of `[bundled]` and stops claiming to be; per-template PEP 723 headers cover the difference, and a test — not a comment — states the relationship** | The comment "Mirrors the `bundled` extra… keep the two lists in sync" was false for ten distributions and could not fail. Replaced by `tests/test_engine_requirements.py`: it read `[bundled]` and the core `dependencies` out of pyproject.toml and pinned the exact deltas (`BUNDLED_NOT_DEFAULT`, `DEFAULT_NOT_BUNDLED`), then walked every template file's imports with `ast` (function-level included) and asserted each `[bundled]` distribution it imports was in DEFAULT_REQUIREMENTS, in the file's own header, or in the header of the file that spawns it. Four real gaps fell out and were fixed here: `log_studio/reader.py` declared `drain3`; `map/map_render.py` gained a `rasterio` header (it had first been put on `map/vector_tile_server.py`, a file `run_python` is never handed, where the declaration was inert while looking correct — which is why the test derives the entrypoint relationship from the source via `_module_refs`/`_venv_roots` instead of a hand-kept table); and `usd/reader.py`'s header had a prose line **inside** the TOML block, so `script_requirements` raised and the whole usd template failed under this engine — notes go below the block. **What D172 keeps and what it discards:** the two derivations are the durable part and survive verbatim; the *baseline set* they were pinning does not exist any more, so the `drain3` and `rasterio` headers this row added were deleted again (both distributions are in `[bundled]`, which the app's interpreter now serves directly). The usd TOML fix stands on its own. The lesson that outlived the mechanism: a header on a non-entrypoint file is never read, and only a derivation from the source can say which files are entrypoints. |
| D171 | **CI gets one dedicated `fused-engine` job that installs the `[fused]` extra on 3.11 — and proves the engine tests ran rather than skipped** | The engine slated to become the default had zero CI coverage: no job installed the extra, so `engine.available()` was False everywhere and every engine test skipped while the matrix went green (D167 and D170 are both things that job would have caught). One job, not a change to the 3.10–3.13 matrix: the matrix must keep exercising the built-in engine, and the extra's direct-URL wheel is marked `python_version >= "3.11"`, so on 3.10 it installs nothing and would prove nothing. Two independent non-skip guards, because "installs the extra and skips everything" is worse than no job at all: `FUSED_RENDER_REQUIRE_FUSED_ENGINE=1` (set by the job) turns `engine.available()` into an assertion in `test_engine.py`, and the job fails if pytest reports **any** skip in test_engine.py/test_engine_parity.py. `cache: pip` keyed on pyproject.toml is for the extra's 27 transitive PyPI deps (boto3/pandas/pyarrow/cryptography), not the 3 MB wheel itself, which S3 serves without `Cache-Control`; `setup-uv` because the backend builds each script venv with `uv venv`/`uv pip` when uv is on PATH. One bug fell out of running it for real: with a **cold** `~/.openfused/venvs` (i.e. every CI run) the real-backend tests race under `-n auto` — `fused`'s venv creation is guarded only by an in-process lock, so losing workers die on `<venv>/bin/python` mid-build — hence the `warm_fused_backend_venv` fixture in `tests/conftest.py`, which serializes *creation* across workers with an O_EXCL lock file in xdist's shared basetemp. |
| D172 | **A script with no PEP 723 header runs on the app's own interpreter with no venv; a header is the script's COMPLETE dependency list, with no baseline unioned in** *(SUPERSEDED IN PART by D230: the declaration moved from the file's header to the folder's `pyproject.toml`. The no-baseline half stands verbatim — a folder's manifest is its complete list.)* | `DEFAULT_REQUIREMENTS` (numpy, pandas, pyarrow, duckdb, polars, matplotlib, scipy, pillow, openpyxl, shapely, geopandas) was installed into every script's venv, so the packaged app downloaded from PyPI — slowly, and needing network it otherwise doesn't — to obtain what `[bundled]` + the core `dependencies` already ship. Now: no header ⇒ `interpreter=<the app's python>` and no venv at all; header ⇒ the venv path unchanged, containing exactly what the header declares. `DEFAULT_REQUIREMENTS` is **deleted**, not kept for the header path, so a header means what PEP 723 says it means — the script's whole dependency list, not a delta against an invisible baseline. It also shrinks the venvs that survive: geotiff's used to install geopandas/matplotlib/polars/scipy/duckdb it never imports, which was pure download time in D173's loader. **The risky part is resolving the interpreter**, because `_execute_sync` spawns it verbatim as argv[0] and silently ignores `requirements` on that branch — no fallback, no error naming the cause. So the candidate is PROVEN rather than assumed: one subprocess per server process runs it and must report this app's own `sys.prefix` (same install ⇒ the app's site-packages), under the child's env rather than ours, since `python_compute` strips PYTHONHOME and a packaged interpreter may need it to find its stdlib. Anything that fails is handled by D175 (a PYTHONHOME wrapper, then a loud error) — never a spawn of something unverified, and never a silent degradation. Two guards on being wrong about `sys.executable`: an autodetected candidate whose basename isn't python-shaped is rejected WITHOUT spawning (if it were ever a py2app launcher stub, spawning it could start a second copy of the app, and the name check means we never have to learn what that stub does with `-c`), and the probe budget is 5s, not 60 — it is paid on the request path in exactly the case it exists to catch, and 60s of nothing reads as a hang. Platform by platform: macOS py2app points `sys.executable` at a real `Contents/MacOS/python` (D33, smoke-tested in build_dmg.sh) so it passes; the Linux AppImage's `usr/python/bin/python3` is an ordinary relocatable python; Windows runs under `pythonw.exe`, and `python.exe` beside it — same install, usable std streams — wins when present. `execute()` cannot carry `interpreter` (it derives one only from a uv workflow project, which a standalone .py has none of), so that path calls the documented `_execute_sync` subclass contract off the event loop; with `cache_storage=None` that is what `execute()` would have done anyway. Consequences accepted: nine core templates lost headers that named only things the app already ships (including the `rasterio` one D170 had just added — rasterio IS in `[bundled]`), and `pano/pano.py` had to declare the numpy and pillow it always imported and had been getting from the baseline. Three more headers went for a different reason — see D174. Four remain: `latex/engine.py`, `docs/docs.py`, `pano/pano.py`, `geotiff/tiff_reader.py`. `[bundled]` is unchanged, deliberately. `tests/test_engine_requirements.py` now enforces both halves — a header must be NECESSARY (declares something outside `[bundled]` + core deps) and COMPLETE — so the decision is self-policing rather than a comment. Rejected: keeping `DEFAULT_REQUIREMENTS` for the header path (it is what made an incomplete header like pano's invisible); adding `--system-site-packages` to the venv path (that is `fused`'s code, and the interpreter switch makes it moot). |
| D173 | **A script whose header needs a download gets a visible, cancellable install with verbatim errors — not a 30-second timeout** *(SUPERSEDED IN PART by D230: the flow, the detached worker, the progress record and the cancel contract are unchanged; the KEY is now the project folder, and the per-folder sharing this row deferred is exactly what D230 builds.)* | `fused.runPython` has roughly a 30s budget and the venv build ran inside it, so the first run of a header-carrying template either timed out or returned `EngineError: fused-render internal error … while running <path>` with uv's actual complaint buried in a traceback nobody reads. That is the worst possible answer to "you need a package you don't have", and it is the whole reason this flow is visible. Now `/api/run` pre-flights (header present + venv absent ⇒ `needs_install`, carrying the venv key and requirement list, plus a populated `error` object so a client that knows nothing about the loader still shows a real message), `POST /api/env/install` spawns a detached worker, `GET /api/env/progress?key=` polls it, `POST /api/env/cancel` stops it, and runtime.js drives the loader and retries the run **once** — in the shell, so every template gets it with no code of its own. The shape is `templates/docs/install_worker.py`'s (a detached worker writing `{stage,pct,detail,done,error,pid,ts}` to `progress.json`), reused rather than reinvented: one pattern in this repo, not two. Three things that fail silently and so are pinned by tests: the venv key is composed from `fused`'s own `requirements_venv_id`/`venv_key` and never re-derived (a local "sha256 of the sorted requirements" would agree until upstream changed the recipe, then fill a venv no run ever reads — a permanent double download with no error anywhere); requirements are re-derived from the .py on disk, never read out of the request body, so loader and run address one venv; and cancellation signals the process GROUP (or the uv download the worker waits on survives) but ONLY when the recorded pid leads its own group, which a `start_new_session` worker always does — without that guard a stale or recycled pid inside the server's group takes the server down, which it did to a pytest session during development. Errors are VERBATIM: `venvs._run_step` raises with uv's stderr inside, and that text reaches `progress.json` and the page unedited ("No solution found when resolving dependencies: … was not found in the package registry"). Progress is coarse and says so — `_run_step` uses `capture_output=True`, so pip's per-package output cannot be streamed without changing `fused`; four named stages, and a test pins the list so a future "62%" implying per-package resolution has to argue with it. Scope is **per-file**: the venv belongs to the requirement set one .py declares, and sharing one venv across several Python files in a folder is deliberately out of scope. Rejected: streaming pip output (needs a change to `fused`); a generic "install failed" message (the resolver's own words are the answer); installing on the request path with a longer timeout (a cold multi-wheel resolve has no bound worth guessing). |
| D174 | **A header belongs only on a file `run_python` is actually handed, and never on a daemon launcher whose real environment is its own venv** *(SUPERSEDED IN PART by D230: headers are gone, so the first half is now "no file may carry one at all". The second half is UNCHANGED and load-bearing — the geotiff and zarr_aoi daemons still own their `DAEMON_VENV`, because the built-in executor honors no declaration of ours.)* | Two distinct wastes, both invisible, both found by reading the templates after D172 rather than by a failure. **(1) The double download.** `geotiff/tile_server.py` and `zarr_aoi/tile_server.py` each declared the full heavy set (numpy/pyproj/imagecodecs/rasterio; numpy/zarr/s3fs/gcsfs/crc32c) *and* built a self-managed uv venv from an identical `DAEMON_DEPS`. But the only thing `run_python` executes in these files is `main("ensure")`, which is stdlib-only — it starts or reuses the daemon and returns a port; every heavy import is in the `--serve` half, which runs under `_daemon_python()`. So a first run built a fused script venv that then ran nothing but stdlib, and D173's loader made that wait *visible* — a progress bar for an environment nothing would ever import from. Headers deleted; `DAEMON_DEPS` is the real declaration. It has to be: the built-in executor (still the default engine) ignores PEP 723 entirely, so the header could never have served it, which is exactly why these templates grew their own venvs. **(2) The inert header.** `geotiff/_tiff_core.py` carried a complete, accurate, never-read dependency list: nothing hands it to `run_python` — `tiff_reader.py` imports it (and its header covers it) and `tile_server.py`'s daemon imports it (and DAEMON_VENV covers it). D170 shipped this same mistake on `map/vector_tile_server.py`, so it is now a third enforced invariant rather than a lesson: a header is only allowed on a file some NON-.py file in its own template folder names — which is what a `runPython('./x.py')` call site is — with sibling .py imports deliberately not counting, since one module importing another is precisely the relationship that does not make the importee an entry point. Verified by reintroducing the header and watching the test fail. **The narrow regression, accepted:** `_daemon_python()` falls back to `sys.executable` when uv is absent, and uv ships in the AppImage and the Windows installer but NOT the macOS DMG — so on a DMG install without user-installed uv, the geotiff daemon loses imagecodecs where the header had incidentally supplied it via the script venv. That fallback already lacked imagecodecs under the default engine (`_daemon_python`'s own docstring says so), and the header path is itself unreliable there — with no uv, `fused` builds venvs via `python -m venv`, the path `venvs.py` warns "cannot reliably self-locate its stdlib" for a packaged interpreter. Paying a guaranteed double venv everywhere uv exists to patch one unreliable case where it doesn't is the wrong trade. **Amendment (D182): the accepted regression is no longer accepted, because its premise is gone.** That reasoning rested on "the DMG ships no uv"; D176 then made `build_dmg.sh` bundle uv at `Contents/Resources/bin/uv` and hard-fail the build without it. The DMG's uv works — it was merely unreachable from `shutil.which`, since nothing put that directory on PATH. It also undercounted the blast radius: zarr_aoi's `s3fs`/`gcsfs`/`crc32c` are in neither the core deps nor `[bundled]`, so every remote store failed to open, not just LZW/JPEG GeoTIFFs. Fixed by putting the bundled uv on PATH once (D182), which is why the header deletion above stands unchanged. Net effect: opening a supported GeoTIFF or Zarr AOI now installs nothing through the loader at all — the daemon builds one venv, once. `geotiff/tiff_reader.py` keeps its header and its install, correctly: it is the fallback decode path for TIFFs the daemon can't read, it is a real runPython target, and it does the decoding in-process. |
| D175 | **A header-less script gets a generated PYTHONHOME wrapper when the app's own interpreter can't be used directly, and a loud error when nothing can be — never a silent empty venv, and never the network** | Two holes in D172. **(1) The fallback was not viable.** On probe failure both `interpreter` and `requirements` were empty, so the backend built a bare stdlib venv — and with `DEFAULT_REQUIREMENTS` deleted that venv has no data stack at all. The outcome was not "slower" but `ModuleNotFoundError: numpy` on a template that works today, reported as the script's own error, on a code path with no user-visible trace. Now: if no interpreter verifies, `/api/run` returns `InterpreterUnavailable` naming `FUSED_RENDER_APP_PYTHON` and runs **nothing**. That loud failure is what made hole (2) diagnosable at all, which is the argument for keeping it. **(2) The probe fails on the macOS DMG by default.** `scripts/build_dmg.sh` launches the bundled interpreter as `env -u PYTHONPATH PYTHONHOME="$RES" …/MacOS/python` and states the reason, while `python_compute` strips PYTHONHOME from the child — so the direct candidate reports the **build machine's** Homebrew framework as its prefix, a path absent on any user's machine. **Measured on a real DMG rather than reasoned**, which matters because two successive designs were wrong before this one: a `venv`-based rescue is *impossible* in that bundle — it ships no `venv` module (not in `Resources/lib/python3.12`, not in `lib/python312.zip`) and the embedded `Python.framework` holds only the dylib with no second interpreter binary, so `-m venv` fails regardless of environment and no `--system-site-packages` or `.pth` work could have helped. The venv rung was therefore deleted, not kept: it served no packaging (the AppImage and Windows both pass rung 1), and its only plausible trigger — a server whose `sys.executable` belongs to a different installation — is a misconfiguration better reported than papered over. **The answer is a wrapper script**, legal because `interpreter=` is merely an executable path: it exports the PYTHONHOME this process was launched with, unsets PYTHONPATH (mirroring build_dmg.sh) and execs the real python. Verified inside the mounted bundle under the fully stripped child env: `sys.prefix` is `Contents/Resources`, pandas / geopandas / rasterio / duckdb all import, and a header-less script runs through `engine.run_python` building **no venv**. Two details are load-bearing, not stylistic. **`exec -a <wrapper>`** (hence bash) makes the child's `sys.executable` the wrapper, so a daemon re-spawned as `[sys.executable, …]` re-enters it: `geotiff/tile_server.py` and `zarr_aoi/tile_server.py` scrub PYTHONHOME from the env they spawn into (deliberately — it would poison a uv venv), and measured on the DMG that spawn dies with `ModuleNotFoundError: No module named 'pandas'` when `sys.executable` is the raw python and succeeds when it is the wrapper, because the wrapper re-establishes PYTHONHOME itself. **`PYTHONEXECUTABLE` was rejected** though it achieves the same `sys.executable` with less machinery: it is inherited by every descendant and applies to *any* python they run, and a measurement showed an unrelated venv interpreter reporting our wrapper as its own `sys.executable` — which is exactly geotiff's uv-venv daemon re-spawning into the wrong interpreter. `exec -a` affects one process. Generation is gated to POSIX **and** to a process that actually has a usable PYTHONHOME, so Windows and the AppImage never get one; the wrapper is 0700 derived state under the app's own cache (never `fused`'s venvs path, where it could collide with a requirements key), regenerated only when its content would differ, and **probed exactly like any other candidate** — one acceptance rule for both rungs, `sys.prefix` equality, since PYTHONHOME makes the wrapper's prefix the app's own. CI cannot mount a DMG, so a stand-in reproduces the property that matters (stdlib and site-packages flattened into one PYTHONHOME-only directory) with a test asserting the stand-in genuinely fails without PYTHONHOME — otherwise every test below it would pass while testing nothing. Scope note: templates that DO declare a header are still expected to download; that is what D173's loader is for. |
| D176 | **`[bundled]` is not the macOS bundle. The bundle now ships ALL of it — the one held-out distribution, `drain3`, was REVERTED (see the amendment at the end of this row) — and the packaging list is derived rather than hand-written** | The DMG shipped with the interpreter switch working (a `.pptx` opened instantly, no venv) and then failed with *"the slides template needs python-pptx: pip install python-pptx"* — advice a DMG user **cannot follow**. Cause: `pyproject.toml`'s `[bundled]` is the dev-install list and, on Linux and Windows, the real shipping list (`uv pip install "<wheel>[bundled,…]"` in build_linux_appimage.sh / build_windows_installer.ps1). macOS is the outlier — py2app **copies** only what `setup_py2app.py` names — and eight `[bundled]` distributions were absent from that hand list. So the omission had taken effect on exactly one platform, silently: slides worked on Windows and not on a Mac. D172 then deleted the PEP 723 headers that had been accidentally covering it, because it read `[bundled]` as "what the app has". **The size-based split first proposed here is SUPERSEDED and must not be reinstated from its old reasoning**: the owner chose completeness over size, accepting ~85 MB of growth (from ~272 MB) to bundle `pymupdf` (60.3), `pikepdf` (13.8), `zarr` (3.1), `fpdf2` (2.8), `python-pptx` (2.5), `google-auth` (1.8) and `msgpack` (0.3). **`drain3` (0.1 MB) was excluded for a different reason entirely — to keep the install loader (D173) exercised on a shipped build rather than being code no packaged app runs — and that exclusion is now REVERTED; see the amendment at the end of this row.** The reasoning at the time: a script venv cannot see the app's site-packages, so an excluded dependency forces its template to declare its COMPLETE third-party closure, and `log_studio`'s closure is exactly `['drain3']`, the only zero-duplication candidate (slides would drag in Pillow and fpdf, pdf_studio openpyxl, zarr_aoi numpy), while still doing real resolution work (`jsonpickle==1.5.1`, `cachetools==4.2.1`, both hard-pinned, and drain3 is sdist-only so uv builds a wheel). `log_studio/reader.py` therefore carried a `sys_platform == 'darwin'` header; the marker (new — `engine._marker_applies`) made it evaporate on Linux and Windows, which already have drain3, instead of making them re-download it. **Two things had to change for macOS to work at all, both measured on an installed DMG:** the bundle contains no `venv`, `ensurepip` or `pip` module, so the loader died with "No module named venv" — uv is now bundled at `Contents/Resources/bin/uv` (as the AppImage and Windows installer already do) and put on the worker's PATH, and it works precisely because uv constructs venvs itself instead of importing `venv`. Verified end to end against the installed app with uv absent from PATH: venv built, drain3 installed, imports. Note the D175 wrapper must NOT be the venv base — measured, uv cannot install into a venv based on it — so it serves the no-venv interpreter path only; and `uv_bin()` deliberately does not gate on `sys.frozen`, which only py2app's boot script sets. `rasterio` was in the bundle only because `fused/workbench/core/_serialization.py` happens to `import rasterio` where modulegraph can see it — incidental, one upstream edit from vanishing, now pinned by the derivation. **`setup_py2app.py` now DERIVES its force-list** from the installed distributions' own metadata (`packages_distributions()`, which handles python-pptx→pptx and pymupdf→fitz/pymupdf), so transitive deps (lxml, xlsxwriter, numcodecs, pyasn1…) come along automatically and the hand-tracing of transitives is retired; the earned exceptions are preserved as explicit sets (`_duckdb`/`_cffi_backend` in `includes`, namespace packages never forced). **`google-auth` cannot go through py2app at all**, learned by failing a real build rather than by reading code: as `"google"` it is a PEP 420 namespace package py2app's bootstrap cannot resolve, and as `"google.auth"`/`"google.oauth2"` the build dies with `ImportError: No module named 'google'` — because `collect_packagedirs()` (build_app.py:1210) maps `get_bootstrap()` over EVERY `packages` entry and `modulegraph.util.imp_find_module` splits the name and calls `imp.find_module("google")`, all of which happens before py2app's own dotted-aware `included_subpkg` path is reached. Citing that dotted-aware path as evidence the entry would work was wrong: it is a real code path but not the first consumer. So `google/` is copied into the bundle after py2app (`STAGED_PACKAGES`, read from one declaration by both build_dmg.sh and the tests), exactly the explicit staging rclone and uv get, with a build-time smoke test that it IMPORTS through the bundled interpreter rather than merely being present. **uv is now a build-host requirement**, like the Linux and Windows scripts already have (`require uv` / `Resolve-Tool "uv"`): build_dmg.sh copies the host's binary in, so it fails loudly when uv is absent — which is what CI's macOS runners are, hence `astral-sh/setup-uv` in both the test and release DMG jobs (the release would have broken identically). **A second CI-only failure came out of the same build**: py2app died right after its own codesign line with no traceback, and only the `==>` heading that had not printed said where. Its missing-modules report `__import__`s every name modulegraph could not resolve and catches only `Exception`, so a name whose import raises SystemExit terminates the build silently; the set of missing names depends on what is installed, which is why local builds passed. `no_report_missing_conditional_import` turns that half off (we never read it), and build_dmg.sh grew an ERR trap that reports line, command, exit status and disk — the next silent `set -e` death names itself. The only hand-written part is `BUNDLED_EXCLUDED`, which `tests/test_bundle_contents.py` requires to name a real `[bundled]` entry and state a measured cost. **AMENDED (same PR): `BUNDLED_EXCLUDED` is now EMPTY — the `drain3` exclusion above is gone, and with it `log_studio/reader.py`'s PEP 723 header.** The justification did not survive counting: `[bundled]` has 20 entries, and `pyproj`, `imagecodecs`, `py360convert` and `pypandoc-binary` are **not** among them, so the `geotiff`, `pano`, `docs` and `latex` headers each already force a real install on a DMG — the loader was exercised four times over without the 0.1 MB holdout. "Keep a dependency out so its code path stays warm" is only worth anything when nothing else covers the path, and here four things did. It also cost something concrete: that header was the only marker-scoped PEP 723 block in the tree, and its `sys_platform == 'darwin'` marker made the file read as header-LESS on Linux, so both header invariants in `tests/test_engine_requirements.py` skipped it silently on CI — a blind spot fixed earlier in this PR by reading headers with `apply_markers=False`. `test_a_header_only_macos_needs_is_scoped_to_macos` lost its subject and was deleted; `apply_markers=False` stays as a forward guard. **The mechanism stays with an empty dict on purpose**: it is what makes "ships, or excluded with a stated cost — never just absent" enforceable, and `test_every_exclusion_is_declared_and_reasoned` is the guard that bites the day a genuinely large dependency does need holding back. |
| D177 | **A hand-maintained list that mirrors another list will diverge. Derive it, or make divergence a test failure — a comment is not a mechanism** | Stated as a standing rule because it happened **three times in one piece of work**, each time silently, each time found from a symptom far from the cause: (1) `engine.DEFAULT_REQUIREMENTS` claimed in a comment to "mirror the `[bundled]` extra" and was wrong for ten distributions (D170); (2) per-template PEP 723 headers maintained on files `run_python` is never handed, so they were complete, accurate and **never read** — twice, on `map/vector_tile_server.py` and `geotiff/_tiff_core.py` (D174); (3) `setup_py2app.py`'s `packages` list was missing eight of the distributions `[bundled]` promises, shipping a DMG whose slides template told the user to run a command a DMG cannot run (D176). The shape is identical: two lists that must agree, agreement maintained by human attention and asserted by a comment. Comments cannot fail. The rule, in preference order: **derive** the second list from the first at build or import time (what `setup_py2app.py` now does, which also retired the transitive-dependency bookkeeping); else **generate and commit** it with a test that regenerates and diffs; else, if it must stay hand-written, **reduce it to the deltas** and make a test demand each delta be justified in the source (`BUNDLED_EXCLUDED` must state a cost; `test_engine_requirements.py` derives entrypoint-ness and invocation from the AST rather than a table). A fourth instance, found by CI on the one interpreter that exercised it: `engine.script_requirements` deferred `import tomllib` behind a comment explaining that this kept Python 3.10 working — and it did, but only for scripts with NO PEP 723 block. Any script that HAD one raised `ModuleNotFoundError` on 3.10, with `requires-python = ">=3.10"` and no `tomli` dependency anywhere. The comment described a real mechanism and asserted a guarantee one size too large, which is the hardest version of this failure to see: nothing was out of sync, the reasoning was just incomplete, and it was invisible until headers became the main path. Fixed with `tomli; python_version < "3.11"` and an import of whichever exists; the test marker that gated those tests on the VERSION rather than on availability had been keeping them skipped on precisely the interpreter where the bug lived. Corollary, which is what made all three expensive: **a deliberate omission that is not written down is indistinguishable from a bug**, and the next reader will either "fix" it or delete whatever was quietly compensating for it — exactly how D172 removed the headers covering (3). A fifth instance was found the same way and **is** fixed here: **D29 claimed the .app bundles python-build-standalone**, when `build_dmg.sh` FATALs on anything but a *framework* python. Nothing was out of sync in code — the decision record itself had drifted from the build it described, which is the version of this failure no test can catch, since there is no second list to diff against. Corrected in place rather than superseded, because the decision (ship our own interpreter, don't freeze) never changed; only the description of how was wrong. The lesson that generalises: **a decision row describing a mechanism should name the check that enforces it**, so a reader can tell drift from intent — D29 now points at the FATAL that makes its claim true. |

*(Bookkeeping: THIRD same-day collision on this range. `main` had already renumbered the fused.ai rows to D168/D169 above — itself a fix for colliding with D166 — while this branch independently took D167/D168/D169. D167 is untouched on `main`, so the binder row keeps it and its `_binding.py` citation stays valid; the other two renumber to **D170/D171**, with the CI workflow comment updated to match. The root cause is structural and unfixed: two branches cut from one base always pick the same "next" number, and nothing here makes that detectable before a merge.)*

### server.py split into a real package (2026-07-29)

| # | Decision | Choice | Rationale / rejected alternatives |
|---|---|---|---|
| D178 | **`server.py` (4900 lines, every concern + a 1180-line `create_app()` with every route inline) is split into `fused_render/server/` — one module per concern, `routers/` for route-wiring-only modules, routes as real `APIRouter`s instead of closures** | `server.py` had grown to mix git-ignore-aware walking, directory listing, a stat cache, an fs watch registry, remote-mount probing, the fused.ai Claude session, template/condition resolution, raw-proxy hardening, fs mutation endpoints, and route wiring in one file. Split into `server/common.py`, `templates.py` (+ condition gating), `gitignore.py`, `walk.py`, `mount.py` (+ stat cache), `watch.py`, `proxy.py`, `fs_mutate.py`, `session.py`, `ai.py`, plus `server/routers/{shell,config,fs_read,render,run,export}.py` for the route groups that carry no reusable logic of their own — `server/app.py` is left with just `create_app()`: `FastAPI()` construction, `app.state` wiring, and `include_router()` calls. `server/__init__.py` re-exports `create_app`/`set_server_origin_env`/`export_app_env` so every existing `from fused_render.server import create_app` call site (cli.py, app.py, ~30 tests) needed no change. First cut used a flat `fused_render/_server_*.py` naming scheme (no subpackage) — correctly called out as not how this should be organized (a stuttering `_server_` prefix on 16 files sitting loose next to unrelated modules is a naming convention standing in for a package structure) and redone as the `server/`+`routers/` layout above. Every moved line is byte-identical to the original (a script diffed the extraction against `git show HEAD:fused_render/server.py`, not eyeballed); the only actual code changes are the closure→`Depends()`/`app.state` conversions `APIRouter` requires (documented per-route) and one real bug the split surfaced: `_STAT_CACHE_GEN` was a plain module-level int incremented via `global` in `mount.py` — imported by value into whichever module read it, that copy would freeze at 0 forever (a rebind via `global` only updates the defining module's own `__dict__`), silently breaking the `/api/fs/stat` TOCTOU race guard (caught by `test_midflight_invalidation_is_not_refilled`, not by inspection). Fixed by reading it live through `mount._STAT_CACHE_GEN` (module-qualified) at the two call sites in `fs_read.py` instead of an imported bare name. Rejected: re-export shims in `server/__init__.py` to keep old `server.X`/`monkeypatch.setattr(server, "X", ...)` call sites working unchanged — tests were retargeted to wherever their code actually lives instead, since a shim would have quietly reintroduced the exact `_STAT_CACHE_GEN`-style bug for every moved mutable global. Full suite matches the pre-split baseline exactly: 2691 passed, 86 skipped, 1 pre-existing unrelated failure (`test_an_abandoned_merge_closes_the_day_s_files` needs `/proc/self/fd`, Linux-only). |
| D179 | **The `@fused.udf` shim in 22 template files is inert locally and load-bearing once deployed — and 31 sibling files that also define `main()` do not have it.** Recorded, not fixed: this is a deploy-path inconsistency, and an entrypoint change does not belong in a packaging diff. The facts, all verified rather than reasoned: the real `fused` wheel has no `_registered_udfs` and `fused.udf(fn)` creates none, so `engine.py`'s generated epilogue always takes its bare-`main()` bridge and the shims do nothing under this engine. The hosted path is the opposite — `export.py` bundles each `runPython` target and the `fused` wheel's hosting layer serves it through a runner that resolves `_registered_udfs` or `result` **with no bare-main fallback**, so there the shim is the only thing that makes the file callable and a template without one returns null. Both halves were previously written down backwards: the shims were described as dead code that could be deleted, which would have broken every deployed template that has one. What makes this a D177 instance rather than a simple bug is that nothing enforces either side — the shim is a hand-applied convention across 53 files with `main()`, 22 carrying it and 31 not, and no test, lint, or export check can tell a deliberate omission (a `condition.py` gate, a daemon, a worker — never a `runPython` target) from a template that will silently return null when someone deploys it. The follow-up owes two things: a derivation of "is this file a `runPython` target" (`export.py` already computes exactly that for bundling, so it exists), and then either the shim on every such file or a bare-main fallback in the hosted runner — the latter being the real fix, since it retires the shim entirely and makes local and hosted agree on the contract `engine.py` documents. |

*(Bookkeeping: this row landed on `main` as D172, colliding with the interpreter-switch D172 above, which this branch had taken independently on the same day. Renumbered to D178 here — the interpreter row keeps D172 because D170's supersession note and a dozen `engine.py`/test citations point at it, while this row's only citation was `ARCHITECTURE.md`'s tree comment, updated to match.)*

### install-loader spawn window (2026-07-30)

| # | Decision | Choice | Rationale / rejected alternatives |
|---|---|---|---|
| D180 | **A claim and a progress record are two facts about one state, so ONE function answers "is this install in flight": `envinstall.progress()` now reports a claim with no record as a starting install, and `start()`'s join branch returns `progress(key)` instead of a synthetic record of its own** | Reported symptom: opening a `.docx` in the packaged app showed the install screen and then hard-failed with *"Cannot open sample.docx: the installer left no progress record"* while the install itself ran to completion — a manual refresh recovered. Cause: `_claim` is written **before** `_spawn`, and the parent's first `_write` lands only **after** `Popen` returns (a fork/exec of a Python interpreter), so for that whole window "claim present, no record" is what a healthy install looks like. `progress()` only knew about records and answered `None` — "never started" — and `runtime.js` treats a null record as fatal by design (correctly: a silent loader is the failure this flow exists to remove).
