It fired only when the folder had NO manifest, so the likelier half-migrated case — manifest written, one stale block left behind — passed straight through it; it never delivered the guarantee it was justified by. It also refused files that were fine: a block with no `dependencies` key, or one whose only dep is marker-scoped away here, declared nothing yet was still rejected — making a file's fate platform-dependent, passing on Linux and failing on macOS. Against that, this is a pre-release product with no installed base to migrate, so the guidance was buying protection nobody needed. Deleting it retires a permanent transitional branch on the run path and a regex scan of every source file. The cost is real and accepted: a stale header now fails at import with nothing pointing at the rule that changed. |

| D235 | **The CHAT half of this decision is OVERTURNED by D237** — there is one chat template, on both kinds of target, named `claude`; the "directory-only *by binding* — no gate needed, and no new code to get wrong" argument below is void for it, and it now ships a `condition.py`. **The HISTORY half is OVERTURNED TOO (2026-08-13)** — the `history` template is DELETED, its 47 file keys with it, and the per-file timeline is now a section of the folder-scoped `git` view (a commit list scoped to the open target; selecting a commit renders the file at that commit via `GET /api/git/show`, nothing materialised on disk). What survives of this row is the `annotate` deregistration and the 47-key authored-file set, which is now `claude`'s binding alone. `git`'s half stands for a NEW reason and after an interruption: it was widened to every file key once its commit log went away, purely to be reachable while a folder had no mode surface, and is folder-only again now that the preview pane peeks folder rows (SPEC GT-2). The durable argument is not "one story, one mode" but "a working tree is the folder's". *Original decision, kept verbatim below because the reversal and its reason are the record:* **Preview modes split by target KIND: a DIRECTORY offers `claude` (chat) + `git` (repo SCM), a FILE offers `claude_split` (chat with the annotation tools) + `history` (that file's history) — and `annotate` is deregistered everywhere** | Owner's decision, and the goal is the annotation/app_state machinery *while editing a standalone file*: it only ever existed in `claude_split`, which the gate offered for project folders alone, so the one file-side chat (`claude`) was the feature-poor twin — 8 mentions of `annotate`/`shot`/`app_state` in its template against 277, and ~15 agent functions it never had. The second goal was **one template set per kind**: a user should not have to learn why the app view and the explorer show different modes for the same thing. So each of the four moved to exactly one side: `claude` and `git` lost every FILE key (they keep the universal `/` key alone, which makes them directory-only *by binding* — no gate needed, and no new code to get wrong), while `claude_split` and `history` gained the same file keys and KEPT `/`. That last asymmetry is deliberate and is not a hole: their gates already narrow `/` to app + linked-app folders, which is exactly the app-builder view (`App.tsx` `APP_MODES`), so an ordinary folder in the explorer still offers only `claude` + `git`. Rejected: dropping `/` from the pair for a clean dirs/files partition — it would have silently removed the chat and the history from `/apps/<tag>/<name>` and broken app creation, which lands on `?_mode=claude_split` over the app FOLDER (`HomeHero.claudeChatUrl`). The `APP_MODES` pin itself is kept, deliberately: it is a curation of the builder view, not a divergence, now that every mode in it behaves identically to the explorer's. **The file set was reviewed rather than copied**: the union of the keys `git` held (source, config, prose, logs, pages), the keys `claude` held (`.parquet .md .markdown .html .htm`), the image assets `history` already carried (`.svg .png .jpg .jpeg`), plus `.csv .tsv .jsonl .ndjson .ipynb` — 47 keys, one set for both modes, so *wherever a chat is offered a history is too*. Including the record streams **overturns GT-2's exclusion of `.jsonl`/`.ndjson`**: that argument was that a commit log over an append-only stream renders no useful *diff*, which says nothing about chatting or about a version timeline. Deliberately still absent: media, 3D, archives, binary documents (`.pdf .docx .xlsx .pptx`), databases, binary geo, rasters and the compressed tabular compounds. Order was normalized while rebinding — the pair takes `claude`'s slot where it had one, else `git`'s, else sits before the trailing meta-mode, with `history` always immediately after `claude_split` (chat then history) — which also moved `history` ahead of `annotate` on the four keys where it had drifted behind it. **`claude_split`'s left pane renders a FILE in that file's OWN default view** (`/render?path=<template>&_file=<file>`), resolved from `/api/fs/stat` by the same "first non-`conditional` entry" rule the shell applies (`Preview.tsx` `defaultTemplate`), skipping the two chat modes so the pane can never frame the view it lives in. Rejected: naming a template per extension (drifts from the registry the moment a binding changes, and ignores a user override, §16) and `/embed` (nests the target one iframe deeper, out of the annotation layer's reach — the same reason #372 moved the pane to `/render`). Its gate tests `os.path.isfile`, **not `not isdir`**: the loose form also swallows every path that does not exist, which handed a `True` to a nonexistent child of a linked-app folder — caught by `test_app_gates_accept_a_linked_folder`, and the reason "cannot tell" must keep reading as "refuse" (CT-12). **`history` now answers for any file in any work tree, and is READ-ONLY there**: the gate asks git (`rev-parse --is-inside-work-tree` from the file's parent, the `git/condition.py` probe) rather than requiring a fused app, `log` is scoped to that one path (`-- :(literal)<name>`), `snapshot` archives only that file and reports it as `file` (with `entry` only when the file is itself a page, so the view frames anything else through its own default template), and `revert` is **refused by kind** in `history.py` — not merely hidden by the gate — because it writes a commit with the `Fused <apps@fused.io>` identity and resets the working tree, and outside a workspace app the repository is the user's own. That is the linked-app rule (`linked_apps.py`) generalized rather than a new one, and fencing it in the module is what makes the gate's read-only promise true for a hand-crafted call (MD-11, the posture `git/log.py` already takes). App-ness is asked FIRST, so a file inside an app still shows the app's auto-commit timeline rather than being demoted to its own single-file log; app snapshot cache keys are left hashing the dir alone so snapshots already on disk stay reused. `git/condition.py` keeps its file branch even though no binding reaches it now — one stat, and a gate that answers only one of the two shapes is a trap for a future binding. **A file target now gets the app_state disclosure too**, which overturns the previous assertion that the file branch is the plain viewer's prompt verbatim: an un-announced tool never gets called, and a file target now has a pane worth reading back. What the two prompts must NOT share is the *description* of that pane — a folder frames the user's own app, a file frames fused-render's preview OF their file — so the file prompt says whose page it is and that the viewer is never to be edited. **`annotate` is deregistered from all 66 core keys, not deleted**: the tools live in the `claude_split` pane now, but the folder still ships so a user can re-bind it themselves (§16), and a test asserts no core key references it — a stale binding is how a superseded mode keeps showing up. SPEC §7.2, §33 GT-2/GT-3. |

| D236 | **A template whose layout needs width collapses ITSELF with a media query; the shell never offers or withholds a mode on account of how wide the host happens to be** | D235 bound `claude_split` and `history` to 47 file keys as well as the `/` directory key, so two **split-layout** templates now render routinely in hosts they were never written for: the explorer's listing **preview pane** (`PANE_MIN_W = 220`, and its default width is *half* its split container — FS-12), a **Panel pane** dragged freely (§14), and **`/embed`**. Below their layout's floor the two halves squeeze past their minimums and then overflow. **The fix is entirely inside the two template files; the shell was not touched** — no `registry.json` field, no new field on stat's template entries, no frontend filter. Each ships one `@media (max-width: 560px)` block that turns the split into **one view at a time with a toggle**, and the breakpoint is derived from each layout's own arithmetic rather than picked: `claude_split` is `#left` 200 + divider 4 + `#chat` 340 ≈ 544, `history` is `#side` 200 + divider 4 + 320 for a still-usable preview frame = 524, both rounded up to **560**, the number this codebase already uses (`duckdb`/`sqlite` 560, `bundle` 640, `map` 650, `log_studio` 780) — plain media queries in a template are the house idiom, not a new mechanism. **Which view leads differs, and follows what the mode is FOR**: `claude_split` opens on the **chat** (that is the mode's subject; the preview is what it is about), `history` opens on the **commit list** (a snapshot has to be picked before there is anything to preview), and in `history` clicking a commit row reveals the preview — placed in the **row click handler, not `select()`**, because `loadLog()` auto-selects the newest commit at boot and would otherwise open the narrow view on a preview with the timeline hidden. **The hidden half of `claude_split` is PARKED, not `display: none`d** (`position: absolute; inset: 0; visibility: hidden; pointer-events: none`): an iframe with no layout box gives its document a **0×0 viewport**, which would make `shotPane` rasterise a 1×1 image (D208) and collapse every element rect the annotation pins are anchored to (§17), so a `display: none` would have converted a hidden pane into two silently broken features. **The inline `width: <split>%` is neutralised differently in the two files, and the difference is a rule, not drift**: `claude_split` carries a hard no-force-flag rule (D146) that a test enforces file-wide, so an inline width there cannot be beaten from CSS and `applySplit()` instead **clears it while narrow and rewrites it from the param above the breakpoint** (the `split` param is never touched, so crossing back restores the user's own ratio with no reload); `history` has no such rule, so `#side` simply gets `width: 100% !important` and `applySplit()` stays unconditional and stateless. `history`'s narrow view is a **body class only** (`body.narrow-preview`, read exclusively from inside the media block and reset on the way back out to wide) — deliberately not a param, since which of two halves a temporarily-narrow host is showing is not state a bookmark should reproduce; `claude_split`'s is the pane-local **`paneview`** param, because there the flip also arms and disarms things. **In the chat-only view a control that acts on the hidden half is ABSENT, not disabled** — `#annbtn`, `#leftmode` and the composer's `.viewshot` pill: a disabled control still claims space and still asserts the feature exists, which is a worse lie than not offering it. `.viewshot` is the interesting one because it still **works** from there (the parked column has a real viewport), so it is hidden under the stronger rule — *a view that shows no preview offers no features of the preview* — rather than because it is dead. Flipping away from Preview also **resets both armed states** (`annSetMode(false)`, `setViewShot(false)`): an armed annotate mode with no visible frame swallows clicks in a document the user cannot see, and an armed shot silently attaches a picture of something invisible to the next message. Two things stay reachable from both views: the **Chat ⇄ Preview toggle** (it is the navigation between the views, not a feature of either) and the **annotation chips** (they are chat content — the user's own words, about to be sent). Shipped in the same change: a **left-pane view picker** for `claude_split` (a pane-local **`leftmode`** param + a `<select>` in the `#anntools` strip, sourced from the single `/api/fs/stat` call the pane already makes, hidden below two offerable views, unknown values falling back silently per PT-9) — the file's left view was previously whatever stat ranked first with no way to change it, and a narrow host makes "which one view am I looking at" a question worth answering. **What was REJECTED: having the shell filter split-layout modes out of narrow hosts.** (a) A pane's width is **dynamic** — the listing pane defaults to half its container, so on a wide window the split fits perfectly and the mode *should* be offered; a host-based ban is simply wrong there. (b) A width-based filter makes modes **appear and disappear from the switcher during a divider drag**, and can yank the **active** mode out from under the user. (c) It needs **per-template width knowledge in the shell** — a new `registry.json` field plus a new field on stat's template entries, which today carry only `mode`/`path`/`icon`/`conditional` (PT-8) — and it would then have to be applied in three separate hosts (`ListingPreviewPane.tsx`, `PaneModeMenu.tsx`, `/embed`). (d) **User templates (§16) would never get it**, whereas a media query in the template works for them for free. SPEC §7.2 PT-15. *(Follow-up, 2026-08-08: every `claude_split` in this row now reads `claude` on disk — D237, later on this same branch, deleted the plain full-width chat template and renamed this one into its place. Nothing decided here changed: the breakpoint arithmetic, the parked hidden half, the `paneview`/`leftmode` params and the absent-not-disabled rule are all the same file. The name is the only edit, and it is recorded rather than applied because the row's reasoning is dated to when there were two chat templates and the `claude_split` spelling is what made "which of the two" answerable.)* *(Amendment, 2026-08-08, same branch: **the breakpoint is now 880 px in both templates, raised from the 560 above.** The mechanism, the parked half, the `paneview`/`leftmode` params and the absent-not-disabled rule are all unchanged — only the number, and the reason it was wrong is instructive. 560 was derived from each layout's **overflow floor** (`claude` 200 + 4 + 340 ≈ 544, `history` 200 + 4 + 320 = 524, both rounded up), i.e. the width below which the halves visibly break. But this row's own strongest argument — that the listing preview pane defaults to **half** its split container — is exactly what makes that floor the wrong one to pick: half of a 1700 px window is ~700 px (measured: 733), comfortably above 560, so the split engaged in a pane that could hold it without breaking and could not hold it **usefully**, and the user's report was "the split view in preview mode feels odd, it needs to be triggered only when we have more screen space". The new figures come from the columns' minimum **useful** widths: `claude` = `#left` 420 (a framed preview that is still a page) + divider 4 + `#chat` 440 (~60 characters of transcript prose) = 864; `history` = `#side` 200 + divider 4 + a **640** px preview frame (the `bundle` family number — the narrowest frame in which the FRAMED template still renders its own wide layout, where the old 320 was a viewport that template had already collapsed itself for) = 844. Both round up to **880**, so the two split-layout templates collapse together, and each file states its sum in the comment beside the query. Two related fixes shipped alongside: the composer placeholder is now **derived from the target kind** instead of hardcoding "this project" (PT-16); and the chat's `/embed` pane URL gained **`modechip=false`** next to `preview=false`, because the embed's corner mode chip (`Preview.tsx` `.preview-browse-chip`, pinned top-right under `body.embed`) read **"Chat"** — a directory's counterpart mode is this chat — and so offered a **second agent and composer nested inside the first one's pane**, the same nesting `preview=false` already closes, one affordance over. It was invisible to source review: the template's own `#viewbtn` is `display: none` outside the media block and provably stays that way, so only the rendered DOM showed a "Chat" button beside a visible split. PT-15/PT-16 amended in place.)* *(Amendment, 2026-08-13: **the `history` half of this row no longer exists** — `templates/history/` is deleted (see D243) and the per-file timeline moved into the folder-scoped `git` view, so `claude` is the only built-in still carrying the 880 px block and the 844 px sum quoted above describes a template that is gone. The RULE is untouched and is the durable part: a template whose layout needs width collapses itself with a media query, and the shell never offers or withholds a mode on account of the host's width.)* |

| D237 | **Gap (2) below — the ordinary folder's left pane — is OVERTURNED by D239, and the pane half of gap (3) with it: an ordinary folder now gets NO pane and a full-width chat, and its system prompt says nothing about a pane. The DELETE, the rename, the gate (1) and the app-folder/file halves of (3) all stand; the paragraphs below are kept verbatim because the reversal and its reason are the record.** **The two chat templates become ONE — the plain full-width `templates/claude/` is DELETED, `claude_split` absorbs the directory case, and is then renamed to `claude` — which overturns the chat half of D235, including its "directory-only *by binding* — no gate needed, and no new code to get wrong" argument** | **The chat half of D235 is overturned, on the owner's decision.** D235 read the two chat templates as *peers* and gave each one kind of target: a directory got `claude`, a file got `claude_split`. They were not peers. `claude` was the **stale side of a fork**: 21 of its 34 shared `agent.py` functions were byte-identical with `claude_split`'s (its `template.html` a 1:1 fork by its own admission), it had exactly one unique function, and it had missed **six** improvements the other side received — retry/529-overload reporting (an overloaded API read as `Thinking…` with a frozen token count, indistinguishable from a hang), message queueing during a live run (#414), the `_defaults` last-used model/effort scan, skill-invocation log rows, `_commit_turn`'s legacy-sidecar exclude sweep, and app-state-aware cancel. A fork that only ever falls further behind is not a mode, it is a bug with a registry key. Its only unique *feature* was the receiving half of `annotate`'s comment handoff, and that was already unreachable code — D235 left `annotate` with zero registry keys. So it went, and `claude_split` — split layout, annotation tools, app_state channel, screenshots, message queueing — became the single chat template for both kinds of target. **One decision, not two, deliberately.** The delete and the `git mv claude_split → claude` are one act, in that order, and splitting them into two rows would separate a cause from its own consequence: the rename is only *defensible* because there is one chat left (a name saying "split" is redundant when nothing is unsplit, and `claude_split` was becoming the switcher's most-seen label, D236), and the rename is also what silently **repaired** the `?_mode=claude` links in `examples_seed/tutorial/claude.html` and `annotations.html` that D235 had broken — the same file keys the deleted template lost are the ones the survivor now holds. Recording the delete alone would leave those repairs unexplained. **Three gaps had to close before the delete was honest, because the survivor's gate and pane assumed an app folder.** (1) **The gate.** `claude/condition.py` (formerly `claude_split`'s) now accepts **any existing file and any existing directory** — the `<workspace>/<tag>/<project>` + linked-app narrowing is gone, and with it D235's claim that the folder chat needs no gate at all. It was **not deleted**, because one refusal is real: `is_mount_backed`. That makes this a **capability deliberately REMOVED**, not an oversight — the deleted template shipped no `condition.py` whatsoever and therefore *did* offer a chat over an rclone/NFS mount, and an agent turned loose on a mount walks and rewrites the tree through FUSE, which is the same reason every peer gate (`app`, `history`, `git`) refuses those paths. Rejected: deleting the gate to restore the old reach (it would re-open exactly the hole the peers close), and keeping the app-folder narrowing (which would have left ordinary folders with no chat at all — the very capability the delete was meant to preserve). (2) **The left pane for an ordinary folder.** *(OVERTURNED by D239 — this pane is removed, not replaced: see there for why closing the throw was the wrong fix for the wrong problem.)* It used to `throw` (`no app entry…`), which would have put a permanent error panel beside a working chat on every non-app folder. Now an **app folder** frames its app entry as before, and a folder with **no** app entry frames **`/explorer/embed/<dir>`** — the chrome-free navigable shell (LM-4/D39), i.e. a real file browser beside the chat, so "what's in here?" is answerable by looking and the user can walk into a file mid-conversation. D235 rejected `/embed` for a FILE target because it nests the target one iframe deeper, out of the annotation layer's reach; that objection is about a target the annotation layer *points at*, and a folder listing is not one — there is no element of a file browser a pin could mean anything about. **The corollary is enforced, not merely documented**: `annSetMode` refuses to arm whenever the pane is the embedded listing, guarded at the *one* function rather than at each caller (arming has four entry points — the button, the boot default, the `annmode` param, the app menu's exit action — and `annmode` defaults ON while the pane resolves asynchronously, so a check per caller is a check the fifth caller forgets), and the button is hidden outright. (3) **The system prompt.** `_split_system_prompt` branches on `shared/app_entry.entry_html` — literally the same rule the pane uses, so the prompt and the pane cannot disagree about what is on screen — and is decided **per run, not cached**, so a folder being scaffolded into starts being described as a project the moment it is one. Three shapes: **app folder** (previous wording — "this is a fused-render project, its HTML is an app fused-render serves"), **ordinary folder** (the deleted template's folder-scoping paragraph, ported *verbatim* rather than reinvented, plus an honest pane description: the pane is fused-render's own file browser, never to be edited, and `app_state` reports the BROWSER, not the folder), and **file** (unchanged). The app_state disclosure is in all three, for D235's reason — an un-announced tool never gets called. **Accepted breaks and fallout.** An old bookmark carrying **`?_mode=claude_split`** now falls through PT-9's forgiving unknown-mode fallback to the target's default view, silently dropping any `session_id`/`run` beside it: accepted, not mitigated (no alias, no redirect — a rename that leaves a compatibility shim behind has not finished). **No data loss anywhere**, because nothing on disk derives from the template folder name: run dirs and transcripts hang off the hard-coded `fused_render_claude-<uid>` literal, `SHOTS` derives from that, the sidecar maps the *target's* path under `home_dir()/sidecar/` (D205), and the permission server's directories arrive as argv. The legacy co-located sidecar filename **`.claude-split.json`** is deliberately NOT renamed — it exists on users' disks, so `app_git.py`'s `_GITIGNORE` and the mirrored `.git/info/exclude` still name it (see the note at `app_git.py:60`). Two **dead links** in `history/template.html` were fixed as part of this: session resume now navigates to `_mode=claude` (it had pointed at a mode files lost in D235, so PT-9 silently dropped the `session_id` — a "resume this session" row that opens the wrong view is the one thing it must never do), and the comment row — which pointed at the deregistered `annotate` — is now **inert**, deliberately not retargeted at the chat, which has no `comment` param and never grew the handoff's receiving half. A new test extracts every `_mode=` literal from that template and resolves it against the real registry for its own target kind, so this bug class now fails loudly instead of degrading into a wrong-but-plausible navigation. `reader/template.html`'s `META` exclusion set (the modes that are not a view *of the file*) had to name `claude` — the survivor is bound to nearly every key `reader` is, so leaving it out offered "read the chat aloud" in reader's own view picker. And `annotate`'s Send-to-Claude handoff is now unreachable for **two independent reasons** — no registry binding *and* no receiver (`claudeComments`/`claudeReturn` never existed in the surviving template) — so its in-template warning was rewritten to say so; the anchor model and review UI are left standing because they are what is worth keeping if the mode ever comes back. SPEC §7.2 PT-14/PT-16, §17, §24 HV-7/HV-8, §33 GT-2. |
| D238 | **The listing preview pane's SELF target (nothing selected) defaults to a neutral placeholder, not to the folder's first opt-in mode — as a STATE (`activePaneMode` → `null`), not as a selectable mode** | *Revised: the diagnosis and the "defaults to nothing" conclusion stand; the `_none`-entry mechanism is **overturned** by the owner. Making "nothing previewed" a real list entry put **"No preview" in the mode menu as something to pick**, and it never was a view: the gesture for "I don't want a preview" is closing the pane. So `PANE_NONE_MODE`, its struck-eye icon and `paneHasRealMode` are deleted, `paneModeList` returns **real modes only** (and an **empty** list for a self target whose every peer is gate-denied), and the no-default rule moves into `activePaneMode`, which returns **`null`** for a self target unless the lead is the folder's own app (`_app`, or the registry `app` hoisted in its place — `hasApp`, the same lone-app probe the list takes, is what distinguishes "this folder HAS an app" from "`app` merely leads because `_listing` was dropped"). `ListingPreviewPane` renders the existing `.pane-hint` empty state for `activeMode === null`, under the ordinary header. Of the three fall-outs claimed below: (a) is answered in the shared control instead — `ModeMenu`'s "one mode is not a choice" rule holds because that one mode is the active one, so it now renders a lone entry when **nothing** is active, which keeps a `claude`-only folder's chat reachable (PT-10); (b) is void — the state is the pane's resting position, so there is nothing to return *to*, and the reverse gesture is closing the pane; (c) cost a `_none` value round-tripping through `_panelMode` and a mode menu entry that highlighted "no preview" as the current view, which is the defect. A **byte-level consequence, deliberately taken**: `platform/lib/mode-name.ts` no longer needs a `_none` label, so this branch leaves that upstream module untouched.* Original entry follows. Toggling the pane on with nothing selected opened the **chat**: an "Ask Claude about this file…" box and a sessions skeleton as a folder's *default preview*. The mechanism was a two-line interaction, not a bug in either half. The self row (the folder already open on the left) deliberately **drops `_listing`** — re-showing the left half in the right half is redundant — and D235/D237 put `claude` on the universal `/` key right after `_listing`, so with `_listing` gone `claude` simply *was* `modes[0]`, and `modes[0]` is the default. **The drop is kept; the default is what changed.** Rejected: pushing `_listing` back for the self row (it restores the redundancy the drop exists to avoid) and filtering the chat out of the self list (it removes a mode the user may well want on the folder they are in, and PT-15/D236 already settled that the shell does not withhold modes for host-shaped reasons). **The fix is a mode, not a branch**: a pane-only `_none` sentinel ("No preview" — the same `Select a file to preview.` hint the empty pane already had) is pushed ahead of every template mode for a self target, so the existing "first = default" rule produces the neutral landing with no exception written into it. The one thing still ranked above it is a lone-app folder's `_app`: an app folder's *own app* is a preview of that folder, not a tool aimed at it, so the fix is deliberately narrowed to the modes that were surprising. Three things fall out of making it a real entry rather than a `null` default, and they are the reason for the shape: (a) the shared mode menu (`BarMenu`'s `ModeMenu`) renders only at **≥2 entries** (PT-10), and an ordinary folder's self list is often `claude` alone — with a `null` default the header would have shown one lone chat button or, worse, no switcher at all, i.e. a neutral pane with the other modes **unreachable**; (b) the choice becomes **reversible** — a mis-clicked chat has a way back, which a "default is nothing" branch cannot offer; (c) the active-mode highlight, the `_panelMode` round-trip and PT-9's unknown-value fallback all work on it for free (`_panelMode=_none` carried onto a *selected* row is simply a mode that row does not offer, so it lands on that row's own default). The neutral branch **keeps the header**, which the pre-existing `modes.length === 0` early-return did not — that return is now reached only when `_none` is genuinely alone (nothing to switch to, so nothing to keep a header for). The ordering rules moved out of `ListingPreviewPane.tsx` into pure functions in **`listing/pane-modes.ts`** (`paneModeList` / `activePaneMode`) so the default is testable without mounting the pane; the component kept icons, fetching and the `gatesPending` skeleton hold unchanged. **`PaneModeMenu` and `/embed` were checked and deliberately not touched**: they resolve a default for an *explicit* path with no self concept, so `_listing` is present and first there and a directory already defaults to its listing — the surprise was only ever the self row's dropped `_listing`. SPEC §6 FS-11. *(Superseded, owner: the self target now has **no mode picker at all**, so it also has no default to argue about. The header shows the folder's icon, its name and its own primary action; the body is always the hint. Everything this entry decided about the self row is deleted rather than left inert — `paneModeList` no longer takes `self` (so `_listing` is never dropped, since only a SELECTED row reaches it) and `activePaneMode` is back to plain first-wins with an override. The reason the picker had to go is the one this entry already identified and then tried to route around: the peers under `/` are heavyweight opt-ins, so the control could only ever offer a chat from a header whose body says "select something" — a `Choose view` chip pointing at a view nobody came for. Fall-out (a) above is void with no menu; (b) and (c) were about reversing a choice that can no longer be made. See FS-11, FS-16.)* |
| D239 | **An ordinary folder's chat gets NO left pane — full-screen conversation — overturning D237's gap (2); the `app_state` tool is withheld where there is no pane to report on; and the narrow-layout view toggle is renamed "Annotate preview" / "Back to chat"** | **D237 gap (2) is overturned, and the reason is that it fixed the wrong thing.** That gap was written as the repair for code that `throw`ew (`no app entry…`) and left a permanent error panel beside a working chat, and the repair chosen was to frame `/explorer/embed/<dir>?preview=false&modechip=false` — fused-render's own file browser — so "what's in here?" was answerable by looking. The throw was a real defect and that did fix it. The real problem was never the throw: **nothing flowed back from that pane.** `claude/template.html` has no `postMessage` and no message listener, so selecting a file in the browser attached nothing, fed nothing to the composer and changed no agent context; annotate was hard-disabled over it by construction (`annSetMode` refused to arm, the button was hidden — gap (2)'s own "enforced, not documented" corollary is the admission), and the `leftmode` picker was inert for it because neither directory branch populates `paneEntries`. So half the width of a folder chat went to a view that reported to nobody, answering a question the agent's ordinary file tools answer directly. The pane is therefore **removed**, not replaced: an ordinary folder (`shared/app_entry.entry_html` resolves nothing — the same predicate the prompt reads, unchanged) gets no `#leftframe`, no divider, no view toggle, no pane-shot pill, no annotate, and the conversation owns the width. **A file and an app folder are untouched** — split, annotate, pane screenshot, `app_state`, the 880 px collapse, the live-reload loop after an edit — and `entry_html` stays the single shared predicate, so a two-page folder or an app at `sub/index.html` reads as an ordinary folder and gets the full-width chat; that is intended, not a gap. **Deliberately given up:** the `state.url` backchannel. Embed navigation rewrote the iframe's path, so `app_state` could tell the agent which folder or file the user had walked into — the one signal that did flow back, and it goes with the pane. **The mechanism is a designed absence, not a missing element**, and that distinction is the whole implementation risk: the frame's `load` hook is wired at top level, so shipping the markup without `#leftframe` throws a `TypeError` there and aborts *every declaration after it* (the agent poll loop, the pane-shot toggle, the composer wiring), and the boot `catch` cannot even report that, because its own first statement removes the same missing element — the throw lands inside the catch and neither the error panel nor `pushAppLog` runs, leaving a blank page with a working-looking composer. So the markup ships the column unchanged, every declaration initialises against it, and `enterNoPane()` removes `#left`, `#divider`, `#anntools` (a child of `#chat`, so it survives removing the column and would otherwise be an empty bordered row of controls for a pane that is not there) and both pane-shot pills **afterwards** — ordering that is guaranteed rather than hoped for, since the template is one `<script>` and the loader reaches that branch only after `await`ing a fetch. A single `noPane` flag then short-circuits `applySplit`, `applyNarrowView`, `renderAnn` and `annSetMode`; `paneEmbedded` is deleted rather than repurposed, since the condition it guarded is now impossible by construction. **Rejected:** hiding the column with CSS and leaving it in the document — the elements stay live, `shotPane` still rasterises them, the hidden controls stay focusable, and a hidden pane is a pane; and guarding each top-level statement that touches the column, which is the shape whose fifth forgotten guard is the bug. **`app_state` is withheld where there is no pane**, and the switch is the CHANNEL's own existence rather than a second flag: `agent.py` spawns `permission_server.py` with the app-state directory only when `_has_pane`, and the server keys both `tools/list` and its dispatch on having that directory, so the roster cannot advertise a tool the server cannot serve. The spawn line's pre-allowance drops with it (pre-allowing a name nothing can call is a rule about nothing); the screenshot directory's `Read` rule stays, being a rule about a directory rather than a claim that this target can annotate. The tool roster varying by target kind is accepted. `_split_system_prompt`'s ordinary-folder branch loses the whole pane paragraph — the file-browser description and "it reports the BROWSER, not the folder" — keeping only the folder-scoping instruction: a prompt that tells the model what the user can see beside the conversation, when there is nothing there, is a false claim about the screen, and announcing a tool the roster does not carry is worse than not announcing one. **Rejected:** offering the tool and answering with an explanatory error (the model calls it after every edit and burns the 20-second app-state timeout learning the same thing once per turn). **Two cleanliness consequences, both of which were live wrong answers waiting to happen:** `appEntry` is write-once and never cleared, and `entry` is the only field in the app-state payload that distinguishes the user's real app from our own UI, so the no-pane path never sets it; and `APP_STATE_UNREADABLE` enumerated causes including "a project may have no app entry", which is now precisely the condition that produces no pane and therefore no tool to ask — that clause is deleted rather than left to explain a structural absence as a failed read. **`?modechip=false` loses its only producer in the codebase**, so its plumbing is removed from its consumer too (`Preview.tsx` no longer reads it; the corner chip has no opt-out). Rejected: keeping the read as tolerance for a caller that no longer exists — a branch nothing can produce is a branch nothing can test, and if another template ever frames an embed of its own counterpart's target the opt-out returns with that caller. `preview=false` is deliberately KEPT: the listing writes it for itself when the user closes the pane (`listing/pane.ts`), so it still has a producer and still means something. With the folder embed gone, `/embed` is no longer used as a pane by any template, so D235's rejection of it for a FILE target stands unqualified. **Stale params are ignored silently and never stripped** — `split`, `paneview`, `leftmode`, `annmode`, `annotations` on a folder URL open a full-screen chat with no error (PT-9's posture for an unknown `_mode`); rewriting them would break the bookmark's round trip for the day that folder grows an `index.html` and gets its pane back. **The view toggle is renamed** "Preview"/"Chat" → **"Annotate preview"/"Back to chat"**, with the `aria-label` as the same single string: the old labels named the two halves and said nothing about why anyone would move between them, and the preview column is where the annotation tools live — the only reason to leave the conversation for it. It stays NAVIGATION and is **not merged** with the annotate switch, which arms the mode once you are there: one control moves the view, the other changes what a click in the frame does, and one button doing both would arm a mode in the same gesture that reveals the surface. The longer labels are why `#viewbtn` gets `flex-shrink: 0` and the annotate switch gets an ellipsis on its own label, so a 220 px host truncates the mode name rather than overflowing the row or half-hiding the only way back; no CSS force flag was introduced (D146). **Fallout found while in there and fixed with it:** the composer's footnote ("files in this **project**") and the annotation block's preamble ("left preview of this **project's** entry HTML") were both still hardcoded to the app-folder noun after D237 made only the placeholder kind-derived, so a chat about `notes.md` described the wrong kind of thing to the user and to the model; all three now read one writer (`setTargetNoun`) and a test asserts no kind noun is hardcoded in the markup. SPEC PT-14/PT-15/PT-16. |
| D240 | **A freshly opened folder auto-selects its FIRST ENTRY — file or directory — overturning FS-16's original skip-directories rule (`firstFilePath` → `firstEntryPath`)** | FS-16's original rule skipped directories on the argument that a folder's preview would "make opening a folder feel like it had navigated somewhere." That argument mistook the pane's directory case for a navigation: selecting a directory shows a **peek** (FS-11's single-app embed or read-only mini child list), the left half stays put, and nothing about the URL or the open folder changes — so landing on a directory is exactly as harmless as landing on a file. Meanwhile the skip had a visible cost with the default dirs-first sort: the highlighted row was NOT the top row the eye lands on but some file below the directory block, and a folder holding only directories auto-selected nothing at all, opening the pane on the `Select a file to preview.` hint it exists to avoid. So `firstEntryPath` returns the first RENDERED row outright; only an **empty folder** keeps the self target. The `byPath` map parameter survives solely as the "is this path actually on screen" check (its `isDir` reading is deleted, so the signature loosens to `ReadonlyMap<string, unknown>`), and the one-shot TIMING in `Listing.tsx` is untouched — this changes only the answer, not when it is asked. Fallout in prose that leaned on "picks a file": the self-target-is-rare arguments in Preview.tsx / ListingPreviewPane.tsx / FS-11 now rest on the folder being **non-empty**, which is the weaker premise they always needed. SPEC §6 FS-16. **Amendment: the shot YIELDS to a selection that already exists** — "takes no reading of the selection" was about the removed `?sel` URL claim, never about the user, and a row clicked in the provisional scaffold during a slow mount (carried over by `recallSelection`) was being overwritten by row one the moment the resolved listing settled. `selectionClaimed(sel)` in the effect spends the shot and stands down; `autoSelectPath` stays pure, since this is a condition on WHEN to ask, which is the effect's half of the split. |
| D241 | **The `git` view's commit box gets an AI "write this message" sparkle (`fused.ai`), fed by a new bounded READ op in `log.py`** | VS Code's parity feature, and the one thing in this view that is expensive to type. Three things it deliberately is not. It is **not a commit**: it fills the textarea and stops, so the user still reads what will be recorded and still presses ✓ Commit (or Ctrl+Enter) — an AI that both writes and commits removes the only review step there is. It is **not in `ops.py`**: the diff it reads (`op="pending"`) forks `git diff` and touches no ref, index or file, so it belongs with the safe reads; routing a read through the write module's confirmation machinery would say it can lose work. And it is **not the pane's diff budget** — the prompt cap is `MAX_PROMPT_DIFF_BYTES` 80 KB / 1 500 lines against the pane's 400 KB / 3 000, because a diff that is merely long for a human is billed per token and summarises worse, and the truncation is reported so the prompt can say the diff was cut rather than let the model describe a change it half saw. What it reads mirrors GT-14: the staged diff **unscoped**, because `git commit` records the whole index wherever it lives, and a message written from a scoped diff would describe less than the commit makes; with nothing staged at all it falls back to the working-tree changes under the open scope (VS Code's own fallback), untracked files named rather than diffed. Streamed via `onChunk` at `effort: "low"` — a commit message is not a thinking problem — into the same `msg` param the textarea already round-trips, which is what lets a mid-stream repaint pick up where it left off. Failure is a `flash` like any git refusal, and `ai_unavailable` reads as "AI is not available on this server", never a traceback. SPEC §33 GT-18. |
| D242 | **The TEMPLATE this row is about is REMOVED (2026-08-13) — `templates/history/` is deleted and the per-file timeline is now the folder-scoped `git` view's Commits section. The MECHANISM stands where that section inherited it: `git/log.py` bounds its log with `--skip`/`--max-count=limit + 1`, keeps the extra record back as the `has_more` probe, and the view grows the list with a "load more" row rather than page numbers — plus a `capped` signal the row below did not have, so a clamped request cannot offer an endless "load more" that never advances. Everything below about page numbers, date cursors and the append-only pager is the reasoning that section runs on; `history.py::PAGE_SIZE` and its `rev` deep link are gone with the template.** *Original form:* **The `history` timeline is PAGED — 20 commits a page, `--skip=N --max-count=21` with the 21st row kept back as the `more` probe — and the view grows it with one quiet "Show older" row rather than page numbers** | `_log` ran `git log` with no bound. That was tolerable only while an app's own auto-commit history was the whole story; D235 widened the target rule to a FILE or an ordinary DIRECTORY in the **user's own repository**, where the subtree can carry decades of commits — all of them formatted, JSON-encoded and shipped across `/api/run` to fill a spine whose first screen is twenty rows. The page size lives in exactly one place (`history.py::PAGE_SIZE`); the view asks for the next page by `skip` alone and reads `more` off the payload, so the two halves cannot disagree about where a page ends. **The +1 probe rather than a second `rev-list --count`:** one git process instead of two, and at exactly PAGE_SIZE commits there is no overflow row, so "show older" is never offered for a page that would come back empty. **Skip/limit rather than a `before`/date cursor:** the log is newest-first by git's own default and pages are only ever APPENDED, so the window only shifts when a commit lands on HEAD mid-paging — one repeated row, deduped by sha in the view, and the only action that can add one (`revert`) reloads from the first page anyway, the new commit being the newest. **No page numbers and no "back":** a timeline is append-only, so the pager is one row at the FOOT of the spine, styled as chrome rather than as a commit, carrying its own loading state and its own error line — a failed page is reported where it was asked for and leaves the framed snapshot alone. The append repaints the whole list (selection, the `latest` badge and the pager row are all derived from one state, and one writer cannot leave them disagreeing) but saves and restores the scroll offset, so the new rows appear below the reader rather than under them. **The one selection consequence:** a `rev` deep link may name a revision that is not on the first page, so the prefix is selected as given (the backend resolves it) instead of silently falling back to HEAD, and the view adopts the full sha the snapshot returns so the row highlights the moment paging reaches it; a junk `rev` still falls back, being shape-checked first. SPEC §7.2 PT-14. |
| D243 | **REMOVED (2026-08-13) — the mode this row gave the word to is itself DELETED. `templates/history/` (the git commit spine: log, snapshot, revert) is gone, bound to zero keys, and the question it answered — what did THIS file look like at THIS commit — is now a section of the folder-only `git` view (SPEC §33): a commit list scoped to the open target, and selecting a commit renders the open file as of that commit through `GET /api/git/show`, resolved on read out of the object database. Both halves of the "two things deliberately do NOT follow the rename" note are moot with it: nothing writes `~/.fused-render/app-versions/<key>/<sha>/` any more — no `git archive` runs and no tree is materialised, so the `?snapshot=1`/`IS_SNAPSHOT` framing has no producer left, though both it and `mount.py`'s read-only guard over that prefix STAY, because trees an older version already extracted are still browsable by URL, and `?_mode=versions` and `?_mode=history` alike now fall back to the target's default view, which is still PT-9's ordinary behaviour. The mode-label collision guard this row cites (`mode-labels.test.ts`) stays — it catches the shape of the bug, not this pair.** *Original form:* **The `versions` mode is renamed `history`, and the template that already held that name — the sidecar inspector of D96 — is DELETED rather than renamed around** | Two modes were reading "History" on the same keys. `versions` (the git commit spine: log, snapshot, revert — §33/PT-14) is what a user means by a file's history, and `versions` is not what they call it; the D96 template (`.*.json` default, appended to `.html`/`.parquet`) was a read-only merged timeline of the `<ext>.json` sidecar — claude sessions, bookmark history, the last-session card, annotate comments — and by D237 its one interactive affordance, the comment deep link, was already removed, leaving a viewer for a store the surfaces that own it already display in context. So the word goes to the mode that earns it and the other view goes away: `templates/versions/` → `templates/history/` (`versions.py` → `history.py`), `templates/history/` deleted, `".*.json"` falls back to `["tree", "code"]`, and `.html`/`.parquet` lose the duplicate entry while keeping the surviving mode in the slot `versions` held. The collision this closes is the one `frontend/src/apps/explorer/listing/mode-labels.test.ts` was written for; that guard stays, because it catches the shape of the bug, not this pair. **Two things deliberately do NOT follow the rename.** The snapshot cache `~/.fused-render/app-versions/<key>/<sha>/` keeps its spelling — it is a materialisation directory no user sees or types, and renaming it would strand every snapshot already extracted on disk behind a prefix nothing looks under (`server/mount.py`'s prefix check is the other half; both ends carry a comment saying so). And no URL alias is shipped for `?_mode=versions`: a stale deep link falls back to the target's default view, which is PT-9's ordinary behaviour, and a permanent alias would keep the dead token alive in every registry a user forks. SPEC §24 is kept as a tombstone so the HV-* ids other sections cite still resolve. |
| D244 | **Long-running work reports to a SERVER-side job registry, and the shell draws one download manager for all of it — reporting is decoration the work cannot depend on, and cancel is a request the reporter honors** | Pages that start work outliving their own call (a model download, a checkpoint pull, a minutes-long generation) each drew their own progress bar inside themselves. The shell replaces a page's frame on every navigation, so browsing to another file while an 8GB model downloaded left multi-GB of traffic running with nothing on screen to say so — indistinguishable from a hang, and unstoppable short of quitting the app. Three choices make the fix: **(1) the record lives on the server** (`fused_render/jobs.py`), not in the page, because the reporter and the viewer are different documents — a rendered page is an iframe the shell tears down, and may not even be in the shell's browser tab — so the record has to outlive the document describing it; the same registry then answers a detached Python worker POSTing to `/api/jobs` with no second mechanism. In memory, deliberately: this is the live picture, the call log (§31) is the durable one. **(2) Reporting can never break the work.** `fused.trackJob()` is fire-and-forget end to end: no method rejects, a transport failure is swallowed, and the page downloads its model whether or not a single report lands. The one concession to authoring is a single console warning when the SERVER rejects a report (a bad id, a missing title) — silence there leaves an author with a working page and a manager that never shows it. Reports are serialized per handle because they are deltas and two in flight can land out of order, which walks a bar backwards. **(3) Cancel is a REQUEST.** The server has no idea what the work is or which process is doing it, so the ✕ only sets `cancel_requested`; the page reads it back in the reply to the progress tick it was already sending and stops the way it knows how. The row keeps saying "running / Cancelling…" until the work really stops — flipping it to "cancelled" while a four-minute download carried on underneath would be a lie told to look responsive — and a job that never declared itself cancellable shows no ✕ rather than a dead one. The same honesty rule produces `stalled`: a running record nobody has updated in 30s says *"no longer reporting — the page that started it was closed"*, dimmed, still listed, because the reporter is gone and the work very likely is not. Computed on read (a late tick un-stalls it with no timer), dropped after 10 minutes so a dead reporter cannot wedge the list — and dismissible, which is the same rule as "a live row can only be cancelled, never hidden" rather than an exception to it: nothing is reporting, so the row conceals nothing the app could otherwise tell you. What a dismissal then refuses is precisely a LATE TICK and never a fresh start — a tick is a delta or a terminal state, while the opening report a `fused.trackJob()` handle sends is the only one that states `running` outright, so that report clears the dismissal and re-opens the row. A plain time window was tried first and is worse at both ends: too short and a slow poll loop resurrects the row anyway, too long and a page reusing a stable id (the documented re-attach pattern) is invisible for its next real run. **Accepted cost, recorded as the follow-up:** a job whose page is closed goes stalled rather than continuing to show real progress, because nothing is left to report it. The fix is for the WORKER to report — it already outlives the page — and the HTTP contract is deliberately plain JSON on `FUSED_RENDER_ORIGIN` (which every spawned child inherits) so a worker can do it with stdlib `urllib` today. What is not built is a server-side poller that would adopt a status file and report on the worker's behalf: that needs a scheduler and a mapping language, and it invites runaway subprocesses. Placement follows the notification stack's existing rule rather than opening a new surface: the manager is a card in the bottom-right column (§3), between the toasts and the server card, because that column is ordered by lifetime — seconds, minutes, the session — and anything long-lived must sit below anything short-lived or its rows shift under the pointer whenever a "Path copied" expires. `/api/jobs` joins `/api/calls` in the call log's skip list for the reason that list exists: ~160 ticks per download are bookkeeping about a call, not calls, and they would spend the rate budget the real ones need. `fused.trackJob` is a no-op in the hosted runtime rather than an export blocker like `fused.ai`, since a page that reports progress should still deploy — it just has nowhere to report. |
| D245 | **A manual edit COMMITS NOTHING — the app-repo history is the scaffold commit plus one commit per completed Claude turn, and nothing else; the debounced committer is deleted** | Editor and file-manager mutations through `/api/fs/*` used to be committed for the user: every write, upload, compress, delete, rename and copy inside an app folder marked its repo dirty and a single background worker (`app_commit_queue`) turned each editing burst into a commit, debounced 1s quiet / 5s max because the code editor autosaves every 250ms. It worked, and that is the problem with it: the timeline it produced was a machine's record of *typing*, not a human's record of *changes*. "Edit index.html" thirty times in an afternoon is not history — it is an undo stack with commit hashes, and it buried the commits that DO mean something (the scaffold, and each completed Claude turn, which are bounded units of work with a description someone can read) under an arbitrary number of pause-shaped ones. The `history` spine (D242/D243) and the `git` view (GT-2) both get better by subtraction: the timeline is short enough that paging is about long-lived apps rather than about one session, and the Source Control view finally shows a working tree the user can stage and commit deliberately, instead of one that had already been committed out from under them a second after they stopped typing. **Deleted, not disabled:** `app_commit_queue.py` in full, the startup/shutdown hooks it needed in `server/app.py`, and `fs_mutate._commit_mutation` with its six call sites — the debounce, the serialization, the requeue-on-cancel and the shutdown flush existed ONLY to make automatic commits survive contention with each other, so with the automatic commits gone the whole apparatus is dead weight rather than a switch worth keeping. **`app_git.py` STAYS** and is unchanged: `init_repo` still gives every new app its boilerplate commit (`POST /api/apps/new`), `app_dir_for` is still the scoping rule `projectenv`, `linked_apps` and `history` ask, and `commit()` remains the helper the Claude turn path mirrors (templates/claude/agent.py, D166) — the module's hard app-dir scoping is the reason a Claude turn in a linked folder still writes nothing to the user's own repository. What is **rejected**: (a) keeping the queue behind a preference — a per-user switch here means every downstream surface (the history spine's contents, what `git` shows as uncommitted) has two shapes to reason about, for a behaviour whose whole defence was that the user never had to think about it; (b) coarsening the debounce instead (commit per minute, per session) — that trades a noisy timeline for an arbitrary one, since a five-minute window is no more a unit of work than a one-second one; (c) committing only "significant" mutations (delete, rename) and not writes — the split is not one a user could predict, and half a safety net is worse than none. **The accepted cost:** an editor mistake in an app folder is no longer recoverable from an automatic commit. That is deliberate — the fused-render editor is not the thing standing between a user and their own file, the editor's undo is, and an app whose changes matter is one the user can commit from the `git` view in the same window. |
| D246 | **The claude chat's transcript renders the turn's SEGMENTS — one collapsible chip per tool call (diff, output, todos), a folded thinking block, markdown prose — and `segments` is authoritative wherever it is non-empty, so the flat `text` field is never rendered beside it. Supersedes POC §6 ("only text turns — and approval cards — render")** | The page received one flat string per turn, so everything that was not prose either vanished (an *allowed* tool call streamed past invisibly behind the spinner; approval cards weren't in the transcript at all, so they disappeared on the next reload) or was silently concatenated into the reply as if Claude had typed it. `agent.py::_segments_from_rows` now returns the turn as an ordered text/thinking/tool record and `renderSegments(container, segments, {typer})` is the one renderer for it — used by the live poll, by history restore and by the re-attach repair path, so a turn looks the same whether it is streaming, restored from disk, or recovered after the frame died mid-run. **Segments are authoritative and `text` is NOT rendered as well:** on a run that carried stream deltas the text segments join back to exactly that string, so rendering both prints the reply twice; `text` survives for the error paths (which produce no segments) and for the token estimate, which must keep counting the same bytes it always did. **Tool input and output go in through `textContent`, never `renderMd`/`innerHTML`** — they are bytes off the filesystem, a command's stdout, an MCP server's answer, and markdown-rendering them would put a `<script>` from a grepped file into the page; the probe suite asserts the payload lands as text and reaches no node's innerHTML. Which chips open by default was a per-tool list here (`Edit`/`Write`); **that clause is superseded by D267** — the transcript now opens exactly its most recent card. The `-`/`+` diff formatter is extracted (`formatEditDiff`) and shared with the approval card, so the change you approve and the change you are shown are formatted by one function. **The typer became re-targetable** (`makeTyper(null)` + `retarget(el)`): only a text segment at the very END of the list is still growing, so when a tool call appears after the streaming prose that prose is final — rendered whole, highlighted once, and the typer moves on or parks. Chips update in place on later polls (a status flip must not snap shut a chip the user opened) and their dirty-key deliberately excludes the input, which agent.py reads only from finalized rows and therefore cannot revise mid-turn — keying on it would re-stringify a `Write`'s whole file every 400 ms. **A thinking block is only rendered when it HAS a body.** Whether the reasoning is on the wire at all is model-dependent on the shipping CLI — measured over the real `out.jsonl` files this template wrote during live runs, `claude-haiku-4-5` streams the trace while `claude-sonnet-5` sends `{"type": "thinking_delta", "thinking": "", "estimated_tokens": 50}` and finalizes `{"type": "thinking", "thinking": "", "signature": "…"}`: redacted, with only the token estimate surviving. The two surfaces always agree inside a run, so there is nothing to recover where it is redacted, and a builder that opened a segment per delta produced the shipped bug — a "Thought for a moment" disclosure that unfolded to nothing. So an empty chunk opens no segment and an empty segment is dropped rather than returned, and the finalized `assistant` row's `thinking` block is read whenever no delta carried text — on its own gate, separate from the text one, because that covers every row set `_history` reads (a persisted transcript has no `stream_event` rows at all, so a restored turn's reasoning previously had no source and never appeared). **Kept as-is, and worth revisiting:** an errored run still drops its whole reply bubble (`runEnding`'s `keepText`), which now discards a record of tool calls that really ran, not just half a sentence. |
| D247 | **`AskUserQuestion` is un-disallowed and renders as an ANSWERABLE question card: the answer rides back on the same `allow` as `updatedInput` = the parked input plus a top-level `answers` record keyed by the exact question text. Supersedes D161's `--disallowed-tools AskUserQuestion,ExitPlanMode` (and the POC's "this chat renders neither") — only `ExitPlanMode` stays off** | The tool reaches the prompt tool like any other request (it is gated on `requiresUserInteraction`, so `auto` and `acceptEdits` escalate it to a card rather than auto-approving — measured, not assumed), and `permission_server._permission_result` grew the one branch that makes it answerable: for `tool_name == "AskUserQuestion"` ONLY, the decision file's `answers` is validated and merged over the parked input as `updatedInput`; everywhere else the input still passes through byte-identical, and `updatedPermissions` is not built on that branch at all. `decide` takes an `answers` param — a JSON string, like `app_state`'s `state`, because params cross into python string-shaped — `poll` returns the recorded `answers` alongside each request so a re-attaching frame can show the choice, and `template.html` routes `tool === "AskUserQuestion"` to `buildQuestionCard`: header chip, question text, one control per option (label bold, description small, all `textContent`), a click answering a single question outright and checkboxes/radios + "Send answer" for a multi-select or a multi-question card, collapsing to "✓ You chose: …". A payload with no answerable question shows itself verbatim with a Dismiss button rather than controls that could only send an invalid answer. | **The wire was spiked before any UI was written, because three shapes look right and only one is** (CLI 2.1.226): a plain `allow` reaches the model as `The user did not answer the questions` — a card the user answered and a turn that learnt nothing; a `deny` carrying the text as its message *does* reach the model, but as `is_error: true` filed under `permission_denials`; `answers` nested inside a question object, or as a `[{header, answer}]` list, or with `options` pruned to the winner, is silently dropped and reads as no answer. What works is a flat record keyed by the **exact `question` string** (not `header`) whose value is the chosen option's **`label`**, and for multi-select the chosen labels joined with `", "` — a JSON list also arrives but 2.1.226 joins it with `","`, which fails the CLI's own label check and downgrades the tool_result to the weaker "follow what they actually say" phrasing. `answers`/`annotations`/`response` are declared fields of the tool's *input* schema, recovered from the binary rather than from `--help` — the same "read the binary, not the docs" caveat D161 already carries, and the same thing that can silently come back. Verified end to end through the real CLI with the shipping (unpatched) server: tool_result `Your questions have been answered: "Alpha or Beta?"="Beta"`, `is_error` unset, no denials, the model's reply `VERDICT: Beta` — and again for a multi-select under `--permission-mode auto`. **`updatedInput` is the parked input PLUS the key, never a rebuilt object:** the CLI re-validates it against the tool's schema, so dropping `questions` is a `<tool_use_error>` the model reports as a broken tool, not a silent no-op. **The blocking prerequisite was that `_permission_result` hardcoded `"updatedInput": tool_input`** and never read the decision file — the answer channel could not exist without opening that, which is why it is opened for exactly one tool name and nothing else: this is the first place the PAGE authors model-visible tool input, so anywhere else `answers` would be an arbitrary key added to input the user already approved. **Fail-closed in both writers, and the validator is duplicated on purpose.** `_answers_from` validates against the PARKED REQUEST's own questions and option labels — every value must be a label that request offered for that exact question — and returns None (⇒ deny with a sentence the model can act on) for a non-dict, an unknown or header-keyed question text, a non-string or non-matching label, a joined answer to a single-select, or a question with no usable option; an *omitted* question is allowed, because the CLI reads it as unanswered and that is true. It lives in `agent.py` (validating the click before it is latched) and in `permission_server.py` (validating before the CLI is told), because that server is spawned standalone by the CLI and imports nothing of ours — so, D146, one test runs both copies over one table rather than a comment asking them to agree. Multi-select is matched by CONSTRUCTION (is the value the `", "`-join of a run of labels in option order?) instead of by splitting on `", "`, because a label may itself contain `", "` and splitting would either accept a label the request never offered or reject one it did; the walk is over reachable offsets, so a question with many options cannot cost exponential time. An answerless `allow` on a question is recorded as a deny rather than forwarded — the model must never be told the user did not answer when they clicked. **Never grantable, never escalatable:** `WHOLE_TOOL_GRANTABLE` deliberately did not grow ("allow all AskUserQuestion in this reply" would answer the *next* question without asking) and a `setMode` is dropped by tool name rather than by trusting the card not to send one, since answering a question says nothing about how much to auto-approve. Every latch invariant of D161 is untouched: the expiry path still shows an abandoned question as unanswered, a double-click still renders the answer that WON the latch (the card shows what `decide` read back, not what was clicked), and a failed send re-enables the controls because the subprocess is still blocked. The card is also the disclosure surface — the question, every label and every description go in through `textContent`, whole, and the `answers` record is built with `Object.fromEntries` for the same reason the leftover dump is (a question whose text is `__proto__` would otherwise vanish into the prototype setter). Pinned by bridge tests over the server's own stdio JSON-RPC plus node probes that run the shipping card builder over a stub DOM, and by a real-browser check (Chrome, both themes) for the things a stub cannot show: radio grouping per question, a `<script>` label rendering as inert text, and no horizontal overflow. |
| D248 | **`ExitPlanMode` is un-disallowed and renders as a PLAN card: "Approve plan" sends a plain `allow` (optionally carrying a `setMode` landing mode), "Keep planning" sends a `deny` whose message agent.py composes from a fixed sentence plus the user's own note. A new `plan` permission mode (picker: "Plan first") maps onto `--permission-mode plan`. Supersedes the `ExitPlanMode` half of D161's `--disallowed-tools AskUserQuestion,ExitPlanMode` (D247 already lifted the other half) and the POC's "this chat renders neither"** | `agent.py` drops `ExitPlanMode` from `--disallowed-tools` — and drops the FLAG entirely rather than passing it with no tools left, which the CLI would read as a tool literally named `""`. `PERMISSION_MODES` gains `"plan": "plan"` (`--permission-mode plan`) alongside the existing three, and `template.html`'s picker gains a fourth `<option>` ("plan first") leading the list. That the CLI touches nothing before a plan is parked is ITS OWN enforcement, not ours, and is unverified here against a live headless run (queued for the end-to-end task) — the prompt tool stays wired exactly as in every other mode, so an ordinary permission card can still surface for some other tool while planning and stays fully answerable if one does; `permChoices` additionally never offers its "Allow, and let Claude decide from here" mode-switch on such a card while the run's derived mode is `"plan"`, so an ordinary card cannot become a side door out of plan mode — the plan card itself is the only intended exit. That derived mode (`_live_mode`) LEAVES `"plan"` the moment a plan allow lands — onto the granted `setMode` when the approval carried one, `"prompt"` (the CLI's own default) otherwise, the same landing the picker write-back computes — because by then the CLI has already left plan mode itself; a guard that stayed on for the rest of the run would be suppressing the affordance for a mode nobody was in. A "keep planning" deny moves nothing, and an ordinary allow with no `setMode` still moves nothing: `ExitPlanMode` is the one tool whose bare approval re-points the derived mode, because it is the one the CLI acts on by itself. `buildPermCard` routes `tool === "ExitPlanMode"` to the new `buildPlanCard`, a third kind of card (not an approval, not a question): it renders `input.plan` through `renderMd` + `attachCodeCopy` — the ONE tool-input payload on any card that goes through the markdown funnel rather than `textContent`, safe only because it is genuinely markdown Claude wrote for the user to read, and because nothing else on the card takes that door — with the disclosure rule unchanged (`leftoverInput` prints anything besides `plan`, and a request with no usable `plan` key falls into that dump wholesale rather than implying one was read). "Approve plan" sends `{decision: "allow", mode}`, where `mode` is the picker's CURRENT choice and only when it is in `SWITCHABLE_MODES` — the same validated channel D161's escalation button uses, so `bypassPermissions` is unreachable from this card by construction, not by convention. "Keep planning" sends `{decision: "deny", note}`; `agent.py::_decide` composes the actual deny `message` itself (`KEEP_PLANNING` = "Revise the plan — the user wants changes.", plus `" The user's note: " + note` when a note was typed) and does so ONLY when `tool == PLAN_TOOL`, so a `note` param riding any other card's deny is silently dropped rather than rewriting its message. The note is bounded (`NOTE_LIMIT` = 2000 chars, mirrored on the page as the textarea's own `maxLength` so an honest user never reaches the cut) and stripped to `\n`/`\t`/printable — a pasted file must not become the sentence the model reads, and control characters that are not whitespace have no business in a JSON string headed for the CLI. The cut is never silent (D241's precedent): a note over the limit gets `NOTE_TRUNCATED` (`"\n[note truncated at 2000 chars]"`) appended to the composed message, rather than being quietly shortened with no record that anything was lost. `ExitPlanMode` deliberately stays out of `WHOLE_TOOL_GRANTABLE` (one plan; "allow all" would pre-approve the next, unseen) and answers on it are still refused by the `AskUserQuestion`-only `ANSWERABLE_TOOL` gate, so a plan card cannot be tricked into the answer channel either. | **Spiked before any UI was written** (CLI 2.1.226, same discipline as D247): a plain `{"decision": "allow"}` is sufficient — the CLI leaves plan mode itself the moment it sees one, emitting `system/status permissionMode:"default"`, and the tool_result the model reads back is "User has approved your plan…"; the plan itself rides back through `updatedInput` byte-identical, because this card authors no tool input (unlike `AskUserQuestion`'s `answers` merge). A `setMode` in that same allow is purely optional and changes only which mode the CLI SESSION lands in once planning ends. **The picker is a separate, second thing the approval has to move, and an approval always moves it:** each turn is a fresh `claude -p --resume` (D161), so the picker's `permission` param is what chooses `--permission-mode` for the NEXT spawn — if it stayed on `"plan"` after an approval that sent no `setMode` (picker parked on "plan" or "prompt", where `landing()` correctly sends nothing to the CLI), the very next turn would silently re-enter plan mode and replan forever, with no card ever explaining why. So `buildPlanCard`'s `send` always calls `fused.params.set("permission", …)` on a landed `allow` — to the granted `setMode` when there was one, `"prompt"` (the CLI default) otherwise — and does so off the AUTHORITATIVE read of what landed (`res.decision`), never off the click, so the losing half of a double-click cannot move the picker for an approval that never actually took. "Keep planning" touches the picker not at all. `deny` + a `message` is what keeps the model planning rather than reading a bare refusal and giving up on the task, which is the one reason this card composes a message at all when every other allow/deny on this bridge (bar D247's answer-refusal) does not. **Why the flag is DROPPED, not emptied:** `--disallowed-tools` with nothing left in it is not "disallow nothing", it is a tool named `""` on the CLI's own parsing — the same class of bug the empty-list case would have reintroduced silently. **Why `plan` leads the picker rather than trailing it:** it is the other end of the auto/acceptEdits/prompt spectrum from "Claude decides" — the CLI is expected to hold off touching anything until a plan is approved — so it reads best first, and `PERMISSION_LABELS`/`SWITCHABLE_MODES`/the four-way agreement test (D146-style) all grew the fourth entry together rather than the picker silently offering a mode the backend does not label. Pinned by bridge tests over the server's own stdio JSON-RPC (plain allow, `setMode` present/absent/rejected per `SWITCHABLE_MODES`, the composed deny message, the note's bounding/truncation-marker/character filter, the `WHOLE_TOOL_GRANTABLE` exclusion, the `ANSWERABLE_TOOL` gate refusing answers on a plan, `permChoices("Bash", "plan")` never offering the mode-switch, the note textarea's `maxLength` agreeing with `NOTE_LIMIT`) and by node probes that run the shipping `buildPlanCard` over a stub DOM (markdown routing including a hostile plan through the real funnel, exactly two buttons and no "allow all", the note surviving as a plain string that never reaches the DOM, re-attach/expiry/double-click latch parity with every other card, and the full landing-mode → picker-write-back matrix: an approval always resets the param, off "plan" onto the granted mode or `"prompt"`, while "keep planning" and a losing double-click move nothing). |
| D249 | **The Hugging Face cache gets a page of its own, read-only, sized by what is actually on disk** | Anything in the app that touches a model — a `transformers` import inside a page's Python, a diffusers pipeline, a template someone pasted in, an `hf download` run in a terminal beside the app — writes into one shared cache under `~/.cache/huggingface` (or wherever `HF_HOME` points), and until now nothing in the product ever named that directory. It grows in multi-GB steps, the app is what grew it, and a checkpoint pulled by a page opened once is still there a month later with nothing on screen to say so. §37 makes it a sidebar entry: one row per cached repo, biggest first, with a click through to the folder. **Read-only on purpose** — evicting a blob out from under a half-loaded pipeline is a different feature with a different set of confirmations, and the row already lands in the explorer, where deleting a folder has the file manager's own confirmation rather than a bespoke one. Three details are load-bearing rather than cosmetic: **(1) the cache location follows huggingface_hub's four-variable precedence** (`HF_HUB_CACHE` > `HUGGINGFACE_HUB_CACHE` > `$HF_HOME/hub` > `$XDG_CACHE_HOME/huggingface/hub` > `~/.cache/huggingface/hub`), because hardcoding `~/.cache` would report "nothing cached" on exactly the machines that care most — the ones with a shared model disk pinned by `HF_HOME`. **(2) Size is measured with `lstat`, skipping the snapshot symlinks and de-duplicating hardlinks by `(st_dev, st_ino)`.** Every file under `snapshots/` points back into the same repo's `blobs/`, so the obvious walk multiplies a repo by its revision count: a page whose entire purpose is disk footprint would be wrong by hundreds of GB on a real cache, and wrong in the flattering direction. The same rule makes the per-row sizes sum to the reported total, which is what lets the header state one number. **(3) The sidebar row is gated on the cache directory existing**, so a machine that has never pulled from the Hub is not offered a page that can only ever say "nothing here" — but unlike the Claude-config gate beside it, this property can flip mid-session (the first download creates the directory), so the probe caches a confirmed *yes* for the session and a *no* for only a minute, and the page's own load refreshes it. **Rejected:** (a) folding this into the Preferences page — it is an inventory that changes on its own, not a setting, and it would have been the one section there that answered differently every time it was opened; (b) scanning on focus/return like Mounts does — that walk is every blob in the cache, and re-walking tens of thousands of files each time the window came back is a disk storm for a page nobody was reading, hence mount + an explicit Refresh; (c) reporting apparent size (following the symlinks) as "what you would download again" — two numbers on a row, one of them not a disk fact, for a page whose one job is disk facts. **Accepted cost:** the page cannot show what a repo is *for* (no metadata is read from the snapshots, and nothing is fetched from the Hub), so a repo id someone does not recognise stays a repo id they do not recognise — the folder is one click away, which is where the config files that would answer that live. |
| D250 | **The AI Models page gets three deletions — a repo, a revision, and a prune — and the revision one is refcounted, not a rmtree** | D249 shipped the cache inventory read-only, on the grounds that "what is on my disk" was the whole question and that evicting a blob out from under a half-loaded pipeline wanted a different set of confirmations. The inventory answered the question and immediately raised the next one: a page whose entire purpose is *this is costing you 40GB* and whose only affordance is a link to the folder is a smoke alarm with no off switch.
