So the page now manages the cache, in exactly three widening steps — one repo, one revision of a repo, everything unread for N days — and nothing wider. **The revision case is the load-bearing one**, and it is why this is a server feature rather than a delete button over the explorer: revisions of a repo SHARE their blobs, so removing a revision's snapshot directory and everything under `blobs/` it points at would silently corrupt the sibling revision that was pointing at the same weights. The delete resolves every snapshot's links, subtracts the blobs any other revision references, and removes only the remainder — plus the refs that pointed at the deleted commit (a dangling ref makes the next `from_pretrained` resolve to nothing), plus the whole repo folder when the last revision goes, which is what `huggingface_hub`'s own delete-cache does. The same arithmetic drives what the UI SHOWS: a revision's number is its exclusive bytes, with the shared amount stated beside it, because two revisions of a 7GB model differing in a config file are 7GB shared and a few KB each and a row claiming 7GB apiece would be a lie in the one column this page exists for. **Prune is deliberately not a server-side rule.** The dialog filters the listing on screen by last-read time, shows every repo that qualifies with its size and age, and sends the resulting NAMES — so the thing deleted is the list that was confirmed, not a threshold re-evaluated against state the user never saw, and the atime caveat (`noatime` volumes never update it) is stated where the decision is made rather than in a doc nobody opens mid-delete. **The safety rules are three:** (1) a target is a cache FOLDER NAME, validated as one path segment with a known kind prefix and joined onto the server's own cache dir — a path from a request body would have made this an arbitrary-rmtree endpoint; (2) a symlinked repo folder is refused rather than followed, since it points at another disk and deleting through it leaves the endpoint's scope; (3) the POST carries the D3 `X-Fused` guard, because it removes multi-GB directories and a blind cross-origin POST must not reach it. A malformed revision is an error rather than a fallback to deleting the repo — the widest possible reading of the narrowest possible request is exactly the bug this class of endpoint gets. One more rule fell out of building it: resolving revisions means READING ref files, which bumps their atime, which is the signal prune depends on — so the atime is restored after the read, or the page would quietly exclude from the next prune everything it had just looked at. **Rejected:** (a) a delete on the explorer's folder view instead — it has no idea blobs are shared, so it can only ever offer whole-repo deletion, and would present it as an ordinary folder delete; (b) a server-side `POST /prune?days=` — one round trip cheaper and it deletes things the user never saw, since the threshold would be re-applied to fresher state than the list they approved; (c) an undo/trash — the bytes are re-downloadable by definition, so the honest recovery story is the Hub, not a second copy on the disk we were asked to free. **Accepted cost:** a delete raced against a live download can take a blob out from under it. Detecting that reliably means reading `*.incomplete` markers and racing anyway, so the page does not pretend to: the deletion is what the user asked for, named in a confirmation. |
| D251 | **A card says what the model is FOR and how big the model is — read from the cache, never from the network, and absent rather than guessed** | The inventory could say a repo was called `black-forest-labs/FLUX.1-schnell` and cost 23GB, which is two facts short of the question people actually ask: *what is this, and is it the big one?* Both are answerable from the bytes already on disk. **Purpose** comes from a chain of evidence, best first: the model card's `pipeline_tag` (the Hub's own answer — nothing is inferred when the card came down with the weights), a diffusers `model_index.json`, a sentence-transformers marker, the `architectures` head in `config.json`, a `.gguf` file. Every answer records WHERE it came from and the card shows it on hover, because a `pipeline_tag` is a fact while an architecture is our reading of one, and rendering them identically would be overclaiming. **Size in parameters** is summed from the SHAPES in the safetensors headers — the format opens with a u64 length and that many bytes of JSON, so an exact count costs one small read per shard instead of the multi-GB file — and is simply absent for `.bin`/`.gguf` weights, where the only alternative was file size ÷ assumed dtype: a guess dressed as a measurement, in a page whose whole credibility is that its numbers are real. **Release date is the one ask that was refused as posed.** It is Hub metadata, not cache metadata; answering it means a network call, and this app's first principle is that it reads the local disk. What the card shows instead is `added` — the oldest file in the repo, i.e. when this machine got it — which is a different question honestly labelled rather than the asked one approximately answered. **A rule fell out of it, again:** model cards and safetensors headers are reached THROUGH the snapshot symlinks, so reading them bumps the blobs' atime — the signal prune is built on (D250). Every read in the module now goes through one atime-preserving helper, and the metadata is cached per snapshot directory keyed by its mtime (a snapshot is immutable once written), so a Refresh over forty repos re-reads nothing. **Rejected:** (a) fetching the Hub's model index for real release dates, task tags and downloads — it makes an offline page online, and a cache inventory that phones home about which models you hold is a different product; (b) parsing the front matter with a YAML library — the package does not depend on one, and the two keys that matter (`pipeline_tag`, `library_name`) are always plain scalars, so a documented scalar-only reader that degrades to the config.json evidence beats adding a dependency to read two lines; (c) a curated model_type -> friendly-name table — it is a list that goes stale every time the ecosystem ships an architecture, while the architecture SUFFIX convention has been stable for years and degrades to silence rather than to a wrong answer. |
| D252 | **A cached model gets two read-only views — what it is, and how it tokenizes — as ordinary gated templates on the folder** | The AI Models page (D249–D251) could say a repo was 7.5GB of `image + text to text`, and then "open" dropped you into a folder of `model-00001-of-00004.safetensors`. The folder is the thing people have; making it answer for itself needs no new surface, because the template registry already resolves DIRECTORIES through a universal `/` key and already lets a template gate itself per-path with `condition.py` — which is exactly how `zarr_aoi` appears on Zarr stores and nowhere else. So the two views are ordinary templates sitting beside it: **`model_card`** (name, parameters, config, weights table, largest tensors, files — instant on a 40GB checkpoint because the counts come from safetensors HEADERS, quantization arithmetic included) and **`model_tokenizer`** (a textarea, token counts, and the text painted token by token). **Read-only, and no framework:** nothing loads weights or imports torch, which is what keeps opening a model as fast as opening a text file and is the difference between a view and an inference feature. The tokenizer view is the first template here to need a library the app does not bundle, and it takes the documented route — its own `pyproject.toml` (PY-16), installed by the fused engine on first use — with the page SPLIT so the half that needs nothing (vocabulary size, kind, merges, special tokens, read from `tokenizer.json`) works regardless, and the half that needs `tokenizers` explains its absence instead of failing. **The gates are the load-bearing part**, because the universal key means they run on every folder anyone opens, including folders on remote mounts where a listing blows past the mount's timeout: constant-time probes only, and for the `config.json` case one bounded read to confirm it is a MODEL config, since `config.json` is among the most common filenames there is and every project folder in a home directory would otherwise sprout a model view. **Reads restore atime** (HF-15's rule, inherited): looking at a model must not protect it from the next prune. **Rejected:** (a) a new page in the shell for model detail — the folder already resolves templates, and a second surface would have needed its own routing, its own navigation and its own answer to "what about a model outside the cache"; (b) making `model_card` the default mode for model folders — CT-12 forbids a gated template from being a default while an ungated one is in the list, and rightly: the listing is what a folder IS, so the AI Models card asks for the mode by name instead; (c) reading the tokenizer with a hand-rolled BPE — real tokenization is merges, normalisers and pre-tokenisers, and an approximation of it would produce plausible wrong token counts, which is worse than saying the library is missing. **Accepted cost:** under the built-in engine the tokenizer view shows facts but cannot encode. That is stated on the page rather than hidden, and it is the same trade every dependency-declaring template makes. |
| D253 | **The model templates resolve the cache repo themselves, and the tokenizer playground is built for a runtime that keeps nothing** | Two things the first cut of D252 got wrong, both of them the same mistake: a rule written in one place and not the other. **(a) The entry path.** The AI Models cards navigate to the cache REPO folder (`models--org--name`), which holds no model files — they live under `snapshots/<commit>/`. `model_card`'s reader already resolved that (through `refs/main`, the revision a load would get); the tokenizer's gate and reader did not, so the tokenizer view was never offered from the one page built to open models, even for models that plainly have one. Both now resolve the same two shapes (MV-6), at a cost of one bounded read of a 40-byte ref — no listing, because these gates run on every folder anyone opens. The gate and the reader keep **separate copies** of the rule, since a template is scripts the engine runs and cannot import a sibling module (PY-15/D166); the drift is guarded by a test that pins the two to the same answer over a matrix of folder shapes, the same way the AI Models task labels are pinned across their two evidence paths. **Rejected:** making the gate list `snapshots/` to find a revision when `refs/main` is absent — a gate may not enumerate at all over a mount, and "no ref" is rare enough that failing closed costs a view nobody was promised. **(b) The cache that could not exist.** `tokenize_text.py` held a module-level dict keyed by (path, mtime), with comments claiming a keystroke never re-reads the vocabulary. Every `runPython` is a fresh subprocess (PY-6), so that dict was empty on arrival every time — dead code reading as an optimisation, and worse, comments asserting a property the code did not have. Deleted, and the page restructured around what actually costs: the whole-file parse for the facts is done **once** on load, on its own request channel (RH-9's default channel is the `.py` path, so a keystroke would otherwise supersede the facts call and it would never settle), and keystrokes ask for encoding only. **Accepted cost:** the library's own `Tokenizer.from_file` still runs per keystroke. It is Rust and takes tens of milliseconds, the debounce covers it, and the real fix — one tokenizer held open across calls — needs a resident process, which is a different design from a script the engine runs. **(c)** The gates read files too, so they now restore atime like the readers (MV-5), and the mount shim **drops** `os.utime` on a mount path rather than routing it — the atime discipline is a local-cache concern and a kernel SETATTR on the mount is the call that shim exists to prevent. |
| D254 | **One model view, not two: the tokenizer becomes a section of the model card** | `model_card` and `model_tokenizer` (D252) were two gated templates on the same folder, which meant the mode switcher offered two answers to "what is this?" for one model, and a person who wanted to know how it tokenized had to leave the page describing it. They are one page now. The merge is mostly subtraction — the tokenizer template, its gate, and its second entry in the universal `/` registry list are deleted — and what remains is a **Tokenizer section** below the files table, present only when the resolved revision has a `tokenizer.json`. **The layout now has exactly one owner.** `inspect_model.py` already worked out which revision `refs/main` names in order to describe it; the page hands that resolved `root` to `tokenize_text.py`, which therefore resolves nothing at all. The previous commit had given the tokenizer its own copy of that rule and a test pinning the two copies to the same answer — passing the answer instead removes the drift rather than testing for it, and the copy, the ref parsing and the agreement test all go. **The section is inert until someone types**, which is what keeps the card instant: `tokenizer.json` is routinely tens of MB, and parsing it on open is work nobody has asked for yet. The first keystroke asks for the facts and the encoding together (one visit to one file, not two), every keystroke after that asks for encoding only, and a facts-only call never loads the library at all — the previous shape paid `Tokenizer.from_file` twice on open, once for a page-load facts call that discarded the tokenizer and again for the first encode. **The gate gained one marker and one ordering rule:** `tokenizer.json` is now decisive on its own (a tokenizer-only repo is a real shape, and the merged view has something to say about it), and the `config.json` case probes with `isfile` **before** `os.stat` — over a mount the first can be answered from the listing the endpoint already took and the second cannot, so statting first made every ordinary folder open pay a remote round trip for a name already known absent. **Accepted cost:** `model_card` now carries the `pyproject.toml` declaring `tokenizers`, so the folder has a venv under the fused engine where before it ran on the app's interpreter. `inspect_model.py` stays stdlib-only, so the card renders identically either way and the declaration only ever costs the tokenizer half. **Rejected:** keeping `model_tokenizer` as a second mode for folders the card's gate declines — after adding `tokenizer.json` to the markers there is no such folder, so the second template would have existed only to be equivalent. |
| D255 | **The AI Models page gets a Discover tab: the Hub, joined to your disk — and no download button** | The cache inventory (D249–D251) could tell you what you had and what it cost, and had nothing to say about what you did not have. The obvious answer is "search the Hub", and the obvious objection is "a browser tab already does that" — which is exactly why the **join** is the feature rather than the search. huggingface.co cannot tell you that the model on screen is already in your cache, was last read three weeks ago, and opens for free; this page can, because its sibling tab already measured precisely that. So every result carries a `local` verdict — downloaded, **partly** downloaded (blobs with no materialised snapshot: an interrupted pull, and calling it "downloaded" would send someone to a model that cannot load), or not — and the estimated size sits next to the name **before** the decision, on the same page whose prune dialog exists because caches fill up. **Read-only, deliberately.** There is no download call and adding one is a different decision: gigabytes of someone's disk, a progress surface, a resumable transfer, a half-written cache to clean up after a cancel. Every route is a GET, so nothing needs the D3 guard. **The server fetches, not the page** — one place for the token, the timeout, the TTL cache, and one place to audit what this app sends to a third party; the host is fixed so no request can redirect it, `HF_ENDPOINT` is honoured but validated as http(s), the query is `urlencode`d rather than concatenated, and the sort is a fixed set of names so a client cannot pass a raw field through. **Nothing reaches the network until the tab is opened**, because a local file explorer that quietly queried a third party on mount would be a surprise; the caption names the host, typing is debounced into one request, and identical queries inside the window are answered from memory. The **local** half of the join is deliberately outside that cache — the Hub's answer is stable for the window and your disk is not. **Sizes are recovered from the dtype→count map the Hub publishes, with the same arithmetic and the same `≈` the model card uses locally**, so a model cannot read 16GB on one tab and 8GB on the other; a repo without safetensors metadata reports no size at all rather than a guess someone would plan a download around. **Rejected:** (a) deriving the task filters by reversing the glossary — "image generation" and "video generation" are this app's reading of a diffusers `_class_name`, not tags anyone publishes under, so those filters would have returned nothing; the Hub's real tags are listed explicitly in the module that talks to the Hub, and a test pins each one to a label the glossary explains. (b) fetching from the page — it would have scattered the token, the timeout and the retry policy across a component. (c) caching errors — the network comes back, and the next keystroke has to be allowed to find out. |
| D256 | **Two doors per card, and three controls removed: no Prune, no Refresh, and a tab strip that never moves** | Four changes to the AI Models page, all of them about a control meaning one thing. **(a) The name goes to the Hub; "Explore" opens it here.** A repo id *is* a Hub address, and the page it names holds the licence, the full model card, the discussions and every revision — none of which is on this disk. Opening the local copy is a different act with a different destination, so it gets its own control in the card footer rather than competing for the same click. One control could not serve both: a name that sometimes meant "read about this" and sometimes meant "open the local copy" is a coin flip. The same rule now holds on the Discover tab, where the name previously changed destination depending on whether you had the model — the one place the ambiguity was already visible. Explore is exempt from the hover-to-reveal treatment the ✕ buttons get: an affordance you must hover to discover is one most people never find, and that rule exists to keep a grid of delete buttons from reading as a page about deleting. **(b) Prune is gone.** A dialog that selects models by an age threshold is one confirm away from a multi-GB re-download, and the number it selects on — `lastUsed`, i.e. filesystem atime — is not written at all on `noatime` volumes. The dialog printed that caveat, which was honest and did not help: a caveat cannot make a bulk list built on an unreliable signal safe to confirm in one action. What the signal *is* good for survives — "used 4 months ago" on a card is a fact someone weighs before deleting **one** model. The multi-target delete API stays, because a revision delete uses it. **(c) Refresh is gone.** It asked the user to know when a re-walk of every blob in the cache was worth paying for, which is not a judgement anyone has the information to make. The scan runs once per mount, and the one action that changes the cache from this page — a delete — already answers with the fresh listing it just measured. **(d) The Cached/Discover toggle never moves.** With Prune and Refresh gone the page head holds the tab strip and nothing else, so the toggle is in the same place in every state instead of sliding as buttons appear and disappear beside it. **(e) The model card view links back out too.** Same reasoning one level down: that view reads the folder on this disk, and the licence, the discussions and the other revisions are on the Hub page it cannot show — so its header carries a `Hugging Face ↗` link, built in `inspect_model.py` where `_KIND_PREFIXES` already lives (one owner for "which URL does this kind get"), with the id passed through `quote()` rather than f-stringed into a URL. Absent for a folder that is not a cache repo: someone's own checkout has no Hub page, and a link built from a local directory name would point at a stranger's repo. **Rejected:** keeping Prune behind a confirmation with stronger wording — the problem was never the wording; and keeping Refresh "for large caches" — a cache large enough to want a manual refresh is exactly the one where a re-walk is most expensive to trigger by accident. |
| D257 | **The AI API grows a local tier: a runner registry, resident worker processes, and one door for cloud and local alike** | `fused.ai(prompt)` meant one thing — the Claude Code CLI — while local models lived inside apps. `local_chat` carried its own MLX server, dependency declaration, download reporter and curated model list; the image app carried the same apparatus again for diffusers. Two copies of "how do I run a model", neither reusable, and an AI Models page that knew what was on disk but not what was RUNNING. **The seam is the slash.** `/api/ai` already took a `model`; a value containing `/` is a Hugging Face repo id and therefore local, one without is a Claude alias. Not a heuristic — a Hub id is always `org/name` and no Claude alias has a slash — so a page reaches a local model with no new parameter, an identical streaming shape, and no change to any call that omits `model` (which still means Claude, and is what keeps every page written before this working). **A runner is a FOLDER**, holding a `pyproject.toml` and a `worker.py`, and its environment is built by the existing `envinstall` loader (PY-18) rather than by anything new: fused-render's own venv must stay a file explorer's and must not carry a Metal-only dependency into a Windows wheel, and "torch is downloading" is a progress surface that already existed. **The worker contract is four HTTP routes** (`/health`, `/generate`, `/cancel`, `/quit`) on an ephemeral port the CHILD publishes — anything the parent reserved could be taken between its bind and the exec — with a per-worker token in a header. Adding a capability is writing a worker. **One resident model per capability, auto-evicting**, which is arithmetic rather than taste: two 8GB models on a 16GB machine is a swap storm and a swap storm reads as a hang. **Load is a job, not an awaited request** — a cold load is a multi-GB download, so it returns a job id into the existing download manager under a deterministic `sys:ai-model:<repo>`, and generating with a model that is not resident starts the load and fails with that id rather than blocking for minutes or refusing flatly. **Rejected:** (a) running models in the server process — a wedged model would take the file explorer with it, and the app would stop starting on machines that have never run a model; (b) a pluggy/entry-point plugin system — there is no out-of-tree consumer yet, and an in-tree table with the interface pinned is what makes the second backend cheap without building the machinery twice; (c) resolving the runner from the model id — the same repo can be servable by two backends and the choice belongs to the machine, not to a string. **Accepted cost:** text generation is Apple-Silicon-only in this cut (MLX is Metal), so a Windows user sees the capability listed with its reason rather than silently missing; the cross-platform text runner is a new module in this table and nothing else. |
| D258 | **Jobs get an owner, and the server's ids are reserved** | The download manager was built (D244) for work a PAGE runs: it reports, and the ✕ sets a flag the page reads back and honours, because the server cannot know what stopping means. Local inference (D257) makes the server a reporter too, and there the ✕ can really kill the process — same control, two meanings — so a record now carries `owner` (`page` / `server`). Two consequences worth writing down. **The ids are reserved.** Server ids are deterministic so the AI Models page can join a job row to a repo card by name; that determinism means a page could otherwise post `state: "done"` for a download still running, and nothing could catch it. `POST /api/jobs` refuses the `sys:` prefix, and `owner` follows from the id at creation rather than being settable from a body. **Observing needed an API.** `trackJob` only writes, so a page that starts server-owned work had no way to show its progress; `fused.watchJob(id)` is the read half. Named as trackJob's sibling on purpose: D244 chose `trackJob` over `job` because a bare `fused.job(...)` reads as the job rather than as a handle, which still holds — TRACK takes a spec and creates a row, WATCH takes an id and looks at one. |
| D259 | **"Loaded" is said loudly, and "downloaded" is not said until it is true** | Two reports from the first real use of the AI Models page, and both are the same kind of bug: a state drawn from the wrong evidence. **(a) A 7px bullet is not a state indicator.** Loaded is the only thing on this page that costs something *continuously* — gigabytes of RAM, right now — and it was a small quiet dot in the middle of a card, in a grid where every other card looked identical. A grid is read by SWEEPING before it is read by reading, so the loaded model has to survive the sweep: a filled badge beside the name and a colour change over the whole card, in the same green as the sidebar's live dot (one colour means "running" across this app, rather than one per surface). **(b) Pressing Download made the card claim it was downloaded**, seconds into a 4.6GB pull. Two causes, both of them a page believing the wrong witness. The "you have this" set was built from any `models--org--name/` folder — and `huggingface_hub` creates that folder on the first byte — so the check-mark rule now requires a **materialised snapshot**, which is the same partial-vs-downloaded line the Hub result cards already drew. And a *running* pull was not a state the page could show at all, because a weights-only download appears in no worker row: `GET /api/ai/runtime` now reports `downloading` beside `loaded`, which is also what tells the page to read job rows — without it, an 8GB fetch reported progress to a poller that had gone idle, and the manager eventually declared the row abandoned. **(c) The bytes themselves were nonsense** — "10 / 11 B" for that same 4.6GB model — because `snapshot_download` exposes only its outer *file* counter through `tqdm_class` and the runner reported that counter as bytes. Progress is now measured from the DISK, on a one-second poll that doubles as the heartbeat the stalled-row detector needs. **Rejected:** keeping Discover's own cache walk beside the page's and just fixing its predicate — two walks meant two definitions of "on this machine" and a window where the two tabs disagreed about the same repo, so the page's one walk is handed down instead. **Found while fixing it:** the port-handshake file was named per capability, so two overlapping bring-ups deleted each other's published port; it is per bring-up now. |
| D260 | **"Cached" becomes "Local", and the toggle moves into the URL** | Three small changes to the AI Models page head, one theme: the page should say where things are and let you go there. **(a) The tab is "Local".** "Cached" names the MECHANISM — a Hugging Face cache directory — where the user's question is about the thing: what does this machine have. Local/Discover is the pair that reads; Cached/Discover asks you to know what a cache is first. **(b) The choice lives in the URL** (`?tab=discover`, default omitted), the shape Preferences already uses for its own tabs. Bookmarkable is the small win; the real one is the BACK BUTTON — a two-tab page where back leaves the page entirely makes the browser's own undo useless for the only state on screen. This forced a structural change: `/ai-models` is now the one shell route NOT keyed on the navigation epoch. Every other route remounts on any navigation, which is fine for a page whose data is one small GET, and wrong for this one — remounting to change a page's own view state would re-walk every blob in the cache and throw away whatever was typed into Discover's search. The page subscribes to the URL itself instead; arriving from another route still mounts it fresh, because the branches differ in their children. **(c) The captions' destinations are links** — the cache directory opens in the explorer, the Hub host opens in a new tab. The app IS a file explorer, so printing a path as plain text asks the user to copy it into the thing they are already looking at; and the host line exists to disclose who this page queries, which is worth being able to go and check. The host comes from the endpoint the server reports, so a machine pointed at a mirror by `HF_ENDPOINT` names and links the mirror rather than huggingface.co. **Also:** "43 GB" reads "43 GB total", because a bare figure at the end of a list of per-model sizes looks like one more of them. |
| D261 | **Images join the AI API, and the worker contract moves into one module** | Phase 3 of D257: `fused.ai.image({prompt})` renders locally through the same door text already uses. **The reply settles everything but the pixels.** `POST /api/ai/image` returns a `jobId` AND the path and the seed, chosen server-side — so no second endpoint answers "what did I get", the job record needs no result field, and an unseeded render is still repeatable (a seed invented inside the worker and never surfaced would not be). The reply carries the CLAMPED request rather than the submitted one, because a caller echoing its own numbers would mislabel the picture: a 4096² render at 500 steps is an OOM, not a request. **An image WAITS for its model where text does not.** `fused.ai` fails fast with a job id because a chat box must not hang through a cold multi-GB load; an image caller is already watching a row for work that takes minutes either way, so the wait belongs inside the job. Two rows, two truths — the load reports its bytes to `sys:ai-model:<repo>`, the render its steps to `sys:ai-image:<uid>`, one row per render so a second does not overwrite the first. **The worker contract moved to `runners/worker_base.py`.** The first cut had it inline in mlx_text/worker.py; copying that for a second runner would have put the SUPERVISOR'S contract — the auth header, the status file's shape, the state vocabulary, the disk-measured download — in two places, which is the shape of every bug this feature has had. It is stdlib-only both because anything imported there becomes a dependency of every backend forever and because it makes the contract testable on CI, which neither concrete worker is (Metal; several GB of torch) — `tests/test_ai_worker_base.py` drives the real routes with stub callables. **Quantized components are a table, not a heuristic:** which quantized checkpoint replaces which part of which model is the same editorial judgement the catalog makes, and it decides what NOT to download — FLUX.2 klein's base repo carries the ~8GB bf16 transformer that the ~2.6GB GGUF replaces. **Rejected:** streaming `/generate` for images (an artefact is not a sequence; its progress is steps, and those already have a home in the job row); a result field on the job record (the POST reply already knows everything); and inferring the quantized file from the repo listing. |
| D262 | *(Its app-card half is REVERSED by D269 — a card opens the app's entry PAGE, not the folder's listing. Everything else here — the deleted `/apps/<tag>/<name>` route, the dropped links, `preview.png` as a thumbnail — stands, and D269 revives none of it.)* **The `/apps/<tag>/<name>` app-view route is DELETED — an app card opens the app's FOLDER in the file explorer, as a plain listing with no `_mode` — and old links are dropped rather than redirected. A root `preview.png` becomes an app's / folder's thumbnail** | Three changes with one premise: an app is a folder, and fused-render already has a first-class way to be at a folder. **The route was a second namespace for the same thing.** `/apps/<tag>/<name>` decoded against `fused_dir` (with the virtual `linked` tag resolved through the registry over an extra `GET /api/apps/linked-path` hop the route had to hold a blank frame for) and rendered the folder under `StatView variant="app"`: no breadcrumb, the builder's own sidebar, and the mode switcher pinned to an `APP_MODES` allowlist. So the SAME folder had two URLs and two chromes, and which one you got depended on how you arrived — the D235/PT-14 note that `APP_MODES` "is a curation of the builder view, not a divergence" was true and was also the tell that the pin was buying nothing but the absence of `git`/`graph`/`zarr_aoi`, which are perfectly sensible over an app folder. Deleted with it: the router codec (`APP_ROUTE_PREFIX`, `appUrlForFsPath`, `appRouteSegments`, `fsPathFromAppRoute`), `Preview`'s `allowModes`/`appChrome` — reachable only from that variant, including the "Open in explorer" overflow whose whole job was getting back OUT of the route — `BuilderSidebar` and its Recents section, `apps/builder/lib/recents.ts`, and the `linked-path` endpoint that existed solely to resolve that route's `linked` tag. The server's `/api/apps/recents` routes are deliberately LEFT standing though nothing now reads them: removing a store is a separate decision from removing the surface that displayed it. **Old links are dropped, not redirected** (owner call): `/apps/<tag>/<name>` 404s at the server, which is D243's posture for `?_mode=versions` restated — a permanent alias keeps a dead shape alive in every bookmark that has one. **The card opens the PLAIN listing, not `?_mode=app`** — also the owner's explicit call, and the part with a real trade in it. The card used to pin the mode, which meant it chose the view; the folder's listing is the door to everything (read a file, open a chat, see its history, AND the app view, which `templates/app/condition.py` offers for exactly these folders), so landing there costs one click and buys back every other thing you might have wanted. `?_mode=app` therefore has exactly one producer left, the explorer's own **"Open as app"** button (`apps/explorer/lib/app-button.ts`, which is where `APP_OPEN_MODE` now lives) — because that button IS the explicit request for that view, where a card is not. Its target is an ordinary fs navigation now rather than a builder-route URL, and its "half-known identity falls back to the folder's own page" branch survives for an unchanged reason: `tag`+`name` both present is how `link-status` says "this folder is exactly the shape `condition.py` admits", so without them `_mode=app` would resolve to nothing and fall back to the listing the button exists to avoid. **`preview.png` at a folder's root is its thumbnail**, resolved server-side into a new `preview_image` key on the shared `app_listing.app_dict` shape (so linked apps get it for free) and rendered through `/api/fs/raw`. It precedes the live scaled iframe of `entry_html`, which is honest but shows the page in whatever state it comes up in — empty, mid-load, asking for a file — and costs a whole page load per card; an authored still is a statement about what the app IS. Resolved inside `app_dict` rather than by the caller, unlike `entry_html`, and the asymmetry is the whole comment: `isfile` cannot distinguish unreadable from absent, and does not need to, because both mean "no picture, fall back". One exact NAME, not a `preview.*` search — a user adding a thumbnail should never have to work out which candidate wins. The explorer's folder card ranks it above `index.html` in its peek order and renders it as an `<img>` rather than framing a whole embed page for one picture, leaving the one-live-preview-per-card budget alone. **Rejected:** a legacy rewrite for the old route (above); keeping `APP_MODES` as an explorer-side curation for app folders (the same "two answers for one folder" that the route was); and letting the card carry `?_mode=app` "just for apps with an entry" — that is the route's mistake one layer down, a surface deciding a view on the user's behalf. |
| D263 | **A freshly opened folder auto-selects its first PAGE in rendered order, falling back to its first entry — superseding D240's ANSWER while leaving the rest of D240 standing** | D240 replaced "first file" with "first row", on the argument that the top row is where the eye already is and that a directory peek is harmless. Both halves of that still hold; what it did not weigh is that the rows are not interchangeable as PREVIEWS. The pane exists to show something, and in an ordinary folder the page is the one row that renders **as itself** rather than as a listing of a directory or a dump of text — a folder holding an `index.html` is opened to look at that page far more often than at whatever the sort happened to put first, and under the default dirs-first sort that is reliably a subdirectory. So `autoSelectPath` walks the rendered rows for the first page and falls back to `firstEntryPath` unchanged; an empty folder still selects nothing. **Rendered order, deliberately NOT a favoured `index.html`** (owner's explicit call): the order is the one the user is looking at, so the winning row is the qualifying one nearest the top of their table and re-sorting re-answers the question — where a favoured name would select a row that may be scrolled off screen, and would need a tie-break story of its own the moment a folder held two of them. **The row KIND is checked, not the name**: `build.html` as a DIRECTORY is a real shape (an exported site tree), and peeking it in place of the page the user can see is the bug the name-only test would ship — which re-tightens D240's own loosening of the `byPath` parameter from `ReadonlyMap<string, unknown>` back to `{ isDir: boolean }`, the reading D240 deleted, now for a different question than the one it was deleted for. **`.htm` counts here and does NOT count in `lib/folder-app.ts`**, and the split is the answer to each question rather than drift: the app gate makes a claim the SERVER also makes (`app_listing.app_entry`), so two surfaces have to agree to the letter and a stray `.htm` was a real bug (the review finding that produced that module); this picks a preview row, where the registry binds `.html` and `.htm` to the same mode list so a `.htm` previews identically, and refusing it would land a folder whose only page is `page.htm` on some adjacent text file for no gain in correctness. **Untouched, and each for D240's own reason:** the one-shot TIMING in `Listing.tsx`, the yield to an already-claimed selection (`selectionClaimed` — this changes the answer, not when it is asked), the blindness to the URL (a `?sel=` is seeded into the selection at mount and the yield covers it), and `searchAutoSelectPath`, which is not a folder — results re-rank per keystroke and "the top hit" is the whole of what that one means. SPEC §6 FS-16. |
| D264 | *(Partly superseded by D269: the app DESTINATION is back — a folder with a top-level page previews and opens as that page, at every surface. The app CONCEPT stays deleted exactly as decided here: no `app` template, no `?_mode=app`, no `_app` sentinel, no "Open as app"/"Add as app" buttons, registry still read-only. D269 needed none of them — an entry page is an ordinary file, and the explorer has always opened files.)* **The app CONCEPT is removed from the UI: the `app` template mode is deleted, its buttons with it, and linked-app registration goes because nothing is left to register for. A folder is a folder — it opens as its listing** | Owner's call, and taken to both of its aggressive readings when asked. The visible ask was "no more Open as app / Add as app buttons", but a button removed while its destination survives is a feature you can only reach by typing a URL, so **the destination goes first**: `fused_render/templates/app/` and its `condition.py` gate are deleted, `"app"` leaves the universal `/` key, and with it go `?_mode=app`, `APP_OPEN_MODE`, and the whole `lib/app-button.ts` module — `appButtonSpec`, the link-status probe, both bar renderings, the embed corner chip and the `pane-action-slot` portal that carried the button across the divider. **The pane-only `_app` sentinel goes too, and that is the part that would have been easy to miss.** It existed so a folder with no registry `app` binding still got an app preview in the listing pane; with the registry entry gone it would have become the ONLY carrier, and the concept would have survived intact under a sentinel labelled "Preview". `paneModeList` loses `hasApp` and its hoist rule, and the pane previews every folder as its embedded listing. **The in-page entry rule (`lib/folder-app.ts`) is deleted in the same pass, days after it was added** — it existed to make the button's gate agree with `app_listing.app_entry`, a real bug at the time, and there is no gate left to agree with. `isAppEntry` goes as well, and with it the row menu's "Open App" wording: a `.html` row is opened, like every other row. **Linked apps: registration is removed, the registry is kept READ-ONLY, and that split is the judgement call.** "Add as app" was `linkApp`'s only caller, so `link_app`/`unlink_app` and the `POST /api/apps/link`, `/api/apps/unlink` and `GET /api/apps/link-status` routes are all dead and deleted. Deleting `fused_render/linked_apps.py` outright was rejected on two facts about installs that already have a registry file: those folders still list on the /apps hub under the `linked` tag, and dropping them would be silent data loss from a change that was about BUTTONS rather than about the user's folders; and `export_linked_apps_env` still fed `templates/history`, which asked `is_linked_app_dir` before it would `revert` — a linked folder is the USER's own repository, so an unexported env var would turn a safety refusal into a quiet no-op for exactly the folders it was written for. *(2026-08-13: that second reader is gone with `templates/history/` (D243) — the per-file timeline is now the read-only `git` view section, which writes nothing and so has no refusal to make. The module and the export still stand on the FIRST fact alone, the `/apps` hub listing; `is_linked_app_dir` currently has no caller.)* So the module survives with its readers, `write_entries` is the one remaining way in (tests, and a user editing the JSON), and the `linked` tag stays on the hub for entries that already exist. **Explicitly kept:** the `/apps` hub itself, whose cards open `/explorer/view/<folder>` as plain listings (D262), and `app_listing.py`'s `entry_html`/`preview_image`, which those cards still use for thumbnails — "app" as a WORD for a folder on the hub is not the thing being removed; a second way to LOOK at that folder is. **Noted rather than acted on:** `templates/shared/app_entry.py` now has one template consumer (`claude/app.py`, whose split view frames a folder's page) plus `history`'s per-commit "does this revision render as a page" question, so it stays shared rather than folding into the chat; and the `condition.py` machinery keeps several users (`claude`, `git`, `history`, `zarr_aoi`, `model_card`), so nothing about the gate mechanism changes. *(2026-08-13: `templates/history/` is deleted (D243), so the chat is now `app_entry.py`'s only template consumer — it stays shared anyway, since the server keeps its own copy of the rule and a second consumer is one binding away — and `history` drops off the `condition.py` list, which still has `claude`, `git`, `graph`, `zarr_aoi`, `model_card` and others.)* **This supersedes D262's central argument** — that "Open as app" is the deliberate one door left into the app view, which is why its gate had to be the server's exactly — and the review findings built on it (the `.htm`/dotfile mismatch, the link-status mount refusal, the linked-tag collision carve-out) evaporate with their consumer rather than being reverted. D262's OTHER half stands untouched and is what makes this coherent: a card already opened the folder's plain listing, so removing the app view changes nothing about where a card goes. SPEC §3 (FS-11), §6, §7.2's `/` row, PT-13b, PT-14. |
| D265 | **The AI Models sidebar entry loses its gate — the row is unconditional, and the probe, the session cache and the publish channel behind it are deleted rather than left unused. The empty Local tab answers with a button to Discover** | Reported as "why is AI Models missing from the bottom left". Nothing was broken: HF-8 hid the row on any machine with no `~/.cache/huggingface/hub`, which is most machines that have never run a model, and the reporter's was one. **The gate's premise expired when Discover shipped.** It was written for a page that could only ever READ the cache (D249's read-only inventory), where an entry leading to "nothing here" is a row that wastes a click. Discover (§39) made the page a place you can DOWNLOAD from — the suggested models, through the local runner (HS-1, amended by D258) — so the machine the gate was hiding the page from is precisely the machine that needs it, and the gate's failure mode inverted: it hid the front door because the room behind it was empty. Getting a first model now requires either knowing the URL or downloading a model some other way FIRST, which is a bootstrap the sidebar should not impose. **The whole mechanism goes, not just the condition.** `GET /api/ai-models/status` (one `isdir`), `getAiModelsStatus`, and `frontend/src/shell/aiModelsAvailable.ts` — a module-level cache with two TTLs (a *yes* held for the session, a *no* for 60s because the first download can create the directory mid-session) and a listener set the page published into on load and after deleting the last repo, so a sidebar already on screen would not sit out the TTL with a missing row. Every line of that existed to decide whether to render one `<NavItem>`; with the answer fixed at "yes" it is dead, and this codebase's own precedent (D264) is that a destination with no caller is deleted, not parked. `AiModels.tsx` keeps `exists` — the listing already reports it, and the empty state still distinguishes the two nothings. **The empty state gains a control, because that is what makes the ungated row honest**: "Nothing cached here yet" / "No Hugging Face cache on this machine" now sits above a **Search the Hub** button that switches to Discover. A sentence naming the tab would be an instruction pointing at a strip the user has already scrolled past; the button is the move itself. **Rejected:** keeping the gate and inverting it to "show the row unless the cache is missing AND Discover is unavailable" (Discover is never unavailable — it is a search box); and keeping the probe endpoint for a future caller (there isn't one, and a docstring that says "cheap availability probe for the sidebar entry" beside a sidebar entry that no longer probes is a lie the next reader has to disprove). SPEC HF-8 revised. |
| D266 | **uv's children stop inheriting the app's Python environment, the diffusers runner stops building from git, and a waiting render reads the worker RECORD instead of the worker TABLE** | One user report — FLUX image generation failing in the packaged macOS app — with two independent bugs stacked, and the second is why the first took five attempts to understand. **(a) The environment could not build, and only inside the .app.** `uv sync` was spawned with `dict(os.environ)` (`_env_install_worker`), so py2app's `PYTHONHOME=<App>/Contents/Resources` reached it. uv is a Rust binary and does not care — but a dependency it must BUILD is compiled by a build backend in an interpreter uv creates, and that interpreter inherits the environment: it resolved its stdlib and site out of the bundle, imported the app's frozen `_distutils_hack` in place of the one belonging to the setuptools actually doing the build, and died with `ModuleNotFoundError: No module named 'jaraco.text'`. The traceback names it exactly — bare `_distutils_hack/override.pyc` and `importlib/__init__.pyc` frames (py2app's frozen zip) interleaved with absolute `.py` frames from uv's fresh build env. **Wheels never touch a build backend, which is why nothing else in the app ever hit this**: `mlx_text` declares three PyPI packages, and `diffusers_image` was the only environment in the product with a `git+` pin. Every uv invocation now goes through `_uv_env`, which strips `PYTHONHOME`/`PYTHONPATH`/`PYTHONEXECUTABLE`/`PYTHONSTARTUP`/`VIRTUAL_ENV` — the union of what `engine._child_env` and `supervisor._child_env` already strip, restated rather than imported because this worker must not import `fused_render` (D152) and paired to the backend's list by a test. Safe to strip now for the same reason it was not before: `Contents/lib -> Resources/lib` (D212's symlink) made the bundled interpreter self-locate with no environment at all, and a build smoke asserts it. **(b) The git pin goes anyway.** It existed because FLUX.2 support was on main and nowhere else; `diffusers>=0.39` is the first release carrying `Flux2KleinPipeline`, so the condition its own comment set is met. Beyond the bug this removes an unpinned `HEAD` from a shipped product — any upstream commit could break the runner between one user's install and the next — and a source build on a laptop is minutes of compiling for something a wheel answers. A test now refuses any ` @ ` dependency in a runner declaration; if one is ever genuinely needed, that test is the conversation. **(c) The image row lied.** `_bring_up`'s failure path stamps `state="error"` on the worker AND deletes it from `_workers` in one critical section, so `_wait_ready`'s `current.state == "error"` branch was unreachable: a waiter polling the table could only ever see the model gone, and reported "was unloaded before it could be used" — which reads like a race, so the user pressed generate five times and re-ran the doomed `uv sync` five times (8.5s the first time, ~1.0s after, the git clone being cached). The real error was on `sys:ai-model:` the whole time; the row they were watching was `sys:ai-image:`. `_start_resident` (split out of `load`) now returns the worker record beside the reply, and the wait reads state, error and eviction off that record in ONE locked block — the same lock the failure path writes under, so "table empty, error not yet written" cannot be observed. **"Unloaded" is kept, not deleted**: a record that never errored and was taken away — evicted by another model, unloaded from the page — has no failure to report, and that is what those words honestly describe. **(d) `build_dmg.sh` stops shipping the orphan.** The prune deletes `setuptools`, `pip`, `wheel` and `pkg_resources` but not `_distutils_hack`/`distutils-precedence.pth`, which setuptools installs as top-level names beside the package rather than inside it — so the bundle carried a distutils shim with no setuptools behind it, which is what (a) then imported. Belt and braces: (a) is the fix, this is a shim for a package that is not there. **Not done, and recorded so it is a choice:** each retry still re-runs the whole failed `uv sync` from scratch — `_ensure_venv` raises on the first round's error and nothing remembers that this environment failed a second ago. Worth a short negative cache, but a retry that re-attempts the real work is defensible where a retry that reports a stale failure is not, and the user-facing damage here was the wrong MESSAGE, not the repeated attempt. SPEC AI-2a, AI-9c. |
| D267 | **Exactly ONE collapsible card in the chat log is open — the most recent one — and a click on a card is a permanent per-card override in both directions. Supersedes D246's per-tool `TOOL_OPEN_BY_DEFAULT` list** | The old rule was a list of tool NAMES (`Edit`, `Write`, `TodoWrite`) that opened themselves, and it does not scale with how the agent actually works: a turn that writes four files was four open diffs plus everything the user had opened earlier, so the reply sat below a screen and a half of tool bodies, while a turn that ended on a `Bash` had nothing open at all. Position is the honest predictor of what is still news — the card that just arrived — so `registerCard` opens the newest card and folds the one it replaced, and nothing else is open. **The user's click outranks the policy, forever, in both directions:** a card they opened is never auto-folded, one they closed is never auto-opened, not even while it is the newest. The override is recorded from the SUMMARY's `click` (synchronously, before the disclosure's own default action flips `open`) and NOT from `toggle`, which fires asynchronously and fires for programmatic opens too — so a `toggle` listener cannot tell the user's fold from the policy's. **Overrides are keyed, not held on the element.** The re-attach repair branches and `loadHistory` both rebuild the `<details>` nodes from `segments`, so state on the node is state the user has to re-apply; a tool call is keyed by its `tool_use` id, which is stable across every re-render of that call, and a thinking segment (which has no id) by its index inside a numbered segment container — that survives the 400 ms poll but not a rebuild of its turn, which is the accepted cost of thinking having no identity in the payload. **History replay needs no branch of its own:** turns are restored through the same `renderSegments`, so the last card built is the last card in the log and a restored transcript opens on it alone. `resetCardPolicy()` clears the overrides at the two places that replace the whole visible transcript (the Back-to-chats handler and `loadHistory`), or a card the user folded in one session would come back folded in a session they have never touched. Probes in `tests/test_claude_template_segments.py` drive the shipping renderer over a stub DOM for each arm: newest-only, arrival folding, sticky open, sticky close across a rebuild, an unchanged poll tick, replay, and the reset. |
| D268 | **The once-per-visit history push (D8/PR-3) is spent only on a param write attributable to a USER GESTURE. A write made before this document has seen any interaction — a page seeding a computed default at load — takes D99's coalesced `replaceState` path instead AND leaves the entry pristine (no `fusedParamEntry` flag), so the user's first real change still gets its one entry** | A view that seeds a param during initialization made the browser's Back button permanently unusable. Concretely: an app whose boot path computes a default download folder and writes it with `fused.params.set("dir", …)`. Entry A (pristine, no `dir`) → the view loads → the init-time write PUSHES entry B → the user presses Back → they land on pristine entry A → the shell remounts the view → its boot path seeds `dir` again → pushes B again, truncating the forward branch. The URL bounced straight back, `history.length` never shrank, and there was no number of presses that got out of the view. D8's flag-on-`history.state` design is what makes it a *loop* rather than a one-off: the flag is what travels back with the entry, so the re-seed is always looking at a pristine entry and always qualifies for the push. The principle the old rule was missing: **a param the page computed for ITSELF at load is the as-loaded state, not a step the user took** — a history entry is a record of navigation, and nobody navigated. So it is folded into the entry the user is already standing on, and because `replaceState` leaves `history.state` untouched, the entry stays pristine and the visit's one push is still available for the first thing the user actually does (after which Back correctly restores the seeded default, which is the state they saw on arrival). Detection is the runtime's OWN capture-phase `pointerdown`/`keydown` listeners on its document (plus the target's, when the target is an ancestor shell — a click in the shell's chrome is still the user), setting a sticky `sawGesture` flag. **Not `navigator.userActivation`:** WKWebView is a supported host (§25) and cross-frame activation semantics differ between engines, where a flag we set ourselves from a real event is the same answer everywhere; capture phase so a template that stops propagation on its own controls still counts, and sticky because the question is "has this document been interacted with at all", not "within N ms". Everything D99 owns is untouched — the pending-search overlay, the 400 ms coalescing, the try/catch around every write, the `fused:urlchange` dispatch, the no-op guard — and the seed now takes the *cheaper* path, so the WebKit 100-writes/30 s budget only gets safer. **Rejected:** having views not seed params (the seed is what makes the URL bookmarkable and is the whole point of D8's "URL wins on load"); a per-template opt-out flag (D8 already rejected per-param configuration, and this is the same burden with a worse failure mode — silently trapping Back is not something an author would think to opt out of); and suppressing the push for some fixed window after load (a timer is a guess about how fast a page boots, and a slow `runPython` in the boot path would push right past it). Sibling fix at the template layer: PT-15's `annmode` no-op guard, where a boot default normalising an absent param to its own meaning cost an entry for the same reason — that one is a page not writing what it does not need to, this one is the runtime not charging for what a page legitimately writes. `frontend/src/platform/lib/runtime-params.test.ts` evaluates the shipped `static/runtime.js` against a fake entry stack with a working `back()` and asserts the whole sequence, including the trap. |
| D269 | **REVERSED, on the owner's instruction: a folder with a top-level `.html` IS that page, and every surface that shows or opens the folder shows or opens THAT PAGE. The listing's preview pane resolves a selected folder to its entry page instead of embedding a nested listing, an app card opens the entry FILE instead of the folder, and the server's entry rule widens to the shared one. Supersedes D262's card half and D264's destination half; revives none of their machinery** | The owner's words: *"a folder that has a top-level .html file is an app, and every surface that shows or opens that folder should show/open THAT PAGE — not a listing of the folder."* Two symptoms, one rule. Selecting `task-forge` in a listing filled the preview pane with a nested listing OF `task-forge` — a file table rendered beside a file table, with the thing the folder is FOR sitting in it as one row called `index.html`; and clicking an app card on the apps hub — a card whose picture IS that page and whose title is read out of that page's `<title>` — landed on the same file table. **A card that shows you a page and then hands you a directory is not keeping its own promise**, and the listing is one crumb-click from the page where the page was several clicks from the listing.<br><br>**This reverses a decision that was right when it was made, and the reversal is narrower than it looks.** D262 deleted the `/apps/<tag>/<name>` route and D264 the `app` template behind it; with no "open as an app" left, the folder's listing looked like the only destination there was. It was not. The entry page is an ORDINARY FILE, and the explorer has always been able to open one, so **D269 brings back none of the machinery those two removed** — no builder route, no `app` template (`templates/app/` stays deleted; the `/` registry key keeps its seven directory modes and gains no eighth), no `?_mode=app`, no `_app` pane sentinel, no "Open as app" / "Add as app" buttons, and linked-app registration stays read-only. What comes back is a DESTINATION, not a CONCEPT.<br><br>**Resolve-at-the-edge, not a conditional template mode** — the design choice, and the reason this is ~40 lines rather than a template. The pane RETARGETS the selected row: a directory row is resolved to its entry page and everything downstream (stat → template list → the `_render` iframe → the expand button) runs on the page, unchanged, exactly as it does for a page the user clicked directly. A card does the same with the `entry` the server already reports. Nothing new renders anything; the folder branch that remains is the one for a folder that genuinely has no page. *Rejected: reintroducing a gated `app` directory mode. It would have meant a new template folder, a `condition.py` gate, a new row in the universal `/` key, a hoist rule in `paneModeList` — and a second answer to "which page is this folder" for D264 to delete again the next time the app concept goes out of fashion.*<br><br>**ONE rule, now in three places instead of two, and pinned by a parity test.** The canonical statement is `templates/shared/app_entry.py::entry_html` — `index.html` if present, else the first non-hidden top-level `.html` in NAME order. `fused_render/app_listing.py::app_entry` (behind `GET /api/apps`) was deliberately NARROWER: the single top-level html, with several meaning `None` — "ambiguous, so open the folder". **That divergence is deleted, not preserved**, because it produced precisely the outcome the owner's rule forbids: a two-page app whose card opened a file listing. "Ambiguous" was never a better answer than the deterministic first page — it is the same answer the chat pane, the history template and the auto-select have all been giving for that folder already. Its ONE remaining divergence is kept and asserted: `app_entry` RAISES `OSError` where the shared copy returns `None`, so `two_level_apps` can still tell "unreadable, skip it" from "no entry, list it as a folder". The third copy is the shell's (`apps/explorer/lib/app-entry.ts`), needed because the pane resolves a folder from a listing it already has in hand and no endpoint answers the question; `tests/test_shared_app_entry.py` asks both Python copies the same nine folder shapes, and the bun suite asks the TypeScript one the same questions. *(Not to be confused with `lib/folder-app.ts`, the narrow gate D264 deleted: `lib/app-entry.ts` is a successor on the broad rule, not that module returning.)*<br><br>**Deliberately NOT changed.** Navigating INTO a folder (`/explorer/view/<folder>`) still opens the folder's LISTING with its page auto-selected in the pane (FS-16 / D263) — the owner did not ask for a folder view to become a page view, and "show me what is in here" is a different request from "open this app". `.htm` still counts for that auto-selection and still does not count as an entry page, for D263's own reason. Community/showcase cards still open a cloned app's folder: the catalog payload carries no entry, and guessing `index.html` for a folder the server never resolved would be the one thing this decision is against. SPEC §3 (FS-11, FS-16), §7.2's `/` row. |
| D270 | **A coalesced history write is queued as a key→value DELTA plus the pathname it was aimed at — never as a formed URL. The flush MERGES the delta into the live location; a history TRAVERSAL or a PATHNAME change discards it instead. And `popstate` joins `fused:urlchange` as a notification source, because a traversal changes the visible params while writing nothing. Amends D99, and is what makes PR-4 true off the browser shell** | D99 defers the write up to 400 ms and, as first built, queued the fully-formed URL to replay at flush time. **A formed URL is a snapshot of where the browser was up to 400 ms ago**, and three things move underneath it inside that window: the shell navigates (a breadcrumb click, a tab open), the user traverses history, or another writer edits the very entry we are about to write (`sel` on every arrow-key move, `_mode` on every mode switch). Replaying that snapshot does not land LATE, it OVERWRITES — five bugs, each reproduced in a browser: a pending write dragged a freshly pushed entry's path back to the old view, so the breadcrumb and the address bar disagreed and a reload or bookmark went somewhere the user had never been; a Back inside the window let the flush land on the entry just traversed TO, destroying the pristine entry and inverting the buttons (Back appeared to do nothing, Forward went backwards); the flush reverted concurrent shell writes to other keys; a pane traversal left the top-level `_layout` stale, so a reload or a bookmark silently undid the Back; and a traversal never re-notified the template at all.<br><br>**So the flush is a MERGE, not a replay.** Only the keys `set()` actually touched are remembered, and the URL is recomputed at flush time from the LIVE `target.location.search` — with the raw `_layout=(…)` span preserved byte-for-byte and emitted last (D51) — so everything the runtime did not touch survives by construction. The pending VALUE is real user intent and is kept; only the stale surroundings are dropped. The same delta is what the pending-search overlay applies, computed per read rather than cached as a string, so the overlay and the flush can never disagree about what the URL is about to say.<br><br>**Two conditions DISCARD the queued write instead of landing it, and both say the same thing: the entry it was meant for no longer exists.** A **`popstate`** — a traversal is a LATER and STRONGER user action than the param write it interrupts, and the write was aimed at the entry we just left; dropping the value is not a loss of intent, it is honouring the newer one. A **pathname change** — a merge fixes the case where the SEARCH moved underneath us but not the case where the whole PAGE did, and folding `zz=8` into a view the user navigated to does not land late either, it INVENTS a param that page never had: visible in the address bar, bookmarkable, and carried by every subsequent write. `pagehide` still FLUSHES, so a genuine unload does not lose the write, and a shell tear-down of an embed frame is caught by the pathname check rather than by that flush.<br><br>**`popstate` is the other way params change (PR-4).** `fused:urlchange` covers writes — the runtime's own `set()` and the shell's wrapped `push`/`replaceState` — and cannot cover Back/Forward, which writes nothing. Both route through the same snapshot-diff guard (D46), so a self-set still never notifies twice. It is listened for on THIS window and on the target/ancestor chain, since which browsing context the event fires in depends on whose entry moved, and any of them moving invalidates a write aimed at the old one; the pending write is cancelled BEFORE the notification, so a listener reading `getAll()` sees settled state rather than the value the user just navigated away from. In the browser shell the gap was masked by App's nav-epoch remount (the view is rebuilt and re-reads its params on a fresh document); on a standalone `/render` page, the hosted stub and the WKWebView popover — the hosts D99 and PR-4 exist for — nothing remounts, so the URL was correct while the template's UI stayed stale indefinitely. The layout shells subscribe to `popstate` per embed frame for the same reason: a pane traversal must re-encode the top-level `_layout`, or the reload/bookmark silently undoes the Back.<br><br>**Caught in the same pass:** `Panel`'s push no longer passes `history.state` through, which copied `fusedParamEntry` into the new entry so it was born pre-flagged and the user's next param write silently got no entry (D8). Latent today — `/panel` sets `_fusedParamBoundary` — and asymmetric with `Tabs.pushHistory`, which never did it. **Rejected:** keeping the formed URL and re-validating it at flush time. There is nothing to validate against: a URL cannot say which of its keys this runtime meant and which it merely inherited from a base that has since moved, which is exactly the information a delta carries for free. Every new test was mutation-checked — six mutants applied, each killed by a named test, then reverted. |
| D271 | **The owner's policy on what belongs in the URL: a default the READER COULD HAVE CHOSEN is stamped into it with `replace`; a value the view DERIVES from something the URL does not record is kept OUT of it entirely, and the MODE that produces it is the bookmark. `fused.params.set()` grows an options object — `{history: "replace"}` and `{default: d}` — to make both cheap to say** | Two audits asked for the same two knobs independently, and the policy is what decides which one a given write wants. **A true default belongs in the URL**: `mode`, `sheet`, `offset`, `sort`, `panel` are values the reader could have picked, so the address bar should show the whole state and a copied link should carry it — the stamp just must not cost a history entry, because it is a description of the state the page already loaded in rather than a step anyone took. **A derived value does not belong in the URL at all**: a map viewport, a fitted camera (D272), a canvas size, an auto-stretch range computed from the pixels currently on screen. `autostretch=1` IS the bookmark; reopening it recomputes the same number from the same data.<br><br>**What derived state in the URL actually did**, in `netcdf` and `h3`: both computed a stretch range from the MAP VIEWPORT — which is not in the URL at all — and wrote it back as `stretch` from a data-loaded callback (`netcdf`'s histogram fetch, `h3`'s `moveend` → `viewportStats`). Every pan therefore spent the visit's one entry (D8) on a number nobody chose, so Back landed on the old derivation over the new viewport: a state that was never on screen. Worse, it was a Back trap BY CONSTRUCTION — Back restores the pristine entry, the restored value disagrees with what the current viewport computes, the queued refresh writes it again, and because a gesture HAS been seen by then, D268's no-gesture gate does not apply, so it pushes over the branch just returned to. Forever. The existing `s !== P.stretch()` value guard never touched this: it stops the render loop, not the trap, because every genuine pan really does change the number. The fix is the general one — keep the derivation in a variable behind an accessor, put only the mode in the URL — and turning the checkbox OFF commits the frozen value, which is a real choice and earns its one entry. Old bookmarks still open: the value they carry was derived from a viewport they never recorded, so restoring it was wrong anyway. A second-order bug fell out with it — writing `stretch` first re-entered `syncControls()`, which re-asserted `checked` from a URL not yet carrying the new `autostretch`, so the second read wrote back the value the user had just turned off; both templates now read the checkbox once up front.<br><br>**The options object.** `{history: "replace"}` forces D99's coalesced replace path whatever the gesture state and leaves the entry PRISTINE, and two callers need it because neither can be served by a value guard — both genuinely change the URL. The stamp is one. The other is a **load-time write behind an `await`** (`annotate`'s sidecar hydration, `pdf_studio`'s doc fallback): D268's gate is read when the write LANDS, not when boot started, so a click during a Python round trip opens it before the seed arrives and the seed takes a real `pushState`; asking for a replace at the call site is the only thing that can see the difference. `{default: d}` makes an ABSENT param MEAN `d`, so writing `d` over an absence drops — `set()` can only short-circuit on a byte-identical search string, and which absence means which default is per-key knowledge only the caller has. Absence is read through `get()`, so a hand-typed global (D72) counts as PRESENT and writing our own copy over it is a real change; and through the D99/D270 overlay, so a drop inside the coalescing window cannot let a stale queued value land. Composed, `{history: "replace", default: d}` is the load-time write that may or may not change anything. **Both throw on a bad option rather than ignoring it**: silently dropping `{history: "replce"}` hands back the very push the option was passed to avoid, and the symptom — a dead Back press — surfaces nowhere near the cause. Two-arg calls are unchanged. Nine private helpers are deleted where the options subsume them; five are kept, because they encode per-key default knowledge AND choose the history mode, which is the stamp itself.<br><br>**The stamp applies `replace` only on the RESTATING branch, never unconditionally**, and that is the part with a trap in it. Once the default is present, every later restatement is byte-identical and the runtime drops it unaided, while a real change still buys its one entry — so Back keeps undoing a mode switch, a slide move and a page turn. An unconditional `replace` at those shared writers would have silently killed Back for all three. **Carve-out, and it is the only one:** `geotiff`'s auto-stretch is derived but NOT re-derivable, because geotiff records no viewport — so unlike `netcdf`/`h3`/`zarr_aoi`, `autostretch=1` alone is not a bookmark; reopening recomputes from whatever viewport the file lands at rather than the one the link was taken from, and dropping the number would lose the colours from a shared link. It stays in the URL as a `replace`-write. Its old `s !== P.stretch()` guard goes with the others: it never stopped the trap, and it suppressed the ABSENT case, which is exactly the one worth recording. SPEC PR-3, PT-15, PT-17.<br><br>**EXTENDED by two more shapes, neither of which the provenance question reaches on its own.** **In-flight bookkeeping** (`claude`'s `run`): the id of a currently-streaming run, put in the URL so the page can re-attach to it after a reload. All five writes replace — the launch, the poll loop's completion, the two dead-run cleanups in `resumeRun`, and the Chats button's clear — and the four clearing sites compose `{history: "replace"}` with `{default: ""}`. **The composition is not what fixes the bug**, which is why it stood: on a fresh chat every clear writes `""` over an absence and `{default: ""}` drops it unaided. Resume onto a live run and the id is PRESENT, so pressing Chats was a real change made by a real gesture and took the visit's push, leaving an entry whose entire content was a run id that had already finished. The general form is that **a value's LIFETIME decides this as much as its provenance**: a param the page will itself erase before the visit ends cannot be a destination, because the state it names will not exist by the time anyone travels back to it. Pinned over the source rather than through the test harness, because the harness ignores `set()`'s third argument — a site regressing to a two-arg call records the same `[key, value]` tuple and every existing expectation still passes.<br><br>**A draft** (the `git` view's `msg`): the commit message, in the URL and always replacing. It is written on every keystroke and on every AI chunk because the textarea is rebuilt by repaints it does not control and the param is what refills it. Typing is a gesture, so the first character would spend the push on an entry holding half a word. And a draft key is by nature outside the view's structural-repaint comparison — repainting per keystroke would tear the textarea out from under the caret — so Back across such an entry clears the param while `draw` short-circuits and leaves the old text on screen, the URL and the DOM disagreeing until something else forces a repaint. The rule worth stating: **a key the view excludes from its own repaint comparison must never push**, because the entry it creates is one the view cannot honour when the user returns to it. Enforced at the view's `setParams` funnel through a `DRAFT_KEYS` set rather than at the three writers, so a fourth cannot forget, and spread into the caller's options rather than substituted for them, so a later `{default: …}` is not dropped for exactly the keys that most need it. `panel`, `pages` and `ask` are real navigation and keep the push. SPEC PT-17. |
| D272 | **A template records where the READER went, not where the DATA is: a viewer that frames itself on load must not write the resulting camera to the URL** | `zarr_aoi` fitted the map to the store's bounds on load and wrote the resulting camera out as params. **D268's pre-gesture gate does not cover it**: the fit lands after an `await` on the daemon's `/meta`, so any click the reader got in during that wait — the Browse button, the explorer row that chose this file — has already opened the gate, and the fit takes a real `pushState`. And it POISONED THE NEXT VISIT, which is the worse half: the camera params are what boot reads to decide whether to fit at all, so one seeded default made every later open of that store skip the fit and show the whole world. Same shape, same answer as D271's derived values — a fit is the view describing the DATA's extent, and the URL is a record of the reader's navigation.<br><br>**The programmatic move is tagged at the SOURCE** — MapLibre `fitBounds` `eventData`, which rides onto the `moveend` the fit produces — because a fit and a drag settle on byte-identical camera state and neither carries an `originalEvent`. *Rejected: a timestamp window around the fit* — an instant fit and an animated one settle on wildly different schedules, and a window wide enough for the slow case swallows a real drag that starts inside it. *Rejected: gating on `originalEvent`* — the NavigationControl's `+`/`−` buttons have none, so a reader who navigates only by those buttons would never get a shareable URL at all.<br><br>**The same commit closed the always-live controls**, which are the non-camera form of one write that means nothing: a **Reset** that is not disabled when there is nothing to reset (`sqlite`, `geotiff`), a **Load** button over a box seeded from the resolved path, so pressing it merely copied that path into a second param (`geotiff`, `zarr_aoi`), and a **"back to the list"** that is live on a list you never left (`claude`'s Chats button on a session-less chat). Each spent the visit's push on a screen that did not change, leaving a Back press that visibly did nothing — the generalisation is SPEC PT-17. |
| D273 | **The shell tells the user when they're on a stale version — and distinguishes "refresh the page" from "restart the app"** | Before this, nothing signaled that an update had left a tab or a running server behind: install a new DMG, keep using an old page against an old process, and nothing on screen knows. The signal rides the existing ServerStatusBanner probe — it already hits `/api/config` every 5s for down-detection, so the version check parses the body that fetch was discarding and costs zero extra requests. Two distinct staleness cases with two distinct verbs, because conflating them tells the user to do something that won't work. **(a) Tab stale (`update-refresh`):** vite bakes `__BUILD_VERSION__` (read from `fused_render/__init__.py`, same regex as setup_py2app) into the bundle; when the served `version` differs, a refresh fixes it, so the card offers "Refresh page". If the served version is seen to MOVE — across a "down" gap or between two healthy probes (a restart can be quicker than the down threshold, or happen while the tab is hidden) — the page reloads itself without asking: a version only changes under a process swap, the user asked for it or was blocked by it, and views are URL-synced so the reload is lossless. The card is for the mismatch that was already there on first observation (an old tab restored after an update). **(b) Server stale (`update-restart`):** `/api/config` now also reports `installed_version`, read from the bundle's Info.plist (`installed.py`, py2app only — unpackaged runs and Windows/Linux report null and never trigger this).
